Every new statement below is\nproved within this document. In particular an earlier unverified cut identity\nor recursive identity is not a correctness premise.\n\nThe requested decision at q=1/M_m is NOT obtained: no feasible common array,\nand no infeasibility proof for the entire collection, is asserted. What is\nobtained is an exact attained legal-primal interpretation of the collection,\nits common two-jump matrix, a quantitative optimum-source budget, and a\nquantitative rejection of one explicitly constructed scalable common array\nfamily by two cuts already in the collection.\n\n## 1. Exact collection and notation\n\nLet n>=2, h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) have nonnegative finite entries, and put\nS=sum h_i, M=1+S, P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, including P_n=S. Normalize L=1 and\nx_star=g_star=f_star=0. The original independent formal vectors are\n(x_0,g_0,...,g_n); x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. For distinct sample indices\nin I={star,0,...,n}, write\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nChoose ONE finite nonnegative array lambda_ij with finite-node flow\n\n sum_j lambda_ij-sum_j lambda_ji=2*1_(i=n).                 (1)\n\nIts value-free quadratic polynomial is\n\n C_lambda=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij.        (2)\n\nFor every 1<=k<l<=n, require (2) to be nonnegative whenever\n\n g_i=u for i<k, g_i=v for k<=i<l, g_i=w for i>=l,         (3)\n\nwith x_0,u,v,w independent arbitrary formal vectors. The same array is used\nfor every (k,l). This is the collection A_2. One-block and two-block tests\nare included by equality of block values. All the earlier queried-j/tail\ntests are included: for j>0 use k=j,l=j+1,u=0; for j=0 use a two-block\nspecialization. No position moment, optimum edge, or initial coordinate is\ndiscarded in (2).\n\n## 2. One common matrix, with all singular cases\n\nIntroduce chronological jumps d_k=g_k-g_(k-1), 1<=k<=n, and b=g_0. Then\ng_i=b+sum_(k<=i)d_k. This is an invertible linear coordinate change. Write\nthe matrix of (2) in coordinates (x_0,b,d_1,...,d_n) as\n\n              [ C       R ]\n A_lambda =   [ R^T     T ],                              (4)\n\nwhere C is 2 by 2, r_k is column k of R, and T is n by n. A restriction\n(3) is EXACTLY the principal block of (4) on x_0,b,d_k,d_l. Thus the entire\ncollection is equivalent to\n\n C>=0;\n r_k in Range(C) for every k;\n Z_kk>=0 and Z_kl^2<=Z_kk Z_ll for every k<l,              (5)\n Z_kl=T_kl-r_k^T C^+ r_l.\n\nHere C^+ inverts positive eigenvalues and vanishes on Ker(C). To prove the\nsingular statement, diagonalize C. A nonzero component of any r_k in its\nkernel makes the corresponding quadratic unbounded below in a free base\nvariable. Once every such component vanishes, complete the squares in the\npositive base coordinates. The remaining two-variable quadratic has matrix\n[[Z_kk,Z_kl],[Z_kl,Z_ll]], whose PSD criterion is exactly (5). This proves\nboth implications, including zero diagonal entries and C=0.\n\nThere are n(n-1)/2 coupled pair tests of ONE residual matrix Z. This is not\nindependent per-partition feasibility. It is also not full PSD of Z.\n\nHere is an explicit matrix-only distinction. In any three jump coordinates\ntake diagonal entries 1 and all off-diagonal entries -3/4, with every other\njump diagonal 1 and other off-diagonals zero; take C=I_2 and R=0. Every\ntwo-jump principal matrix is positive definite, but the vector with these\nthree jumps equal to 1 has quadratic value 3-9/2=-3/2. This exhibits the\nthree-jump correlation absent from A_2. It does NOT assert that this matrix\nis realized by a nonnegative interpolation array for a particular h.\n\n## 3. Quantitative budget for actual optimum-source edges\n\nSet mu_i=lambda_(i,star), nu_i=lambda_(star,i), and nu=sum_i nu_i.\nSumming (1) gives sum_i mu_i=2+nu. On the constant-gradient subspace\ng_i=b, direct expansion gives\n\n C = [ q                -(2+nu)/2 ]\n     [ -(2+nu)/2        1+2S+sum_i(1+P_i)nu_i ].          (6)\n\nFor the lower-right coefficient, finite edges contribute\nsum_i P_i(out_i^finite-in_i^finite)\n=2S-sum_i P_i mu_i+sum_i P_i nu_i. The finite-to-star position terms\nadd back sum_i P_i mu_i; the optimum-edge norm terms add 1+nu. The x_0\ncoupling is -(sum_i mu_i)<x_0,b>. These are exactly (6).\n\nAt the proposed q=1/M, positivity of C is equivalent to\n\n sum_i (S-P_i)nu_i + (M/4)nu^2 <= S.                     (7)\n\nIndeed its determinant is\n1-1/M-sum_i(S-P_i)nu_i/M-nu^2/4, and its first diagonal is positive.\nIn particular nu<=2sqrt(S/M), with a stricter penalty on early\noptimum-source mass. This uses the actual optimum edges, not a choice to\ndelete them. The budget alone does not prove infeasibility, since nu=0\nremains allowed.\n\n## 4. The legal primal cone represented by the collection\n\nLet V_(k,l) be the subspace of real coordinate lists\n(X,G_0,...,G_n) in which G is constant on the three blocks (3), while X is\nfree. Define the cone\n\n K_2=sum_(1<=k<l<=n) { positive semidefinite matrices with range in V_(k,l) }.\n                                                                    (8)\n\nEquivalently, K_2 consists of finite sums vv^T where each scalar gradient\nlist in v has at most two chronological jumps. This follows by diagonalizing\neach positive semidefinite summand. Conversely each such vv^T lies in one\nof the summands, after inserting an unused cut if necessary.\n\nThus G belongs to K_2 if and only if it has a Gram realization in a finite\nEuclidean space with an orthonormal coordinate system in which every\ncoordinate of (g_0,...,g_n) changes at most twice. Different coordinates may\nuse different cuts. The x_0 coordinate in each of these directions is free.\nThis is substantially more flexible than a whole vector gradient with only\ntwo jumps: many cut patterns coexist in a single trajectory.\n\nThere is also an exact intrinsic test on the incremental Gram matrix. In\njump coordinates write a candidate Gram matrix as [[A,B],[B^T,D]], with\nthe first two coordinates x_0,b. It lies in K_2 if and only if it is PSD\nand D is a sum of PSD matrices each supported on at most two jump indices.\nNecessity follows from (8). For sufficiency choose D=VV^T with every\ncolumn of V supported on at most two indices. Full PSD gives\nB D^+D=B and A-B D^+B^T>=0 by the square-completion argument in Section2.\nThe columns of [[B D^+V],[V]] give the prescribed B,D and base block\nB D^+B^T. Add base-only columns factoring A-B D^+B^T. Every resulting\ncolumn is in an allowed subspace, proving the claim, including singular D.\n\nEquivalently, D admits nonnegative allocations z_ij for ordered distinct\nindices with\n\n sum_(j!=i)z_ij<=D_ii,    z_ij z_ji>=D_ij^2.             (8a)\n\nTo prove this description, group every two-index PSD summand by its support\n{i,j}; its off-diagonal must total D_ij, and its two diagonal allocations\ngive the displayed determinant condition. Conversely insert the PSD matrix\n[[z_ij,D_ij],[D_ij,z_ji]] on each pair and add the nonnegative leftover\ndiagonal entries. The resulting sum is exactly D. This does not require\nthat the two-jump coordinate components be individually interpolable:\nthe full summed Gram matrix and the common function values obey the\ninterpolation constraints together.\n\nThe cone is closed. If G_r converges and G_r=sum_a G_(r,a) is a decomposition\nover the finitely many summands of (8), each summand is PSD and has trace at\nmost trace(G_r). In fixed finite dimension every PSD matrix has norm bounded\nby its trace. Pass to a common subsequence on which all summands converge;\ntheir ranges remain in their closed subspaces and their limits are PSD.\nTheir sum is the limit of G_r and belongs to K_2.\n\nThe cone is full dimensional in the space of symmetric (n+2)-matrices. In\njump coordinates every pair of coordinates occurs in one allowed block\n{x_0,b,d_k,d_l}. Therefore the linear map from the direct sum of the\nsymmetric block spaces onto the ambient symmetric space is surjective.\nIt has a linear right inverse. Starting from identity matrices on all these\nblocks, sufficiently small ambient perturbations can be lifted by this\nright inverse while every block remains positive definite. The sum of the\nembedded identities is consequently an interior point of K_2.\n\nFor n>=3 this is a PROPER subcone of the full PSD cone. Choose v with three\nnonzero jumps. If vv^T=sum_a G_a with all G_a PSD, then every vector orthogonal\nto v has zero quadratic value in each G_a, so Range(G_a) is contained in\nspan(v). Every nonzero summand is therefore a positive multiple of vv^T.\nSuch a summand cannot be supported on an allowed two-jump subspace. Hence\nvv^T is not in K_2. This is a cone statement, not a claim that the particular\nv is a legal GD transcript.\n\nFinally, by (8), a matrix has nonnegative pairing with every G in K_2 if and\nonly if every restriction (3) is PSD. Thus (5) is exactly the dual-cone\ncondition K_2^*, with the original array flows imposed separately.\n\n## 5. Exact attained equality on the actual positive-step family\n\nAssume now h_i>1 for every i<n, as holds for every critical H_m below. Let\nw_2(h) be the maximum of 2f_n over G in K_2, G_(x0,x0)<=1, and every original\nordered constraint Q_ij<=0 including the optimum. Then\n\n w_2(h) = min { q : some common lambda>=0 satisfies (1) and A_2 at q }.\n                                                                    (9)\n\nBoth extrema are attained. The left side is an actual legal smooth-convex\nGD performance over the coordinate class described above, not an aggregate\nof independently chosen functions. In particular w_2(h)<=W_n(h).\n\nHere is a proof including strict feasibility rather than an assumed generic\nstrong-duality theorem.\n\nFirst construct a strict interpolation point within K_2. Put\n\n kappa=min(1,min_i(h_i-1))/4 >0,\n r=kappa/[2(kappa+1)], a_i=r^i,\n g_i=sqrt(a_i)e_i, f_i=(kappa+1/2)a_i,\n x_0=(kappa+2)sum_(i=0)^n sqrt(a_i)e_i+e_extra,             (10)\n\nwhere the displayed coordinate vectors are orthonormal. For i<j, write\ndelta_i=f_i-a_i/2=kappa a_i. Direct substitution of orthogonality into the\ntwo finite interpolation constraints shows they are strict exactly when\n\n                   a_j < delta_i-delta_j < (h_i-1)a_i.   (11)\n\nFor the lower inequality use a_j/a_i<=r and\nkappa(1-r)-r=kappa/2>0. For the upper use\ndelta_i-delta_j<kappa a_i<(h_i-1)a_i. The optimum residuals are\nQ_(i,star)=-a_i and Q_(star,i)=-kappa a_i. Thus ALL ordered residuals are\nstrict. The extra coordinate makes the full Gram matrix positive definite.\nEach gradient coordinate in (10) is supported on one query, hence has at\nmost two jumps; the extra coordinate has zero gradients. The Gram matrix\nlies in K_2. Scale all vectors by one small positive number and all values\nby its square to make the radius inequality strict as well.\n\nThis point may be on the boundary of K_2. Add a sufficiently small positive\nmultiple of the interior point constructed in Section 4 to its Gram matrix,\nholding the values fixed. Finitely many strict linear inequalities remain\nstrict for small enough perturbations, including the radius. A point of a\nconvex cone plus an interior point lies in its interior: translate a small\nball in the cone by the cone point. This gives full conic strict feasibility.\n\nThe primal feasible set is compact. Every feasible Gram matrix is a genuine\nfinite set of Euclidean vectors. Adding Q_(i,star) and Q_(star,i) gives\n||g_i||^2<=<g_i,x_i>, hence ||g_i||<=||x_i||. Inductively\n||x_i||<=product_(t<i)(1+h_t), and all Gram entries are bounded by\nCauchy--Schwarz. Also ||g_i||^2/2<=f_i<=||x_i||^2/2. The cone is closed by\nSection 4; all constraints are closed. Compactness proves primal attainment.\nThe interpolation assertion in accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies a legal\nfinite-dimensional smooth convex function for each feasible full transcript.\nThe maximum is positive: take x_0=1, every g_i=t=1/(2S+1), and\nf_i=t-(P_i+1/2)t^2. Finite-pair and finite-to-star residuals vanish, while\nQ_(star,i)=-t+(P_i+1)t^2<0. This constant-gradient Gram matrix is in K_2 and\n2f_n=t>0. Homogeneous scaling consequently forces an optimal transcript to\nhave ||x_0||=1, so the maximum agrees with the normalized objective ratio.\n\nFor explicit separation, form the open convex set of tuples (s,t,c) for\nwhich some G in K_2 and real f obey s_ij>Q_ij, t>G_(x0,x0)-1, c<2f_n.\nThe point (0,0,w_2) is outside. Separate it, obtaining nonnegative\ncoefficients (lambda,rho,eta) on (s,t,-c). If eta=0, substitution of the\nstrict feasible point makes the limiting separation inequality strictly\nnegative, contrary to separation. Hence eta>0; normalize eta=1. Because\nthe values f_i are unrestricted in the separation inequality, their\ncoefficients vanish and give exactly (1). Scaling G in K_2 implies that\nthe matrix of C_lambda with q=rho lies in K_2^*. Setting G=f=0 gives\nrho<=w_2. The matrix condition and lambda>=0 give the reverse weak-duality\ninequality w_2<=rho. Thus equality holds and the common array is attained.\nThis is the same elementary separation mechanism as the accepted full-cone\nproof, applied here only after proving its needed hypotheses for K_2.\n\nConsequently, on H_m, failure of the entire common-array relaxation at\nq=1/M_m would have an actual legal witness in this coordinate class with\n2f_n>1/M_m. This conclusion relies on (9), not on regarding any formal\nnegative vector of a particular certificate as a legal trajectory.\n\n## 6. An explicit scalable common array that fails a retained two-cut test\n\nThis section screens one concrete construction; it does NOT disprove common\narray feasibility in (9).\n\nFor a schedule a of sum A-1, define the intrinsic polynomial\n\n V_a=2sum_i a_i(f_i-f_end)+sum_i a_i(1-a_i)||g_i||^2\n                                      -(A-1)A||g_end||^2.\n\nTake two identical step lists of capacity A, separated by a physical bridge\n1+r, and put t=r/A, T=(2+t)A. (12)\n\nFor a direct coefficient derivation, put C_r=G+rQ_(p,z)+J. No\npositivity of delta is used in this polynomial identity.\n\nStart with the empty array at a leaf. Recursively retain the left array,\nmultiply the right array by k, and add uG+vQ_(p,z)+jcoef J. All actual\ncoefficients are nonnegative for r>=0. If R_left and R_right are the two\nvalue-free intrinsic remainders, the joined remainder is exactly\n\n R_join=R_left+kR_right+delta||W-Ap||^2.                  (13)\n\nNow fix m>=128 and, for this WHOLE horizon, set\n\n sigma=1+sqrt(2), c=sigma(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j),\n r_j=a c^j, M_j=1+sum H_j,\n t_j=r_j/M_j, delta_j=2-(1+t_j)^2.                       (14)\n\nThe parameters are not recomputed inside the recursion. Counting gives\n\n M_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j,\n A_mass=a/(c-2),\n t_j=(c-2)/[1+B(2/c)^j],\n B=(1-A_mass)/A_mass\n   =[(sigma^2+2)m+sigma^2]/[m(m-1)].                    (15)\n\nFor m>=128, 0<B<8/m, 2/c<5/6 and 0<c-2<1/2. The first estimate follows\nfrom sigma^2=3+2sqrt(2)<35/6 and m>83; the others follow from\n12/5<sigma<17/7 and m>=128. For j=m-2 or m-1, we have j>=12 and\n(2/c)^j<(5/6)^12<1/8. Using 1/(1+x)>=1-x in (15) gives\n\n t_j>(c-2)-(c-2)/m\n     =(sigma-2)+[sigma-(c-2)]/m\n     >(sigma-2)+1/m.\n\nTherefore delta_j<-2/m for these two levels.\n\nUse the actual three-block cuts\n\n                   k=2^(m-1), l=3*2^(m-2).             (16)\n\nWrite U=M_(m-1), V=M_(m-2), r=r_(m-1), s=r_(m-2).\nSet all gradients on the three blocks to respectively\n\n        u0=-(V+s)/(U+r) v0,       v0,       0,           (17)\n\nwhere v0 is any nonzero scalar or vector. The full step displacement is\nD=sum h_i g_i=(U+r)u0+(V+s)v0=0, and g_n=0. All node squares in (13)\nvanish except those at the root and its right child: every smaller node\nhas constant gradients. Thus\n\n R_Hm=M_m^2 { delta_(m-1)(V+s)^2/(U+r)^2\n                             +delta_(m-2)V^2/U^2 }||v0||^2\n      < -8 M_m^2/(25m) ||v0||^2.                       (18)\n\nFor the last inequality use both negative deltas and U/V=2+t_(m-2)<5/2.\n\nThe obstruction survives the whole following objective conversion family.\nFor any 0<alpha<=1/S with S=M_m-1, multiply the intrinsic array by alpha,\nadd lambda_(i,star)=2alpha h_i for i<n, add\nlambda_(n,star)=2beta with beta=1-alpha S>=0, and take ANY q. Every\ncoefficient is nonnegative and (1) holds. Direct expansion gives\n\n C_lambda=q||x_0||^2-2<x_0,alpha D+beta g_n>\n          +alpha||D||^2+2beta<D,g_n>\n          +(alpha S M_m+beta)||g_n||^2+alpha R_Hm.       (19)\n\nTo check (19), expand 2sum h_i Q_(i,star)-V_h as\n||D||^2-2<x_0,D>+2S f_n+S M_m||g_n||^2, and then add 2beta Q_(n,star).\nThe values cancel because alpha S+beta=1. On (17), set x_0=0; then\n(19)=alpha R_Hm<0 independently of q. In particular this array does NOT\npass A_2 at q=1/M_m. Its failure is quantitative and occurs in the already\nrequested collection, with actual moments and full original flows.\n\nThe vector (17) is not a legal adversary: it is a negative test direction\nfor this particular coefficient family. Only a proof excluding EVERY\nnonnegative common array, together with (9), would produce the claimed\nlegal lower obstruction. No such exclusion has been established here.\n\n## 7. Remaining decision and next interface\n\nThe task's central dichotomy remains open. Section 5 converts it exactly to\nan attained legal performance problem on a specified proper Gram cone;\nSection 6 rejects an explicit scalable candidate at every q. Neither gives\nthe common feasible-array formula or the collection-wide lower bound at\n1/M_m that the assignment requests.\n\nAdding all four-interval partitions would test every three-jump principal\nblock in the common matrix (4). It adds the determinant/correlation excluded\nby the explicit example after (5), without yet reverting to arbitrary\nsingleton PEP. This names the new constraint precisely; it is not a claim\nthat those extra partitions are sufficient for the quantitative target.\nThe exact all-schedule F/G comparison and the original target are unchanged.\n\n## 8. General strict feasibility: equality (9) extends to every h>=0\n\nThe restriction h_i>1 in Section5 can be removed. Define the collection for\ncompressed horizons n<=2 to contain the entire original formal space; this\nis exactly the condition of at most two chronological jumps at those horizons.\nWith that convention, (9), primal attainment and common-array attainment hold\nfor EVERY finite nonnegative schedule. Here is an explicit proof.\n\nFirst assume every h_i>0 and n>=1. Write h_min=min_i h_i, S=sum h_i>0, and\nchoose the following strictly positive scalar:\n\n theta=min{1/4, 1/(4S), min(h_min^2,1)/[64S^2(S+1)]}.     (20)\n\nUse the scalar quadratic with curvature theta as a starting transcript:\n\n X_i=product_(t<i)(1-theta h_t),\n gamma_i=theta X_i, f_i=theta X_i^2/2, X_0=1.\n\nAll X_i lie in [1/2,1]. Indeed every factor is positive, and induction gives\nproduct_(t<i)(1-theta h_t)>=1-theta P_i>=3/4. The weaker 1/2 bound suffices.\nThe scalar increments are\n\n d_i=gamma_i-gamma_(i-1)=-theta^2 h_(i-1) X_(i-1)<0,\n eta_i=|d_i|sum_(j!=i)|d_j|, 1<=i<=n.                    (21)\n\nEnlarge the space to have orthonormal coordinates e_0,e_1,...,e_n and set\n\n x_0=e_0,\n g_i=gamma_i e_0+sum_(k<=i)sqrt(eta_k)e_k,\n x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t.                              (22)\n\nKeep the scalar values f_i unchanged. The Gram matrix is PSD because the\nvectors are explicit. Its incremental Gram matrix is\n\n                   D=d d^T+diag(eta_1,...,eta_n).        (23)\n\nThis matrix has an explicit two-index PSD decomposition: on each pair i<j\ninsert [[|d_i d_j|,d_i d_j],[d_i d_j,|d_i d_j|]], and on coordinate i\ninsert the remaining singleton d_i^2. Thus the Gram matrix of (22) lies\nin K_2 by the exact marginal criterion in Section4. That criterion was\nproved directly there and is part of this same candidate proof.\n\nEvery original interpolation residual remains STRICTLY negative. To check\nit quantitatively, the scalar quadratic has, for finite i!=j,\n\n Q_ij^base=-theta(1-theta)(X_i-X_j)^2/2.\n\nFor i<j,\nX_i-X_j=theta sum_(i<=t<j) h_t X_t\n        >=theta(P_j-P_i)/2>=theta h_min/2.\nSince theta<=1/4, it follows that\n\n Q_ij^base<=-theta^3 h_min^2/16.\n\nBoth optimum residuals of this quadratic satisfy\nQ_(i,star)^base=Q_(star,i)^base\n=-theta(1-theta)X_i^2/2<=-theta/16.\n\nOn the added coordinates, sum eta_i<=theta^4 S^2. Consequently each added\ngradient has norm at most theta^2 S, and each added position has norm at\nmost theta^2 S^2. Cauchy--Schwarz bounds the absolute added contribution\nto any finite or optimum interpolation residual by\n\n                         2theta^4 S^2(S+1).             (24)\n\nFor finite pairs, (20) makes (24) at most\ntheta^3 h_min^2/32. For optimum pairs it is at most theta/32: use theta<=1\nand 2theta^3 S^2(S+1)<=2theta S^2(S+1)<=1/32. These estimates leave every\nresidual strictly negative, with the original common values f_i. The\ncoordinate components need not be individually legal; their joint residuals\nhave just been checked in full.\n\nScale the vectors and values to give strict radius, and add a sufficiently\nsmall interior K_2 Gram matrix as in Section5. This supplies the missing\nstrict feasible point for every positive schedule, however small its steps.\nThe compactness and explicit separation proof in Section5 now proves (9)\nand both attainments for every positive schedule.\n\nFor zero steps, compress to the chronological positive-step indices i_0,\n...,i_(p-1), retaining original n as the terminal representative. The\ninterpolation constraints at two identical positions force identical\ngradients, since their summed residual is ||g_i-g_j||^2; the individual\nresiduals then force identical values. Deleting such repetitions preserves\nthe coordinate property of at most two jumps, and duplicating them in an\nexisting Gram realization also preserves it. Hence w_2 for the original\nand compressed schedules is the same, and primal attainment lifts.\n\nTake an attained common array for the positive compression and embed its\ncoefficients on the original representative indices, setting all other\ncoefficients zero. Every original value flow is correct. Each embedded Q\nhas precisely its compressed polynomial after selecting those gradients,\nbecause all omitted steps have coefficient zero. An original two-jump\ngradient list remains a two-jump list on the representative subsequence.\nThe embedded array consequently passes every original test and attains the\nsame q. This argument takes place on the original independent formal\ngradient space and does not impose duplicate-gradient identities in the\ndual. If there are no positive steps, w_2=1 and lambda_(n,star)=2 supplies\nthe full identity ||x_0-g_n||^2 at q=1.\n\nThe explicit orthogonal point (10) is a strict-feasibility device, not a\nquantitative lower witness at the requested scale. More generally, accepted\nfact `0e7e78056dc6258c` bounds EVERY legal orthogonal-gradient trajectory by\n2/n^2. On the actual critical family, M_m<=c^m, n=2^m-1 and c<5/2 for\nm>=128. Hence M_m times this whole subclass's normalized error is at most\n2c^m/(2^m-1)^2, which tends to zero. A useful legal K_2 obstruction must\nuse correlations; strict feasibility alone cannot establish it.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max4_joint_cut_collection", "supersedes_scope_of": "869f567eefca9076", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "0e7e78056dc6258c"], "status": "self-contained unverified candidate; central quantitative dichotomy unresolved"}, "glossary": {"K_2": "Closed cone of sums vv^T where each scalar gradient-coordinate history has at most two jumps; equivalently full Gram PSD plus pair-supported PSD decomposition of its gradient-increment Gram.", "A_2": "All chronological three-interval gradient-constant restrictions with original initial vector and one common interpolation array.", "w_2(h)": "Maximum legal normalized terminal objective with full interpolation and Gram in K_2; equals the attained common-array A_2 minimum for every finite h>=0 in this candidate.", "z_ij_pair": "Nonnegative diagonal allocation to index i in the pair {i,j}, satisfying sum_j z_ij<=D_ii and z_ij z_ji>=D_ij² for the gradient-increment Gram D.", "theta_strict": "The explicit positive small quadratic curvature in formula20 used only for strict feasibility, not for a hard instance at the target scale.", "eta_i_strict": "The added increment variance |d_i| sum_(j!=i)|d_j| in the strict legal Gram construction."}}
{"id": "1f135237f7524f69", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T21:27:17.323147+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Complete all-depth FULL Silver calibration, plus two scoped whole-array continuation obstructions. The actual AP array gives q=2/(1+sqrt(4M^2-3)) and its exact rank-one remainder; a separately derived sharp full array gives q=1/(2M-1). An explicit dense rank-one transport preserves every value/full-matrix identity but has an exact zero-coefficient sign failure at three steps, even after ALL free skew repairs with its prescribed boundary-weight derivative. At every depth, ANY rank-one continuation at either displayed physical-mass rate requires relative boundary-weight derivative growing asymptotically (sqrt2)^m/(2m). Higher-rank full arrays and thin boundary layers remain open; no profile theorem or separation is claimed.", "evidence": "# Full Silver calibration and whole-array continuation tests\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint proof draft. No fact_submit or numerical optimization.\nThis does not establish the original exact profile converse or a separation.\nEvery mathematical assertion below is proved in this draft; no unverified\nshared finding is used as a correctness premise. The accepted interpolation\nand full-certificate framework is fact `1252b8823bb5a290`.\n\nThe source starting point is Altschuler--Parrilo, *Acceleration by Stepsize\nHedging II: Silver Stepsize Schedule for Smooth Convex Optimization*,\narXiv:2309.16530 (2023), equations (3.1)--(3.9), Theorem 3.4 and its proof.\nIts C>=0 convention is C_ij=-2Q_ji in the present notation. The exact local\nPDF and extracted text were read. We reconstruct the needed identities,\nincluding the complete objective and remainder, instead of importing a\ndistance-only statement. The generic skew-completion idea has an earlier\nintrinsic-potential analogue in shared candidate666b5b9b14038a0f; the new\ntests here concern actual full objective certificates and the assigned path.\n\n## 1. Exact coefficients at every depth\n\nSet rho=1+sqrt(2), n_j=2^j-1, and define the undeformed word by\n\n H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+rho^(j-1),H_j).\n\nIts mass is M_j=1+sum H_j=rho^j, since M_(j+1)=2M_j+rho^(j-1).\nNormalize smoothness to1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. All formal vectors\nx_0,g_0,...,g_n remain independent. Put\n\n x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n C_ij=-2Q_ji=2(f_i-f_j)+2<g_j,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2.\n\nHere is one finite-node array L^(j), without optimum entries. At depth0 it\nis zero. In a join let p=n_j, q=2n_j+1, M=rho^j, d=M/rho, and b=1+d.\nEmbed L^(j) on the left and rho^2 L^(j) on the right. Let right weights\nw_i=h_i for p<i<q and w_q=1. For every p<i<=q add rho w_i to L_(p,i)\nand to L_(q,i), omitting the diagonal entry L_(q,q). Add M to L_(q,p).\nAll coefficients are explicitly nonnegative. Repeated contributions add.\n\nDefine\n\n J_j=2sum_(i<n_j)h_i(f_i-f_(n_j))\n                       +sum_(i<n_j)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2.\n\nThe full finite identity is\n\n J_j=sum_(i!=k)L^(j)_ik C_ik+M_j(M_j-1)||g_(n_j)||^2.       (1)\n\nHere is an induction with every nontrivial cancellation displayed. It is\nzero=zero at j=0. In a join write g=g_p, e=g_q,\nP=sum_(p<i<q)h_i g_i, W=P+e, and S=M-1. The quadratic parts of the two\nweighted fans, before multiplication by rho, are respectively\n\n sum_i w_i C_(p,i): -M||g||^2-2d<g,W>-||W||^2\n                                      +sum_right h_i(h_i-1)||g_i||^2,\n sum_i w_i C_(q,i):  ||W||^2-M||e||^2\n                                      +sum_right h_i(h_i-1)||g_i||^2.\n\nThe quadratic part of C_(q,p) is\n(1+2d)||g||^2-||e||^2+2<g,W>.\nThese formulas follow by expanding the definitions and using\n2sum_i<h_i g_i,sum_(p<t<i)h_t g_t>=||P||^2-sum_i h_i^2||g_i||^2.\nThus the added fans and M C_(q,p) have zero g,W cross coefficient\nbecause M=rho d. Their g and e diagonal coefficients are\nM(1+2d-rho) and -(rho+1)M. Their interior diagonal coefficients are\n2rho h_i(h_i-1), exactly the difference between the parent and\nrho^2 times the right child. Their value coefficients are\n\n f_p: 2M(rho-1)=2(S+b),\n f_q: 2rho(M-2)+2M=2[(rho^2-2)S-b],\n f_i, p<i<q: -4rho h_i=2(1-rho^2)h_i.\n\nThey therefore equal all value coefficients of J_(j+1)-J_left-rho^2 J_right.\nAfter using both child identities, the g diagonal left over is\n\n M(M-1)-d(1+d)-M(1+2d-rho)=0,\n\nand the e diagonal is\nrho^2 M(M-1)+(rho+1)M=(rho M)(rho M-1).\nThis proves (1) at every depth.\n\nWrite M=M_m, S=M-1, v=2M-1. A sharp full objective array in the current\nQ convention is\n\n Lambda_ik=2L^(m)_ki/v for finite i!=k,\n Lambda_(i,star)=2h_i/v for i<n,\n Lambda_(n,star)=2M/v,\n Lambda_(star,i)=0 for all finite i.\n\nIts COMPLETE certificate, not just a primitive consequence, is\n\n (1/v)||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum Lambda_ik Q_ik\n                         =||x_n-Mg_n||^2/v.                 (2)\n\nTo check this multiply by v and use (1). The query optimum entries give\n2sum h_i f_i-2sum h_i<g_i,x_i>+sum h_i||g_i||^2. The terminal optimum\nentry gives2M f_n-2M<g_n,x_n>+M||g_n||^2. The finite array gives\n-J_m+M(M-1)||g_n||^2. All values cancel because v=M+S. The remaining\nquery terms telescope by\n||x_0||^2-2sum h_i<g_i,x_i>+sum h_i^2||g_i||^2=||x_n||^2.\nThe terminal terms complete the displayed square. This verifies every\noriginal formal-space coefficient. The scalar Huber function with slope\n1/v, quadratic core radius1/v, and x_0=1 attains2f_n=1/v: its iterates\nare x_i=1-(sum_(t<i)h_t)/v>=M/v>1/v. Thus (2) is tight.\n\nThe actual canonical AP objective certificate is different. Define\n\n t=(1+sqrt(4M^2-3))/2, so t^2-t+1=M^2.\n\nIn the C convention its finite coefficients are the SAME L^(m), and its\noptimum entries are C_(star,i):h_i for i<n, C_(star,n):t,\nC_(n,star):S, with the other C_(i,star) coefficients zero. In Q notation,\n\n Lambda_ik=2L^(m)_ki/t,\n Lambda_(i,star)=2h_i/t (i<n), Lambda_(n,star)=2,\n Lambda_(star,n)=2S/t, Lambda_(star,i)=0 (i<n).\n\nAll are nonnegative, and the exact full identity is\n\n (1/t)||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum Lambda_ik Q_ik\n                         =||x_n-tg_n||^2/t.                 (3)\n\nIndeed (1) and the same norm telescope give the C sum\n||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2+2t<x_n,g_n>-2t f_n\n                         -(M^2+t-1)||g_n||^2.\nSince M^2+t-1=t^2 this is the required identity. The source paper uses\nf_n<=r_m||x_0||^2 with r_m=1/(2t); our normalized objective coefficient\nis 2r_m=1/t. No factor2 is suppressed.\n\nBoth (2) and (3) have rank-one complete remainder. Their kernels are,\nrespectively, x_0=sum h_i g_i+M g_n and x_0=sum h_i g_i+t g_n. The\nkernel dimension is n+1 in scalar formal coordinates. These are actual\nall-depth baseline arrays, with different objective constants and optimum\nincidence; they must not be identified with one another.\n\n## 2. Full coordinates and exact singular conditions\n\nLet N=n+1, e the vector of N ones, e_n the last coordinate, and\nV=I+T with T_it=h_t for t<i. For an arbitrary real N by N array A put\n\n b=Ae, a=A^T e-2e_n,\n G=sym(AV)-e_n e_n^T, sym(D)=(D+D^T)/2.\n\nThe actual multipliers are Lambda_ij=-A_ij (i!=j), Lambda_(i,star)=b_i,\nLambda_(star,i)=a_i. All actual signs and flows hold precisely when\n\n A_ij<=0 (i!=j), b>=0, a>=0.                              (4)\n\nExpanding Q, or first canceling its value coefficients by these flows,\ngives the complete matrix\n\n Mcert(q,A,h)=[[q,-b^T/2],[-b/2,G]].                       (5)\n\nAt either baseline put w0=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),tau), where tau=M or t,\nand q0=1/v or1/t, as appropriate. Make the invertible fixed-coordinate\nsubstitution x_0=z+w0^T g. At any nearby parameters the matrix becomes\n\n [[q,r^T],[r,D]],\n r=q w0-b/2,\n D=G+q w0w0^T-(w0b^T+bw0^T)/2.\n\nFor q>0 its PSD condition is EXACTLY\n\n                    D-r r^T/q >=0.                     (6)\n\nAt the baseline r=D=0. If q,A,h are analytic in s, write\nr=s r1+s^2 r2+..., D=s D1+s^2 D2+.... Then D1>=0 is necessary, but\non ker D1 one also needs D2-r1 r1^T/q0>=0. At any subsequent singular\nblock [[B,C],[C^T,E]], the exact conditions are B>=0,\nrange(C) contained in range(B), and E-C^T B^dagger C>=0. Here B^dagger\ninverts B on its range and is zero on its kernel. To see the range\ncondition, a vector in ker B must have zero mixed product with every\nother vector under a PSD form; completing squares on range B then gives\nthe last condition. Thus there is no license to discard mixed blocks.\n\nAt an initially zero A_ij, a one-sided feasible derivative requires\ndot A_ij<=0; at an initially zero component of a or b, the corresponding\nderivative must be nonnegative. Positive coefficients allow signed\nvariations only while their FINAL values satisfy (4).\n\n## 3. An explicit dense rank-one transport, with no unknown PSD\n\nHere we attempt continuation from the sharp baseline (2). The chosen\nphysical path is exactly the assignment, with epsilon=s/m:\n\n c=rho(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,\n H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), j<m.\n\nLet h be that word, M=1+sum h, v=2M-1, q=1/v, and\np=V^(-1)e. Thus p_0=1 and p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i.\nIn a neighborhood of epsilon=0 define\n\n mu=1-sqrt(1-v p_n^2),\n wphys=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),M),\n delta=(mu-wphys^T p)/h_0,\n w=wphys+delta(e_0-e_1).                                (7)\n\nThis is analytic at zero, since there 1-v p_n^2=(M-1)^2/M^2>0,\nand delta(0)=0. It satisfies e^T w=v and w^T p=mu. All w_i are\npositive sufficiently near zero. For n=1 the second coordinate is the\nterminal; the formula still applies.\n\nPut S0=e_n e_n^T+q w w^T and define\n\n U=[e,p], H=U^T U,\n R=[e_n+w-2V^T e_n, q(2-mu)w-p_n e_n],\n P=U H^(-1)U^T,\n Kpart=R H^(-1)U^T-U H^(-1)R^T\n                             -U H^(-1)(U^T R)H^(-1)U^T.\n\nThe two columns of U are independent because h_0>0. Directly,\nU^T R is skew: its first diagonal uses sum w=v=1+2sum h, the second\nuses q mu(2-mu)=p_n^2, and its mixed entries sum to zero using Vp=e.\nIt follows by multiplication that Kpart is skew and Kpart U=R.\n\nAt the undeformed baseline let A0 be the exact array from (2), V0 its\nV, and K0=(A0 V0-V0^T A0^T)/2. Define the ACTUAL proposed full array\n\n K=Kpart+(I-P)K0(I-P),  A=(S0+K)V^(-1).                  (8)\n\nThere is no support deletion in (8); it generally creates dense interior\nentries. It preserves A(0)=A0. Indeed every skew matrix with KU=R has\nthe form Kpart+(I-P)Omega(I-P), where Omega is arbitrary skew, because\nthe difference annihilates U on both sides. Taking Omega=K0 recovers\nK0 at zero by this decomposition. Also\n\n Ae=2q w, A^T e=2e_n, sym(AV)=e_n e_n^T+q w w^T.\n\nConsequently the FULL matrix (5) is identically\nq[1;-w][1;-w]^T. All nullspace, mixed-block and range requirements hold\nexactly. Optimum signs hold locally because w>0 and a=0. The remaining\nindispensable condition is A_ij<=0 for EVERY distinct pair. It is not\nimplied by the identities just proved.\n\n## 4. Exact sign failure, including all free skew repairs at three steps\n\nFor m=2 write r=sqrt(2). At epsilon=0, h=(r,2,r), M=3+2r,\nv=5+4r. The baseline A0, with all entries divided by7, is\n\n [[6-2r, 10-8r, 0, 0],\n  [-6+2r, -8+12r, 0, -6+2r],\n  [0, 4-6r, 10+6r, 2-10r],\n  [0, -6+2r, -10-6r, 18+8r]].\n\nThe physical derivative with respect to epsilon is\ndot h=(-2/rho,-1,-2/rho). Formula (7) gives dot delta=1-4r.\nDifferentiating the EXPLICIT formula (8), using only product and inverse\nrules, gives\n\n dot A_02=(-302+467r)/98,\n dot A_30=(-187+221r)/49.                               (9)\n\nFor reproducibility K0, with all entries divided by7, is\n\n [[0,-6+2r,10-8r,-4-r],\n  [6-2r,0,-12+4r,-8-2r],\n  [-10+8r,12-4r,0,-2-11r],\n  [4+r,8+2r,2+11r,0]].\n\nThe values (9) can thus be checked by substituting into (7)--(8),\nwith d(X^(-1))=-X^(-1)(dX)X^(-1). The first value is positive, since\nsqrt(2)>1 and467>302. Because A0_02=0, formula (8) makes the actual\nmultiplier Lambda_02=-A_02 negative for every sufficiently small positive\nepsilon. This is an exact sign counterexample to this proposed transport.\n\nMore strongly, keeping the boundary weights (7), NO choice of the free\nskew part can repair this derivative at depth2. At zero put\n\n u=(r-2,1-r,1,0)^T,\n z=(2r-3,2-2r,0,1)^T.\n\nThey span the orthogonal complement of e,p, where\np=(1,1-r,r-1,2r-3)^T. Hence all alternative skew first derivatives,\nwhile retaining the same A0 and dot w, differ by gamma(u z^T-z u^T).\nAfter multiplication by V0^(-1), their changes at entries02 and30 are\nrespectively gamma and -r gamma. Therefore every such derivative obeys\n\n r dot A_02+dot A_30=(40+10r)/7>0.                       (10)\n\nBoth baseline entries are zero, so both derivatives would need to be\nnonpositive. Equation (10) rules this out. This test permits EVERY finite\ninterpolation pair and every free skew direction for the specified w.\nIt does NOT exclude changing w differently, creating a nonzero PSD\nremainder, relaxing q, or choosing another baseline certificate.\n\nThe accompanying tiny exact Q(sqrt(2)) audit checks all value flows,\ncomplete matrices and derivatives at one and three steps. It is an\narithmetic audit, not a solver and not a correctness authority; the\ndisplayed exact matrices and formulas are the mathematical evidence.\n\n## 5. All-depth stiffness of any rank-one continuation\n\nThere is a separate quantitative necessity that does not fix the two-weight\nchoice (7) or the free skew part. It describes rank-one continuation at the\nsharp Huber rate, not arbitrary PSD continuations.\n\nAt depth m let N=2^m and M0=rho^m. For an integer i let pop(i) be the\nnumber of ones in its binary expansion. On the undeformed Silver word,\n\n p_i=(-1)^i rho^(-pop(i)),\n dot p_i=-(i+pop(i))p_i,                                (11)\n\nwhere dots denote derivatives in epsilon=s/m at zero. To verify this,\nsum_(t=1)^i nu_2(t)=i-pop(i): summing floor(i/2^k) over k gives the\nleft side and summing the binary expansion gives the right side.\nThe product of h_t-1 therefore has exponent -pop(i). Under the physical\npath the logarithmic derivative of h_t-1 is nu_2(t+1)-2, whose sum is\n-i-pop(i). This proves (11), including p_n=-1/M0 and\ndot p_n=(N+m-1)/M0.\n\nLet D=rho^2+2=2rho+3. The physical mass has exact formula\n\n M(epsilon)=A(epsilon)c^m+(1-A(epsilon))2^m,\n A(epsilon)=a/(c-2).\n\nHere A(0)=1, dot A(0)=-D, and hence\n\n                    dot M=M0(m-D)+D N.                (12)\n\nSuppose ANY differentiable full certificate, with every pair available,\nhas q=1/(2M(epsilon)-1), a rank-one remainder q||x_0-w^Tg||^2, and\nstarts at (2). No multiplier formula or support pattern is assumed.\nOn the actual full-curvature quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, all Q_ij are zero,\nx_0=1 and g_i=p_i. Thus\n\n q(1-w^T p)^2=q-p_n^2.\n\nThe baseline selects the analytic minus branch, so w^T p=mu in (7).\nLet dot wphys=(dot h_0,...,dot h_(n-1),dot M), and let\nzeta=dot w-dot wphys. Differentiating the preceding scalar equality gives\n\n p^T zeta=(dot M-M0(N+m-1))/(M0-1)=:Delta_m.             (13)\n\nFor detail, dot mu=[dot M-(2M0-1)(N+m-1)]/[M0(M0-1)].\nAlso wphys^T p=1+(M-1)p_n, whose derivative is\n-dot M/M0+(M0-1)(N+m-1)/M0. Subtracting gives (13).\n\nTightness at q=1/(2M-1) additionally forces every optimum-source\nmultiplier to vanish: on the attaining Huber trajectory every Q_(star,i)\nis strictly negative, while all finite Q and all Q_(i,star) are zero.\nThe PSD certificate must have zero value there, so complementarity gives\nthis assertion. Then A^T e=2e_n and sum w=2M-1, whence sum zeta=0.\nSince max_i p_i=1 and min_i p_i=-1/rho, this also yields\n\n sum_i |zeta_i| >=sqrt(2)|Delta_m|.                    (14)\n\nIndeed subtract the midpoint of the range of p before applying the\ntriangle inequality, and use sum zeta=0. The two-weight derivative (7)\nattains (14), so its failure in Section4 is not caused by excessive\ntotal redistribution in this particular norm.\n\nMore significantly, put w0=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),M0). Since all h_i>1,\nh_i|p_i|=|p_i|+|p_(i+1)|. Also\nsum_(i=0)^n |p_i|=(1+1/rho)^m=(sqrt(2))^m. Therefore\n\n sum_i (w0)_i |p_i|=2(sqrt(2))^m-1/M0.\n\nIt follows that every such rank-one continuation satisfies\n\n max_i |zeta_i|/(w0)_i\n       >= |Delta_m|/[2(sqrt(2))^m-1/M0].               (15)\n\nBy (12), Delta_m/N tends to -1 as m tends to infinity. Consequently,\nin the original s parameter the right side of (15) is asymptotic to\n\n                         (sqrt(2))^m/(2m).            (16)\n\nThus no horizon-uniform bound on the componentwise relative FIRST\nderivative of this rank-one boundary vector is possible, even with the\ncomplete coefficient array available. Natural physical weights have\nbounded relative s derivatives at zero, so the same divergence cannot\nbe hidden by merely counting their physical variation.\n\nThis is NOT a bound on total finite-path change: an analytic path may\nhave a very thin boundary layer. It is NOT an obstruction to PSD\nremainders of higher rank. Such a remainder can absorb the quadratic\nprobe, so (13) need no longer hold as an equality.\n\nThe same phenomenon occurs at the actual canonical source baseline (3).\nKeep q(epsilon)=1/t(epsilon), t^2-t+1=M(epsilon)^2, and assume the\ncomplete remainder remains rank one starting at (3). Set\nwphys=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),t). The quadratic calibration again forces\nw^T p=1-sqrt(1-t p_n^2). Differentiation gives\n\n p^T(dot w-dot wphys)\n                   =(dot M-M0(N+m-1))/(t0-1),         (17)\n\nwhere t0=(1+sqrt(4M0^2-3))/2. To check, use\ndot t=2M0 dot M/(2t0-1),\nsqrt(1-t0/M0^2)=(t0-1)/M0,\nand (t0-1)^2+t0=M0^2. The weighted denominator is now\n2(sqrt(2))^m-1+(t0-1)/M0, so the same asymptotic lower bound (16)\nholds. This calculation allows both directions of optimum incidence;\nit uses only the rank-one full remainder and the specified rate.\n\n## 6. What remains open\n\nThe canonical full baseline has now been calibrated at every depth, and\nthe sharper full baseline is separately explicit. The dense proposal\n(7)--(8) solves its full PSD and all mixed-block conditions identically,\nbut its ACTUAL coefficient signs fail. Even arbitrary free skew changes\ncannot fix its prescribed boundary-weight derivative at three steps.\nAny rank-one continuation at either displayed rate also has the growing\nrelative-derivative requirement (16). These are scoped obstructions,\nnot a claim that every optimal certificate has either shape.\n\nThe assignment permits a higher-rank PSD remainder, different boundary\nweights, nonzero optimum sources, and a relaxed q. Those freedoms are\nnot excluded here. To obtain a separation, one must control the FINAL\nsigns and the exact finite-path Schur remainder uniformly in m, with\nlimsup q_m M_m<e/rho at s=1 (or the corresponding strict fixed-s gap).\nNeither a derivative, the rank-one scalar moment, nor the real-skew\nparameterization supplies that remaining estimate. No such certificate\nor unrestricted obstruction has been proved in this round.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "related_finding_ids": ["666b5b9b14038a0f", "273cc121acfd549e"], "artifact": "research/max_full_silver_continuation.md", "source": "https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.16530"}, "glossary": {"rho": "Silver ratio 1+sqrt(2), called lambda in TASK.", "L^(j)": "Explicit finite C-multiplier array recursively defined in Section1.", "C_ij": "-2Q_ji, the nonnegative interpolation convention used for baseline gluing.", "M": "One plus the physical step sum at the specified depth and path parameter.", "v": "2M-1, the sharp Huber denominator.", "t": "(1+sqrt(4M^2-3))/2, the canonical AP full objective denominator.", "A": "Full operator whose negative off-diagonal entries are actual finite Q multipliers.", "V": "I+T with T_it=h_t for t<i.", "p": "V inverse times the all-ones vector; coordinates are full-curvature prefix products.", "w": "Boundary vector in the exact full rank-one remainder q||x0-w^Tg||^2.", "Delta_m": "The compulsory derivative moment (dot M-M0(2^m+m-1))/(M0-1) in the sharp continuation.", "pop(i)": "The number of ones in the binary expansion of the nonnegative integer i.", "epsilon": "s/m for the assigned physical deformation."}}
{"id": "e8ab1de28a654fb0", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T21:32:35.890862+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Whole-array higher-rank continuation is explicitly feasible at ALL depths and along the ENTIRE assigned physical path, but the constructed repair has an unaffordable objective coefficient. Starting from either actual full Silver baseline, a displayed complete positive array Asafe gives Mcert>=I/2; its analytic mixture with the baseline dominates every physical perturbation and retains all pairs, both optimum incidences and every original formal direction.
