The generic skew-completion idea has an earlier\nintrinsic-potential analogue in shared candidate666b5b9b14038a0f; the new\ntests here concern actual full objective certificates and the assigned path.\n\n## 1. Exact coefficients at every depth\n\nSet rho=1+sqrt(2), n_j=2^j-1, and define the undeformed word by\n\n H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+rho^(j-1),H_j).\n\nIts mass is M_j=1+sum H_j=rho^j, since M_(j+1)=2M_j+rho^(j-1).\nNormalize smoothness to1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. All formal vectors\nx_0,g_0,...,g_n remain independent. Put\n\n x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n C_ij=-2Q_ji=2(f_i-f_j)+2<g_j,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2.\n\nHere is one finite-node array L^(j), without optimum entries. At depth0 it\nis zero. In a join let p=n_j, q=2n_j+1, M=rho^j, d=M/rho, and b=1+d.\nEmbed L^(j) on the left and rho^2 L^(j) on the right. Let right weights\nw_i=h_i for p<i<q and w_q=1. For every p<i<=q add rho w_i to L_(p,i)\nand to L_(q,i), omitting the diagonal entry L_(q,q). Add M to L_(q,p).\nAll coefficients are explicitly nonnegative. Repeated contributions add.\n\nDefine\n\n J_j=2sum_(i<n_j)h_i(f_i-f_(n_j))\n                       +sum_(i<n_j)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2.\n\nThe full finite identity is\n\n J_j=sum_(i!=k)L^(j)_ik C_ik+M_j(M_j-1)||g_(n_j)||^2.       (1)\n\nHere is an induction with every nontrivial cancellation displayed. It is\nzero=zero at j=0. In a join write g=g_p, e=g_q,\nP=sum_(p<i<q)h_i g_i, W=P+e, and S=M-1. The quadratic parts of the two\nweighted fans, before multiplication by rho, are respectively\n\n sum_i w_i C_(p,i): -M||g||^2-2d<g,W>-||W||^2\n                                      +sum_right h_i(h_i-1)||g_i||^2,\n sum_i w_i C_(q,i):  ||W||^2-M||e||^2\n                                      +sum_right h_i(h_i-1)||g_i||^2.\n\nThe quadratic part of C_(q,p) is\n(1+2d)||g||^2-||e||^2+2<g,W>.\nThese formulas follow by expanding the definitions and using\n2sum_i<h_i g_i,sum_(p<t<i)h_t g_t>=||P||^2-sum_i h_i^2||g_i||^2.\nThus the added fans and M C_(q,p) have zero g,W cross coefficient\nbecause M=rho d. Their g and e diagonal coefficients are\nM(1+2d-rho) and -(rho+1)M. Their interior diagonal coefficients are\n2rho h_i(h_i-1), exactly the difference between the parent and\nrho^2 times the right child. Their value coefficients are\n\n f_p: 2M(rho-1)=2(S+b),\n f_q: 2rho(M-2)+2M=2[(rho^2-2)S-b],\n f_i, p<i<q: -4rho h_i=2(1-rho^2)h_i.\n\nThey therefore equal all value coefficients of J_(j+1)-J_left-rho^2 J_right.\nAfter using both child identities, the g diagonal left over is\n\n M(M-1)-d(1+d)-M(1+2d-rho)=0,\n\nand the e diagonal is\nrho^2 M(M-1)+(rho+1)M=(rho M)(rho M-1).\nThis proves (1) at every depth.\n\nWrite M=M_m, S=M-1, v=2M-1. A sharp full objective array in the current\nQ convention is\n\n Lambda_ik=2L^(m)_ki/v for finite i!=k,\n Lambda_(i,star)=2h_i/v for i<n,\n Lambda_(n,star)=2M/v,\n Lambda_(star,i)=0 for all finite i.\n\nIts COMPLETE certificate, not just a primitive consequence, is\n\n (1/v)||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum Lambda_ik Q_ik\n                         =||x_n-Mg_n||^2/v.                 (2)\n\nTo check this multiply by v and use (1). The query optimum entries give\n2sum h_i f_i-2sum h_i<g_i,x_i>+sum h_i||g_i||^2. The terminal optimum\nentry gives2M f_n-2M<g_n,x_n>+M||g_n||^2. The finite array gives\n-J_m+M(M-1)||g_n||^2. All values cancel because v=M+S. The remaining\nquery terms telescope by\n||x_0||^2-2sum h_i<g_i,x_i>+sum h_i^2||g_i||^2=||x_n||^2.\nThe terminal terms complete the displayed square. This verifies every\noriginal formal-space coefficient. The scalar Huber function with slope\n1/v, quadratic core radius1/v, and x_0=1 attains2f_n=1/v: its iterates\nare x_i=1-(sum_(t<i)h_t)/v>=M/v>1/v. Thus (2) is tight.\n\nThe actual canonical AP objective certificate is different. Define\n\n t=(1+sqrt(4M^2-3))/2, so t^2-t+1=M^2.\n\nIn the C convention its finite coefficients are the SAME L^(m), and its\noptimum entries are C_(star,i):h_i for i<n, C_(star,n):t,\nC_(n,star):S, with the other C_(i,star) coefficients zero. In Q notation,\n\n Lambda_ik=2L^(m)_ki/t,\n Lambda_(i,star)=2h_i/t (i<n), Lambda_(n,star)=2,\n Lambda_(star,n)=2S/t, Lambda_(star,i)=0 (i<n).\n\nAll are nonnegative, and the exact full identity is\n\n (1/t)||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum Lambda_ik Q_ik\n                         =||x_n-tg_n||^2/t.                 (3)\n\nIndeed (1) and the same norm telescope give the C sum\n||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2+2t<x_n,g_n>-2t f_n\n                         -(M^2+t-1)||g_n||^2.\nSince M^2+t-1=t^2 this is the required identity. The source paper uses\nf_n<=r_m||x_0||^2 with r_m=1/(2t); our normalized objective coefficient\nis 2r_m=1/t. No factor2 is suppressed.\n\nBoth (2) and (3) have rank-one complete remainder. Their kernels are,\nrespectively, x_0=sum h_i g_i+M g_n and x_0=sum h_i g_i+t g_n. The\nkernel dimension is n+1 in scalar formal coordinates. These are actual\nall-depth baseline arrays, with different objective constants and optimum\nincidence; they must not be identified with one another.\n\n## 2. Full coordinates and exact singular conditions\n\nLet N=n+1, e the vector of N ones, e_n the last coordinate, and\nV=I+T with T_it=h_t for t<i. For an arbitrary real N by N array A put\n\n b=Ae, a=A^T e-2e_n,\n G=sym(AV)-e_n e_n^T, sym(D)=(D+D^T)/2.\n\nThe actual multipliers are Lambda_ij=-A_ij (i!=j), Lambda_(i,star)=b_i,\nLambda_(star,i)=a_i. All actual signs and flows hold precisely when\n\n A_ij<=0 (i!=j), b>=0, a>=0.                              (4)\n\nExpanding Q, or first canceling its value coefficients by these flows,\ngives the complete matrix\n\n Mcert(q,A,h)=[[q,-b^T/2],[-b/2,G]].                       (5)\n\nAt either baseline put w0=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),tau), where tau=M or t,\nand q0=1/v or1/t, as appropriate. Make the invertible fixed-coordinate\nsubstitution x_0=z+w0^T g. At any nearby parameters the matrix becomes\n\n [[q,r^T],[r,D]],\n r=q w0-b/2,\n D=G+q w0w0^T-(w0b^T+bw0^T)/2.\n\nFor q>0 its PSD condition is EXACTLY\n\n                    D-r r^T/q >=0.                     (6)\n\nAt the baseline r=D=0. If q,A,h are analytic in s, write\nr=s r1+s^2 r2+..., D=s D1+s^2 D2+.... Then D1>=0 is necessary, but\non ker D1 one also needs D2-r1 r1^T/q0>=0. At any subsequent singular\nblock [[B,C],[C^T,E]], the exact conditions are B>=0,\nrange(C) contained in range(B), and E-C^T B^dagger C>=0. Here B^dagger\ninverts B on its range and is zero on its kernel. To see the range\ncondition, a vector in ker B must have zero mixed product with every\nother vector under a PSD form; completing squares on range B then gives\nthe last condition. Thus there is no license to discard mixed blocks.\n\nAt an initially zero A_ij, a one-sided feasible derivative requires\ndot A_ij<=0; at an initially zero component of a or b, the corresponding\nderivative must be nonnegative. Positive coefficients allow signed\nvariations only while their FINAL values satisfy (4).\n\n## 3. An explicit dense rank-one transport, with no unknown PSD\n\nHere we attempt continuation from the sharp baseline (2). The chosen\nphysical path is exactly the assignment, with epsilon=s/m:\n\n c=rho(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,\n H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), j<m.\n\nLet h be that word, M=1+sum h, v=2M-1, q=1/v, and\np=V^(-1)e. Thus p_0=1 and p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i.\nIn a neighborhood of epsilon=0 define\n\n mu=1-sqrt(1-v p_n^2),\n wphys=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),M),\n delta=(mu-wphys^T p)/h_0,\n w=wphys+delta(e_0-e_1).                                (7)\n\nThis is analytic at zero, since there 1-v p_n^2=(M-1)^2/M^2>0,\nand delta(0)=0. It satisfies e^T w=v and w^T p=mu. All w_i are\npositive sufficiently near zero. For n=1 the second coordinate is the\nterminal; the formula still applies.\n\nPut S0=e_n e_n^T+q w w^T and define\n\n U=[e,p], H=U^T U,\n R=[e_n+w-2V^T e_n, q(2-mu)w-p_n e_n],\n P=U H^(-1)U^T,\n Kpart=R H^(-1)U^T-U H^(-1)R^T\n                             -U H^(-1)(U^T R)H^(-1)U^T.\n\nThe two columns of U are independent because h_0>0. Directly,\nU^T R is skew: its first diagonal uses sum w=v=1+2sum h, the second\nuses q mu(2-mu)=p_n^2, and its mixed entries sum to zero using Vp=e.\nIt follows by multiplication that Kpart is skew and Kpart U=R.\n\nAt the undeformed baseline let A0 be the exact array from (2), V0 its\nV, and K0=(A0 V0-V0^T A0^T)/2. Define the ACTUAL proposed full array\n\n K=Kpart+(I-P)K0(I-P),  A=(S0+K)V^(-1).                  (8)\n\nThere is no support deletion in (8); it generally creates dense interior\nentries. It preserves A(0)=A0. Indeed every skew matrix with KU=R has\nthe form Kpart+(I-P)Omega(I-P), where Omega is arbitrary skew, because\nthe difference annihilates U on both sides. Taking Omega=K0 recovers\nK0 at zero by this decomposition. Also\n\n Ae=2q w, A^T e=2e_n, sym(AV)=e_n e_n^T+q w w^T.\n\nConsequently the FULL matrix (5) is identically\nq[1;-w][1;-w]^T. All nullspace, mixed-block and range requirements hold\nexactly. Optimum signs hold locally because w>0 and a=0. The remaining\nindispensable condition is A_ij<=0 for EVERY distinct pair. It is not\nimplied by the identities just proved.\n\n## 4. Exact sign failure, including all free skew repairs at three steps\n\nFor m=2 write r=sqrt(2). At epsilon=0, h=(r,2,r), M=3+2r,\nv=5+4r. The baseline A0, with all entries divided by7, is\n\n [[6-2r, 10-8r, 0, 0],\n  [-6+2r, -8+12r, 0, -6+2r],\n  [0, 4-6r, 10+6r, 2-10r],\n  [0, -6+2r, -10-6r, 18+8r]].\n\nThe physical derivative with respect to epsilon is\ndot h=(-2/rho,-1,-2/rho). Formula (7) gives dot delta=1-4r.\nDifferentiating the EXPLICIT formula (8), using only product and inverse\nrules, gives\n\n dot A_02=(-302+467r)/98,\n dot A_30=(-187+221r)/49.                               (9)\n\nFor reproducibility K0, with all entries divided by7, is\n\n [[0,-6+2r,10-8r,-4-r],\n  [6-2r,0,-12+4r,-8-2r],\n  [-10+8r,12-4r,0,-2-11r],\n  [4+r,8+2r,2+11r,0]].\n\nThe values (9) can thus be checked by substituting into (7)--(8),\nwith d(X^(-1))=-X^(-1)(dX)X^(-1). The first value is positive, since\nsqrt(2)>1 and467>302. Because A0_02=0, formula (8) makes the actual\nmultiplier Lambda_02=-A_02 negative for every sufficiently small positive\nepsilon. This is an exact sign counterexample to this proposed transport.\n\nMore strongly, keeping the boundary weights (7), NO choice of the free\nskew part can repair this derivative at depth2. At zero put\n\n u=(r-2,1-r,1,0)^T,\n z=(2r-3,2-2r,0,1)^T.\n\nThey span the orthogonal complement of e,p, where\np=(1,1-r,r-1,2r-3)^T. Hence all alternative skew first derivatives,\nwhile retaining the same A0 and dot w, differ by gamma(u z^T-z u^T).\nAfter multiplication by V0^(-1), their changes at entries02 and30 are\nrespectively gamma and -r gamma. Therefore every such derivative obeys\n\n r dot A_02+dot A_30=(40+10r)/7>0.                       (10)\n\nBoth baseline entries are zero, so both derivatives would need to be\nnonpositive. Equation (10) rules this out. This test permits EVERY finite\ninterpolation pair and every free skew direction for the specified w.\nIt does NOT exclude changing w differently, creating a nonzero PSD\nremainder, relaxing q, or choosing another baseline certificate.\n\nThe accompanying tiny exact Q(sqrt(2)) audit checks all value flows,\ncomplete matrices and derivatives at one and three steps. It is an\narithmetic audit, not a solver and not a correctness authority; the\ndisplayed exact matrices and formulas are the mathematical evidence.\n\n## 5. All-depth stiffness of any rank-one continuation\n\nThere is a separate quantitative necessity that does not fix the two-weight\nchoice (7) or the free skew part. It describes rank-one continuation at the\nsharp Huber rate, not arbitrary PSD continuations.\n\nAt depth m let N=2^m and M0=rho^m. For an integer i let pop(i) be the\nnumber of ones in its binary expansion. On the undeformed Silver word,\n\n p_i=(-1)^i rho^(-pop(i)),\n dot p_i=-(i+pop(i))p_i,                                (11)\n\nwhere dots denote derivatives in epsilon=s/m at zero. To verify this,\nsum_(t=1)^i nu_2(t)=i-pop(i): summing floor(i/2^k) over k gives the\nleft side and summing the binary expansion gives the right side.\nThe product of h_t-1 therefore has exponent -pop(i). Under the physical\npath the logarithmic derivative of h_t-1 is nu_2(t+1)-2, whose sum is\n-i-pop(i). This proves (11), including p_n=-1/M0 and\ndot p_n=(N+m-1)/M0.\n\nLet D=rho^2+2=2rho+3. The physical mass has exact formula\n\n M(epsilon)=A(epsilon)c^m+(1-A(epsilon))2^m,\n A(epsilon)=a/(c-2).\n\nHere A(0)=1, dot A(0)=-D, and hence\n\n                    dot M=M0(m-D)+D N.                (12)\n\nSuppose ANY differentiable full certificate, with every pair available,\nhas q=1/(2M(epsilon)-1), a rank-one remainder q||x_0-w^Tg||^2, and\nstarts at (2). No multiplier formula or support pattern is assumed.\nOn the actual full-curvature quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, all Q_ij are zero,\nx_0=1 and g_i=p_i. Thus\n\n q(1-w^T p)^2=q-p_n^2.\n\nThe baseline selects the analytic minus branch, so w^T p=mu in (7).\nLet dot wphys=(dot h_0,...,dot h_(n-1),dot M), and let\nzeta=dot w-dot wphys. Differentiating the preceding scalar equality gives\n\n p^T zeta=(dot M-M0(N+m-1))/(M0-1)=:Delta_m.             (13)\n\nFor detail, dot mu=[dot M-(2M0-1)(N+m-1)]/[M0(M0-1)].\nAlso wphys^T p=1+(M-1)p_n, whose derivative is\n-dot M/M0+(M0-1)(N+m-1)/M0. Subtracting gives (13).\n\nTightness at q=1/(2M-1) additionally forces every optimum-source\nmultiplier to vanish: on the attaining Huber trajectory every Q_(star,i)\nis strictly negative, while all finite Q and all Q_(i,star) are zero.\nThe PSD certificate must have zero value there, so complementarity gives\nthis assertion. Then A^T e=2e_n and sum w=2M-1, whence sum zeta=0.\nSince max_i p_i=1 and min_i p_i=-1/rho, this also yields\n\n sum_i |zeta_i| >=sqrt(2)|Delta_m|.                    (14)\n\nIndeed subtract the midpoint of the range of p before applying the\ntriangle inequality, and use sum zeta=0. The two-weight derivative (7)\nattains (14), so its failure in Section4 is not caused by excessive\ntotal redistribution in this particular norm.\n\nMore significantly, put w0=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),M0). Since all h_i>1,\nh_i|p_i|=|p_i|+|p_(i+1)|. Also\nsum_(i=0)^n |p_i|=(1+1/rho)^m=(sqrt(2))^m. Therefore\n\n sum_i (w0)_i |p_i|=2(sqrt(2))^m-1/M0.\n\nIt follows that every such rank-one continuation satisfies\n\n max_i |zeta_i|/(w0)_i\n       >= |Delta_m|/[2(sqrt(2))^m-1/M0].               (15)\n\nBy (12), Delta_m/N tends to -1 as m tends to infinity. Consequently,\nin the original s parameter the right side of (15) is asymptotic to\n\n                         (sqrt(2))^m/(2m).            (16)\n\nThus no horizon-uniform bound on the componentwise relative FIRST\nderivative of this rank-one boundary vector is possible, even with the\ncomplete coefficient array available. Natural physical weights have\nbounded relative s derivatives at zero, so the same divergence cannot\nbe hidden by merely counting their physical variation.\n\nThis is NOT a bound on total finite-path change: an analytic path may\nhave a very thin boundary layer. It is NOT an obstruction to PSD\nremainders of higher rank. Such a remainder can absorb the quadratic\nprobe, so (13) need no longer hold as an equality.\n\nThe same phenomenon occurs at the actual canonical source baseline (3).\nKeep q(epsilon)=1/t(epsilon), t^2-t+1=M(epsilon)^2, and assume the\ncomplete remainder remains rank one starting at (3). Set\nwphys=(h_0,...,h_(n-1),t). The quadratic calibration again forces\nw^T p=1-sqrt(1-t p_n^2). Differentiation gives\n\n p^T(dot w-dot wphys)\n                   =(dot M-M0(N+m-1))/(t0-1),         (17)\n\nwhere t0=(1+sqrt(4M0^2-3))/2. To check, use\ndot t=2M0 dot M/(2t0-1),\nsqrt(1-t0/M0^2)=(t0-1)/M0,\nand (t0-1)^2+t0=M0^2. The weighted denominator is now\n2(sqrt(2))^m-1+(t0-1)/M0, so the same asymptotic lower bound (16)\nholds. This calculation allows both directions of optimum incidence;\nit uses only the rank-one full remainder and the specified rate.\n\n## 6. A complete higher-rank analytic repair, with unaffordable exact cost\n\nThere is no qualitative obstruction to analytic continuation when the\nobjective coefficient is unrestricted. The following construction gives\nan explicit full-array path from EITHER baseline, at every m>=2, with\nall actual pairs and both optimum directions strictly positive for s>0.\nIts objective cost is far too large. This is a quantified failed repair,\nnot the uniform rate conclusion requested in TASK.\n\nUse N=2^m, n=N-1, M0=rho^m, and set\n\n Hmax=1+3M0, Lmax=6M0, R=(1+4Hmax)^2,\n d_i=8R^(n-i), D=diag(d_i), Qsafe=4sum_i d_i,\n eta0=1/[2N(1+n Hmax)],\n Asafe=D+eta0(NI-ee^T).\n\nAll physical h_i(s) lie below Hmax, and |h_i(s)-h_i(0)|<=Lmax s.\nFor the latter, differentiate\nrho^(j-1)(1-s/m)(1+s/m)^(j-1), j=0,...,m-1. The j=0 derivative is\nat most2/(m rho) in magnitude. For j>=1 its magnitude is at most\n(j/m)rho^(j-1)(1+1/m)^(j-1)<=3M0. Both asserted bounds follow.\nThe bound (1+1/m)^m<3 follows by expanding the binomial and using\nbinom(m,k)/m^k<=1/k! and sum_(k>=0)1/k!<3.\n\nFirst omit the eta0 term. The gradient block is sym(DV)-e_n e_n^T.\nAfter congruence by D^(-1/2), its off-diagonal entries have absolute\nvalues at most (Hmax/2)R^(-|i-j|/2). The sum of absolute off-diagonal\nentries in each row is at most Hmax/(sqrt(R)-1)=1/4. Its diagonal is1,\nexcept the terminal value1-1/8=7/8. The inequality\n2|z_i z_j|<=z_i^2+z_j^2 therefore proves\n\n sym(DV)-e_n e_n^T >= D/4.\n\nFor every scalar z and gradient vector g, weighted Cauchy--Schwarz gives\n\n Qsafe z^2-z sum_i d_i g_i+g^T[sym(DV)-e_n e_n^T]g\n >=(Qsafe/2)z^2+[1/4-(sum_i d_i)/(2Qsafe)]sum_i d_i g_i^2\n >=z^2+sum_i g_i^2.\n\nHere Qsafe=4sum d_i and min d_i=8. Thus the complete matrix before\nthe eta0 term is at least the identity, including its initial row.\nThe eta0 term has zero row and column sums, so it changes only the\ngradient block by eta0 sym((NI-ee^T)V). Its norm is at most\neta0 N(1+n Hmax)=1/2: NI-ee^T has norm N, and\n||V||<=1+||T||_F<=1+n Hmax. Consequently\n\n                    Mcert(Qsafe,Asafe,h(s)) >= I/2.     (18)\n\nIts actual finite multipliers are all eta0>0. Its optimum multipliers\nare b_i=d_i and a_i=d_i-2*1_(i=n), all strictly positive. This proves\nthe full signs and PSD directly, without an appeal to Slater theory.\n\nLet A0,q0 be either FULL baseline in Section1, and let\nC_A=sum_(i,j)|A0_ij|, K=2 C_A N Lmax. Define\n\n A(s)=[A0+K s Asafe]/(1+K s),\n q(s)=[q0+K s Qsafe]/(1+K s).                            (19)\n\nThe physical h(s) stays the assigned word. The frozen baseline array\nchanges its full matrix by a gradient block sym(A0(T(s)-T(0))). Its\nnorm is at most C_A N Lmax s=K s/2. Hence\n\n Mcert(q0,A0,h(s))>=-(K s/2)I.\n\nConvexly combining this with (18) proves FULL PSD of (19) at every\ns in[0,1]. All final multiplier signs hold by convex combination, and\nevery pair is positive for s>0. The matrix and array are real analytic;\nthe baseline singular kernel and all mixed directions are included in\nthe displayed bounds. Everything is predetermined by m and s.\n\nThe exact cost explains why this does not solve the research task.\nC_A>=sum b_i>=2 for either baseline, so K tends to infinity. For every\nfixed s>0, the fraction K s/(1+K s) eventually exceeds1/2. Therefore\n\n q(s)>=Qsafe/2>=16(1+4Hmax)^(2n).                       (20)\n\nThe resulting certificate level diverges rather than being O(1/M_m).\nThis specific dense repair is feasible but fails the scalar comparison\nby an enormous margin. It rules out interpreting singularity of the\nchosen baseline as an absolute obstruction to whole-array analytic\ncontinuation; an obstruction relevant to TASK must be quantitative.\nNo optimality is claimed for (19), and its cost is not a lower bound on\nother higher-rank repairs.\n\n## 7. What remains open\n\nThe canonical full baseline has now been calibrated at every depth, and\nthe sharper full baseline is separately explicit. The dense proposal\n(7)--(8) solves its full PSD and all mixed-block conditions identically,\nbut its ACTUAL coefficient signs fail. Even arbitrary free skew changes\ncannot fix its prescribed boundary-weight derivative at three steps.\nAny rank-one continuation at either displayed rate also has the growing\nrelative-derivative requirement (16). These are scoped obstructions,\nnot a claim that every optimal certificate has either shape.\n\nThe explicit higher-rank path (19) uses every such sign direction and\nis feasible at every depth, but its actual objective coefficient (20)\nis unaffordable. The assignment permits other higher-rank PSD remainders,\ndifferent boundary weights, nonzero optimum sources, and a relaxed q.\nThose freedoms are not excluded here. To obtain a separation, one must control the FINAL\nsigns and the exact finite-path Schur remainder uniformly in m, with\nlimsup q_m M_m<e/rho at s=1 (or the corresponding strict fixed-s gap).\nNeither a derivative, the rank-one scalar moment, nor the real-skew\nparameterization supplies that remaining estimate. No such certificate\nor unrestricted obstruction has been proved in this round.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["1f135237f7524f69"], "artifact": "research/max_full_silver_continuation.md"}, "glossary": {"Asafe": "Explicit complete strictly positive multiplier operator diag(d_i)+eta0(NI-ee^T) in Section6.", "Qsafe": "4sum_i d_i, the costly objective coefficient of Asafe.", "Hmax": "1+3rho^m, an explicit uniform bound for physical steps along the assigned path.", "Lmax": "6rho^m, an explicit uniform Lipschitz bound in s for each physical step.", "K": "2N Lmax sum_ij|A0_ij|, the exact positive analytic mixing coefficient.", "d_i": "8(1+4Hmax)^(2(n-i)), the diagonal safety weights in Section6.", "eta0": "1/[2N(1+n Hmax)], the positive coefficient on every finite ordered interpolation pair.", "A0": "One of the two completely reconstructed full Silver baseline operators.", "rho": "1+sqrt(2), the Silver ratio."}}
{"id": "b4f25156d6599097", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T21:41:02.862072+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C59 complete finite legal-primal reconstruction candidate: for ACTUAL H6 (epsilon1/6,n63), one explicit convex1-smooth function on R66 with UNIQUE minimizer0 has true-distance-normalized payoff >56/2711>1/50>4/M6. Every one of4160 original interpolation pairs is repaired by an analytic auxiliary coordinate after an exact rational-coordinate audit. The repaired increment Gram cannot have an orthonormal representation with at most SIX jumps per coordinate, so in particular it lies outside the two-jump cone. This diagnoses finite H6, not eventual large-m behavior or the all-schedule target.", "evidence": "# C59: a fully specified legal H6 adversary above 4/M6\n\nStatus: **exact finite reconstruction and joint proof candidate; UNVERIFIED**.\nNo new fact is claimed and no verifier submission is allocated. The only\naccepted mathematical predecessor is `1252b8823bb5a290`, for full\nsmooth-convex interpolation and its explicit conjugate construction.\n\nThis is a finite diagnosis of the actual H6 word, not an asymptotic theorem.\nThe word here uses epsilon=1/6. It is not the length-63 subword obtained by\nfixing epsilon=1/m for a much larger horizon m.\n\n## 1. Statement and exact input object\n\nPut\n\n    c=(7/6)(1+sqrt(2)), a=5(sqrt(2)-1)/7,\n    H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<6,\n    h=H6, n=63, S=sum_(i<63)h_i, M6=1+S.\n\nThere is a completely specified convex differentiable 1-smooth function\non R^66 with unique minimizer0 and an actual GD trajectory for this h\nsuch that\n\n    2(F(X_63)-F(0))/dist(X_0,argmin F)^2\n             > 56/2711 > 1/50 > 4/M6.              (1)\n\nIn particular, this finite word cannot have a full valid upper certificate\nat level1/M6. No assertion is made about eventual large-m feasibility.\n\nThe rational construction data are the lower-triangular integer matrix T\nof order65 and the64 integer values v_i in\n\n    research/max3_c59_h6_rational_primal.json.\n\nThe PUBLIC file's SHA256 is\n\n    7376e26ca81edc6438c0563e008c845ce439e424bd42f9f65c37a07867d6b113.\n\nIts fields `coordinate_integer_rows_lower_triangular` and\n`function_value_numerators` are part of the definition, with the common\ndenominator D=10^15. For reproducibility the complete defining integer\ndata are also appended to the shared proof finding. No numerical solver\nclaim is used as a premise of the construction below.\n\nLet the rational vectors in R^65 be\n\n    v_start=T[0,:]/D,  q_i=T[i+1,:]/D (0<=i<=63),\n    u_i=v_i/D.\n\nDefine the core positions by the EXACT algebraic GD recursion\n\n    z_0=v_start,\n    z_(i+1)=z_i-h_i q_i.                            (2)\n\nHere q_63 is not used in (2), but will be the core terminal gradient.\nThe original numerical Gram is not treated as an exact Gram. These\nrational vectors define their own positive semidefinite Gram by dot\nproducts, regardless of any properties of the source solver output.\n\n## 2. The finite exact checks, with their integer certificate\n\nFor these core data include the zero optimum jet. Define\n\n    Q^core_ij=u_i-u_j+<q_i,z_j-z_i>+||q_i-q_j||^2/2,\n    delta^core_i=u_i-||q_i||^2/2,\n\nwith the star's position, gradient and value all zero. The following\nstrict bounds are verified by integer comparisons of the defining data:\n\n    Q^core_ij < eta=1/500000000000 for EVERY i!=j,\n    delta^core_i > -eta for every finite i,\n    ||v_start||^2 < 1001/1000,\n    u_63 > 21/2000,\n    200<S<256,  h_i>1 for every i.                  (3)\n\nThe first line checks all65*64=4160 ordered pairs, including both optimum\ndirections and both terminal directions. Here are the exact formulas used\nto audit (3), so the finite part is not a feasibility-tolerance assertion.\n\nThe data file gives each step as\n\n    h_t=(A_t+B_t sqrt(2))/54432\n\nwith integer arrays A_t,B_t. These are exactly the recursively defined\nsteps: arithmetic in Q(sqrt(2)) with a=(-5/7,5/7) and c=(7/6,7/6)\nproduces those integer pairs. Let\n\n    G_ab=sum_k T[a,k]T[b,k].\n\nThus <q_i,q_j>=G_(i+1,j+1)/D^2 and\n<q_i,v_start>=G_(i+1,0)/D^2. Set Hden=54432 and define integer arrays\n\n    R_ij=Hden G_(i+1,0)-sum_(t<j) A_t G_(i+1,t+1),\n    I_ij=                  -sum_(t<j) B_t G_(i+1,t+1).\n\nThen <q_i,z_j>=(R_ij+I_ij sqrt(2))/(Hden D^2).\nWhen j=star, set both of these numerators to zero. When i=star, every\nterm containing q_i is zero. Set v_star=0 and the corresponding gradient\nGram entries to zero. The exact pair numerator formula is\n\n    Qa_ij=2 Hden D(v_i-v_j)+2(R_ij-R_ii)\n                   +Hden(G_(i+1,i+1)+G_(j+1,j+1)-2G_(i+1,j+1)),\n    Qb_ij=2(I_ij-I_ii),\n    Q^core_ij=(Qa_ij+Qb_ij sqrt(2))/(2 Hden D^2),    (4)\n\nusing the displayed star conventions. The missing diagonal terms for a\nstar index in (4) are zero.\n\nFor the radical, use the rational interval\n\n    Lrad/10^50 < sqrt(2) < (Lrad+1)/10^50,\n    Lrad=141421356237309504880168872420969807856967187537694.\n\nIts validity follows by squaring the two positive integer endpoints.\nFor each pair an upper-bound numerator with denominator\n2 Hden D^2*10^50 is\n\n    Qa_ij*10^50+Qb_ij*(Lrad+1)  if Qb_ij>=0,\n    Qa_ij*10^50+Qb_ij*Lrad      if Qb_ij<0.          (5)\n\nThe data file contains all4160 integer triples from (4)--(5). Every one\nsatisfies `upper_numerator*500000000000 < denominator`. The largest\nupper bound occurs at the directed pair (31,30) and is approximately\n1.371014491112536e-12; the exact numerator and denominator are retained\nin `audit.max_Q_upper_numerator` and `audit.max_Q_upper_denominator`.\nThis decimal is descriptive, whereas the integer comparison proves the\ninequality used in (3).\n\nThe other checks in (3) use the same radical interval for S and each h,\nand use, without a radical,\n\n    delta^core_i=(2v_i D-G_(i+1,i+1))/(2D^2),\n    ||v_start||^2=G_00/D^2.\n\nThe minimum core delta is already positive in these data (approximately\n8.5986e-11), but only the weaker stated lower bound is needed. The audit\nscript is PUBLIC at `research/max3_c59_h6_exact_primal_repair.py`.\nIts single finite integer check completed in under0.1second and used no\noptimization, search, eigenvalue clipping, or large symbolic computation.\nThis is a transparent finite arithmetic component of an unverified proof\ncandidate; it is not a substitute for the verifier's acceptance.\n\n## 3. One controlled auxiliary coordinate repairs all pairs\n\nChoose\n\n    kappa=1/512, t0=1/8,\n    xi_0=1, xi_(i+1)=(1-kappa h_i)xi_i.\n\nEvery factor is positive since h_i<=S<256. For any initial segment,\nthe elementary product inequality\n\n    product_t(1-a_t)>=1-sum_t a_t  (a_t>=0, sum a_t<1)\n\nfollows inductively from (1-a)(1-b)>=1-a-b. It gives\n\n    1>=xi_i>=1-kappa S>1/2.\n\nFor i<j, positivity and h_i>1 imply\n\n    xi_i-xi_j>=xi_i-xi_(i+1)=kappa h_i xi_i>kappa/2.\n\nThe distance to the optimum sample xi_star=0 is also greater than\nkappa/2. Thus these bounds cover every distinct pair, including star.\n\nDefine the66-dimensional jets and values\n\n    X_i=(z_i,t0 xi_i),\n    G_i=(q_i,t0 kappa xi_i),\n    f_i=u_i+t0^2 kappa xi_i^2/2\n       =u_i+xi_i^2/65536, 0<=i<=63,\n    X_star=G_star=0, f_star=0.                      (6)\n\nTheir GD recursion is EXACT by (2) and the recursion for xi. The full\ninterpolation residual separates by coordinates into\n\n    Q_ij=Q^core_ij\n          -t0^2 kappa(1-kappa)(xi_i-xi_j)^2/2.      (7)\n\nThis is a polynomial identity, not a claimed separability of the final\nfunction. The second term is the residual for the single quadratic\nauxiliary coordinate at the displayed samples. Since1-kappa>1/2,\nits magnitude is strictly greater than\n\n    t0^2 kappa^3/16=2^(-37)>eta.\n\nCombining with (3) proves Q_ij<0 for ALL4160 ordered pairs simultaneously.\nThe same common values and coordinates are used in every pair.\n\n## 4. One global function and the true initial distance\n\nFor each finite sample put\n\n    Y_i=X_i-G_i, delta_i=f_i-||G_i||^2/2.\n\nEquation (6) gives\n\n    delta_i=delta^core_i+t0^2 kappa(1-kappa)xi_i^2/2\n             >-eta+t0^2 kappa/16>0.                (8)\n\nAdd the star with Y_star=0,delta_star=0, and define\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(i in {star,0,...,63})\n                              [<Y_i,v>-delta_i],\n    F(X)=sup_v[<X,v>-H(v)].                         (9)\n\nBy accepted1252b8823bb5a290 and (7), this is one globally defined finite\nconvex differentiable function with1-Lipschitz gradient, interpolating\nevery value and gradient in (6). Therefore its GD trajectory is the one\nalready specified, with the actual ordered word H6.\n\nThe finite set of strict gaps in (8) makes the star's zero affine function\nuniquely active in a neighborhood of v=0. Hence H(v)=||v||^2/2 there and\npartial H(0)={0}. The maximizing-vector condition for (9) identifies the\nminimizers of F with partial H(0), as in the accepted predecessor. Thus\nargmin F={0}; no remote or nonclosest minimizer is used in normalization.\n\nThe true squared distance and terminal value satisfy\n\n    R^2=||v_start||^2+1/64 < 1001/1000+1/64\n                              =8133/8000,\n    2F(X_63)=2u_63+t0^2 kappa xi_63^2 >21/1000.\n\nConsequently\n\n    2F(X_63)/R^2> (21/1000)/(8133/8000)\n                 =56/2711>1/50>4/(1+S),\n\nwhich proves (1), subject only to the explicitly supplied finite integer\nchecks on the defining data. If a unit initial norm is desired, use\nF_unit(x)=F(Rx)/R^2 and initial point X_0/R; the smoothness remains1,\nthe steps remain h, and the normalized payoff is unchanged. Multiplication\nof the function by any L>0 supplies the corresponding L-smooth instance\nwith steps h_i/L.\n\n## 5. A concrete feature of the surviving geometry\n\nThe rational core gradients have the exact inner product\n\n    <q_35,q_36>\n      =-14715168270572693173715883215/10^30\n      <-14/1000.\n\nThe extra coordinate changes this by at most t0^2 kappa^2=2^(-24),\nsince0<xi_i<=1. Thus\n\n    <G_35,G_36><-1/100.\n\nBoth indices are queried. The actual object retains substantial signed\ngradient correlation; it is not a Huber common-gradient line, a mutually\northogonal gradient transcript, or an all-gradients acute-cone trajectory.\nThe auxiliary coordinate repairs small interpolation errors in an already\nstrong correlated core, in contrast to attempting to repair the grossly\nillegal fixed scalar C55 template. No claim is made that this description\nis a scalable generating rule.\n\nThere is also a basis-independent distinction from a bounded-jump\ncoordinate construction. Let d_i=G_i-G_(i-1),1<=i<=63, and let J be their\nGram matrix. If an orthonormal coordinate system makes every coordinate\nof the gradient list change at most k times, then\n\n    2 sum_(i<j)|J_ij| <= (k-1) trace(J).             (10)\n\nIndeed each scalar coordinate of the jump list has at most k nonzero\nentries. Cauchy--Schwarz gives (sum|a_i|)^2<=k sum a_i^2, hence\n2sum_(i<j)|a_i a_j|<=(k-1)sum a_i^2. Sum over coordinates and apply the\ntriangle inequality to the resulting off-diagonal entries. Since J is\nunchanged by orthogonal changes of physical coordinates, (10) applies to\nevery such representation.\n\nFor the exact rational core, the integer increment Gram is saved PUBLICLY\nin `research/max3_c59_exact_increment_gram.json`. Its entries follow\ndirectly from the rational vectors already defined, with denominator D^2.\nExact integer summation gives\n\n    trace(J_core) approximately 0.44249400386221016,\n    2sum_(i<j)|J_core_ij| approximately 2.306374594722243,\n    2sum_(i<j)|J_core_ij|-5trace(J_core)>9/100.       (11)\n\nOnly the last, rational inequality is used. The auxiliary jump vector is\na_i=t0 kappa(xi_i-xi_(i-1)); all entries are negative and\n(sum|a_i|)^2<t0^2 kappa^2=2^(-24). Its contribution is aa^T. By the\ntriangle inequality, adding aa^T can lower the left side of (11) by at\nmost\n\n    2sum_(i<j)|a_i a_j|+5sum_i a_i^2\n           <=5(sum_i|a_i|)^2<5*2^(-24).\n\nThus the full repaired J still satisfies\n2sum_(i<j)|J_ij|>5trace(J). Equation (10) rules out every representation\nin which all coordinate gradients have at most SIX chronological jumps.\nIn particular this actual finite adversary is outside the common two-jump\nprimal cone. The large core margin in (11) ensures this distinction is\nnot caused only by the tiny auxiliary quadratic coordinate.\n\n## 6. Scope and next open interface\n\nThis candidate is finite, exact in its definitions, and includes all\nfunction values, gradients, positions, actual GD equations, and the true\ndistance. It does not rely on a numerically feasible Gram or a nearly\npositive semidefinite dual. The source solver arrays are preserved\nunchanged; the rational data and added coordinate are separately labeled\nnew objects.\n\nThe estimate rules out1/M6 for this one actual word. It does NOT refute an\nupper bound valid only at sufficiently large horizons, establish a common\npattern for the changing parameters epsilon=1/m, or prove the all-schedule\nZhang--Jiang profile. In particular the length63 block changes physically\nas the enclosing horizon changes. The missing scalable interface is an\nanalytic rule for the correlated core that controls ALL interpolation\npairs and distance under those parameter changes. Repeating this finite\ndata set, extrapolating its numerical eigenvectors, or fixing only one\nturning pair would not provide that rule.\n\nNo routine verification allowance was used. This remains a complete\nfinite proof candidate, not an accepted fact.\n\n\n## Complete defining finite rational data\n\nThe following T rows list only their lower-triangular part; all entries above it are zero. The named integer comparisons are given in the proof and in the public audit {"id": "ac92ae33abcc02c4", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T21:45:27.498942+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Partial repair beyond the metric-normal policy: for the SAME double-zero data and common positive quadratic heights delta_i=<R_i,(A-I)R_i>/2, A>=I, but assuming ONLY actual ordinary Q inequalities, the highest active level j>=3 satisfies <w_j,A w_j>/||w_j||²>=d_j-261/16>d_j-17. Two actual small-step pairs in the isolated right block prove this with no normal assumption, conditioning bound, limit passage, or summed isolated charges. The full Q-only common-height policy remains OPEN: a single-mode Rayleigh cost does not supply effective smoothness or the common initial-distance budget.", "evidence": "Notation and data: m>=128, lambda=1+sqrt2, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c, d_j=1+a c^j, H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j). Queries use H_m with its last d0 excluded. p_ij=product_(t<i)(1-h_t/d_j); R_i=sum_j p_ij w_j, Z_i=sum_j d_j p_ij w_j, x_i=u-s_i v+Z_i, g_i=v+R_i. One common selfadjoint A>=I gives f_i=<v,x_i>-<v,A v>/2+<R_i,A R_i>/2, optimum jet zero. All original Q_ij<=0. The following complete calculation uses only necessity from fact1252; no unverified normal-model conclusion is a premise.\n\n## 10. A partial repair using only ordinary interpolation, not normals\n\nReturn to data (1) and the common quadratic values (6), with A>=I, but now\nassume ONLY every ordinary Q_ij<=0. The normals (4) are not assumed, and no\nmetric-distance representative or effective smoothness is inferred. Let\nj>=3 be the highest nonzero level, d=d_j, w=w_j, and set\n\n            a_w=<w,A w>/||w||^2, r_w=<v,w>/||w||^2.\n\nThen the actual ordinary inequalities alone force\n\n                         a_w >= d-261/16 > d-17.    (28)\n\nThis is an approximate scalar curvature cost, not the missing full gradient\nor initial-distance budget. It applies to arbitrary conditioning of A.\n\nHere is a direct proof. Throughout the isolated right block, R_t=p_t w,\nZ_t=d p_t w. At an even queried index t before the last positive p, the\nnext physical step is h=d_0 and p'=p_t(1-h/d)>0. Expanding BOTH Q inequalities\nusing (8), which is an algebraic identity independent of (4), gives\n\n (a_w/d-1)p_t-(a_w-1)h p_t/(2d^2)\n       <=r_w\n       <=(a_w/d-1)p_t+(a_w-1)h p_t^2/(2d^2 p').      (29)\n\nChoose the actual even indices I=2^(j-1) and K=2^j-2. Both pairs I,I+1 and\nK,K+1 precede the annihilating step, and lower modes have vanished. Put\np_I=P_I(1/d) and p_K=P_K(1/d). The subword between these two query indices\nis H_(j-1) with its last d_0 removed. Since j>=3, it includes a d_(j-2).\nEvery factor is in (0,1), so 1-z<=exp(-z) gives\n\n p_K/p_I<=exp(-d_(j-2)/d)<=exp(-4/25)<=25/29.         (30)\n\nFor the middle inequality, d_j<c^2 d_(j-2) and c<5/2. The last follows\nfrom exp(4/25)>=1+4/25. Thus p_I-p_K>=(4/29)p_I.\nAlso d>=d_3>3 and h=d_0<3/2, so 1-h/d>1/2 and\np_I^2/p_(I+1)<2p_I, while p_K<=p_I.\n\nIf a_w>=d, (28) already holds. Otherwise use the upper bound (29) at I and\nthe lower bound at K. Since a_w>=1, they imply\n\n (d-a_w)(p_I-p_K)/d\n    <=(a_w-1)h[p_I^2/p_(I+1)+p_K]/(2d^2)\n    <(a_w-1)(9/4)p_I/d^2.\n\nWith (30), this yields\n\n d-a_w <= (261/16)(a_w-1)/d <=261/16,\n\nproving (28). Every inequality used here is an actual queried pair; there\nis no continuous-time limit or sum of purported independent level charges.\n\nIf A<=2tau I is imposed in addition, (28) yields only\ntau>=(d-17)/2. It does NOT establish that F is 1/tau-smooth, that the queried\ngradients obey (15), or that the prefix is nonexpansive. Those implications\nin Sections2--4 used the stronger normal model, and Section8.1 disproves an\nautomatic passage from Q to that model. The Q-only common quadratic-height\npolicy therefore remains open after (28). Its next precise interface is\nto combine its genuine joint values and (25)--(27) with the approximate\nRayleigh costs, without replacing a single-mode Rayleigh bound by a bound\non every full gradient (the latter inference is refuted in Section8.2).\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["28db18b9be162b29", "456e36fd0eb02833", "30e88f2351b2aa4f"], "artifact": "research/joint_positive_height_metric_obstruction.md", "plan_id": "10bd4d79197b0379", "status": "partial", "counterexample_search_for_stuck_subgoal": {"performed": true, "result": "refuted", "summary": "Normal automaticity and whole-gradient directional-Rayleigh transfer both fail in actual legal examples456e36fd0eb02833; neither refutes the remaining full Q-only policy."}}, "glossary": {"a_w": "Rayleigh quotient <w,A w>/||w||² of the highest active residual mode.", "r_w": "Ratio <v,w>/||w||² for that mode.", "I": "Actual query index2^(j-1) in this local lemma; identity operator elsewhere is explicitly written.", "K": "Actual query index2^j-2 in this local lemma.", "p_I": "Exact surviving modal factor at queryI.", "p_K": "Exact surviving modal factor at queryK.", "d": "Actual step d_j of the highest active mode.", "A": "Common positive definite height operator, A>=identity; no normal-cone or condition-number hypothesis in this lemma."}}
{"id": "e09fd8046ee1daf2", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T21:55:36.493766+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Signed interval construction completed and quantitatively excluded in its explicit zero-height class: each coordinate starts at Aamp_j, reflects to -Bamp_j for one dyadic cell, then vanishes. One finite common interpolant satisfies ALL original pairs with true initial distance; every legal member at every cutoff of actual H_m has V_ref<3/M_m² and B_(2^m-1)V_ref<=15[3/(1+sqrt(2))²]^m→0. Exact shared-height charges identify what the positive-height extension must pay; that extension and the general increment cone remain open.", "evidence": "# Signed interval gradients: a legal construction and its quantitative loss\n\nStatus: **self-contained draft candidate, not verifier accepted**. This round\nuses no optimization, installation, or fact submission. The accepted\ndependencies are `1252b8823bb5a290` (smooth convex interpolation, including\nits explicit conjugate construction) and `b98bb807d514e80d` (the exact\nbenchmark B_n and its bound B_n<=1+4 n^(log_2 3)). Every new assertion below\nis proved within this one candidate. Neither the prior cone interface nor\nC57 is adopted as a correctness premise.\n\nThe generating rule is different from C57's ordered repair: each coordinate\nhas a positive initial amplitude, an opposite amplitude for one cell, and\nthen vanishes. Amplitudes may be unequal. These gradients have negative\ncross inner products in the explicit construction, and are not ordered in\nan acute cone. They are correlated, even across remote cells. Every scalar\ncoordinate changes at most twice, so the increment Gram has an explicit\nsum of PSD blocks on at most two increment indices.\n\nThe scope restriction that causes failure is explicit: the gradient is\nconstant on each selected ordinary block, and its jet at the end of every\nsuch block has **zero proximal height**. The entire resulting class on the\nactual H_m, over every dyadic cutoff, has normalized payoff <3/M_m^2.\nThis is not a bound on arbitrary signed interval mixtures or on W_n(H_m).\nSection 7 gives the exact charged inequality when endpoint heights are\nallowed to be positive; it does not assert that these charges can be met.\n\n## 1. The actual schedule and one finite coordinate rule\n\nFix m>=128 and, for this whole horizon, set\n\n sigma=1+sqrt(2), c_rul=sigma(1+1/m),\n a_rul=(1-1/m)/c_rul,\n H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_rul c_rul^j,H_j),\n M_j=1+sum H_j, n=2^m-1.\n\nThe parameters are not recomputed within the recursion. Choose an integer\ncutoff 0<=ell<m. Put q=m-ell, k=2^q-1 and C=M_ell. The actual word is\n\n H_m=(H_ell,1+r_0,H_ell,...,1+r_(k-1),H_ell),\n r_j=a_rul c_rul^(ell+nu_2(j+1)), 0<=j<k,                 (1)\n\nwhere nu_2(v) is the largest integer d>=0 with 2^d dividing the positive\ninteger v. There are k+1 ordinary cells, numbered 0,...,k. Each contains\nthe exact steps H_ell, of mass C-1. No final step has been changed: the\nlast ordinary cell includes the original last step of H_m.\n\nIn an orthonormal coordinate system e_0,...,e_k choose real amplitudes\nAamp_j>=0 for 0<=j<=k and Bamp_j for 0<=j<k. Define the cell gradients\n\n p_0=sum_(j=0)^k Aamp_j e_j,\n p_i=-Bamp_(i-1)e_(i-1)+sum_(j=i)^k Aamp_j e_j,\n                                      1<=i<=k.          (2)\n\nThus coordinate j<k equals Aamp_j in cells 0,...,j, equals -Bamp_j in\ncell j+1, and equals zero thereafter. Coordinate k stays Aamp_k. Zero\namplitudes are permitted; choosing a coordinate orientation makes the\ninitial amplitudes nonnegative without loss within this rule. The Bamp\namplitudes are initially unrestricted in sign.\n\nUse the same gradient p_i at every query in cell i and at its endpoint.\nFor i<k that endpoint is the query immediately before checkpoint 1+r_i;\nfor i=k it is the terminal sample after the complete original word. Denote\nthese endpoint positions by Z_i. Define Y_i=Z_i-p_i. The actual recursion\nforces\n\n Y_(i+1)=Y_i-r_i p_i-C p_(i+1).                          (3)\n\nIndeed the checkpoint sends Z_i=Y_i+p_i to Y_i-r_i p_i,\nand the next ordinary cell uses mass C-1 of p_(i+1). A sample in cell i\nwith remaining ordinary mass u in [0,C-1] has position\n\n X_(i,u)=Y_i+(1+u)p_i.                                  (4)\n\nDefine the tail energy, squared norms and cross inner products by\n\n Eref_i=sum_(j=i)^k Aamp_j^2,\n Nref_0=Eref_0,\n Nref_i=Eref_i+Bamp_(i-1)^2,                 1<=i<=k,\n Cref_i=Eref_i-Aamp_(i-1)Bamp_(i-1),         1<=i<=k.\n\nDirect coordinate multiplication gives\n\n ||p_i||^2=Nref_i,    <p_i,p_j>=Cref_j for every i<j.    (5)\n\nThese are generally nonzero, signed correlations between remote cells.\n\n## 2. Explicit membership in the two-jump increment cone\n\nFor j<k-1, the only gradient changes in coordinate j occur just after\ncheckpoints j and j+1. Their amplitudes are respectively\n-(Aamp_j+Bamp_j) and Bamp_j. The corresponding incremental Gram summand\nis the outer product of this two-supported scalar list with itself.\nCoordinate k-1 changes only at the final checkpoint, and coordinate k\ndoes not change. Summing these real PSD summands is the complete increment\nGram. This is a decomposition, not an inferred membership from numerics.\n\nThe initial-vector and initial-gradient rows are retained: the full Gram\nis obtained from the actual vectors X_0,p_0,...,p_k and their repetitions\nat the original query indices. For the explicit member in Section 4,\nX_0=C p_0. Appending its coordinate to each scalar list gives the full\nGram decomposition with the base rows included.\n\n## 3. Zero endpoint heights impose an exact energy contraction\n\nConsider ANY legal convex differentiable 1-smooth function F realizing\n(1)--(4), normalized by min F=0. Impose only the stated endpoint-height\ncondition\n\n                  F(Z_i)=Nref_i/2, 0<=i<=k.             (6)\n\nIntermediate values in a constant-gradient cell are forced to be\n\n                  F(X_(i,u))=(u+1/2)Nref_i.             (7)\n\nFor proof, the two interpolation inequalities between equal gradients\nforce the value difference to equal their inner product with the position\ndifference. Condition (6), smoothness on the unit step from Z_i, and\nminimality imply F(Y_i)=0. All Y_i are genuine minimizers.\n\nThe accepted interpolation inequalities between endpoint jets are exactly\n\n                     <p_i,Y_j-Y_i><=0.                (8)\n\nIn particular, the inequality from endpoint i+1 to endpoint i, combined\nwith (3),(5), is\n\n (C+r_i)Eref_(i+1)+C Bamp_i^2\n                         <=r_i Aamp_i Bamp_i.           (9)\n\nThis is one necessary joint interpolation pair inside the actual history;\nit is independent of an assumed separable function. Completing its square\nand using Aamp_i^2=Eref_i-Eref_(i+1) gives\n\n (2C+r_i)^2 Eref_(i+1)\n   +4C^2[Bamp_i-r_i Aamp_i/(2C)]^2 <=r_i^2 Eref_i.      (10)\n\nConsequently, with\n\n gamma_ref_i=[r_i/(2C+r_i)]^2,\n\n                  Eref_(i+1)<=gamma_ref_i Eref_i.       (11)\n\nThere is also a useful bound on the whole next gradient:\n\n                  Nref_(i+1)<=(r_i/C)^2 Aamp_i^2.       (12)\n\nIf Aamp_i=0, (9) forces Bamp_i=Eref_(i+1)=0, so (12)\nholds. Otherwise (9) forces 0<=Bamp_i<=r_i Aamp_i/C.\nSolving (9) for Eref_(i+1) yields\n\n Nref_(i+1)<=r_i Bamp_i(Aamp_i+Bamp_i)/(C+r_i),\n\nan increasing function of Bamp_i on that interval. Substitution of its\nupper endpoint proves (12). Thus allowing either sign of Bamp initially\ndoes not evade (9): legality itself forces the reflected sign unless the\nsubsequent gradient is zero.\n\nThe true initial distance is also fixed, rather than reset at a later cut.\nBy (4), X_0=Y_0+C p_0. For any minimizer z, smooth convex interpolation\nbetween Z_0 and z gives <p_0,z-Y_0><=0. Therefore\n\n ||X_0-z||^2>=C^2 Nref_0,\n dist(X_0,argmin F)^2=C^2 Nref_0,                       (13)\n\nwith equality at the genuine minimizer Y_0. This proof permits additional\nminimizers; it does not silently assume uniqueness. If Nref_0=0, (9)\nforces the entire terminal value to be zero, and there is no positive\nnormalized witness. For Nref_0>0 the normalized terminal payoff is\n\n V_ref=Nref_k/(C^2 Nref_0).\n\nApplying (11) up to i=k-2 and then (12) gives the uniform bound\n\n V_ref <= r_(k-1)^2/C^4 * product_(i=0)^(k-2)gamma_ref_i.\n                                                               (14)\n\nFor k=1 the product is empty. This is a bound for EVERY legal member of\nthe generating rule with (6), regardless of how it was constructed.\n\n## 4. An explicit finite member satisfies ALL original pairs\n\nThe zero-height class above is nonempty and contains genuine signed,\ncorrelated trajectories. Here is a deterministic finite formula, without\nsolving an interpolation or matrix optimization problem.\n\nFor this construction only, set r_k=0 and define, for 0<=j<k,\n\n zeta_j=r_j/[4(C+r_(j+1))],\n Lref_j=zeta_j(r_j-C zeta_j)/(C+r_j)>0.\n\nWrite r_min=min_(j<k)r_j, r_max=max_(j<k)r_j,\nLref_min=min_(j<k)Lref_j, and choose\n\n eta^2=min{1/4, Lref_min/[2(1+Lref_min)],\n                                      r_min/(2r_max)}.  (15)\n\nSet Aamp_j=eta^j for j<k, Aamp_k=0, and\nBamp_j=zeta_j Aamp_j. Define p_i by (2), Y_0=0, and Y_i by (3).\nAll these are exact finite real formulas. In fact 0<eta<1 and Bamp_j>0.\n\nFor every j<k, a finite geometric-series estimate gives\n\n Eref_(j+1)<=eta^2 Aamp_j^2/(1-eta^2)\n                         <Lref_j Aamp_j^2.              (16)\n\nTo check the strict inequality, (15) implies\neta^2/(1-eta^2)<=Lref_min/(2+Lref_min)<Lref_min.\nThus (9) is satisfied strictly. Moreover Lref_j<zeta_j, so\n\n                 Cref_(j+1)<0.                         (17)\n\nIt remains to prove the remote normal constraints (8), rather than infer\nthem from adjacent checks. For i<j, iterating (3) gives\n\n Y_j-Y_i=-r_i p_i-sum_(u=i+1)^(j-1)(C+r_u)p_u-C p_j.\n                                                               (18)\n\nFor the later gradient, (5) implies\n\n <p_j,Y_i-Y_j>\n  =[r_i+sum_(u=i+1)^(j-1)(C+r_u)]Cref_j+C Nref_j.\n\nThe bracket is at least r_(j-1). Since Cref_j<0, this is at\nmost r_(j-1)Cref_j+C Nref_j, which is negative by (9),(16).\n\nFor the earlier gradient, (18) gives the desired inequality precisely\nwhen\n\n r_i Nref_i+sum_(u=i+1)^(j-1)(C+r_u)Cref_u+C Cref_j>=0. It also includes i=0,\nso every Y_j is in the supporting halfspace <p_0,Y_j><=0.\n\nGive EVERY original sample, including the terminal, the single value\narray (7), position (4), and gradient p_i. Why this is not a result for the entire increment cone\n\nThe cone also permits general pairs of changes in each coordinate,\narbitrary initial amplitudes, different net changes, overlapping\nintervals, and shared positive endpoint heights. Equations (9)--(14)\nuse the specific history in (2), constant ordinary-cell gradients,\nand zero endpoint heights. A general cone member need not satisfy them.\nThe result therefore does not upper-bound W_n(H_m), refute another\nworker's unrestricted primal, or decide common-array feasibility at1/M_m.\n\nThe explicit construction supplies an actual legal function but its\npayoff is far too small. The exact height charge that a changed construction must pay\n\nRetain only the coordinate rule and the constant-gradient ordinary cells,\nbut now allow the endpoint heights\n\n delta_i=F(Z_i)-Nref_i/2>=0\n\nof ONE jointly legal function to be arbitrary. Formula (3) still holds.\nThe same endpoint interpolation pair becomes\n\n (C+r_i)Eref_(i+1)+C Bamp_i^2-r_i Aamp_i Bamp_i\n                                    <=delta_i-delta_(i+1).\n\nEquivalently, with omega_ref_i=4C/(2C+r_i)^2,\n\n Eref_(i+1)+[4C^2/(2C+r_i)^2]\n                    [Bamp_i-r_i Aamp_i/(2C)]^2\n       <=gamma_ref_i Eref_i\n                       +omega_ref_i(delta_i-delta_(i+1)).       (29)\n\nIn particular, retaining Eref_(i+1)>=theta Eref_i requires\n\n delta_i-delta_(i+1)\n  >=C[(2+t_i)^2 theta-t_i^2]Eref_i/4\n                      +C[Bamp_i-t_i Aamp_i/2]^2.       (30)\n\nAt any lowest-level checkpoint of (1), t_i=t<1/2. If at least\none quarter of the untouched tail energy is to survive that checkpoint,\n(30) forces delta_i-delta_(i+1)>=C Eref_i/4. Indeed\n(2+t)^2/4-t^2=1+t-3t^2/4>=1 for 0<=t<=1/2.\nThese are charges in a shared value sequence, not separately selectable\nper-cut credits; other checkpoints may replenish a height only subject\nto that same sequence and the remaining interpolation pairs.\n\nFor any 0<=s<k, iterating (29) after discarding its nonnegative square\nalso gives the exact necessary weighted budget\n\n Eref_(s+1)<=Eref_0 product_(i=0)^s gamma_ref_i\n  +sum_(i=0)^s omega_ref_i(delta_i-delta_(i+1))\n                                  product_(u=i+1)^s gamma_ref_u. Its exact payoff\nand true squared distance are\n\n              2F_H(x_n)=1/(2M_m-1),  R^2=1.            (33)\n\nFor M_m>=6 this exceeds3/M_m^2, since M_m^2>6M_m-3.\nThe actual endpoints have delta_j=tau Z_j-tau^2>0, and\n\n              delta_j-delta_(j+1)=(C+r_j)tau^2.\n\nThus every height inequality (29) is met with equality in its original\nunsquared form, using Eref_j=tau^2 and Aamp_j=Bamp_j=0 for j<k.\nThe initial cost (32) is also exact: delta_0=tau-C tau^2, so\n(C Nref_0+delta_0)^2/Nref_0=1. This does not furnish an improvement\nover Huber or solve the positive-height rule; it refutes only the\nunjustified extension of (27) to arbitrary endpoint heights.\n\nFor a finite algebraic sanity check of Section 4, two fixed rational\nfixtures used c_rul=5/2, a_rul=2/5, m=3, and ell=0 or1.
