{"id": "f2d2743d0d681bdd", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T21:58:34.834497+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C60 new analytic correlated-primal mechanism after the closed batch: one explicit NONQUADRATIC convex1-smooth projection function on R² has exact two-state GD recursion for EVERY finite nonnegative schedule, including zeros/large steps, full all-pair law Q_ij=-(v_j+r_j²)(r_i-r_j)²/2, and exact CLOSEST-minimizer normalization. It supports dense turning gradients with arbitrarily many jumps in dimension2. The quantitative payoff on actual H_m is still OPEN; small-amplitude and excessively large vertical-state limits are analyzed without overgeneralizing.", "evidence": "# C60: a nonlinear two-state legal trajectory with dense turning gradients\n\nStatus: **new explicit construction and scoped calculations; UNVERIFIED**.\nNo solve or verifier call is used. The numerical allocation is closed.\nThis branch follows the finite C59 reconstruction by seeking an analytic\ncorrelated core. It does not assume that the C59 data belong to this family.\n\nThe construction works for every finite nonnegative schedule, including\nzeros and arbitrarily large entries, with one fixed globally convex\n1-smooth function. Its all-pair law and true closest-minimizer distance\nare explicit. The quantitative payoff on the critical H_m remains open.\n\n## 1. The global function, its gradients, and its minimizers\n\nIn R^2 let\n\n    C_par={(t,s): s>=t^2/sqrt(2)},\n    F_par(X)=sup_(g in C_par) [<X,g>-||g||^2/2].      (1)\n\nThe set is closed, convex and nonempty, and contains0. Completing the\nsquare shows that the maximizer in (1) is the Euclidean projection of X\nonto C_par. It exists and is unique because the squared-distance objective\nis coercive and strictly convex. Thus F_par is finite and convex.\n\nFor completeness write p(X) for that projection. Its elementary normal\ncondition is\n\n    <X-p(X),g-p(X)><=0 for every g in C_par.\n\nApplying this twice and adding gives\n\n    ||p(X)-p(Y)||^2<=<p(X)-p(Y),X-Y>.\n\nThus p is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizers in (1) gives\n\n    0<=F_par(Y)-F_par(X)-<p(X),Y-X>\n        <=<p(Y)-p(X),Y-X><=||Y-X||^2.\n\nConsequently F_par is differentiable, its gradient is p, and it is\n1-smooth. This proof supplies the needed global property directly.\n\nThe minimizers are exactly\n\n    argmin F_par={(0,y):y<=0}.                      (2)\n\nIndeed F_par>=0 by the feasible vector0. For X=(0,y), y<=0, every g=(t,s)\nin C_par has <X,g>=ys<=0, so the maximum in (1) is0. Conversely, if\nthe vertical coordinate y>0, taking g=(0,s) with small s>0 makes the\nobjective positive. If the horizontal coordinate x is nonzero and y<=0,\ntake g=(t,t^2/sqrt(2)) with sufficiently small nonzero t of the sign of x:\nthe linear term xt dominates the terms of quadratic and quartic order.\nThis again makes the objective positive. These cases prove (2).\n\nThis F_par is not a convex quadratic: its minimizer set is a ray, whereas\nthe nonempty minimizer set of an unconstrained convex quadratic is an\naffine subspace.\n\n## 2. Coordinates that make every interpolation pair explicit\n\nFor a real r and v>=0, put\n\n    u=v+r^2,\n    X(r,v)=(r(1+v+r^2), -v/sqrt(2)),\n    G(r)=(r,r^2/sqrt(2)).                           (3)\n\nThen X-G=u(r,-1/sqrt(2)). For any (t,s) in C_par,\n\n    <X-G,(t,s)-G>\n      =u[r(t-r)-(s-r^2/sqrt(2))/sqrt(2)]\n      <=-(u/2)(t-r)^2<=0.\n\nThe normal condition therefore proves that G(r) is the actual gradient\nof F_par at X(r,v). Direct substitution into (1) gives\n\n    F_par(X(r,v))=r^2(1+v)/2+3r^4/4.                (4)\n\nFor any two sampled states r_i,v_i and r_j,v_j, with the actual values\nfrom (4), the complete signed interpolation residual is\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2\n        =<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>\n        =-(u_j/2)(r_i-r_j)^2<=0.                   (5)\n\nThe first equality after Q follows by substituting\nf_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2. The second uses the displayed parabolic\ncoordinates. This proves EVERY ordered pair simultaneously for one\nfunction; no local, adjacent, or cycle relaxation is being used.\n\nFor an initial nonzero r_0 and v_0>=0, the closest minimizer in (2) is\n\n    X_star=(0,-v_0/sqrt(2)),\n    R_0^2=dist(X_0,argmin F_par)^2\n            =r_0^2(1+v_0+r_0^2)^2.                (6)\n\nThe optimum sample fits the same formulas with r_star=0, v_star=v_0,\nu_star=v_0. Thus (5) includes both of its directions. Choosing the origin\ninstead would generally overestimate the true initial distance; (6) is\nthe normalization used throughout.\n\n## 3. The exact GD transition, with no interpolation optimization\n\nFor ANY step h>=0, starting from state(r,v), define\n\n    v_plus=v+h r^2,\n    r_plus^3+(1+v_plus)r_plus\n                      =r(1+v+r^2-h).              (7)\n\nThe cubic's left side is strictly increasing from minus infinity to\nplus infinity because its derivative is1+v_plus+3r_plus^2>=1. Thus (7)\ndefines exactly one real r_plus, by an explicit scalar algebraic rule.\nThere is no nearest-feasible-trajectory problem hidden in this definition.\n\nThe vertical coordinate of X-hG is -v_plus/sqrt(2), and its horizontal\ncoordinate is the right side of (7). Hence\n\n    X(r_plus,v_plus)=X(r,v)-h G(r).\n\nThis proves actual GD for the same global function. In particular h=0\nleaves the state unchanged, and every finite positive step gives finite\nnew state. All v_i remain nonnegative, regardless of gradient sign changes.\n\nFor a supplied finite nonnegative schedule h_0,...,h_(n-1), iterate (7)\nfrom any r_0!=0,v_0>=0. Its exact normalized payoff is\n\n    P_par(h;r_0,v_0)\n       =[r_n^2(1+v_n)+(3/2)r_n^4]\n                        /[r_0^2(1+v_0+r_0^2)^2].  (8)\n\nThe function (1), all values(4), gradients(3), positions(3), and initial\ndistance(6) are fixed together. Thus (8) is an actual lower witness for\nW_n(h), albeit not yet a profile-strength one on the critical schedules.\n\nSome immediate identities expose the changing geometry:\n\n    u_plus-u=(h-1)r^2+r_plus^2,\n    X_plus,horizontal=X_horizontal[1-h/(1+u)].      (9)\n\nIn particular, for schedules with all h>=1, the normal parameter u is\nnondecreasing, but r and the horizontal gradient can turn repeatedly.\nThe Hessian direction changes with r; this is not a fixed linear spectral\ntranscript. The vertical state accumulates h r^2 rather than discarding\nprevious turns.\n\n## 4. Exact dense-jump calibration on a different schedule\n\nTake any sigma in(0,1), v_0>=0, and define\n\n    U_0=1+v_0+sigma,\n    rho=(1+sigma)/(1-sigma),\n    h_i=2U_0 rho^i/(1-sigma),\n    r_i=(-1)^i sqrt(sigma),\n    v_i=U_0 rho^i-1-sigma.                         (10)\n\nThese v_i are nonnegative. Substitution into (7) gives\nv_(i+1)-v_i=h_i sigma and\nr_(i+1)(1+v_(i+1)+sigma)=r_i(1+v_i+sigma-h_i),\nso (10) is an exact trajectory of F_par. Its payoff is\n\n    P_par=rho^n/U_0+sigma/(2U_0^2).                 (11)\n\nThis calibration is legal and can have large payoff. It is a geometric\nstep sequence, NOT the critical ruler word, so (11) proves no claim about\nH_m or the original profile. It only checks that the mechanism supports\ncorrelated turns without a feasibility repair.\n\nThere are n nonzero collinear gradient jumps in (10):\n\n    G_i-G_(i-1)=(2(-1)^i sqrt(sigma),0), 1<=i<=n.\n\nTheir Gram J has trace4sigma n and twice the absolute off-diagonal sum\n4sigma n(n-1). For any Gram realizable with at most k nonzero jumps in\neach orthonormal coordinate, Cauchy--Schwarz coordinate by coordinate\nimplies2sum_(i<j)|J_ij|<=(k-1)trace(J). Hence this trajectory requires\nsome coordinate with at least n jumps in every orthonormal realization.\nDense chronological correlation does not require a large ambient dimension.\n\nFor a completely rational parameter example, sigma=1/4 and v_0=0 give\nrho=5/3, h_0=10/3, h_1=50/9, and r_i=(-1)^i/2. Formula (11) is then\nan exact expression, not a numerical simulation.\n\n## 5. Two simple parameter limits do not yet give the desired mechanism\n\nFirst fix a finite schedule and fix v_0=A>=0. Let r_0=t tend to zero.\nInduction in (7) gives\n\n    v_i=A+O(t^2),\n    r_i=t product_(j<i)[1-h_j/(1+A)]+O(t^3).\n\nAt each induction step, the inverse cubic has nonzero derivative1+A\nat zero, and its cubic term is O(t^3), giving the displayed expansion.\nThus the payoff tends to the ordinary scalar quadratic value\n\n    [1/(1+A)] product_(j<n)[1-h_j/(1+A)]^2.         (12)\n\nThe implicit constants here depend on the entire fixed finite schedule.\nThey have NOT been bounded uniformly along growing H_m. Consequently\n(12) is a calibration and an explanation of why an infinitesimal core\ndoes not itself add a new mechanism; it is not a class obstruction.\n\nSecond put S=sum h_i and sigma=r_0^2. If v_0>=S, no horizontal sign flip\noccurs and |r_(i+1)|<=|r_i|. Indeed h_i<=v_i and the right side of (7)\nhas the sign of r_i. Evaluating the increasing cubic at r_i shows that\nits excess over that right side is h_i r_i(1+r_i^2), with the appropriate\nsign, so the claimed decrease in absolute value follows. Hence\n\n    v_n<=v_0+S sigma,\n    P_par <= [1+v_0+S sigma+(3/2)sigma]\n                                  /(1+v_0+sigma)^2. (13)\n\nAlong any growing-horizon choices with\nv_0/[S(1+sigma)] tending to infinity, this gives S P_par tending to zero.\nThus placing the initial state arbitrarily far down the flat minimizer\ndirection also loses the desired scale. This rules out only the displayed\nparameter regime; intermediate coupled regimes remain open.\n\n## 6. The concrete remaining critical-word problem\n\nFor the actual critical word fix, within each horizon m,\n\n    c_m=(1+sqrt(2))(1+1/m), a_m=(1-1/m)/c_m,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m c_m^j,H_j).\n\nLet T_h denote the explicit two-state transition (7). The full block map\nis defined EXACTLY by\n\n    Phi_0=identity,\n    Phi_(j+1)=Phi_j composed with T_(1+a_m c_m^j)\n                                     composed with Phi_j.\n\nThe central task in this branch is to select explicit r_0(m),v_0(m), or\nprove a useful uniform upper bound for this mechanism, while controlling\nthe exact payoff (8) under Phi_m. The model is already an actual legal\nfunction for every such selection, so no separate interpolation optimization\nis required. What is missing is the QUANTITATIVE estimate on that nonlinear\nrecursion. Neither (11), the small-amplitude calibration, nor (13) fills it.\n\nAn open subgoal is to compare the increase in v across both copies of a\ndyadic block with the horizontal sign and gain at its central bridge.\nThe state v must remain shared between the copies; restarting it, freezing\nthe curvature, or replacing the two copies by independent instances would\nchange the function and lose the construction.\n\n## 7. Source grounding and verification scope\n\nJiang, Yue and Zhou, arXiv:1911.12545v2 (2021), Theorem2.2, gives the\nEuclidean projection onto S={(x,y):||x||^2<=y}: inside S it is the point,\nand outside it is(x/(1+mu),y+mu/2), where mu>=max(0,-2y) is the unique\nsolution of ||x||^2/(1+mu)^2=y+mu/2. The paper's surrounding definition\nof S and full proof were read. It supplies a closely matched projection\nmechanism, not a GD complexity result for the present function. Its\ncubic-regularization convergence theorem is not invoked.\n\nSections1--6 derive the two-dimensional specialization and all required\nproperties directly. No external theorem is used as a black box, and no\nunverified earlier project result is a premise. The finite C59 data merely\nmotivated seeking dense turning gradients. Every new statement here remains\na draft until accepted by the verifier; no claim is made that this family\nsolves the all-schedule exact-profile problem.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["b4f25156d6599097"], "artifact": "research/c60_parabolic_projection_dynamics.md", "source": {"authors": ["Rujun Jiang", "Man-Chung Yue", "Zhishuo Zhou"], "title": "An accelerated first-order method with complexity analysis for solving cubic regularization subproblems", "arxiv": "1911.12545v2", "year": 2021, "theorem_id": "Theorem2.2", "use": "Projection mechanism checked; all needed specialization proved directly"}, "verification_status": "UNVERIFIED; no solve or routine fact_submit allocation"}, "glossary": {"C_par": "Closed parabolic epigraph {(t,s):s>=t²/sqrt2}", "F_par": "Convex1-smooth function sup over g in C_par of <X,g>-||g||²/2", "r": "Real parabolic gradient coordinate in the explicit two-state recurrence", "v": "Nonnegative accumulated vertical state for the parabolic projection trajectory", "u": "Normal parameter v+r²", "P_par": "Exact true-initial-distance normalized payoff of the parabolic trajectory", "Phi_j": "Exact two-state block map for H_j, with physical parameters fixed within the horizon"}}
{"id": "f0164c899fadfdea", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T22:01:06.993106+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Actual exact Zhang-Jiang benchmark reconstructed at every horizon with deterministic optimizing splits and every full multiplier: rank-one certificate at1/B_n and identically zero selected prefix remainder. Two new finite obstructions: the fully mixed relaxed one-fan join has mass at mostG(A,D), equality only at the source parameters; arbitrary global doubly stochastic mixing and rescaling of positive step gaps cannot improve1/B_n even with unrestricted full-array re-selection.", "evidence": "# Actual exact Zhang--Jiang baseline and two finite variation obstructions\n\nStatus: **joint, unverified proof draft**, sixth launch, cumulative round 14.\nNo verifier or optimization call was made. This is not the original all-schedule\nasymptotic theorem. Every new assertion below is proved within this draft;\nnone is imported as a correctness premise from an earlier unverified finding.\n\nAccepted inputs: `b98bb807d514e80d` supplies the exact benchmark definitions;\n`0be8b54e0a33381c` supplies the legal Huber lower bound;\n`1252b8823bb5a290` supplies the full formal-vector certificate convention and\ninterpolation necessity. The baseline reconstruction follows the proofs in\nZehao Zhang and Rujun Jiang, *Accelerated Gradient Descent by Concatenation of\nStepsize Schedules*, arXiv:2410.12395v2 (14 February 2026), Definitions 2.4--2.7,\nTheorems 3.1/3.2, Lemmas 3.5/3.6. The exact PDF and extracted text were read.\nIts HTML uses different theorem numbers. Matlas returned unrelated results;\nno Matlas grounding is claimed. The identities needed here are derived below.\n\n## 1. Notation, normalization, and deterministic step words\n\nNormalize smoothness to one and the minimizer to zero. Thus\n\n    x_i = x_0 - sum_{t<i} h_t g_t,\n    Q_ij = f_i-f_j + <g_i,x_j-x_i> + ||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    Q_i* = f_i-<g_i,x_i>+||g_i||^2/2.\n\nAn actual trajectory has every Q_ij <= 0. A full upper certificate at level q\nis the identity\n\n    q||x_0||^2 - 2f_n + sum lambda_ij Q_ij = R,\n\nwith all lambda_ij >= 0 and R PSD in the ORIGINAL vectors\n(x_0,g_0,...,g_n). An upper bound derived this way uses the true initial\ndistance; translating/scaling back gives the project's normalization\n2(f_n-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2).\n\nWrite P_n = C_n = 1+S_circle(n), D_n = B_n = 1+2S_bullet(n). These aliases\nare used only in this note. Their exact recurrences are\n\n    P_0=D_0=1,\n    P_n=max_{0<=k<n} Omega(P_k,P_{n-k-1}),\n    D_n=max_{0<=k<n} G(P_k,D_{n-k-1}),\n    Omega(A,C)=(A+C+sqrt((A+C)^2+4AC))/2,\n    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nLet k_P(n), respectively k_D(n), be the SMALLEST index attaining the\ncorresponding maximum. These are exact real comparisons among a finite list\nof nested radicals, with a specified tie rule, independent of the objective.\nSet p^(0)=d^(0)=(). Recursively define the complete words\n\n    p^(n) = (p^(k), phi(P_k-1,P_l-1), p^(l)),\n        k=k_P(n), l=n-k-1,\n    d^(n) = (p^(k), psi(P_k-1,(D_l-1)/2), d^(l)),\n        k=k_D(n), l=n-k-1.\n\nHere phi and psi are exactly those in accepted b98bb807d514e80d. Induction\ngives length n, sums P_n-1 and (D_n-1)/2, respectively. All entries exceed\none: at a primitive join b-1 is the positive root of\nr^2+(A+C)r-AC=0; at a dominant join b=1+A/r with r>1 below.\nThus these are actual predetermined legal words at EVERY horizon, with no\nassumption on maximizing split patterns. Sections 2--4 specify every one of\ntheir certificate coefficients, not just their scalar values.\n\n## 2. Primitive finite-array identity\n\nFor a segment from sample a to sample z, with steps h_a,...,h_(z-1) and sum s,\ndefine\n\n    E_P = (||x_a||^2-||x_z||^2)/2\n          + sum_{a<=i<z} h_i Q_i* - s f_z\n          - s(s+1)||g_z||^2/2.\n\nWe construct a nonnegative finite-node array L_P with zero diagonal such that\n\n    E_P = -sum_{a<=i,j<=z; i!=j} L_P(i,j) Q_ij.          (P)\n\nThe empty segment has L_P=0 and E_P=0. All arrays below are embedded at their\nactual chronological indices and added if supports overlap.\n\nFor a join, let p be the last left sample, p+1 the first right sample, and z\nthe final sample. Let the left/right step sums be x,y, and b=phi(x,y). Put\n\n    t=(x+b)/(y+2-b)>0.\n\nThe denominator is positive: substituting the defining radical gives\n1<b<y+2. Let L_L,L_R be the child arrays. The complete recursion is\n\n    L_P = L_L + (1+2t)L_R\n          + t sum_{p<i<z} h_i (E_(i,p)+E_(i,z))\n          + t(b-1)E_(p,z) + t E_(z,p),                 (P-array)\n\nwhere E_(i,j) denotes one unit at the indicated ordered pair. Empty sums are\nzero. Every displayed coefficient is nonnegative.\n\nHere is the polynomial identity behind the recursion. Put C_R=y(y+1) and\nF_j=sum_{p<i<z} h_i Q_(i,j), for j=p,z. Direct substitution of the definition\nof Q gives, for ANY bridge b,\n\n    (y+2-b)(f_p-f_z)\n      -(y+3b-2b^2)||g_p||^2/2\n      -(2y^2+3y+b)||g_z||^2/2\n    = 2 E_R - F_p-F_z-(b-1)Q_(p,z)-Q_(z,p).          (P-decrease)\n\nTo check it without a black-box inequality, substitute twice the identity\n\n    sum h_i Q_i* = F_j+y(f_j-||g_j||^2/2)\n                  + <x_(p+1)-x_z,g_j-x_j>\n\ninto 2E_R, then use x_(p+1)=x_p-bg_p. Adding the two last Q terms cancels\nthe mixed product involving x_(p+1)-x_z and leaves exactly the displayed\ntwo squared-gradient coefficients.\n\nWith s=x+b+y and C_L=x(x+1), C=s(s+1), expansion also gives\n\n    E_P = E_L+E_R+(x+b)(f_p-f_z)\n          +(C_L+b-b^2)||g_p||^2/2+(C_R-C)||g_z||^2/2.\n\nThe defining quadratic b^2+(x+y)b-(xy+2x+2y+2)=0 yields\n\n    (x+b)(y+3b-2b^2)/(y+2-b)=b^2-b-C_L,\n    (x+b)(2y^2+3y+b)/(y+2-b)=C-C_R.\n\nInserting (P-decrease) proves (P-array) and (P) by induction. This proves\nthe primitive certificate on every formal vector, not merely on a compressed\nendpoint matrix.\n\n## 3. Dominant finite-array identity\n\nFor a segment with sum y, let its mass be D=2y+1. We construct positive\nweights v_i on ALL its samples, summing to D, and a nonnegative finite array\nL_D such that\n\n    E_D := ||x_a||^2/2 + sum v_i Q_i* - D f_z\n           - ||x_a-sum v_i g_i||^2/2\n         = -sum L_D(i,j) Q_ij.                        (D)\n\nFor the empty segment take v_a=1 and L_D=0. The identity then follows by\nexpanding Q_a*.\n\nJoin a primitive left segment of sum x to a dominant right segment of mass\nD=2y+1, with its weights v and array L_R. Set A=x+1 and\n\n    mu=2A/D,\n    r=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D))/2,\n    lambda=r^2=r+mu,\n    gamma=A+1=x+2,\n    b=1+A/r=psi(x,y).\n\nThe new weights u and finite array are\n\n    u_i=h_i                  (i<p),\n    u_p=gamma,\n    u_i=r v_i                (p<i<=z),\n    L_D=L_L+lambda L_R+mu sum_{p<i<=z} v_i E_(i,p).   (D-array)\n\nThey have the exact mass\n\n    M=sum u_i=x+gamma+rD=2A+rD=Dr^2\n      =G(A,D)=2(x+b+y)+1.\n\nFor completeness, the whole algebra follows from the more general identity\nin section 6. Setting these parameters makes all three entries of its mixed\nmatrix K zero: gamma=A+1 gives K_11=0, lambda=r^2 gives K_22=0, and\nlambda b=mu+gamma r gives K_12=0. The value-flow equality is mu D=x+gamma.\nThus (D) follows from (P), the right child identity, and the displayed fan.\nThere is no discarded nonnegative square or omitted mixed term.\n\n## 4. The actual full certificate at exactly 1/B_n\n\nApply section 3 recursively to the deterministic word d^(n). Its mass is\nM=B_n. On I_n={*,0,...,n}, set\n\n    lambda_(i,*)=2u_i/B_n,\n    lambda_(*,i)=0,\n    lambda_(i,j)=2L_D(i,j)/B_n  (finite i!=j).\n\nThen the exact full identity is\n\n    ||x_0||^2/B_n - 2f_n + sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n       = ||x_0-sum u_i g_i||^2/B_n.                  (Full)\n\nEvery coefficient and every formal-vector term has now been specified.\nThe function-value flow is out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n), by equating the independent\nf_i coefficients in (D). The remainder is a full rank-one PSD quadratic.\nIts kernel is x_0=sum u_i g_i. It is not a PSD assertion on a quotient.\n\nThe actual source array has no incoming terminal edge. This follows\ninductively from (D-array): its left subtree is strictly before the final\nsample, and every new edge involving that sample leaves it. Also\nu_n=sqrt(B_n), by the dominant recursion u_n=r v_n and B_n=r^2 D.\nConsequently, in the exact terminal framework of accepted1252b8823bb5a290,\n\n    alpha_star=1/sqrt(B_n), alpha_j=L_D(n,j)/B_n (j<n),\n    V_alpha=(x_0-sum_{i<n}u_i g_i)/sqrt(B_n),\n    R_alpha=0 IDENTICALLY on the entire original prefix space.       (Terminal)\n\nThe probabilities sum to one by the terminal value flow. Indeed (Full) is\nexactly ||g_n-V_alpha||^2, and the terminal identity of that accepted fact\nthen gives the displayed zero prefix remainder. This records the full\nbaseline kernel and its mixed-block normalization explicitly; the claim is\nnot just that R_alpha vanishes on one extremal trajectory.\n\nThe legal canonical Huber instance in accepted0be8b54e0a33381c, with\nx_0=1, x_star=0, threshold 1/B_n, gives 2f_n=1/B_n for this physical word.\nConsequently the constructed upper bound is attained: W_n(d^(n))=1/B_n.\n\nSome explicit horizons also check the orientation and normalization. Put\nrho=1+sqrt(2).\n\n* n=1: d=(3/2), B_1=4, u=(2,2), L_(1,0)=2.\n  Thus lambda_(1,0)=lambda_(0,*)=lambda_(1,*)=1 and the remainder is\n  ||x_0-2g_0-2g_1||^2/4.\n* n=2: put z=sqrt(9+8sqrt(2)). The maximizing split is k=1 and\n  d=(sqrt(2),(3+z)/4), B_2=(5+4sqrt(2)+z)/2.\n  The weights are u=(sqrt(2),2+sqrt(2),(1+z)/2).\n  The ONLY finite entries are L_(0,1)=1, L_(1,0)=rho,\n  L_(2,1)=2rho. The full multipliers are 2L/B_2 and 2u/B_2.\n* n=3: the maximizing split is k=1,\n  d=(sqrt(2),1+sqrt(2),3/2), B_3=6+4sqrt(2),\n  u=(sqrt(2),2+sqrt(2),2+sqrt(2),2+sqrt(2)).\n  The ONLY finite entries are\n  L_(0,1)=1, L_(1,0)=rho, L_(2,1)=rho,\n  L_(3,1)=rho, L_(3,2)=rho^2.\n\nHere the small split comparisons can themselves be checked exactly. At n=2,\nG(1,4)=4+2sqrt(3)<15/2, whereas G(rho,1)>15/2: use\nsqrt(2)>45/32 and sqrt(2)>7/5 to obtain\nsqrt(9+8sqrt(2))>9/2 and the latter strict bound. At n=3, B_2<8 since\nrho<5/2 and G(5/2,1)=(11+sqrt(21))/2<8. Thus\nG(1,B_2)<G(1,8)=6+4sqrt(2). Also\nP_2=(2+sqrt(2)+sqrt(10+8sqrt(2)))/2<(4+3sqrt(2))/2,\nbecause (2+2sqrt(2))^2=12+8sqrt(2)>10+8sqrt(2).\nThe inverse relation G(A,1)=M iff A=(M-sqrt(M))/2 shows\nG(P_2,1)<6+4sqrt(2), while G(rho,4)=6+4sqrt(2).\n\n## 5. A second exact tight instance and full-array implications\n\nFor a primitive source word of mass A,\n\n    product_i |1-h_i|=1/A.                            (Q-P)\n\nIndeed it is 1 for the empty word. At a primitive join, put C=Omega(A,E)\nand d=b-1. Then C=A+E+d and dC=AE, so the joined product is\nd/(AE)=1/C. Similarly the dominant source word satisfies\n\n    product_i |1-h_i|=1/sqrt(B_n).                    (Q-D)\n\nThe dominant induction is d/(A sqrt(D)) with d=A/r and B_n=Dr^2.\nThe actual scalar function f(x)=x^2/2, x_0=1, therefore gives exactly\n2f_n=product_i(1-h_i)^2=1/B_n. It is a second normalized tight instance.\n\nAny full certificate, however its multipliers and rank are reselected, obeys\nfor a changed nonnegative word h'=h+e\n\n    q B_n >= max{B_n/(B_n+2sum e_i),\n                 product_i(1+e_i/(h_i-1))^2}.        (Two tests)\n\nThe second expression is valid without assuming h'_i>1 because it is\nsquared; when logs are used below require h'_i>1. The first is the canonical\nHuber bound with the TRUE new step sum, the second the actual unit quadratic.\nIn particular qB_n<=1-delta, delta in (0,1), requires\n\n    sum e_i >= delta B_n/[2(1-delta)],\n    sum log(1+e_i/(h_i-1)) <= log(1-delta)/2           (if h'_i>1).\n\nFor a differentiable one-sided continuation h(t),q(t) at t=0 this gives\n\n    q'(0) >= -2 B_n^(-2) sum h_i'(0),\n    q'(0) >= (2/B_n) sum h_i'(0)/(h_i-1).            (Tangent tests)\n\nThese are NECESSARY only. At the exact baseline, complementarity on the\nHuber instance forces every optimum-source multiplier lambda_(*,i) to be\nzero, for n>=1: Q_(*,i)=tau(tau-x_i)<0, since s(h)>0 and\nx_i>tau. This conclusion applies to every tight baseline certificate,\nnot merely the particular source array. It does NOT force those entries\nto remain zero after a perturbation with non-Huber worst-case behavior.\n\n## 6. Exact obstruction for a relaxed one-fan dominant join\n\nThis class changes the source coupling and bridge, including signed\nperturbations of every positive parameter within their final sign constraints.\nIt is strictly more general than fixing b=psi or fixing the rank-one remainder.\nIt still retains a specified interface and a single cross fan; it is not the\nfull cone of arbitrary cross-copy interpolation edges.\n\nTake ANY primitive left certificate of the form\n\n    E_L+sum L_L Q = R_L,\n\nand ANY sharp dominant right certificate of the form\n\n    E_R+sum L_R Q = R_R,\n\nwhere E_L and E_R have the definitions above, D=2y+1, sum v_i=D,\nthe child finite arrays are nonnegative, and R_L,R_R are full PSD quadratics.\nThe steps and arrays inside either child need not be their source selections.\nAllow an arbitrary bridge b>=0 and parameters\n\n    gamma>=0, lambda>=mu>=0, r=lambda-mu,\n    mu D=x+gamma.                                   (Flow)\n\nThe new optimum-sink weights and finite array are\n\n    u=(h_left,gamma,r v),\n    L=L_L+lambda L_R+mu sum v_i E_(i,p),\n    M=x+gamma+rD>0.\n\nPositive child weights and multipliers may be independently reselected so\nlong as their displayed child identities remain true. No sign restriction\nis placed on their DERIVATIVES; only the final nonnegative arrays are required.\nLet V=sum_{right} v_i g_i and A=x+1. Direct expansion on the original space\ngives the exact identity\n\n    ||x_0||^2/2 + sum u_i Q_i* - M f_z + sum L_ij Q_ij\n    = ||x_p-gamma g_p-rV||^2/2\n      + R_L+lambda R_R\n      + (K_11||g_p||^2+2K_12<g_p,V>+K_22||V||^2)/2, (Join)\n\nwhere the ENTIRE mixed block is\n\n    K_11=A^2-(gamma-1)^2,\n    K_22=lambda-r^2,\n    K_12=lambda b-mu-gamma r.                        (K)\n\nTo verify the identity, use\n\n    sum v_i Q_i* = sum v_i Q_(i,p)\n                  +D(f_p-||g_p||^2/2)+<V,g_p-x_p>,\n\nmultiply the right certificate by lambda, subtract mu times this identity,\nadd the left certificate and gamma(Q_p*-f_z), and substitute\nx_(p+1)=x_p-bg_p. The coefficient of f_p-f_z is\nx+gamma-mu D and hence is zero. Completing the indicated square leaves\nx(x+1)+mu D+gamma-gamma^2=A^2-(gamma-1)^2,\nlambda-r^2, and lambda b-mu-gamma r as claimed.\n\nThe condition K PSD is necessary for the FULL parent quadratic to be PSD,\neven if both child remainders have arbitrarily large ranks. Here is why this\nis stronger than an unsupported endpoint compression. Put all left formal\ngradients, INCLUDING g_p, equal to an arbitrary vector a, and all right\ngradients equal to an independent arbitrary vector c. Choose affine function\nvalues separately on each child: f_i=f_child_end+(remaining child step sum)\ntimes its constant gradient norm squared. All within-child Q vanish. Direct\nsubstitution gives E_L=0 and E_R=D(2y+1-D)||c||^2/2=0. Thus\nR_L=R_R=0 on this restriction for EVERY such child certificate. Moreover\nV=Dc, and choosing x_0=(x+gamma)a+rDc makes the first square zero.\nAll choices of a,c are still available, so the restriction is precisely K.\nThis is a legitimate test of a full formal PSD matrix; it does not claim these\nindependent constants are one legal primal trajectory.\n\nIt follows that\n\n    gamma<=A+1,\n    r^2-r-(x+gamma)/D<=0.\n\nLet w=x+gamma. Then w<=2A and\n\n    M=w+rD <= w+(D+sqrt(D^2+4Dw))/2 <= G(A,D).        (Mass cap)\n\nBoth scalar expressions are strictly increasing in w>=0. Equality forces\n\n    gamma=A+1, mu=2A/D,\n    r=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D))/2, lambda=r^2.\n\nAt equality both diagonals of K are zero. PSD then forces the mixed entry\nto vanish too, giving b=(mu+gamma r)/lambda=1+A/r=psi(x,y).\nThus the exact source mass is the UNIQUE maximizing local parameter choice\nwithin this relaxed join, including its bridge. A positive extra remainder\ndoes not buy a larger mass because the independently constant child modes\nannihilate every sharp child remainder.\n\nThis is a finite inequality, not an infinitesimal one. Apply it at any number\nof dominant nodes with primitive prefix sums at most S_circle of their\nlengths and sharp dominant children. At a node of total length n, the exact\nDP monotonicity gives M<=G(P_k,B_l)<=B_n inductively. The root need only have\nthe displayed valid certificate of level 1/M; it need not itself be sharp.\nConsequently this entire recursive one-fan family cannot certify\nq B_n<1 at ANY horizon. It allows changes at unboundedly many scales and a\nchanged bridge rule, so its obstruction is not just the fixed finite\nterminal-library result. Its retained hypotheses are the sharp child\ninterfaces, the physical primitive prefix weights, and the single cross fan.\nNon-Huber children, independent history weights, extra interior cross edges,\nand couplings outside this construction remain open.\n\n## 7. A separate global schedule class, with unrestricted full re-selection\n\nLet n>=1, h=d^(n), and a_i=h_i-1>0. Let T be ANY n-by-n doubly stochastic\nmatrix (nonnegative entries; every row and column sums to one), and c>=0.\nDefine a new actual word in its new chronological order by\n\n    h'_i=1+c(Ta)_i.                                  (Mix)\n\nThen\n\n    W_n(h')>=1/B_n.                                  (Mix obstruction)\n\nNo hypothesis whatsoever is made here on its certificate array, its rank,\nor its worst-case function. This class includes every permutation, arbitrary\nmixing of different scales, and any common dilation or contraction of the\npositive step gaps; most such words are not ConPD words.\n\nProof. Since column sums of T equal one,\ns(h')=n+c sum a_i. If 0<=c<=1, the actual Huber lower bound is at least\n1/(1+2s(h))=1/B_n. If c>=1, log concavity gives\n\n    sum_i log((Ta)_i)\n      >=sum_(i,j) T_ij log(a_j)=sum_j log(a_j).\n\nThe unit quadratic therefore gives\n\n    W_n(h') >= c^(2n) product_i((Ta)_i)^2\n             >= c^(2n) product_i a_i^2=c^(2n)/B_n.\n\nThis proves the finite, uniform claim. It does not exclude making the\nnormalized gap vector more dispersed, or incomparable in the majorization\norder, nor any schedule with some entries below one.\n\n## 8. What is still blocked\n\nThe original target asks for either a uniform matching all-schedule lower\nbound, or one fixed delta>0 and a certified finite continuation with\nq_n B_n<=1-delta on an explicit unbounded sequence. Neither follows here.\n\nThe all-horizon source word and full array are now explicit. The one-fan\nclosure and global gap mixing classes have exact finite obstructions.\nThe remaining useful variation must leave those classes. Passing (Two tests)\nor (Tangent tests) alone is not a PSD continuation. In particular positive\noptimum-source multipliers and a higher-rank remainder after deformation\nare allowed by the original problem and must not be excluded by inference\nfrom sharp baseline complementarity.\n\nNo verification authority was added by this reconstruction. original target unresolved; no verifier allocation", "source": "arXiv:2410.12395v2 Definitions2.4-2.7, Theorems3.1/3.2 and their proofs"}, "glossary": {"P_n": "Alias of accepted primitive mass C_n=1+S_circle(n).", "D_n": "In this draft only, alias of accepted benchmark B_n.", "k_P": "Smallest exact maximizing split in the P recurrence.", "k_D": "Smallest exact maximizing split in the B recurrence.", "p^(n)": "Actual recursively selected n-step primitive word.", "d^(n)": "Actual recursively selected n-step dominant benchmark word.", "E_P": "Half-normalized primitive potential residual defined in the draft.", "E_D": "Half-normalized dominant potential residual defined in the draft.", "L_P": "Nonnegative finite Q multiplier array in E_P=-sum L_P Q.", "L_D": "Nonnegative finite Q multiplier array in E_D=-sum L_D Q.", "u": "All-sample dominant optimum-sink weights of total mass B_n.", "v": "All-sample right child dominant weights of total mass D.", "A": "One plus physical sum x of the left primitive child.", "D": "Right child sharp dominant mass 2y+1.", "M": "Parent certificate mass x+gamma+rD.", "gamma": "Bridge optimum-sink weight.", "lambda": "Scale of the inherited right child certificate, not an individual normalized multiplier.", "mu": "Scale of the right-to-bridge finite interpolation fan.", "r": "lambda-mu in a relaxed join;
