{"id": "611f193b6f8f483e", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T22:04:11.791421+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C60 strengthened full candidate: the explicit parabolic projection model remains legal for every nonnegative schedule, but its NATURAL geometric-ratio starting rule r0²=(c_m-1)/(c_m+1),v0=0 has payoff <(2500/m²)2^(-2^m) on the ENTIRE actual critical H_m for every m>=128. Exact first-step cancellation, all subsequent queried states, true distance, and nonlinear accumulated drift are controlled. This excludes only that one starting rule, not all parabolic initial states. No new solve or verifier call.", "evidence": "# C60: a nonlinear two-state legal trajectory with dense turning gradients\n\nStatus: **new explicit construction and scoped calculations; UNVERIFIED**.\nNo solve or verifier call is used. The numerical allocation is closed.\nThis branch follows the finite C59 reconstruction by seeking an analytic\ncorrelated core. It does not assume that the C59 data belong to this family.\n\nThe construction works for every finite nonnegative schedule, including\nzeros and arbitrarily large entries, with one fixed globally convex\n1-smooth function. Its all-pair law and true closest-minimizer distance\nare explicit. The quantitative payoff on the critical H_m remains open.\n\n## 1. The global function, its gradients, and its minimizers\n\nIn R^2 let\n\n    C_par={(t,s): s>=t^2/sqrt(2)},\n    F_par(X)=sup_(g in C_par) [<X,g>-||g||^2/2].      (1)\n\nThe set is closed, convex and nonempty, and contains0. Completing the\nsquare shows that the maximizer in (1) is the Euclidean projection of X\nonto C_par. It exists and is unique because the squared-distance objective\nis coercive and strictly convex. Thus F_par is finite and convex.\n\nFor completeness write p(X) for that projection. Its elementary normal\ncondition is\n\n    <X-p(X),g-p(X)><=0 for every g in C_par.\n\nApplying this twice and adding gives\n\n    ||p(X)-p(Y)||^2<=<p(X)-p(Y),X-Y>.\n\nThus p is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizers in (1) gives\n\n    0<=F_par(Y)-F_par(X)-<p(X),Y-X>\n        <=<p(Y)-p(X),Y-X><=||Y-X||^2.\n\nConsequently F_par is differentiable, its gradient is p, and it is\n1-smooth. This proof supplies the needed global property directly.\n\nThe minimizers are exactly\n\n    argmin F_par={(0,y):y<=0}.                      (2)\n\nIndeed F_par>=0 by the feasible vector0. For X=(0,y), y<=0, every g=(t,s)\nin C_par has <X,g>=ys<=0, so the maximum in (1) is0. Conversely, if\nthe vertical coordinate y>0, taking g=(0,s) with small s>0 makes the\nobjective positive. If the horizontal coordinate x is nonzero and y<=0,\ntake g=(t,t^2/sqrt(2)) with sufficiently small nonzero t of the sign of x:\nthe linear term xt dominates the terms of quadratic and quartic order.\nThis again makes the objective positive. These cases prove (2).\n\nThis F_par is not a convex quadratic: its minimizer set is a ray, whereas\nthe nonempty minimizer set of an unconstrained convex quadratic is an\naffine subspace.\n\n## 2. Coordinates that make every interpolation pair explicit\n\nFor a real r and v>=0, put\n\n    u=v+r^2,\n    X(r,v)=(r(1+v+r^2), -v/sqrt(2)),\n    G(r)=(r,r^2/sqrt(2)).                           (3)\n\nThen X-G=u(r,-1/sqrt(2)). For any (t,s) in C_par,\n\n    <X-G,(t,s)-G>\n      =u[r(t-r)-(s-r^2/sqrt(2))/sqrt(2)]\n      <=-(u/2)(t-r)^2<=0.\n\nThe normal condition therefore proves that G(r) is the actual gradient\nof F_par at X(r,v). Direct substitution into (1) gives\n\n    F_par(X(r,v))=r^2(1+v)/2+3r^4/4.                (4)\n\nFor any two sampled states r_i,v_i and r_j,v_j, with the actual values\nfrom (4), the complete signed interpolation residual is\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2\n        =<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>\n        =-(u_j/2)(r_i-r_j)^2<=0.                   (5)\n\nThe first equality after Q follows by substituting\nf_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2. The second uses the displayed parabolic\ncoordinates. This proves EVERY ordered pair simultaneously for one\nfunction; no local, adjacent, or cycle relaxation is being used.\n\nFor an initial nonzero r_0 and v_0>=0, the closest minimizer in (2) is\n\n    X_star=(0,-v_0/sqrt(2)),\n    R_0^2=dist(X_0,argmin F_par)^2\n            =r_0^2(1+v_0+r_0^2)^2.                (6)\n\nThe optimum sample fits the same formulas with r_star=0, v_star=v_0,\nu_star=v_0. Thus (5) includes both of its directions. Choosing the origin\ninstead would generally overestimate the true initial distance; (6) is\nthe normalization used throughout.\n\n## 3. The exact GD transition, with no interpolation optimization\n\nFor ANY step h>=0, starting from state(r,v), define\n\n    v_plus=v+h r^2,\n    r_plus^3+(1+v_plus)r_plus\n                      =r(1+v+r^2-h).              (7)\n\nThe cubic's left side is strictly increasing from minus infinity to\nplus infinity because its derivative is1+v_plus+3r_plus^2>=1. Thus (7)\ndefines exactly one real r_plus, by an explicit scalar algebraic rule.\nThere is no nearest-feasible-trajectory problem hidden in this definition.\n\nThe vertical coordinate of X-hG is -v_plus/sqrt(2), and its horizontal\ncoordinate is the right side of (7). Hence\n\n    X(r_plus,v_plus)=X(r,v)-h G(r).\n\nThis proves actual GD for the same global function. In particular h=0\nleaves the state unchanged, and every finite positive step gives finite\nnew state. All v_i remain nonnegative, regardless of gradient sign changes.\n\nFor a supplied finite nonnegative schedule h_0,...,h_(n-1), iterate (7)\nfrom any r_0!=0,v_0>=0. Its exact normalized payoff is\n\n    P_par(h;r_0,v_0)\n       =[r_n^2(1+v_n)+(3/2)r_n^4]\n                        /[r_0^2(1+v_0+r_0^2)^2].  (8)\n\nThe function (1), all values(4), gradients(3), positions(3), and initial\ndistance(6) are fixed together. Thus (8) is an actual lower witness for\nW_n(h), albeit not yet a profile-strength one on the critical schedules.\n\nSome immediate identities expose the changing geometry:\n\n    u_plus-u=(h-1)r^2+r_plus^2,\n    X_plus,horizontal=X_horizontal[1-h/(1+u)].      (9)\n\nIn particular, for schedules with all h>=1, the normal parameter u is\nnondecreasing, but r and the horizontal gradient can turn repeatedly.\nThe Hessian direction changes with r; this is not a fixed linear spectral\ntranscript. The vertical state accumulates h r^2 rather than discarding\nprevious turns.\n\n## 4. Exact dense-jump calibration on a different schedule\n\nTake any sigma in(0,1), v_0>=0, and define\n\n    U_0=1+v_0+sigma,\n    rho=(1+sigma)/(1-sigma),\n    h_i=2U_0 rho^i/(1-sigma),\n    r_i=(-1)^i sqrt(sigma),\n    v_i=U_0 rho^i-1-sigma.                         (10)\n\nThese v_i are nonnegative. Substitution into (7) gives\nv_(i+1)-v_i=h_i sigma and\nr_(i+1)(1+v_(i+1)+sigma)=r_i(1+v_i+sigma-h_i),\nso (10) is an exact trajectory of F_par. Its payoff is\n\n    P_par=rho^n/U_0+sigma/(2U_0^2).                 (11)\n\nThis calibration is legal and can have large payoff. It is a geometric\nstep sequence, NOT the critical ruler word, so (11) proves no claim about\nH_m or the original profile. It only checks that the mechanism supports\ncorrelated turns without a feasibility repair.\n\nThere are n nonzero collinear gradient jumps in (10):\n\n    G_i-G_(i-1)=(2(-1)^i sqrt(sigma),0), 1<=i<=n.\n\nTheir Gram J has trace4sigma n and twice the absolute off-diagonal sum\n4sigma n(n-1). For any Gram realizable with at most k nonzero jumps in\neach orthonormal coordinate, Cauchy--Schwarz coordinate by coordinate\nimplies2sum_(i<j)|J_ij|<=(k-1)trace(J). Hence this trajectory requires\nsome coordinate with at least n jumps in every orthonormal realization.\nDense chronological correlation does not require a large ambient dimension.\n\nFor a completely rational parameter example, sigma=1/4 and v_0=0 give\nrho=5/3, h_0=10/3, h_1=50/9, and r_i=(-1)^i/2. Formula (11) is then\nan exact expression, not a numerical simulation.\n\n## 5. Two simple parameter limits do not yet give the desired mechanism\n\nFirst fix a finite schedule and fix v_0=A>=0. Let r_0=t tend to zero.\nInduction in (7) gives\n\n    v_i=A+O(t^2),\n    r_i=t product_(j<i)[1-h_j/(1+A)]+O(t^3).\n\nAt each induction step, the inverse cubic has nonzero derivative1+A\nat zero, and its cubic term is O(t^3), giving the displayed expansion.\nThus the payoff tends to the ordinary scalar quadratic value\n\n    [1/(1+A)] product_(j<n)[1-h_j/(1+A)]^2.         (12)\n\nThe implicit constants here depend on the entire fixed finite schedule.\nThey have NOT been bounded uniformly along growing H_m. Consequently\n(12) is a calibration and an explanation of why an infinitesimal core\ndoes not itself add a new mechanism; it is not a class obstruction.\n\nSecond put S=sum h_i and sigma=r_0^2. If v_0>=S, no horizontal sign flip\noccurs and |r_(i+1)|<=|r_i|. Indeed h_i<=v_i and the right side of (7)\nhas the sign of r_i. Evaluating the increasing cubic at r_i shows that\nits excess over that right side is h_i r_i(1+r_i^2), with the appropriate\nsign, so the claimed decrease in absolute value follows. Hence\n\n    v_n<=v_0+S sigma,\n    P_par <= [1+v_0+S sigma+(3/2)sigma]\n                                  /(1+v_0+sigma)^2. (13)\n\nAlong any growing-horizon choices with\nv_0/[S(1+sigma)] tending to infinity, this gives S P_par tending to zero.\nThus placing the initial state arbitrarily far down the flat minimizer\ndirection also loses the desired scale. This rules out only the displayed\nparameter regime; intermediate coupled regimes remain open.\n\n## 6. The natural geometric calibration is small on the entire critical word\n\nOne tempting choice is to match the geometric growth ratio rho in(10)\nto the physical critical parameter c_m. This means\n\n    m>=128, epsilon=1/m,\n    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,\n    sigma=(c-1)/(c+1), r_0=sqrt(sigma), v_0=0.       (14)\n\nFor THIS particular starting rule, the full actual H_m trajectory obeys\n\n    P_par(H_m;r_0,0)\n              < (2500/m^2) 2^(-2^m).              (15)\n\nThe same is true with the opposite initial horizontal sign, by symmetry.\nThis does not bound the family over other choices of r_0,v_0.\n\nHere is a proof that tracks all later queried states, rather than inferring\nthe answer only from the first cancellation. Elementary parameter bounds are\n\n    12/5<c<5/2, 2/5<a<3/7,\n    2/5<sigma<3/7, 7/5<b0=1+a<10/7.\n\nFor the lower bound on a, use 1+sqrt(2)<17/7 and\n(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon)>=127/129, giving a>889/2193>2/5.\nThe other inequalities follow by direct comparison of positive fractions.\nSince c is greater than1+sqrt(2),\n\n    0<sigma-a\n      =epsilon[2lambda(lambda-1)+lambda^2 epsilon+c+1]\n                                               /[c(c+1)]\n      <2epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2).                 (16)\n\nFor the last bound the numerator in brackets is less than12, using\nlambda<5/2 and epsilon<=1/128, while c(c+1)>204/25>8.\n\nAt the first actual step b0, (7) gives\n\n    14/25<v_1=b0 sigma<30/49<5/8,\n    r_1(1+v_1+r_1^2)=r_0(sigma-a),\n    0<r_1<2/m.                                    (17)\n\nWe now prove that the entire remaining trajectory stays in the region\n\n    1/2<=v<=3/4, |r|<=1/10.                        (18)\n\nInside this region the exact step rule gives explicit amplitude bounds.\nFor a level-zero step b0, the numerator factor\n1+v+r^2-b0 is positive and at most1+3/4+1/100-7/5=9/25.\nThe denominator1+v_plus+r_plus^2 is at least3/2, so\n\n    |r_plus| <= L0 |r|, L0=1/4.\n\nFor a level j>=1 step b_j=1+a c^j, one has b_j>=2-epsilon>1.76,\nwhereas1+v+r^2<=1.76. The numerator is therefore negative, and\n\n    |r_plus| <= Lj |r|, Lj=(2/7)c^j.               (19)\n\nIndeed its absolute value is less than a c^j and its denominator is at\nleast3/2, while a<3/7. These bounds use the current state in (18) but\ndo not require the next state to be in that region.\n\nLet P_j be the product of these scalar bounds over the full word H_j,\nwith P_0=1, and put A0=2/7 and beta=c^2/14<1/2. For j>=1,\n\n    P_j=beta^(2^(j-1))/(A0 c^(j+1)),\n    theta_j=P_j Lj=beta^(2^(j-1))/c<1.             (20)\n\nThis follows either by multiplying the known counts of each ruler level\nor by P_(j+1)=P_j^2 Lj and P_1=1/4. Every prefix product in every H_j\nis at most1: it is true for H_0; in the right child the left-child and\ncentral-step prefactor is P_j Lj=theta_j<1, and the induction applies\ninside both children. At j=0 that prefactor is1/4.\n\nFor the accumulated vertical drift, let E_j be the weighted prefix sum\n\n    E_j=sum_(i in H_j) h_i [product_(t<i)L_(level(t))]^2.\n\nThen E_1=b0<3/2 and, for j>=1,\n\n    E_(j+1)=(1+theta_j^2)E_j+P_j^2 b_j.            (21)\n\nThe two needed infinite bounds are elementary. Since c>2,beta<1/2,\n\n    sum_(j>=1)theta_j^2\n        <(1/4)sum_(j>=1)2^(-2^j)<=1/8.\n\nAlso b_j<c^j for j>=1: both a and c^(-j) are less than3/7. Therefore\n\n    sum_(j>=1)P_j^2 b_j\n       <sum_(j>=1)49*2^(-2j-5)=49/96.\n\nFor this estimate insert(20), use A0^(-2)=49/4, c>2,\nbeta^(2^j)<2^(-2^j), and2^j>=j+1. Finally\nproduct(1+theta_j^2)<=1/[1-sum theta_j^2]<8/7,\nas follows by expanding the finite products and bounding their elementary\nsymmetric sums by powers of the total sum. Passing to the limit preserves\nthe bound. Equations(21) consequently imply\n\n    E_j<(3/2+49/96)(8/7)=193/84<3                 (22)\n\nfor every j.\n\nIn the suffix of H_m after its first step, every prefix product is at\nmost4, because reinserting that first L0 multiplies it by1/4. The suffix\nweighted drift sum is at most16E_m<48. Hence, as long as(18) has held,\n\n    |r_i|<=4r_1<8/m<=1/16<1/10,\n    v_i<=v_1+48r_1^2<5/8+192/m^2<3/4.\n\nThe lower bound v_i>=v_1>1/2 follows from v_plus=v+h r^2. These strict\nbounds prove(18) throughout by a first-exit argument: the same estimates\ncontrol a proposed first exiting step, which is impossible.\n\nAt the terminal sample the entire suffix product is4P_m. From(17),(20),\n\n    r_n^2<(64/m^2)P_m^2.\n\nThe numerator of(8) is less than2r_n^2 by(18), and the initial denominator\nsigma(1+sigma)^2 exceeds98/125>3/4. Thus\n\n    P_par<3r_n^2<(192/m^2)P_m^2\n          <(2352/m^2)2^(-2^m),\n\nwhich proves(15). For the exact benchmark, accepted factb98bb807d514e80d\ngives B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3), so this particular parabolic witness divided\nby U_n^ZJ tends to zero. Only this fixed starting rule is excluded.\n\nThe responsible state variable is visible in(17): matching the geometric\nratio makes the critical first step almost annihilate the horizontal\ncoordinate. The subsequent normal state remains near its post-first-step\nvalue, and the complete ruler-product/drift bounds above prevent recovery.\nThis is a statement about the entire actual legal trajectory, not a\none-pair dual test or a comparison with an independently reset right child.\n\n## 7. The concrete remaining critical-word problem\n\nFor the actual critical word fix, within each horizon m,\n\n    c_m=(1+sqrt(2))(1+1/m), a_m=(1-1/m)/c_m,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m c_m^j,H_j).\n\nLet T_h denote the explicit two-state transition (7). The full block map\nis defined EXACTLY by\n\n    Phi_0=identity,\n    Phi_(j+1)=Phi_j composed with T_(1+a_m c_m^j)\n                                     composed with Phi_j.\n\nThe central task in this branch is to select explicit r_0(m),v_0(m), or\nprove a useful uniform upper bound for this mechanism, while controlling\nthe exact payoff (8) under Phi_m. The model is already an actual legal\nfunction for every such selection, so no separate interpolation optimization\nis required. What is missing is the QUANTITATIVE estimate on that nonlinear\nrecursion. Neither (11), the small-amplitude calibration, nor (13) fills it.\n\nAn open subgoal is to compare the increase in v across both copies of a\ndyadic block with the horizontal sign and gain at its central bridge.\nThe state v must remain shared between the copies; restarting it, freezing\nthe curvature, or replacing the two copies by independent instances would\nchange the function and lose the construction.\n\n## 8. Source grounding and verification scope\n\nJiang, Yue and Zhou, arXiv:1911.12545v2 (2021), Theorem2.2, gives the\nEuclidean projection onto S={(x,y):||x||^2<=y}: inside S it is the point,\nand outside it is(x/(1+mu),y+mu/2), where mu>=max(0,-2y) is the unique\nsolution of ||x||^2/(1+mu)^2=y+mu/2. The paper's surrounding definition\nof S and full proof were read. It supplies a closely matched projection\nmechanism, not a GD complexity result for the present function. Its\ncubic-regularization convergence theorem is not invoked.\n\nSections1--6 derive the two-dimensional specialization and all required\nproperties directly. No external theorem is used as a black box, and no\nunverified earlier project result is a premise. The finite C59 data merely\nmotivated seeking dense turning gradients. Every new statement here remains\na draft until accepted by the verifier; no claim is made that this family\nsolves the all-schedule exact-profile problem.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["f2d2743d0d681bdd"], "artifact": "research/max3_c60_parabolic_projection_dynamics.md", "scope": "one natural initial-state rule only; intermediate coupled regimes open", "verification_status": "UNVERIFIED"}, "glossary": {"C_par": "Closed parabolic epigraph {(t,s):s>=t²/sqrt2}", "F_par": "Convex1-smooth function sup over g in C_par of <X,g>-||g||²/2", "P_par": "Actual true-distance-normalized payoff of the explicit nonlinear parabolic GD trajectory", "A0": "Scalar majorant coefficient2/7 in the level-dependent critical-word amplitude bound", "beta": "Scalar c_m²/14, strictly below1/2 for m>=128", "P_j": "Product of the explicitly displayed amplitude majorants over the full critical word H_j, local to Section6", "E_j": "Weighted prefix-product sum controlling the complete accumulated vertical drift, local to Section6"}}
{"id": "55622337e6b3b4fd", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T22:09:15.496135+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "The positive-height signed-simplex plan now has an explicit all-pair candidate via exact cancellation of each extinguished proximal coordinate. The construction is legal in the two-increment cone, but its quantitative screening gives a new scoped loss below the Huber witness on every actual H_m cutoff.", "evidence": "Draft derivation, full proof being consolidated. In k signed coordinates, p0=sum A_j e_j and p_i=-B_(i-1)e_(i-1)+sum_(j>=i)A_j e_j, A_k=0. Put w0=r0, w_j=C+r_j (j>=1), R_j=sum_(u<j)w_u, L_j=R_j+C; r_k>0 is an artificial terminal shape parameter, not a changed physical step. Require B_(j-1)=R_j A_(j-1)/(C+r_j). Then Y0=0 and actual GD gives Y_j=-r_j B_(j-1)e_(j-1)-L_j sum_(l>=j)A_l e_l: every older proximal coordinate is exactly0. Choose A0=1 and A_j²=r_j B_(j-1)²/[L_j(1+R_(j+1)/(C+r_(j+1)))]. With K_s=sum_(l>=s)A_l²-A_(s-1)B_(s-1), one gets K_(j+1)-K_j=C B_(j-1)(A_(j-1)+B_(j-1))/L_j>0. Hence future normal comparisons reduce to the next equality, older normal comparisons are negative, and the same finite projection function interpolates every ordinary sample. All endpoint heights after0 are strictly positive. The unique minimizer is0 because0 lies in the interior of conv(p0,...,pk), so true squared distance is C² sum A_j² and payoff is (1+2r_k)B_(k-1)² divided by it. A product bound using lambda_j=r_j R_j/[(C+r_j)(C+R_j)] and actual2-adic counts yields M_m*payoff<1/2 for all cutoffs m>=128; the complete rational constants and scope will be published next. This rejects the cancellation rule only. Without cancellation, the exact next normal instead has RHS r_j B_(j-1)²-z_(j-1)B_(j-1), where z_(j-1)=(C+r_j)B_(j-1)-R_j A_(j-1) is the retained extinguished proximal coordinate; negative z can supply real additional budget.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "2b44a2d3c85cda91", "branch_id": "positive_height_signed_simplex", "status": "partial", "used_results": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "key_stuck_points": ["Cancellation makes the transfer product quantitatively insufficient; shared nonzero extinct-coordinate residuals remain outside the candidate."]}, "glossary": {"w_j": "C+r_j for j>=1 and r0 for j=0.", "R_j": "sum of w_u over0<=u<j.", "L_j": "R_j+C.", "K_s": "Cross inner product of any earlier gradient with p_s.", "z_j": "Frozen proximal coordinate after coordinate j vanishes from the gradient.", "lambda_j": "Positive scalar contraction r_j R_j/[(C+r_j)(C+R_j)].", "r_k": "Positive artificial terminal shape parameter, not a physical step."}}
{"id": "0187e698076c31c3", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T22:25:50.339991+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Positive-height signed interval simplex completed and quantitatively excluded under its explicit extinct-proximal-coordinate cancellation rule. One finite real function has all original pairs, unique minimizer, true initial distance, and a two-increment Gram decomposition. Every legal amplitude choice at every actual H_m cutoff, m>=128, has M_m*V_can<275/648<1/2 and limsup B_(2^m-1)*V_can<=275/[162 exp(1)]<1. Nonzero residuals obey an exact propagation charge; a scalable forward-only repair fails an original earlier-vertex pair.", "evidence": "# Positive-height signed interval simplexes: construction and a scoped loss\n\nStatus: **complete joint draft, not verifier accepted**. No optimization,\ninstallation, or fact submission is used. Accepted facts\n`1252b8823bb5a290` and `b98bb807d514e80d` supply, respectively, the\nsmooth-convex interpolation convention and the exact benchmark recursions.\nEvery other assertion used below is proved in this document. The sharper\nbenchmark comparison was recalled from shared finding `70e9da73936cb7b3`\nas a lead; its complete elementary proof is included here and that finding\nis not a correctness premise.\n\nThis is a new positive-height construction, rather than an extension of\nthe previous zero-height bound. It gives one explicit finite-dimensional\nprojection function, one value array, the actual initial distance, all\noriginal interpolation pairs, and a two-increment Gram decomposition.\nAll endpoint heights after the first can be strictly positive.\n\nThe quantitative conclusion is a failure of a precisely specified rule:\nafter a coordinate vanishes from the gradient, its proximal coordinate is\nset to zero. Uniformly over every dyadic cutoff and every legal amplitude\nchoice in this rule, on the actual H_m with m>=128,\n\n    V_can < (275/648)/M_m < 1/(2M_m) < 1/(2M_m-1).\n\nHere V_can is the normalized last objective. For the exact benchmark,\n\n    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) sup V_can\n        <= 275/[162 exp(1)] < 1.\n\nThis does not bound the unrestricted W_n(H_m), all positive-height\nreflection/extinction histories, or the full two-increment cone.\nSection 8 identifies the missing signed residual and an exact necessary\npropagation condition. Section 9 refutes a forward-only repair on the\nactual H_m with an explicit positive residual in an original pair.\n\n## 1. The physical schedule and the generating rule\n\nFix m>=128. Throughout this whole horizon, put\n\n    sigma=1+sqrt(2), c_rul=sigma(1+1/m),\n    a_rul=(1-1/m)/c_rul,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_rul c_rul^j,H_j),\n    M_j=1+sum H_j, n=2^m-1.\n\nThe parameters are not recomputed inside the recursion. Choose an integer\n0<=ell<m, and put q=m-ell, k=2^q-1, C=M_ell. Then the exact word is\n\n    H_m=(H_ell,1+r_0,H_ell,...,1+r_(k-1),H_ell),\n    r_j=a_rul c_rul^(ell+nu_2(j+1)), 0<=j<k.             (1)\n\nThe integer nu_2(v) is the largest d for which 2^d divides v. Each of\nthe k+1 ordinary cells has mass C-1. Its ending query is the checkpoint\nquery, except in the last cell, where it is the original terminal sample.\nIn particular the physical final step is never changed.\n\nIn R^k with its orthonormal coordinate vectors e_0,...,e_(k-1), choose\npositive amplitudes Aamp_j,Bamp_j for 0<=j<k, and define\n\n    p_0=sum_(l=0)^(k-1) Aamp_l e_l,\n    p_j=-Bamp_(j-1)e_(j-1)+sum_(l=j)^(k-1)Aamp_l e_l,\n                                                    1<=j<=k.       (2)\n\nThus coordinate l is initially Aamp_l, becomes -Bamp_l in cell l+1,\nand then vanishes. There is no persistent extra coordinate in this rule.\nSet\n\n    P_can=conv{p_0,...,p_k},\n    F_can(x)=max_(v in P_can)(<x,v>-||v||^2/2),\n    X_0=C p_0.                                                     (3)\n\nThe family being screened consists of actual trajectories of this ONE\nfunction starting from this X_0 and having the gradients in (2) on their\nwhole respective cells. The initial endpoint has proximal point zero.\n\nLet Z_j be the endpoint of cell j and Y_j=Z_j-p_j. Actual GD with these\ncell gradients requires\n\n    Y_0=0,  Y_(j+1)=Y_j-r_j p_j-Cp_(j+1).                         (4)\n\nA query inside cell j, with remaining ordinary mass u in [0,C-1], has\n\n    X_(j,u)=Y_j+(1+u)p_j.                                         (5)\n\nIntroduce one freely chosen parameter r_k>=0. It is a terminal shape\nparameter, NOT an additional physical step. Define\n\n    wpre_0=r_0,  wpre_j=C+r_j (1<=j<=k),\n    Rpre_j=sum_(v=0)^(j-1)wpre_v (1<=j<=k+1),\n    Lpre_j=Rpre_j+C.\n\nThe extra hypothesis of the cancellation rule is\n\n    Bamp_(j-1)=Rpre_j Aamp_(j-1)/(C+r_j), 1<=j<=k.                 (6)\n\nFor j<k this says exactly that coordinate j-1 of the proximal point\nbecomes zero after that coordinate leaves the gradient. For j=k,\n(6) just parameterizes the terminal amplitude with nonnegative terminal\nheight. Indeed that height is\nRpre_k Aamp_(k-1)Bamp_(k-1)-C Bamp_(k-1)^2.\nEvery positive terminal amplitude with nonnegative height has a unique\nr_k>=0 in (6).\n\nDirectly expanding (4) coordinate by coordinate gives\n\n    Y_j=-r_j Bamp_(j-1)e_(j-1)\n               -Lpre_j sum_(l=j)^(k-1)Aamp_l e_l, 1<=j<=k.        (7)\n\nFor example, an older coordinate l<j-1 has coefficient\n-Rpre_(l+1)Aamp_l+(C+r_(l+1))Bamp_l=0 by (6). The current coordinate\nis C Bamp_(j-1)-Rpre_j Aamp_(j-1)=-r_j Bamp_(j-1).\nAn untouched coordinate l>=j accumulates the coefficient -Lpre_j.\n\nPut\n\n    Ecan_j=sum_(l=j)^(k-1)Aamp_l^2,  Ecan_k=0,\n    Ncan_0=Ecan_0, Ncan_j=Ecan_j+Bamp_(j-1)^2 (j>=1),\n    Kcan_j=Ecan_j-Aamp_(j-1)Bamp_(j-1) (j>=1).\n\nCoordinate multiplication gives\n\n    ||p_j||^2=Ncan_j,\n    <p_i,p_j>=Kcan_j whenever i<j.                                (8)\n\nThese are correlated gradients, generally with negative cross products.\nTheir full initial norm is C^2 Ecan_0. Also, independently of r_k,\n\n    M_m=Rpre_k+2C.                                                (9)\n\nBoth sides equal (k+1)C+sum_(j<k)r_j by counting the physical cells.\n\n## 2. One explicit amplitude law and one smooth convex function\n\nChoose any r_k>0, for example r_k=C. All Rpre_j,Lpre_j are now explicit\nfinite real numbers. Set Aamp_0=1. Use (6) to determine Bamp_0. For\nj=1,...,k-1 recursively set\n\n    Aamp_j^2 = r_j Bamp_(j-1)^2 /\n                 [Lpre_j(1+Rpre_(j+1)/(C+r_(j+1)))],\n    Aamp_j = positive square root of the preceding expression,\n    Bamp_j = Rpre_(j+1) Aamp_j/(C+r_(j+1)).                       (10)\n\nEvery amplitude is positive. These are finite formulas; no feasibility\nproblem or optimization is hidden in the definition. Their key identity is\n\n    Lpre_j Aamp_j(Aamp_j+Bamp_j)=r_j Bamp_(j-1)^2,\n                                                       1<=j<k.  (11)\n\nWe first justify the function in (3). Projection onto the compact convex\nset P_can has a unique minimizer of squared distance. Completing the\nsquare in (3) makes this projection its unique maximizing v. A vector p\nin P_can is the projection of x precisely when\n\n    <x-p,v-p><=0 for every v in P_can.                            (12)\n\nNecessity follows by considering the segment from p to v. Sufficiency\nfollows by expanding ||x-v||^2. Applying (12) at x and x' and adding gives\n||p-p'||^2<=<p-p',x-x'>, hence the projection is 1-Lipschitz. Comparing\nthe two maxima in (3) gives\n\n    0<=F_can(x')-F_can(x)-<p,x'-x>\n        <=<p'-p,x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.\n\nThus F_can is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable, and 1-smooth,\nwith gradient the projection onto P_can.\n\nThe origin is in the interior of P_can. To exhibit its barycentric\ncoefficients, choose alpha_0>0 and recursively set\n\n    alpha_(l+1)=(Aamp_l/Bamp_l)sum_(i=0)^l alpha_i,\n\nthen choose alpha_0 so the total sum is 1. Every coefficient is positive,\nand the l-th coordinate of sum alpha_i p_i is zero. The vectors\np_1,...,p_k are linearly independent: their coordinate matrix is triangular\nwith nonzero diagonal -Bamp_0,...,-Bamp_(k-1). The same coordinate\nrecursion makes the space of linear relations among all k+1 vertices\none-dimensional. Its positive generating relation has nonzero sum, so\nthere is no affine dependence. Consequently P_can is a full-dimensional\nsimplex and the displayed positive convex combination is interior.\n\nSince 0 is an interior point, the only vector x satisfying\n<x,v><=0 for all v in P_can is 0. By (12) this is precisely the set\nof x with projection zero. Therefore F_can has UNIQUE minimizer 0,\nF_can(0)=0, and its true initial squared distance is C^2 Ecan_0.\n\n## 3. All original interpolation pairs, with shared positive heights\n\nDefine delta_can_j=<Y_j,p_j>. For j>=1, (7) gives\n\n    delta_can_j=r_j Bamp_(j-1)^2-Lpre_j Ecan_j.                  (13)\n\nFor every s<j, coordinate multiplication gives\n\n    <Y_j,p_s>=-r_j Aamp_(j-1)Bamp_(j-1)-Lpre_j Ecan_j\n                 <=delta_can_j.                               (14)\n\nFor every s>j the corresponding value is\n\n    <Y_j,p_s>=-Lpre_j Kcan_s.                                   (15)\n\nTo control ALL future vertices at once, use (6) and (11) to compute\n\n    Kcan_(j+1)-Kcan_j\n       =Aamp_(j-1)Bamp_(j-1)-Aamp_j(Aamp_j+Bamp_j)\n       =C Bamp_(j-1)(Aamp_(j-1)+Bamp_(j-1))/Lpre_j >0.           (16)\n\nMoreover Kcan_k=-Aamp_(k-1)Bamp_(k-1)<0, so every Kcan_j is negative.\nEquation (11) also gives, for 1<=j<k,\n\n    delta_can_j=-Lpre_j Kcan_(j+1)>0.                           (17)\n\nThus (15) is at most delta_can_j for every s>j, by (16).\nThe terminal height is delta_can_k=r_k Bamp_(k-1)^2>0. At j=0,\nY_0=0 satisfies every normal comparison with equality. Equations\n(14)--(17) establish (12) at every endpoint, including against vertex 0,\nwhich belongs to the convex hull. They retain both orientations and every\nolder and later vertex.\n\nThe same argument covers all original samples inside each ordinary cell.\nFor any i,j, <p_j,p_i-p_j><=0. For i<j, this follows from\nKcan_j-Ncan_j=-Bamp_(j-1)(Aamp_(j-1)+Bamp_(j-1))<0.\nFor i>j, Kcan_i<=Ecan_i<=Ecan_j<=Ncan_j. Add u times this inequality\nto the endpoint normal, with u>=0. Thus (12) holds at every position (5),\nand its actual gradient is p_j. Equations (4)--(5) now verify every\nphysical GD step on the original word.\n\nThere is ONE value array, namely\n\n    f_(j,u)=delta_can_j+(u+1/2)Ncan_j.                          (18)\n\nThis is its actual value under F_can. For an explicit all-pair check,\nthe residual convention in accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290 is\n\n    Q_ab=f_a-f_b+<g_a,X_b-X_a>+||g_a-g_b||^2/2.\n\nSubstituting (5),(18) gives exactly\n\n    Q_((i,u),(j,v))=<Y_j+v p_j,p_i-p_j><=0.                   (19)\n\nThe inequalities against the optimum jet (0,0,0) are\n\n    Q_((j,u),star)=0,\n    Q_(star,(j,u))=-delta_can_j-u Ncan_j<=0.                   (20)\n\nNo independent selection of values for different pairs has occurred.\nThe normalized terminal objective is exactly\n\n    V_can=(1+2r_k)Bamp_(k-1)^2/(C^2 Ecan_0).                 (21)\n\nFinally, this legal trajectory belongs to the requested increment cone\nby an explicit decomposition. In coordinate l<k-1 the only gradient\nchanges are -(Aamp_l+Bamp_l) at the transition from cell l to l+1,\nand Bamp_l at the transition from cell l+1 to l+2. Coordinate k-1\nhas only its first change before the terminal sample. The Gram of all\ngradient increments is the sum, over l, of the outer products of these\ntwo-supported lists. Each is a real PSD block. For the full Gram retain\nalso the initial-vector and initial-gradient entries C Aamp_l and Aamp_l\nin the same scalar coordinate lists. No original initial row or gradient\nhas been removed; every weight in this finite decomposition is 1.\n\n## 4. Necessary product loss for EVERY legal cancellation-rule member\n\nThe amplitudes need not follow (10) in this section. Suppose any positive\namplitudes satisfying (6) give an actual trajectory of (3). The endpoint\nnormal comparing current j to future vertex j+1 is necessary. Equations\n(13),(15) show that it is exactly\n\n    Lpre_j Aamp_j(Aamp_j+Bamp_j)<=r_j Bamp_(j-1)^2,\n                                                   1<=j<k.      (22)\n\nDefine\n\n    lambda_can_j=r_j Rpre_j/[(C+r_j)(C+Rpre_j)] in (0,1).\n\nSubstituting (6) into (22) gives\n\n    Aamp_j^2/Aamp_(j-1)^2\n       <= [wpre_(j+1)/wpre_j] lambda_can_j\n                                  [Rpre_j/Rpre_(j+2)].          (23)\n\nIndeed Rpre_(j+2)=Rpre_(j+1)+wpre_(j+1), and the right side\nexpands to r_j Rpre_j^2 wpre_(j+1) divided by\nwpre_j^2 Lpre_j(Rpre_(j+1)+wpre_(j+1)). This is precisely the\ncoefficient obtained from (22).\n\nThe products in (23) telescope, giving\n\n    Bamp_(k-1)^2/Aamp_0^2\n      <= Rpre_k r_0 Rpre_2 /\n             [wpre_k wpre_1(Rpre_k+wpre_k)]\n                     * product_(j=1)^(k-1)lambda_can_j.         (24)\n\nFor k=1 the empty-product identity still holds, with\nRpre_2=Rpre_1+wpre_1. For k>=2, the quantities r_0,Rpre_2,wpre_1\nare physical prefix data, independent of the terminal shape parameter.\n\nLet d_can=2C-1>=1. Combining Ecan_0>=Aamp_0^2 with (21),(24) yields\n\n    V_can <= Rpre_k r_0 Rpre_2/(C^2 wpre_1)\n          * (2wpre_k-d_can)/[wpre_k(Rpre_k+wpre_k)]\n          * product_(j=1)^(k-1)lambda_can_j\n      < 2r_0 Rpre_2/(C^2 wpre_1)\n          * product_(j=1)^(k-1)lambda_can_j,  k>=2.              (25)\n\nThe last strict inequality follows from d_can>0 and Rpre_k>0.\nIt is uniform in every legal terminal amplitude and in all amplitudes\nbefore it. Positive endpoint heights have not been set to zero.\n\n## 5. Uniform loss on the actual ruler, over every cutoff\n\nThe physical mass recursion is M_(j+1)=2M_j+a_rul c_rul^j. Hence\n\n    A_mass=a_rul/(c_rul-2),\n    M_j=A_mass c_rul^j+(1-A_mass)2^j.                           (26)\n\nFor m>=128,\n\n    sigma<c_rul<5/2,  3/8<a_rul<sigma-2<c_rul-2<1/2,\n    3/4<A_mass<1,\n    (3/4)c_rul^j<M_j<=c_rul^j.                                (27)\n\nFor explicit rational checks, sigma<17/7 implies\nc_rul<2193/896<5/2; a_rul>(127/128)/(5/2)=127/320>3/8;\nand a_rul<1/sigma=sigma-2. These prove every inequality used in (27).\nSet t_can=a_rul c_rul^ell/C. Then\n\n    0<t_can<c_rul-2<1/2,  M_m/C<(4/3)c_rul^q.                 (28)\n\nFor q>=2, the first three checkpoint excesses are\nr_0=t_can C, r_1=c_rul t_can C, r_2=t_can C. Therefore\n\n    2r_0 Rpre_2/(C^2 wpre_1)\n       =(2t_can/C)[1+t_can/(1+c_rul t_can)]<3/(2C),\n    lambda_can_1\n       =[c_rul t_can/(1+c_rul t_can)]\n           [t_can/(1+t_can)]<5/27.                            (29)\n\nThere are exactly 2^(q-1)-1 lowest-level checkpoints among indices\n1,...,k-1. Each has lambda_can_j<1/3, because r_j=t_can C.\nIndex 1 is the first high checkpoint and is not among them. All other\nfactors are less than 1. Equations (25),(28),(29) give, for q>=3,\n\n    M_m V_can\n      < (10/27)(5/2)^q 3^(-(2^(q-1)-1))\n      <=625/2916.                                             (30)\n\nAt q=3 the expression is 625/2916. Its ratio at q+1 to its value at q\nis (5/2)3^(-2^(q-1))<1, proving the last inequality for every q>=3.\n\nWhen q=2, k=3 and\n\n    M_m/C=4+(2+c_rul)t_can<25/4,\n    Rpre_2/C=1+(1+c_rul)t_can<11/4,\n    lambda_can_2< (1/3)(11/15)=11/45.\n\nThe same product bound now gives the sharper estimate\n\n    M_m V_can < (25/4)(3/2)(5/27)(11/45)=275/648.             (31)\n\nWhen q=1, there is just one checkpoint. Writing A=Aamp_0,B=Bamp_0,\nthe terminal numerator before normalization is\n\n    2r_0 AB-(2C-1)B^2 <= r_0^2 A^2/(2C-1).\n\nSince M_m=(2+t_can)C and C=M_(m-1)>=2, this gives\n\n    M_m V_can <=(2+t_can)t_can^2 C/(2C-1)<5/12.              (32)\n\nAs 5/12<275/648 and 625/2916<275/648, (30)--(32) prove\n\n    sup_(all cutoffs and legal cancellation amplitudes)\n          M_m V_can < gamma_can:=275/648<1/2.                (33)\n\nThe no-checkpoint, constant-gradient extension with terminal proximal\npoint zero has payoff 1/M_m^2 and also obeys this bound for m>=128.\nIt can be realized by the projection function for the segment [0,p],\nstarting at M_m p; zero is a closest minimizer. This degenerate extension\nis not needed for the simplex construction in Sections 1--3.\n\nIn particular (33) is below the legal Huber witness 1/(2M_m-1), whose\nexistence is part of accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d. The new construction\ntherefore supplies no gain even over that baseline.\n\n## 6. Comparison with the actual exact benchmark\n\nFor completeness, the required sharper scalar bound follows directly\nfrom the EXACT recursions in accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d. Let\nrho=log_2(sigma)>1. Their primitive shifted mass C_n=S_circle(n)+1\nhas join\n\n    Omega(A,B)=[A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB)]/2,\n\nand their dominant denominator B_n has join\n\n    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nWe prove\n\n    C_n<=(n+1)^rho, B_n<=1+4n^rho.                            (34)\n\nFor t>0 set J(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. Differentiating with t=exp(z) gives\n\n    d^2/dz^2 log J(exp(z))\n      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)\n            [(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/J(t)^2 >0.\n\nThe bracket is positive because it vanishes at 0 and its derivative\nrho[(1+t)^(rho-1)-1] is positive. Convexity at z and -z implies\nJ(t)J(1/t)>=J(1)^2=(sigma-1)^2=2. For A=t^rho B and\nD=(1+t)^rho B, this says (D-A)(D-B)>=2AB, so\nD^2-(A+B)D-AB>=0. The positive root of that polynomial is Omega(A,B).\nThus\n\n    Omega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho,\n\nincluding zero arguments by continuity. Induction in the exact primitive\nrecursion proves the first bound in (34). Also G(A,D)<=4A+D, since\nsqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A. Induct in the dominant recursion and use\n(k+1)^rho+(n-k-1)^rho<=n^rho to prove its second bound. The last\npower inequality follows by integrating the increasing derivative of\nx^rho; it includes a zero summand.\n\nEquation (26) gives, with e=exp(1),\n\n    M_m/sigma^m\n      =(1+1/m)^m[A_mass+(1-A_mass)(2/c_rul)^m] -> e.           (35)\n\nIndeed a_rul tends to 1/sigma=sigma-2, so A_mass tends to 1,\nand 2/c_rul is bounded above by 2/sigma<1. Consequently (33)--(35)\nprove the finite and asymptotic exact-profile bounds\n\n    B_n V_can < gamma_can [1+4(2^m-1)^rho]/M_m,\n    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) sup V_can\n       <=4 gamma_can/e=275/(162e)<1.                          (36)\n\nThe supremum includes all legal amplitudes and all cutoffs. If q=m-ell\ntends to infinity, the right side of (30) tends to zero, so the exact-B\nratio tends to zero as well. No fitted dyadic constant or assumed\nasymptotic phase for B_n was used.\n\n## 7. What has, and has not, been excluded\n\nThe constructor (10) proves nonemptiness and simultaneous attainability\nof every original pair, including optimum and remote pairs. The bound\n(33) applies more broadly to every legal projection trajectory satisfying\nthe stated cancellation rule, even when (22) is strict. It does not assume\nan acute cone: in the explicit constructor every distinct-gradient cross\ninner product is strictly negative by (16).\n\nThe assumptions of the obstruction include (2), the fixed initial point\nX_0=Cp_0 (equivalently Y_0=0), the one projection function (3), and the\nextinguished-coordinate cancellation in (6). Positive endpoint heights\nalone do not evade it. General joint value arrays, different initial\nproximal points, a persistent final coordinate, different two-change\nhistories, and nonzero extinct-coordinate residuals are outside its scope.\nNo unrestricted upper certificate and no common-array A2 separator is\nclaimed.\n\n## 8. Exact residual equations for a changed rule\n\nDrop cancellation but retain (2)--(5), positive amplitudes, and the same\nprojection function. For 1<=j<=k define its effective endpoint parameter\n\n    tau_eff_j=Rpre_j Aamp_(j-1)/Bamp_(j-1)-C.                 (37)\n\nHere Rpre_j uses ONLY physical r_0,...,r_(j-1). If the data are legal,\nthe optimum normal requires tau_eff_j>=0; it is strictly positive for\nj<k, since Ecan_j>0. The frozen proximal coordinate l<k-1 is\n\n    z_ext_l=(r_(l+1)-tau_eff_(l+1))Bamp_l.                    (38)\n\nThe exact endpoint coordinates are now z_ext_l for l<j-1,\n-tau_eff_j Bamp_(j-1) at l=j-1, and -Lpre_j Aamp_l for l>=j.\nIts height is\n\n    delta_j=tau_eff_j Bamp_(j-1)^2-Lpre_j Ecan_j.\n\nFor future vertices s>j the normal inequalities are exactly\n\n    Lpre_j(Ecan_j-Kcan_s)<=tau_eff_j Bamp_(j-1)^2.            (39)\n\nFor every old vertex 0<=s<j they are exactly\n\n    -1_(s>=1) Bamp_(s-1)z_ext_(s-1)\n      +sum_(l=s)^(j-2)Aamp_l z_ext_l\n      <=tau_eff_j Bamp_(j-1)(Aamp_(j-1)+Bamp_(j-1)).         (40)\n\nThese formulas follow by multiplying the displayed endpoint coordinates\nby p_s-p_j. An empty sum is zero. They retain all old residuals, including\ntheir signs. Cancellation is exactly z_ext_l=0.\n\nIn particular the next-future inequality and the next-old inequality\ngive a necessary propagation condition. For 1<=s<k, they read\n\n    Lpre_s Aamp_s(Aamp_s+Bamp_s)\n                <=tau_eff_s Bamp_(s-1)^2,\n    (tau_eff_s-r_s)Bamp_(s-1)^2\n                <=tau_eff_(s+1)Bamp_s(Aamp_s+Bamp_s).\n\nSubstitute Bamp_s/Aamp_s=Rpre_(s+1)/(C+tau_eff_(s+1)) and\nRpre_(s+1)=Lpre_s+r_s. If tau_eff_s>r_s, cancel the positive\nBamp_(s-1)^2 and rearrange; if tau_eff_s<=r_s, use tau_eff_(s+1)>=0.\nThe resulting necessary condition in both cases is\n\n    tau_eff_(s+1)\n       >= C Lpre_s(tau_eff_s-r_s)_+\n                       /[r_s(Lpre_s+tau_eff_s)].               (41)\n\nThus a negative extinct residual can increase the future budget in (39),\nbut the same residual imposes a positive cost in the next old comparison.\nEquation (41) is only necessary; it does not replace the remote sums in\n(40) or establish a competitive construction.\n\n## 9. An actual-ruler countercheck against forward-only repair\n\nFor every actual H_m above with q>=2, the following exact relaxed data\npass ALL future endpoint normal comparisons and ALL optimum comparisons,\nwith strictly positive later endpoint heights, yet fail an old pair.\nThey are a counterexample to sufficiency of that one-sided test, NOT a\nlegal lower-bound witness.\n\nUse the same physical Rpre_j,Lpre_j, and choose\n\n    tau_eff_1=2r_1, tau_eff_2=r_2/100,\n    tau_eff_j=r_j for 3<=j<k, and tau_eff_k=C.                 (42)\n\nFor k=3 the last assignment is just tau_eff_3=C. Set Aamp_0=1,\nBamp_0=Rpre_1/(C+tau_eff_1), and, for 1<=j<k, set\n\n    Aamp_j^2=tau_eff_j Bamp_(j-1)^2 /\n                [Lpre_j(1+Rpre_(j+1)/(C+tau_eff_(j+1)))],\n    Bamp_j=Rpre_(j+1)Aamp_j/(C+tau_eff_(j+1)).                 (43)\n\nAll amplitudes are positive. The same algebra as in (16), now using\n(37),(43), shows that Kcan_(j+1)-Kcan_j is strictly positive and\ndelta_j=-Lpre_j Kcan_(j+1)>0 for j<k. Since Kcan_k<0 and\ndelta_k=tau_eff_k Bamp_(k-1)^2>0, every future normal in (39) and\nevery optimum comparison holds. Adding ordinary remaining mass preserves\nthese inequalities because <p_j,p_s-p_j><=0, as proved in Section 3.\n\nBut the endpoint-2 normal against vertex 1, equivalently the original\nresidual Q_((1,0),(2,0)), equals\n\n    (tau_eff_1-r_1)Bamp_0^2\n       -tau_eff_2 Bamp_1(Aamp_1+Bamp_1)\n      =r_1 Bamp_0^2\n         [1-2tau_eff_2 Rpre_2/((C+tau_eff_2)Lpre_1)].           (44)\n\nHere r_0=t_can C, r_1=c_rul t_can C, r_2=t_can C. Thus\n\n    Rpre_2/Lpre_1=1+c_rul t_can/(1+t_can)<11/6,\n    tau_eff_2/(C+tau_eff_2)<1/200.\n\nThe bracket in (44) is greater than 589/600. This is a strictly positive\nviolation on the actual schedule for every q>=2 and m>=128. One cannot\nrepair the cancellation rule by independently increasing an endpoint\nheight and clearing its propagated cost at the following checkpoint.\nAll the heights belong to the same proposed array, but that array is\nincompatible with the retained earlier-vertex inequality.\n\nThe remaining quantitative task is to choose nonzero residuals satisfying\nALL of (39)--(40), or change another stated generating-rule assumption,\nand obtain an actual payoff at the exact benchmark scale. The original\nall-schedule exact-profile target is unresolved. Current TASK and guidance\nallocate no routine verifier call, so this entire document remains a draft.\n\n## 10. Tiny exact sanity checks\n\nThe local script `check_positive_height_cancellation.py` checks only three\nfixed fixtures, with rational c_rul=5/2 and a_rul=2/5. These coefficients\ncheck the general algebra and are not asserted to be the m>=128 parameters.\nRational multiples of sqrt(Aamp_j^2) represent the coordinates, so every\ninner product and residual is an exact rational number.\n\nFor H_3 at ell=0 and ell=1, the constructor (10) with r_k=C passes all\n72 ordered original interpolation pairs, including the optimum, and has\nrespectively seven and three strictly positive later endpoint heights.\nThe full original step lists agree. The exact normalized payoffs are\n1562301543/5336489025232 and 828765625/160552296576. A third H_2 fixture\nchecks (42)--(44): all future and optimum comparisons hold, while the old\nresidual is exactly 6932/395325>0. All three checks together took 0.00521\nseconds. They are finite sanity checks; the symbolic proof above, not\nthese computed values, is the evidence for the universal statements.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "2b44a2d3c85cda91", "branch_id": "positive_height_signed_simplex", "attempt_type": "direct", "status": "complete_scoped_construction_and_obstruction_unverified", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "prior_findings": ["55622337e6b3b4fd", "e09fd8046ee1daf2"], "benchmark_derivation_lead": "70e9da73936cb7b3", "local_artifact": "local_memory/positive_height_cancellation_simplex.md", "subgoals": {"explicit_finite_law": "complete within joint draft", "all_pairs": "complete, including every optimum and remote pair", "true_norm": "complete, unique minimizer0", "quantitative_payoff": "uniformly below Huber and exact benchmark for this specified class", "nonzero_residual_repair": "exact necessary equations and propagation; future-only proposal refuted"}, "new_solver_calls": 0, "new_fact_submit_calls": 0, "scope": "Projection function with signed reflection/extinction coordinate history, Y0=0, no persistent extra coordinate, and zero extinct proximal coordinates.
