no raw numerical feasibility or membership premise is used in this analytic proof."}}, "glossary": {"V_can": "Actual normalized last objective2F_can(X_n)/(C^2 Ecan_0).", "P_can": "The single simplex conv(p_0,...,p_k) in R^k.", "F_can": "Projection-gradient function max over v in P_can of <x,v>-||v||^2/2.", "gamma_can": "Uniform score constant275/648 for the specified cancellation rule.", "sigma": "1+sqrt(2).", "c_rul": "sigma(1+1/m), fixed at the full horizon.", "a_rul": "(1-1/m)/c_rul, fixed at the full horizon.", "H_m": "Actual recursive ruler word defined in Section1; physical last step unchanged.", "M_j": "1+sum H_j with full-horizon parameters kept fixed.", "ell": "Ordinary-cell cutoff level0<=ell<m.", "q": "m-ell, the number of retained ruler levels.", "k": "2^q-1, the number of checkpoints.", "C": "M_ell, one plus each ordinary cell's mass.", "r_j": "Checkpoint excess a_rul c_rul^(ell+nu_2(j+1)) for j<k; r_k is a nonnegative terminal shape parameter, not a physical step.", "nu_2": "Largest exponent of2 dividing its positive integer argument.", "Aamp_j": "Positive initial amplitude of coordinate j.", "Bamp_j": "Positive magnitude of its reflected gradient amplitude.", "p_j": "Gradient in cell j specified in equation2.", "Y_j": "Cell-end proximal point Z_j-p_j.", "Z_j": "Position at the endpoint of cell j.", "X_(j,u)": "Original query in cell j with remaining ordinary mass u, equal toY_j+(1+u)p_j.", "wpre_j": "r0 at j0, C+r_j at j>=1.", "Rpre_j": "Sum of wpre_v over0<=v<j.", "Lpre_j": "Rpre_j+C.", "Ecan_j": "Sum of squared Aamp_l over j<=l<k.", "Ncan_j": "Squared norm of p_j.", "Kcan_j": "Common inner product of p_j with each earlier cell gradient.", "delta_can_j": "Actual endpoint height <Y_j,p_j>.", "lambda_can_j": "r_j Rpre_j/[(C+r_j)(C+Rpre_j)], the product contraction.", "d_can": "2C-1.", "A_mass": "a_rul/(c_rul-2), coefficient in the exact mass formula.", "t_can": "a_rul c_rul^ell/C.", "rho": "log_2(1+sqrt(2)), used in the complete exact-recursion bound.", "J": "Scalar function J(t)=(1+t)^rho-1 used only in the benchmark proof.", "G": "Dominant benchmark mass join2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.", "tau_eff_j": "Rpre_j Aamp_(j-1)/Bamp_(j-1)-C for a general uncancelled trajectory.", "z_ext_l": "Frozen proximal coordinate(r_(l+1)-tau_eff_(l+1))Bamp_l after coordinate l leaves the gradient."}}
{"id": "6e15eb1431be727d", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T22:28:10.206985+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Uniform all-horizon obstruction at the actual exact ZJ word: dilating its positive step gaps by a fixed factor off any o(n) arbitrary exceptional indices forces B_n W_n to diverge exponentially, despite unrestricted whole-array re-selection. An explicit one-exception family improves Huber and makes the unit quadratic zero, yet fails exponentially.", "evidence": "# Exact benchmark obstruction to dilation with arbitrary exceptional steps\n\nStatus: **self-contained, unverified proof candidate**, cumulative round 15.\nNo optimization or verifier call was made. This is a restriction on a specified\nvariation class, not the original unrestricted asymptotic theorem.\n\nCorrectness premises: accepted `b98bb807d514e80d` supplies the exact benchmark\nrecurrences and the bound C_j <= (j+1)^p, p=log_2(3). Accepted\n`1252b8823bb5a290` supplies the full-certificate convention. All other claims\nneeded below, including the polynomial estimate, are proved in this package.\nEarlier unverified findings are not premises.\n\n## 1. Actual source words and the new variation class\n\nLet C_n=1+S_circle(n) and B_n=1+2S_bullet(n), with C_0=B_0=1, and write\n\n    Omega(A,E)=(A+E+sqrt((A+E)^2+4AE))/2,\n    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.\n\nThus C_n=max_k Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)) and\nB_n=max_k G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)). At every node choose the SMALLEST maximizing\nindex, comparing these exact real numbers. Define the primitive and dominant\nwords recursively, starting from empty words:\n\n    p^(n)=(p^(k), phi(C_k-1,C_l-1), p^(l)),\n    d^(n)=(p^(k), psi(C_k-1,(B_l-1)/2), d^(l)),\n\nwhere l=n-k-1, and k is the relevant predetermined maximizing index. The\nfunctions phi and psi have exactly the definitions in the accepted fact.\nThese choices do not depend on the function queried. No eventual split\npattern is assumed.\n\nThe actual full multiplier recursion for this same word was reconstructed in\n`max_exact_zj_baseline_and_variations.md`, sections 2--4. That draft is not\nused as a truth premise here: the present lower bound needs only the explicit\nword just defined and the identities proved below. In particular it does not\nsubstitute Silver, a scalar limiting profile, or a different schedule for d^(n).\n\nPut h=d^(n), sigma_i=h_i-1, and rho=1+sqrt(2). Fix c>1. Choose ANY set\nE_sub of m indices among 0,...,n-1, and ANY nonnegative finite word h' such that\n\n    h'_i >= 1+c sigma_i       for i not in E_sub.             (V)\n\nThere is NO condition on h'_i for i in E_sub other than nonnegativity and\nfiniteness. The set and its entries may depend on n, c, the complete source\ntree, and one another. The exceptional entries can be zero, equal to one,\nor arbitrarily large. Their number need not be bounded. Condition (V) also\nincludes full-step dilation h'_i>=c h_i off E_sub.\n\nEvery interpolation multiplier, both optimum incidences, every value flow,\nthe terminal probabilities, and the entire PSD remainder may be reselected\narbitrarily at h'. The result below is a legal primal lower bound and thus\nconstrains every valid full upper certificate, regardless of its support or rank.\n\nThis class changes an unbounded collection of scales and permits an unbounded\nexceptional library. It does not retain the original ConPD rule or the primitive\nphysical sum caps. Hence the finite-terminal-library obstruction does not\nalready decide it. A unit exceptional step can make the unit-curvature\nquadratic vanish, so that single quadratic cannot decide it either.\n\nDefine the exact constants\n\n    eta_c=(c+1)/2,\n    tau_c=(eta_c+rho)/(c+rho),\n    r_c=(1+sqrt(tau_c))/(1-sqrt(tau_c)).\n\nThey satisfy eta_c>1 and 0<tau_c<1. With the conventions 0!=1 and a^0=1,\nthe claimed FINITE inequality is\n\n    B_n W_n(h') >= tau_c eta_c^(2(n-m)) r_c^(-2m)\n                         [m!/(2n)^m]^(2p).                  (F)\n\nThe same bound holds with W_n(h') replaced by any valid full certificate\nlevel q. Here W_n uses the original normalized error\n2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), not an intermediate reset distance.\n\nConsequently, for any fixed c>1 and any sequence m_n=o(n), the lower bound\nin (F) tends to infinity exponentially. In particular no fixed relative\nimprovement q_n B_n<=1-delta, delta>0, is possible in this variation class.\n\nOne explicit uniform subcase is\n\n    c=2,  m<=n/100   ==>   B_n W_n(h') > (3/4) exp(2n/5).   (F2)\n\nFor a concrete word at every n>=3, choose the earliest index i_min having\nthe smallest source gap and set\n\n    h'_i=1+2 sigma_i  (i!=i_min),       h'_(i_min)=1.          (Example)\n\nThe total step sum increases by sum sigma_i-2 sigma_(i_min), which is at\nleast (n-2)sigma_(i_min)>0. Its canonical Huber payoff is therefore smaller\nthan 1/B_n, and its unit-curvature quadratic payoff is ZERO. Nevertheless,\nthere is just one exception, so (F2) applies for every n>=100. This is a\nfully predetermined all-horizon family for which the interval of genuine\nquadratic curvatures detects failure missed by those two individual tests.\n\nNeither claim excludes perturbations with a sufficiently large proportion\nof undilated steps, or with c_n decreasing to one too quickly. The exact\nfinite bound (F), rather than an unproved derivative argument, governs those\nregimes as well.\n\n## 2. Source gap identities and a counting estimate independent of splits\n\nAt a primitive node with child masses A,E>=1, let C=Omega(A,E) and let d\nbe the bridge minus one. Direct substitution gives\n\n    C=A+E+d,       d C=AE,\n    d^2+(A+E)d-AE=0.                                 (P)\n\nThe last polynomial is increasing at d>=0. Its value at sqrt(2)-1 is at\nmost zero: as a function of A,E>=1 it is decreasing in either variable\nthere, and its value at A=E=1 is zero. Thus d>=sqrt(2)-1. Also\nd=AE/C<min(A,E).\n\nAt a dominant node, put\n\n    r=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D))/2,       d=A/r.\n\nThe defining formulas give\n\n    r^2-r=2A/D,       G(A,D)=D r^2,\n    2d^2+Dd=AD.                                      (D)\n\nThe left-minus-right polynomial in the last equation has value\n1/2+D/2-AD<=0 at d=1/2, for A,D>=1. Hence d>=1/2>\nsqrt(2)-1, and d<A since r>1.\n\nInduct on the construction tree, beginning with the empty product one.\nAt a primitive join the product of all positive gaps is\nd/(AE)=1/C. At a dominant join it is\nd/(A sqrt(D))=1/sqrt(G(A,D)). Therefore the actual benchmark word satisfies\n\n    sigma_i >= sqrt(2)-1=1/rho,\n    product_(i<n) sigma_i = 1/sqrt(B_n).              (Product)\n\nWe now prove, for every z>0,\n\n    #{i<n: sigma_i>z} <= 2n z^(-1/p).                (Count)\n\nEvery primitive component with k steps is a full binary tree with k+1\nempty leaves. A primitive bridge exceeding z has each child mass exceeding\nz, by (P). A child with a_steps steps has mass at most\n(a_steps+1)^p, by the accepted bound on C. Thus BOTH children have at\nleast K leaves, where K=floor(z^(1/p))+1.\n\nFor a full binary tree with N leaves, the number of nodes whose two\nchildren each have at least K leaves is at most\n\n    max(0,floor(N/K)-1).                             (Tree count)\n\nHere is a complete induction. If N<K, no node qualifies. Otherwise write\nN=N_L+N_R at the root. When both child leaf counts are at least K, induction\nbounds their qualified nodes by floor(N_L/K)-1 and floor(N_R/K)-1; adding\nthe qualifying root gives at most floor(N/K)-1. If only one child has at\nleast K leaves, the root does not qualify and the same bound for that\nchild is at most floor(N/K)-1. If neither child has K leaves, the count\nis zero, which is at most floor(N/K)-1 whenever N>=K. A one-leaf tree\nhas zero qualified nodes and satisfies the assertion directly.\n\nThe dominant construction has a single chain of right dominant children.\nIf their left primitive lengths are k_1,...,k_J, then\n\n    sum_(j=1)^J (k_j+1)=n,\n\nbecause each dominant node contributes its left steps and one bridge,\nand the final dominant child is empty. The primitive components thus\nhave a total of n empty leaves. The preceding tree estimate bounds the\nnumber of primitive bridges above z by n/K.\n\nEach dominant bridge above z requires its left primitive mass above z,\nby d<A in (D); hence that left component has at least K leaves. The\ncomponents are disjoint and their leaf counts sum to n. At most n/K\ndominant bridges qualify. The combined count is at most 2n/K,\nwhich is at most 2n z^(-1/p). This proves (Count), including z<1 and\nempty primitive components.\n\nIf sigma_(1)>=...>=sigma_(n) are the sorted source gaps, (Count) implies\n\n    sigma_(j) <= (2n/j)^p,\n    product_(i in E_sub) sigma_i <= [(2n)^m/m!]^p.    (Exceptional product)\n\nIndeed, if the first inequality failed, choose z strictly between its\nright side and sigma_(j). Then at least j gaps exceed z, contradicting\n(Count). Multiplying the first m sorted bounds bounds the product on\nany m-element subset. No distribution or balancing hypothesis on the\nmaximizing splits entered this argument.\n\n## 3. The polynomial estimate, with an explicit finite curvature selection\n\nLet 0<tau<1 and let R be a real polynomial of degree at most m with R(0)=1.\nPut r=(1+sqrt(tau))/(1-sqrt(tau)). There is a theta in [tau,1] such that\n\n    |R(theta)| >= 1/T_m((1+tau)/(1-tau)) >= r^(-m).  (Cheb)\n\nFor m=0 this holds at every point. For m>=1 define T_0(x)=1,T_1(x)=x,\nT_(j+1)(x)=2xT_j(x)-T_(j-1)(x). The cosine addition formula proves\nT_m(cos t)=cos(mt). Define\n\n    x_j=cos(j*pi/m),\n    theta_j=(1+tau)/2+(1-tau)x_j/2,  0<=j<=m,\n    x_ext=-(1+tau)/(1-tau).\n\nLet L_j be the Lagrange cardinal polynomial for these distinct descending\nnodes x_j. The numerator of L_j(x_ext) has sign (-1)^m and its denominator\nhas sign (-1)^j. Thus L_j(x_ext) has sign (-1)^(m-j).\nInterpolation of T_m, whose value at x_j is (-1)^j, gives\n\n    sum_j |L_j(x_ext)|=|T_m(x_ext)|.\n\nApply interpolation to P(x)=R((1+tau)/2+(1-tau)x/2), noting P(x_ext)=1:\n\n    1 <= max_j |R(theta_j)| sum_j |L_j(x_ext)|.\n\nThis proves the first inequality in (Cheb), and shows that a suitable\ntheta can be selected from the EXPLICIT m+1 points theta_j. The first\nmaximizer of |R(theta_j)| is a deterministic selection after h' is fixed.\n\nFor X=(1+tau)/(1-tau)>1, the recurrence for T_m gives\n\n    T_m(X)=(r^m+r^(-m))/2,\n    r=X+sqrt(X^2-1)=(1+sqrt(tau))/(1-sqrt(tau)).\n\nThe displayed expression follows by checking m=0,1 and the recurrence.\nIt is at most r^m, proving the second inequality. No external extremal\npolynomial theorem is a premise of this proof.\n\n## 4. Actual quadratic adversary and the full-array conclusion\n\nFix h' and its exceptional set. Apply (Cheb) on [tau_c,1] to\n\n    R(theta)=product_(i in E_sub)(1-theta h'_i),\n\nwhich has degree at most m and R(0)=1, including exceptional zero steps.\nFor i outside E_sub and theta>=tau_c, condition (V) and (Product) give\n\n    theta h'_i-1 >= c theta sigma_i-(1-theta)\n      >= [c theta-rho(1-theta)] sigma_i\n      >= eta_c sigma_i >0.                          (Amplification)\n\nThe last equality at theta=tau_c is the definition of tau_c. Thus, at the\nselected theta_j in section 3,\n\n    |product_(i<n)(1-theta_j h'_i)|\n      >= eta_c^(n-m) r_c^(-m)\n                        /[sqrt(B_n) product_(i in E_sub) sigma_i].\n\nUse the one-dimensional function f(x)=L theta_j x^2/2, with x_0=1 and\nx_star=0. It is convex, has gradient Lipschitz constant at most L, and\nhas UNIQUE minimizer zero since theta_j>=tau_c>0. Its actual GD trajectory\nfor h' has x_n=product_(i<n)(1-theta_j h'_i), and its normalized error is\ntheta_j x_n^2. The true initial distance is exactly one. The last displayed\nbound and (Exceptional product) prove (F).\n\nIf a full certificate has level q, its nonnegative interpolation weights\nand full PSD remainder imply q>=W_n(h'), as in accepted1252b8823bb5a290.\nIn particular no moving coefficient, new edge, optimum-source incidence,\nkernel change, or higher-rank remainder can evade this legal instance.\nThis is why no unproved continuation radius or sign repair is needed\nto obtain the stated negative conclusion for class (V).\n\n## 5. Uniformity, finite constants, and the boundary of the obstruction\n\nFor m>=1, integrating the increasing function log(x) gives\nlog(m!)>=m log(m)-m; equivalently m!>=(m/e)^m. Set s=m/n and define\n\n    F_c(s)=(1-s)log(eta_c)-s log(r_c)-p s log(2e/s),  0<s<=1,\n    F_c(0)=log(eta_c).\n\nThen (F) implies the exact, slightly weaker convenient bound\n\n    B_n W_n(h') >= tau_c exp(2n F_c(m/n)).          (Rate)\n\nSince s log(1/s) tends to zero as s decreases to zero,\nF_c(m_n/n) tends to log(eta_c)>0 when m_n=o(n). This proves exponential\ndivergence with constants depending on c alone; exact B_n has already\ncancelled by (Product). No decimal approximation or profile limit for B_n\nwas used.\n\nThere is a unique s_c in (0,1) with F_c(s_c)=0. Indeed F_c(0)>0,\nF_c(1)<0, and for 0<s<=1 its derivative is\n\n    -log(eta_c)-log(r_c)-p[log(2e/s)-1]<0.\n\nThus any sequence in (V) whose ratios B_n W_n(h') stay bounded must obey\nliminf m_n/n>=s_c. This is a positive-density cost of the exceptional\nrepair, not merely an exclusion of a fixed finite terminal library.\n\nFor (F2), c=2 gives eta_c=3/2 and\ntau_c=1-1/(6+2sqrt(2)), so 3/4<tau_c<9/10. Consequently\nsqrt(tau_c)<19/20 and r_c<39. The elementary estimates\nlog(3/2)>2/5, log(39)<4, p<2, and log(200e)<7 show for 0<=s<=1/100 that\n\n    F_2(s) > (99/100)(2/5)-(1/100)4-2(1/100)7\n             =27/125 >1/5.\n\nHere s log(2e/s) is increasing on [0,1/100] and is interpreted as zero\nat s=0. For completeness, log(3/2)>2/5 follows by integrating 1/x and\nthe strict convexity of 1/x on [1,3/2]; e^4>39 follows from the terms\nthrough degree five of its positive power series; and e^6>200 follows\nfrom the terms through degree six. These imply the two remaining log\nestimates. Substitution in (Rate) proves (F2).\n\nAn actual finite path may set c=1+t for t in a fixed positive interval\nand choose arbitrary nonnegative exceptional entries at every t. For\nevery fixed t>0, the obstruction is uniform in horizon if m_n=o(n).\nIt also holds uniformly over t>=t_min>0, by using c=1+t_min in (V).\nIf t=t_n tends to zero, (F) still holds but need not diverge. Such a\nvanishing-radius path, or a change to a positive fraction of the steps\noutside (V), is explicitly outside the claimed no-go.\n\n## 6. Literature check and scope\n\nThe source word is the actual Zhang--Jiang primitive/dominant construction:\nZehao Zhang and Rujun Jiang, *Accelerated Gradient Descent by Concatenation\nof Stepsize Schedules*, arXiv:2410.12395v2, Theorems 3.1--3.2 and\nDefinitions 4.1/4.3. Its local PDF and both concatenation proofs were read\nin the previous round; the present proof uses the accepted exact recurrences.\n\nMatlas returned Rachid Ait-Haddou, *New Stability Results for Explicit\nRunge--Kutta Methods*, arXiv:1804.09896, Theorem 4.1, concerning polar-form\ncoefficients of bounded polynomials. It prompted the interval-polynomial\nscreen, but that polar-form theorem is NOT used here. Section 3 proves\nthe exact needed Lagrange/Chebyshev estimate directly.\nThe exact paper was downloaded and extracted with the existing local\npypdf reader; Definition 2.1, Theorem 4.1, and its application in the proof\nof Theorem 4.2 were read. The paper quotes Theorem 4.1 from Lubinsky--Ziegler\nrather than reproving it. No imported assertion from that theorem is needed\nin this package.\n\nThe full original target remains OPEN. The theorem-shaped assertion above\nis an unverified candidate about (V). It does not infer global minimax\noptimality, Huber tightness of an altered word, or a universal first-order\nseparator from failure of this specified dilation class.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max15_sparse_exception_dilation", "status": "Complete self-contained UNVERIFIED class obstruction; no verifier allocation", "research_path": "research/max_sparse_exception_dilation.md", "fact_ids": ["b98bb807d514e80d", "1252b8823bb5a290"], "related_findings": ["f0164c899fadfdea", "a6a07e7cfa5a843c"], "original_target": "OPEN", "new_fact_submit_calls": 0, "new_optimizer_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"sigma_i": "Positive source gap h_i-1 for the actual deterministically selected exact ZJ word.", "E_sub": "Arbitrary exceptional subset of step indices; its h'_i entries are unrestricted nonnegative finite numbers.", "m": "Cardinality of E_sub.", "c": "Fixed multiplicative lower dilation factor on the positive source gaps outside E_sub; c>1.", "eta_c": "(c+1)/2.", "tau_c": "(eta_c+rho)/(c+rho), the lower end of the quadratic-curvature interval.", "r_c": "(1+sqrt(tau_c))/(1-sqrt(tau_c)).", "rho": "1+sqrt(2), with all source gaps at least 1/rho.", "p": "The accepted exponent log_2(3) in C_j<=(j+1)^p.", "F_c": "Explicit density-rate function (1-s)log eta_c-s log r_c-p s log(2e/s).", "s_c": "Unique zero of F_c in (0,1), a necessary lower bound on exceptional density for bounded benchmark ratios.", "T_m": "Chebyshev polynomial defined by T_0=1,T_1=x,T_(j+1)=2xT_j-T_(j-1).", "i_min": "Earliest source index having the smallest positive gap."}}
{"id": "7bc886cf037bdcd4", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T22:29:18.916240+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max2 complete identity and partial joint-storage proof: the all-query weighted budget has explicit nonnegative original-Q multipliers, exact recursive K/T composition, a positive two-mode Schur base, and a conditional exact-B gap lambda/e. The whole growing T PSD and constant-column charge remain OPEN; no whole-class bound or new fact is claimed.", "evidence": "# Joint chronological storage for the full double-zero interface\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED research package. The certificate-family obstruction in\nSection 3 and the identities in Sections 2 and 4 are complete derivations.\nThe PSD assertion in Section 5 is OPEN; consequently this file does not\nexclude the full quotient class and does not establish the original target.\nNo optimization, installation, or fact submission was used. Accepted inputs:\n1252b8823bb5a290 (the ordinary interpolation inequalities) and\nb98bb807d514e80d (the exact benchmark recursions). Every other assertion\nneeded below is derived here; earlier unverified findings are not premises.\n\n## 1. Actual chronology and one common set of data\n\nLet m>=128, lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c, and\n\n d_j=1+a c^j,  H_0=(),  H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j).\n\nLet n=2^m-1, q=n-1, and h_0,...,h_(q-1) be H_m with its last d_0 removed.\nThe last, unqueried step is b in [0,2]. Write\n\n s_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S=s_q,\n p_ij=product_(t<i)(1-h_t/d_j), 0<=j<m,\n R_i=sum_j p_ij w_j, Z_i=sum_j d_j p_ij w_j,\n x_i=u-s_i v+Z_i, g_i=v+R_i, ||x_0||=1.\n\nAll vectors belong to ONE real Euclidean space with an arbitrary common PSD\nGram matrix. All values f_i belong to ONE array, with the optimum sample\nx_star=g_star=f_star=0. Assume EVERY original ordered queried interpolation\ninequality\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.\n\nFor an actual function these follow from accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290.\nConversely that fact gives a common interpolant when the entire array is\nlegal. No subset of the inequalities below is asserted sufficient for\nlegality. The normalization is with respect to an actual minimizer; one\nmay choose the closest minimizer and then normalize its distance to one.\n\nThe first d_j step has index 2^j-1. Every step before it is smaller than d_j.\nThus 0<p_ij<=1 before its annihilation and p_ij=0 for i>=2^j. In particular\nR_q=Z_q=0 and g_q=v. Also\n\n Z_i-Z_(i+1)=h_i R_i,  sum_i h_i p_ij=d_j.                 (1)\n\nThe sums run over 0<=i<q. These identities hold in the original coordinates;\nthere is no orthogonalization or change of values.\n\nFor comparison with the previous height interface, set\nC_min=f_q-<v,x_q>, eta=-C_min-||v||^2/2, and\ndelta_i=f_i-<v,x_i>-C_min-||R_i||^2/2.\nTilting the actual function by -<v,.> gives a convex 1-smooth function with\ngradient zero at x_q. Its optimum inequalities give delta_i>=0 and eta>=0.\nExpanding Q_(0,star) gives exactly\n\n eta>=delta_0+||R_0||^2+<v,R_0>-<R_0,x_0>.              (2)\n\nNeither eta nor any of the shared heights is set to zero below.\n\n## 2. What the unweighted chronological chain actually charges\n\nDefine\n\n E_i=h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>+||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2/2,\n A_j=sum_i (h_i^2/d_j)p_ij,\n K_jk=sum_i p_ij p_ik\n       [h_i-h_i^2(d_j+d_k-1)/(2d_j d_k)],\n C_jk=1+2K_jk.\n\nThe forward inequality is Q_(i+1,i)=f_(i+1)-f_i+E_i<=0.\nConsequently f_q<=f_0-sum_i E_i. Direct expansion using (1) gives\n\n sum_i E_i=S||v||^2+<v,sum_j(2d_j-A_j)w_j>\n                             +sum_jk K_jk<w_j,w_k>.     (3)\n\nThe coefficient K_jk contains every modal cross term. It is not obtained\nby summing independently interpolable one-mode instances.\n\nBefore a mode dies, h_i<=d_j and 0<=p_ij<=1. Hence\n\n 0<=A_j<=sum_i h_i p_ij=d_j.                            (4)\n\nTelescoping p_ij^2-p_(i+1,j)^2 gives\n\n K_jj=d_j/2-(d_j-1)/(2d_j^2) sum_i h_i^2 p_ij^2\n       <=d_j/2,\n C_jj<=1+d_j.                                         (5)\n\nFor the first mode only i=0 contributes: K_00=1/2 and C_00=2.\nNo assertion that the entire K matrix is PSD is made here.\n\n## 3. A complete obstruction to ONE scalar initial-budget mixture\n\nThis section concerns a precisely specified certificate family, not the\nfull legal quotient class. Mix, with a scalar theta in [0,1], the two valid\nupper estimates\n\n f_q<=<v,x_q>-||v||^2/2,\n f_q<=<g_0,x_0>-||g_0||^2/2-sum_i E_i.\n\nUse the actual final-step descent\n2F(x_q-bv)<=2f_q-b(2-b)||v||^2. Put\n\n D=2S+1+b(2-b), c_j(theta)=(1+theta)d_j-theta A_j+theta.\n\nThe resulting necessary upper estimate is exactly\n\n 2F(x_q-bv)<=2<v+theta R_0,x_0>-D||v||^2\n       -2<v,sum_j c_j(theta)w_j>\n       -theta sum_jk C_jk<w_j,w_k>.                    (6)\n\nTo certify an upper level gamma from (6) by a common PSD quadratic form\nrequires the following form to be nonnegative on the formal vector space:\n\n M_theta=gamma||x_0||^2-2<v+theta R_0,x_0>+D||v||^2\n       +2<v,sum_j c_j(theta)w_j>\n       +theta sum_jk C_jk<w_j,w_k>.                    (7)\n\nFor EVERY theta and b for which (7) is PSD, necessarily\n\n gamma >= d_(m-1)^2/[2D(1+d_(m-1))].                  (8)\n\nProof. If theta=0, the w_(m-1) diagonal in (7) is zero but its coupling to\nv equals d_(m-1)>0. The corresponding 2x2 principal matrix is indefinite.\nFor theta>0, the principal matrix in (x_0,w_0) is\n\n [ gamma, -theta; -theta, 2theta ],\n\nso gamma>=theta/2. The principal matrix in (v,w_(m-1)) gives\n\n D theta C_(m-1,m-1)>=c_(m-1)(theta)^2.\n\nBy (4), c_(m-1)(theta)>=d_(m-1), and by (5), C_(m-1,m-1)<=1+d_(m-1).\nThus theta>=d_(m-1)^2/[D(1+d_(m-1))]. Combining proves (8).\nThese are genuine necessary PSD conditions, with no mode independence\nassumption. If a condition fails, its 2x2 negative direction supplies a\nreal rank-one Gram counterexample to the proposed quadratic certificate.\nIt is NOT claimed to be an interpolable adversarial trajectory.\n\nTo see the quantitative failure, the actual mass satisfies\n\n 1+sum H_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j,\n A_mass=a/(c-2).\n\nThis solves the defining recurrence 1+sum H_(j+1)=2(1+sum H_j)+a c^j.\nSince A_mass->1 and c^m/lambda^m->exp(1),\n\n S/lambda^m->exp(1),  d_(m-1)/S->1/lambda^2.\n\nThe limit is uniform in b in [0,2], since 0<=b(2-b)<=1. Therefore every\nsequence of PSD certificates (7) has\n\n liminf gamma >=1/(4lambda^2)>0.                       (9)\n\nIn particular this specific use of the common initial budget cannot even\ngive a vanishing error bound. The issue is not a small loss in the highest\nmode charge: suppressing its v-coupling forces theta to remain of order one,\nand that same theta attaches order-one weight to the first mode. Additional\nearlier/earlier or optimum inequalities remain available and can change\nthis conclusion. Optimizing theta separately on already legal data is also\noutside the unconditional PSD family (7).\n\n## 4. A genuinely joint replacement using EVERY optimum budget\n\nThe exact positive quadrature in (1) suggests weights h_i/S rather than one\ncommon theta at the initial query. There is a direct identity which avoids\nintroducing any new unknown heights. Define the known quadratic form\n\n J_h(g)=sum_(i<q){\n       2s_(i+1) h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>\n       +s_(i+1)||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2\n       +h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2 }.\n\nThen, for arbitrary formal data satisfying the GD equations, without any\nlegality assumption, one has\n\n ||x_0||^2-2S f_q\n    +2sum_i h_i Q_(i,star)\n    +2sum_i s_(i+1)Q_(i+1,i)\n              =||x_q||^2+J_h(g).                     (10)\n\nProof. The GD norm identity gives\n\n 2sum_i h_i Q_(i,star)\n =2sum_i h_i f_i-||x_0||^2+||x_q||^2\n                         +sum_i h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2.\n\nDiscrete summation by parts, using s_0=0 and s_q=S, gives\n\n 2sum_i s_(i+1)Q_(i+1,i)\n =2S f_q-2sum_i h_i f_i+2sum_i s_(i+1)E_i.\n\nAdding proves (10). All multiplier signs are nonnegative. In particular\nthe common values cancel exactly, rather than being chosen mode by mode.\nThe true initial distance remains the coefficient ||x_0||^2. The optimum\nconstraints include every queried index, and no projection is performed.\n\nIf J_h(g)>=0 holds for all vector outputs in the prescribed quotient, (10)\nwould give 2f_q<=1/S and, for every b in [0,2],\n\n                   2F(x_q-bv)<=1/S.                 (11)\n\nAll of the original interpolation inequalities are still required of any\nlegal transcript. Equation (10) merely chooses a sufficient nonnegative\ncombination for an upper certificate; it does not delete unused pairs from\nthe definition of the class.\n\n## 5. The exact remaining deterministic kernel question\n\nIntroduce a constant temporal column r_i,inf=1 and r_i,j=p_ij for 0<=j<m.\nFor indices a,b in {inf,0,...,m-1}, define the explicit symmetric matrix\n\n T_ab=sum_(i<q){\n   s_(i+1)h_i(r_i,a r_(i+1),b+r_(i+1),a r_i,b)\n   +s_(i+1)(r_i,a-r_(i+1),a)(r_i,b-r_(i+1),b)\n   +h_i(1-h_i)r_i,a r_i,b }.\n\nThus J_h(g)=sum_ab T_ab<z_a,z_b>, with z_inf=v and z_j=w_j. This is ONE\nknown matrix depending only on the physical word and its Newton factors;\nthere are no unknown interpolation multipliers or values in its definition.\n\nThe open sufficient claim is\n\n             T is PSD for every actual m>=128.        (12)\n\nIt is not the original all-schedule minimax question in disguise. It is a\nspecific sufficient certificate on a proper (m+2)-dimensional temporal\nquotient for the specified words. Failure of (12) would reject this chosen\ncertificate, not construct a legal trajectory or decide the quotient class.\nConversely, (12) has NOT been established by this package. This is BLOCKED\nresearch, not a routine remaining check.\n\nHere is why the elementary scalar-storage proof does not finish it. For a\nstep h beginning at time t, add a carried term alpha||g_i||^2. Its weighted\none-step form is\n\n A||g_i||^2+2B<g_i,g_(i+1)>+C||g_(i+1)||^2,\n A=alpha+t+h(2-h), B=(t+h)(h-1), C=t+h.\n\nWhen A>0 its exact square completion carries\n\n alpha'=t+h-(t+h)^2(h-1)^2/[alpha+t+h(2-h)].          (13)\n\nThis recurrence is legitimate algebra, but it discards positive squares\ncontaining dependent future mode values. It can encounter a negative next\npivot even when the restriction to the Newton columns is positive. Keeping\nthe discarded squares as a non-scalar conditional covariance is therefore\nessential if this route works. No positivity of the actual matrix follows\nfrom entrywise positive Newton factors or from a change of modal basis.\n\nFor the analogous unweighted chain, the scalar recurrence is especially\ntransparent. Starting alpha=0 and completing the one-step chain square gives\n\n alpha' = 1/2-(h-1)^2/(4alpha+2).                      (14)\n\nAt the actual first steps d_0,d_1,d_0,d_2, the first three updates are valid\nand at most 1/2. Indeed 11/8<d_0<3/2 and 1<d_1<2 give alpha_1>3/8,\nalpha_2>3/14, and alpha_3>0. Since lambda>241/100 and\n(241/100)(1-1/16384)>12/5, one has d_2-1=lambda(1-1/m^2)>12/5 for m>=128,\nso the fourth update satisfies alpha_4<1/2-(12/5)^2/4<-1/2. The next scalar\npivot fails. This rules out that particular discarded-square argument,\nnot PSD of the full restricted K or T kernels.\n\n### 5.1 Exact composition without expanding the exponentially long word\n\nFor a finite word W, let P_W(z)=product_(h in W)(1-hz), S_W=sum W, and\nlet K_W(z,w), T_W(z,w) be the unweighted and weighted kernels defined\nabove, evaluated at arbitrary real rate variables z,w. Their time starts\nat zero. For concatenation U,V, direct separation of the sums gives\n\n K_UV=K_U+P_U(z)P_U(w)K_V,\n T_UV=T_U+P_U(z)P_U(w)[T_V+2S_U K_V].                 (15)\n\nThe second identity retains the elapsed-time offset; omitting it gives\nthe wrong storage matrix. For one step h,\n\n K_[h](z,w)=h-h^2(z+w-zw)/2,\n T_[h](z,w)=h(h+1)-h^3(z+w-zw).                      (16)\n\nThus for the actual recursive full word, write L=P_Hj(z)P_Hj(w),\nr=(1-d_j z)(1-d_j w), s=S_Hj, k=K_[d_j], t=T_[d_j]. Then\n\n K_H(j+1)=(1+Lr)K_Hj+Lk,\n T_H(j+1)=(1+Lr)T_Hj+L[t+2s k+2(s+d_j)r K_Hj].       (17)\n\nThese are exact algebraic recursions, not numerical optimization or an\ninvocation of an unproved kernel property.\n\nThere is a useful terminal simplification. At every residual rate 1/d_j,\nthe queried-prefix polynomial is zero. Removing the last d_0 from H_m\ntherefore changes none of the modal/modal or constant/modal entries of T.\nOnly the constant/constant entry changes, from\n(S+d_0)(S+d_0+1) to S(S+1). This follows from (15) with the last one-step\nword; its correction is multiplied by P_pre(z)P_pre(w), which vanishes\nwhenever at least one argument is a residual rate. In particular the\nconstant-gradient suffix still contributes to the decisive constant\ndiagonal, and cannot be discarded from the budget.\n\nEach modal/modal entry and each constant/modal entry stabilizes once the\nfirst of its residual modes dies: subsequent products and differences for\nthat mode vanish identically. This supplies a nested sequence of KNOWN\nmatrices. If A_m denotes its residual block and t_m its constant/modal\ncolumn, the exact remaining PSD test, when A_m is positive definite, is\n\n                 t_m^T A_m^(-1)t_m <=S(S+1).         (18)\n\nNo positivity or bound in (18) is assumed. The covariance at every level\nhas to include the contributions of the repeated lower-level steps in\n(17); scalar carry (13) loses them.\n\nFor a fully analytic base check, let t=d_0 and u=d_1, both in (1,2), and\nr=1-t/u>0. The first two residual columns give\n\n T_00=A=t(2-t),\n T_01=A+B r,  B=t(t-1),\n T_11=A+2B r+[u(2-u)+2t]r^2.\n\nThe Schur complement of T_00 is exactly\n\n r^2[u(2-u)+2t-t(t-1)^2/(2-t)].                      (19)\n\nFor the actual t<3/2, (t-1)^2/(2-t)<1/2, so (19) is greater than\n(3/2)t r^2>0. This proves the first two residual columns are positive\ndefinite, while keeping their cross term. It supplies an induction base,\nnot the missing uniform claim for growing m or the constant column.\n\n## 6. Exact benchmark consequence IF the open kernel claim is proved\n\nThis section is conditional on (12); it must not be quoted as a completed\nupper bound. It records the actual target constant rather than silently\nreplacing B_n by a sum-based benchmark.\n\nFrom accepted b98bb807d514e80d, put P_n=S_circle(n)+1. Its recursions are\n\n P_0=B_0=1,\n P_n=max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(P_k,P_l),\n B_n=max_(k+l=n-1) G(P_k,B_l),\n Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.\n\nWe now prove B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1). Write alpha=2lambda-1 and\nLlin=(4x+lambda^2 y)/alpha. Its two coefficients exceed one and\n\n alpha^2[Llin^2-(x+y)Llin-xy]\n                     =(2x/lambda-sqrt(2)lambda y)^2>=0.\n\nIt follows that Llin>=Omega(x,y). At z=lambda Omega(x,y),\n\n z^2-(4x+lambda y)z+4x^2\n        =x[4x+lambda^2 y-alpha Omega(x,y)]>=0.\n\nAlso z>=lambda(x+y)>=(4x+lambda y)/2. Thus z lies to the right of the\nlarger root G(x,lambda y), proving G(x,lambda y)<=lambda Omega(x,y).\nMonotonicity of G in its second argument and induction give B_n<=lambda P_n.\n\nSet rho=log_2(lambda)>1 and Dpow(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. For\nell(z)=log Dpow(exp z), differentiation with t=exp z gives\n\n ell''(z)=rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)\n       [(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/Dpow(t)^2>0.\n\nThe bracket is positive because it starts at zero and has positive\nderivative for t>0. Convexity gives Dpow(t)Dpow(1/t)>=Dpow(1)^2=2.\nFor x,y>0, Z=(x^(1/rho)+y^(1/rho))^rho then satisfies\n(Z-x)(Z-y)>=2xy and Z>=x+y, so Omega(x,y)<=Z. Zero arguments follow by\nsubstitution. Induction yields P_n<=(n+1)^rho; balanced joins give equality\nat n=2^m-1. This proves the claimed exact-B upper comparator.\n\nCombining this comparator, S/lambda^m->exp(1), and the CONDITIONAL bound\n(11) would yield\n\n limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) sup_(full quotient,completions,b)\n                         2F(x_q-bv) <=lambda/exp(1)<15/16<1.\n\nThe rational strict comparison follows from lambda<5/2 and exp(1)>8/3.\nThis would exclude the ENTIRE stated double-zero quotient with arbitrary\njoint heights and arbitrary common PSD Gram, with no conditioning policy.\nIt would still say nothing decisive about the original all-h target.\n\n## 7. Literature audit and current stopping point of the argument\n\nMatlas returned Liao--Liu--Zhao, arXiv:2210.12514, Lemma 2.3, as a possible\ndiscrete-energy analogy. Its proof was read from the exact PDF. It uses\nsummation by parts and nonnegative kernel differences; its row/column\nmonotonicity and algebraic-convexity hypotheses have NOT been established\nfor T. It is not an external premise of any proof here. The PDF is retained\nat downloads/2210.12514.pdf. Local pdftotext and Python PDF readers were\nabsent; the arXiv HTML endpoint returned a cache miss; browser PDF extraction\nsucceeded. No installation was made. Further Newton and silver-energy\nMatlas searches supplied no applicable theorem.\n\nTwo fixed, tiny four-mode, eight-step Schur-pivot checks on the first four\nACTUAL m=128 modes found positive residual K and T pivots. A final fixed\neight-mode, 128-step check of T also had positive scaled pivots, ending near\n0.0928. These are numerical intuition only, not a proof of (12), and do not\ntest the constant column or unbounded horizons. There was no optimization\nor sweep; no further numerical evaluation is planned in this round.\n\nComplete new result: the single-initial scalar-mixture family (7) cannot\nproduce any vanishing bound, with the quantitative obstruction (8)--(9).\nOpen new mechanism: prove or refute the fixed known T kernel in (12), keeping\nthe conditional covariance from repeated lower-level steps. Neither an\nisolated highest-mode cost nor an unproved total-positivity assertion fills\nthis gap. The full project target and the requested whole-interface charge\nremain unresolved.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max2_elapsed_time_joint_storage", "attempt_type": "direct", "status": "partial_blocked", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["4cd717742b582ea8"], "artifact": "research/joint_height_chronological_storage.md", "counterexample_search_for_stuck_subgoal": {"performed": true, "summary": "Scalar discarded-square recurrences fail at an ACTUAL large ruler step. Three tiny fixed low-dimensional checks did not refute the actual modal kernel, but are explicitly not proofs and did not test its constant column.", "result": "not_refuted"}}, "glossary": {"J_h": "Elapsed-time weighted chain form defined in Section4.", "T": "Known coefficient matrix defined in Section5, with one constant and all m actual residual temporal columns.", "A_mass": "a/(c-2), the explicit coefficient of the actual mass recurrence.", "K_W,T_W": "Unweighted and elapsed-time weighted bivariate kernels of finite word W, Sections2 and5.1.", "P_W": "Ordered product product_(h in W)(1-hz).", "gamma": "Attempted upper-certificate objective level of Section3.", "eta": "The original common optimum height -C_min-||v||²/2.", "delta_i": "One common tilted height f_i-<v,x_i>-C_min-||R_i||²/2.", "A_j": "The scalar chronological coefficient sum_i h_i²p_ij/d_j.", "c_j(theta)": "The scalar modal coupling (1+theta)d_j-theta A_j+theta.", "C_jk": "1+2K_jk in the first attempted certificate.", "A_m,t_m": "Residual block and constant/modal column of the fixed known kernel T."}}
{"id": "436df4c878e9ba06", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T22:33:04.589108+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "Actual ZJ tight-face package: full complementarity yields legal projection-on-a-convex-set realizations; an exact n=3 finite path lowers Huber, unit quadratic and active root cut but a fourth legal function raises payoff; maximizing roots satisfy bridge-gap>=sqrt(B_n)/4, and an unchanged scalar plateau-to-quadratic lift fails on an explicit unbounded set of horizons.", "evidence": "# Tight faces, a three-test counterexample, and exact root geometry\n\nStatus: **joint unverified candidate**, cumulative round 15. No solver or\nverifier call. These are scoped interfaces and counterexamples, not a full\nanswer to the original asymptotic problem.\n\nAccepted premises: `1252b8823bb5a290` for the complete interpolation and\ncertificate convention, and `b98bb807d514e80d` for the exact recurrences,\nthe Huber/single-cut bounds, and C_n<=(n+1)^p with p=log_2(3)<2.\nAll additional identities used here are proved in this package. No earlier\nunverified finding is treated as a fact.\n\n## 1. A precise consequence of positive optimum-directed weights\n\nNormalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0, and ||x_0||=1. Suppose an actual\ntrajectory attains a full certificate level q and that this particular\ncertificate has lambda_(i,star)>0 at every finite sample i. Write\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    y_i=x_i-g_i.\n\nThe certificate equality at an attaining trajectory reads\nsum lambda_ij Q_ij=M_cert>=0, while every summand is nonpositive.\nConsequently M_cert=0 and each positively weighted Q is zero. In particular\n\n    Q_(i,star)=0,      f_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2.\n\nSubstitution, including the zero optimum jet, gives the complete identity\n\n    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>.                              (N)\n\nLet K=conv{0,g_0,...,g_n}, a compact convex subset of the same Euclidean\nspace. For every i the full interpolation inequalities and (N) imply\n\n    <v-g_i,y_i><=0   for every v in K.                (Normal)\n\nConversely these inequalities characterize the fact that g_i is the\nEuclidean projection of x_i onto K. Indeed, for v in K,\n\n    ||x_i-v||^2-||x_i-g_i||^2\n      =||v-g_i||^2-2<y_i,v-g_i>>=0,\n\nwith strict inequality unless v=g_i. Define the global function\n\n    F_K(x)=max_(v in K) {<x,v>-||v||^2/2}.\n\nIts unique maximizing vector is Proj_K(x). The projection variational\ninequalities at x and x', added, imply\n||Proj_K(x)-Proj_K(x')||<=||x-x'||. Evaluating the two maxima at each\nother's maximizing vectors then proves differentiability, with this\nprojection as the gradient. Hence F_K is convex and 1-smooth. Since\n0 belongs to K, F_K(0)=0=min F_K. At every supplied x_i it has gradient\ng_i and value <g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2=f_i. Thus every attaining trajectory\nunder the stated positive-weight hypothesis has a legal realization by\nthis particular projection function, with the SAME initial point and\noptimum zero.\n\nThis does not assert that K is an interval, a box, or a product of\none-dimensional components. It need not give a unique minimizer. More\nimportantly, an arbitrary trajectory of F_K need not attain the certificate:\nthe positively weighted finite-edge equalities and M_cert=0 still have\nto hold. Merely replacing the unknown tight primal by an arbitrary\nprojection trajectory supplies no stationary separator.\n\n## 2. The actual three-step exact benchmark\n\nPut s=sqrt(2), rho=1+s and B=6+4s. The exact source word at n=3 is\n\n    h=(s,1+s,3/2),       B_3=B.\n\nHere is a split check directly from the accepted recurrences. C_1=rho,\nB_1=4 and C_2=(2+s+sqrt(10+8s))/2. Both candidates for B_2 are below8:\nG(1,4)=4+2sqrt(3)<8, and G(rho,1)<G(5/2,1)<8. Thus the k=0 candidate\nat n=3 is below G(1,8)=B. The k=2 candidate is below B because\nC_2<(4+3s)/2, by squaring sqrt(10+8s)<2+2s, and\nG((4+3s)/2,1)=B. The k=1 candidate equals G(rho,4)=B. It is therefore\nthe unique maximizing root split, and its bridge is 1+s.\n\nFor reference the complete certificate of this actual word is finite and\nexplicit. Let u=(s,2+s,2+s,2+s), and let the only nonzero finite entries\nof an array L be\n\n    L_01=1, L_10=rho, L_21=rho, L_31=rho, L_32=rho^2.\n\nSet lambda_(i,star)=2u_i/B, lambda_(star,i)=0, and\nlambda_ij=2L_ij/B for finite i!=j. All coefficients are nonnegative.\nThe finite-node differences out_L-in_L are\n(-s,-(2+s),-(2+s),4+3s), so adding u gives (0,0,0,B).\nThus all value coefficients cancel in\n\n    ||x_0||^2/B-2f_3+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n       =||x_0-s g_0-(2+s)(g_1+g_2+g_3)||^2/B.      (Certificate)\n\nThe Gram identity can also be checked directly: after multiplication by B,\nthe x_0 coefficient is1, its mixed matrix entries with g_i are -u_i,\nthe g_0 diagonal is2, every other gradient diagonal is B, the three\ng_0--g_i mixed matrix entries are 2+2s, and the other three mixed gradient\nmatrix entries are B. These are exactly the entries of (1,-u)(1,-u)^T.\nThe GD substitutions used are x_1=x_0-s g_0,\nx_2=x_1-(1+s)g_1 and x_3=x_2-(3/2)g_2.\n\nThe canonical Huber function and the unit quadratic both give 2f_3=1/B,\nso the certificate is tight. All its optimum-directed weights are positive,\nand section 1 applies to every attaining trajectory for this certificate.\n\n## 3. A finite coordinated deformation that passes three tests but is worse\n\nFor 0<t<=1/100 define the actual nonnegative word\n\n    h(t)=(s-t, 1+s-t/5, 3/2+31t/25).                (Path)\n\nIts total sum increases by t/25. Thus its canonical Huber payoff is\n1/(B+2t/25)<1/B.\n\nThe unit quadratic payoff, divided by 1/B, is\n\n    [(1-t/(s-1))(1-t/(5s))(1+62t/25)]^2.           (Q)\n\nAll factors are positive in the stated interval. Using log(1+v)<=v for\nv>-1, its unsquared logarithm is at most\n\n    t[-1/(s-1)-1/(5s)+62/25]\n       =t(37s-55)/(25s)<0,\n\nbecause (37s)^2=2738<3025=55^2. Hence (Q) is strictly below one.\n\nThe legal single-cut payoff at the MIDDLE/root step, divided by 1/B, is\n\n    R(t)=(1-t/(5s))^2 (1-t/(1+s))^(-2)\n                                    (1+31t/50)^(-1).        (Root)\n\nIt is also strictly below one throughout this finite interval. Indeed\n\n    (log R)'/2=-1/(5s-t)+1/(1+s-t)\n                              -(31/100)/(1+31t/50).\n\nUse 7/5<s<10/7 and 0<=t<=1/100. The three terms on the right are at\nmost -7/50, strictly below21/50, and strictly below-3/10, respectively:\n1/(1+s-t)<=100/239<21/50, and\n(31/100)/(1+31t/50)>=1550/5031>3/10. Their sum is strictly below-1/50.\nSince R(0)=1, this proves the assertion without relying only on a derivative\nat zero.\n\nNevertheless there is a fourth explicit, UNIQUE-minimizer legal instance\nwhose payoff increases. Set a=s-1 and define\n\n    F(x)=x^2/2                      if x<=a,\n         a x-a^2/2                 if x>=a.\n\nThe derivative min(x,a) is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz.\nZero is the unique minimizer. Start at x_0=1. The entire three-step\ntrajectory for (Path) is\n\n    g_0=a,\n    x_1=a(1+t)>a,                  g_1=a,\n    x_2=a(-s+6t/5)<0,              g_2=x_2,\n    x_3=a[s/2+(31s/25-3/5)t-(186/125)t^2].          (Trajectory)\n\nThe last point is in (0,a). Its bracket is positive, and its upper bound\ns/2+(31s/25)t is at most 5/7+31/1750<1. Thus the stated formulas use\nthe correct piece of F at EVERY query and at the terminal value.\n\nAt t=0, 2F(x_3)=a^2/2=1/B. At positive t its exact payoff ratio is\n\n    B*2F(x_3)\n       =[1+(62/25-6/(5s))t-(372/(125s))t^2]^2\n       >=(1+3t/2)^2>1.                             (Bad)\n\nFor the inequality, the coefficient of t inside the bracket is bounded\nbelow after subtracting its quadratic correction by\n284/175-(372/175)(1/100)>3/2, using s>7/5. All squared expressions\nare positive. The true initial distance is one, and multiplication of F\nby L gives the same normalized statement at arbitrary L.\n\nConsequently ANY full certificate for h(t), however its array or PSD rank\nchanges, obeys q B>1. This is a finite counterexample to sufficiency of\nthe Huber/unit-quadratic/root-cut screen, even when all three improve\nsimultaneously and both children are changed. It is a three-step result,\nnot an asymptotic obstruction to arbitrary joint deformations.\n\n## 4. A root rotation inequality for exact maximizing trees\n\nFor A>0,z>=0 define T_A(z)=(z+sqrt(z^2+8A))/2, so\nsqrt(G(A,D))=T_A(sqrt(D)). This section applies to EVERY maximizing root\nin the exact recurrence, without assuming which split is selected.\n\nSuppose the right dominant child is nonempty and itself has first left\nprimitive mass C and final dominant mass z^2. Write\n\n    u=T_C(z)=sqrt(D), v=T_A(u), E=Omega(A,C).\n\nThe alternative legal tree first forms a primitive join of masses A,C and\nthen a dominant join with z^2. Its primitive mass is at least E, because\nthat join is an allowed split in the exact primitive recurrence. The two\ncomplete trees have the same number of steps.\n\nThe defining equations give\n\n    E^2=(A+C)E+AC,    u^2=z u+2C,\n    v(v-u)=2A,       v(v-z)/2=A+C(v/u).\n\nAlso E=A+C(1+A/E). Since w(w-z)/2 is increasing at w>=z, it follows that\n\n    T_E(z)>v\n       iff v/u<1+A/E\n       iff u^2(E+A)>2E^2\n       iff z u(E+A)>2AE.                           (Rotation)\n\nFor the middle equivalence, set r=v/u>1 and use\nr(r-1)=2A/u^2 and the strict monotonicity of r(r-1) at r>1.\nThe final equivalence follows by substituting u^2=z u+2C and the\ndisplayed defining equation for E.\n\nAt a maximizing root, the strict improvement in (Rotation) is impossible.\nTherefore z u<=2AE/(E+A)<2A. Since z>=1,\n\n    sqrt(D)<2A.                                    (Balance)\n\nIf the right child is empty, D=1 and the same strict bound follows from\nA>=1. Thus (Balance) holds at every maximizing dominant root with n>=1.\n\nLet d=bridge-1 at this root and u=sqrt(D). Direct substitution gives\n\n    A=d+2d^2/u^2,       sqrt(B_n)=u+2d/u.\n\nWe claim d>=sqrt(B_n)/4. Set d_0=u^2/(4u-2), which is positive since\nu>=1. The increasing function d+2d^2/u^2 at d=d_0 equals\nA_0=u^3/(2u-1)^2. For 1<=u<=2, A_0<=1 because\n\n    (2u-1)^2-u^3=-(u-1)(u^2-3u+1)>=0.\n\nFor u>=2, A_0<=u/2 because\n(2u-1)^2-2u^2=2u^2-4u+1>0. We have A>=1 and A>u/2 by (Balance),\nso A>=A_0 and d>=d_0. This is equivalent to\n\n    4d>=u+2d/u=sqrt(B_n).                           (Root growth)\n\nAll source steps exceed one by their defining quadratics, so B_n>=2n+1.\nThus the source root gap diverges at least as sqrt(2n+1)/4. This is an\nexact structural estimate, not evidence for a fixed or dyadic split pattern.\n\n## 5. Why a plateau followed by an entirely quadratic suffix cannot lift\n\nThere is an explicitly defined UNBOUNDED set of horizons at which the\nchosen exact source root has a nonempty dominant suffix: take all n with\nk_D(n)<=n-2, where k_D chooses the smallest maximizing split.\n\nTo prove unboundedness, suppose instead that every sufficiently large n\nhas an empty chosen suffix. Then the root value G(C_(n-1),1) must be at\nleast the legal candidate G(C_(n-2),4). Set z=T_(C_(n-2))(2). Since\nT_(C_(n-1))(1)>=z, the defining root equations imply\n\n    C_(n-1)-C_(n-2)>=z/2\n       =(1+sqrt(1+2C_(n-2)))/2>=sqrt(C_(n-2)/2).\n\nLet v_j=sqrt(C_j)>=1. This forces, for all sufficiently large j,\n\n    v_j-v_(j-1)\n      >=1/[sqrt(2)(sqrt(1+1/sqrt(2))+1)]>0.\n\nIndeed substitute the lower bound C_j>=C_(j-1)+sqrt(C_(j-1)/2),\ntake square roots and rationalize their difference. It follows that\nC_j grows at least quadratically in j, contradicting the accepted\nC_j<=(j+1)^p with p<2. The specified set is therefore unbounded.\n\nNow take one of these horizons. A proposed scalar lifting rule uses the\none-sided Huber function with derivative min(x,c_plus), starts at1, and\nuses a constant-gradient prefix ending at the root with x_root=c_plus.\nFor the actual source prefix this forces c_plus=1/A. The root bridge\n1+d maps it to -d c_plus. The first suffix step h_next is a source step,\nso h_next-1>=sqrt(2)-1. If the suffix were entirely in the quadratic\nregion, its next point would be\n\n    d(h_next-1)c_plus.\n\nFor B_n>16rho^2, (Root growth) gives d>rho=1/(sqrt(2)-1), so this point\nis STRICTLY above c_plus. The alleged entirely quadratic suffix has\nalready left its defining region after its first step. If that point is\nterminal, the asserted quadratic terminal VALUE is still false.\n\nThe lower gap bound used here follows directly from the node equations:\nat a primitive node, d^2+(A+E)d-AE is nonpositive at d=sqrt(2)-1 for\nA,E>=1, and at a dominant node, 2d^2+Dd-AD is nonpositive at d=1/2.\nThe polynomials increase at nonnegative d. Hence every source step has\ngap at least sqrt(2)-1, as claimed.\n\nThis refutes the stated plateau-to-quadratic lifting rule on an explicit\nunbounded set of genuine maximizing words. It does NOT refute the legal\none-sided Huber function itself, a more detailed piecewise trajectory,\nor a multidimensional projection set K. It explains why the finite\ncounterexample in section 3 cannot simply be replicated up the source\ntree while suppressing its region conditions.\n\n## 6. Remaining interface\n\nPositive optimum-directed weights give the complete normal-cone equations,\nbut no joint family satisfying all active source edges and the required\nstationarity equations has been constructed here. Two routes were screened:\nusing only the three standard active tests fails by section 3; lifting\ntheir extra one-sided quadratic branch as an unchanged scalar block fails\nby sections 4--5. These failures do not establish a separator for the\nwhole full-certificate cone.\n\nAny proposed all-horizon first-order separator from this face remains\nBLOCKED until it supplies one legal Gram/value array satisfying every\nprefix interpolation inequality, source complementarity, and the claimed\ncoefficient inequalities. The actual interface and notation\n\nFor an integer m>=128, put\n\n lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j).\n\nSet n=2^m-1 and q=n-1. The queried steps h_0,...,h_(q-1) are the word\nH_m with its final d_0 deleted. The actual final step is b in [0,2]. Define\n\n s_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S=s_q,\n p_ij=product_(t<i)(1-h_t/d_j),\n R_i=sum_(j<m)p_ij w_j, Z_i=sum_(j<m)d_j p_ij w_j,\n x_i=u-s_i v+Z_i, g_i=v+R_i, 0<=i<=q.\n\nAll these vectors use ONE arbitrary real PSD Gram matrix. The values f_i\nform ONE array; the optimum jet is x_star=g_star=f_star=0; ||x_0||=1 is\nthe distance normalization relative to the chosen actual minimizer. Every\noriginal queried inequality is retained:\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.       (1)\n\nThe first d_j occurs at step 2^j-1. Thus p_ij is positive before query\n2^j and zero thereafter, and R_q=Z_q=0. The recursion gives\n\n Z_i-Z_(i+1)=h_i R_i, sum_i h_i p_ij=d_j.             (2)\n\nHere and below unqualified time sums range over 0<=i<q.\n\nFor clarity about the original value budget, define\n\n C_min=f_q-<v,x_q>, eta=-C_min-||v||^2/2,\n delta_i=f_i-<v,x_i>-C_min-||R_i||^2/2.\n\nOn an actual legal function, tilting by -<v,x> makes x_q a minimizer;\nthe smooth optimum inequalities give delta_i>=0. Also eta>=0 follows\nfrom Q_(q,star). Expanding Q_(0,star) gives the exact common budget\n\n eta>=delta_0+||R_0||^2+<v,R_0>-<R_0,x_0>.          (3)\n\nNo height is set to zero and neither eta nor the norm of x_0 is discarded.\n\n## 2. A lossless stochastic change of coordinates\n\nDefine polynomials\n\n phi_k(z)=product_(r<k)(1-d_r z), 0<=k<=m.\n\nWork modulo z phi_m(z), which has the m+1 distinct nodes\n0,1/d_0,...,1/d_(m-1). The double zero in the position quotient leaves\nexactly this simple-zero gradient quotient. Put\n\n V_k=v+sum_(j<m)phi_k(1/d_j) w_j, 0<=k<=m.\n\nThen V_0=g_0 and V_m=v. This change from (w_0,...,w_(m-1),v) to\n(V_0,...,V_m) is invertible: the evaluation matrix\nphi_k(1/d_j), with columns ordered j=0,...,m-1, then the node0, is\nupper triangular with strictly positive diagonal.\n\nThere are coefficients B_ik determined by B_00=1, B_0k=0 for k>0 and\n\n B_(i+1),0=(1-h_i/d_0)B_i0,\n B_(i+1),k=(1-h_i/d_k)B_ik+(h_i/d_(k-1))B_i,(k-1), 0<k<m,\n B_(i+1),m=B_im+(h_i/d_(m-1))B_i,(m-1).             (4)\n\nAlthough an unused factor 1-h_i/d_k can be negative for a dead state,\nit always multiplies zero. More precisely, if r_i distinct ruler levels\nhave occurred before query i, induction gives B_ik=0 for k<r_i.\nThe next step has level at most r_i, so h_i<=d_k on every occupied\nstate k<m. If it is the first d_(r_i), the mass at r_i moves entirely\nto r_i+1. Consequently all B_ik are nonnegative, every row sums to one,\nand after the first d_(m-1) the row is the point mass at state m.\n\nThe polynomial identity\n\n (1-hz)phi_k=(1-h/d_k)phi_k+(h/d_k)phi_(k+1)\n\nfor k<m, together with (1-hz)phi_m=phi_m modulo z phi_m, proves\n\n g_i=sum_(k=0)^m B_ik V_k.                           (5)\n\nThus B is exactly the marginal law of a time-inhomogeneous pure-birth\nchain: from occupied state k<m it stays with probability 1-h_i/d_k\nand advances one state with probability h_i/d_k; m is absorbing.\nThis probability space is auxiliary. No state vector V_k is assumed\nto be a gradient at an additional sample of the legal function.\n\nThe positions are equally explicit. Let U_k=V_k-v and\n\n Y_k=sum_(r=k)^(m-1)d_r U_r, Y_m=0.\n\nThe conditional expected change of Y at step i is -h_i U_k when the\nstate is k. Hence sum_k B_ik Y_k changes by -h_i R_i. Initially\n\n Y_0=sum_k d_k U_k=sum_j d_j w_j=Z_0,\n\nwhere the middle equality follows by telescoping\nd_k phi_k(z)=(phi_k(z)-phi_(k+1)(z))/z at z=1/d_j.\nTherefore\n\n Z_i=sum_k B_ik Y_k, x_i=u-s_i v+sum_k B_ik Y_k.      (6)\n\nIn particular the troublesome coupling remains, explicitly,\n\n <v,Z_0>=sum_(k<m)d_k<v,U_k>.                        (7)\n\nEquations (5)-(6) preserve the original vectors, not just their norms.\nSubstitution in (1) gives EXACTLY\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j\n   +<sum_k B_ik V_k, (s_i-s_j)v+sum_k(B_jk-B_ik)Y_k>\n   +(1/2)||sum_k(B_ik-B_jk)V_k||^2.                  (8)\n\nAll earlier/earlier, suffix, and optimum conditions remain in this one\narray. In particular (5) does not authorize independent state heights,\northogonality of state vectors, or projected legality.\n\nTwo occupation identities retain quantitative physical costs:\n\n sum_i h_i B_ik=d_k,                                (9)\n sum_i s_(i+1)h_i B_ik=d_k sum_(r<=k)d_r, 0<=k<m.    (10)\n\nTo prove (9), sum the flow equation (4). State0 has initial mass1 and\nfinal mass0, so its total outflow is1. Successive transient states have\nequal total inflow and outflow, so their total outflows are also1.\nFor (10), write M_k=sum_i s_(i+1)h_i B_ik and M_(-1)=0.\nSummation by parts gives\n\n sum_i s_(i+1)(B_(i+1),k-B_ik)=-sum_i h_i B_ik=-d_k.\n\nUsing (4), M_k/d_k-M_(k-1)/d_(k-1)=d_k, with the second term zero\nat k=0. Induction proves (10). The terminal occupation is\nsum_i h_i B_im=S-sum_(k<m)d_k; it is nonnegative and keeps the full\nconstant-gradient suffix in the budget.\n\n## 3. Total nonnegativity for the ACTUAL chronological evaluations\n\nThis is a theorem about temporal coefficient matrices, not yet the\nweighted energy matrix. It holds for arbitrary strictly increasing positive\nlevels d_0,...,d_(m-1) with the same ordered first-occurrence rule.\n\nLet P be the matrix with chronological rows i=0,...,q and columns\np_i0,...,p_i,(m-1),1, in that order. Then P is totally nonnegative:\nevery square minor, with its inherited row and column order, is >=0.\nThe same is true of B, and of the jump matrix\n\n A_ij=(h_i/d_j)p_ij, 0<=i<q, 0<=j<m.                (11)\n\nEvery column of A sums to1 by (2).\n\nExternal theorem used, with its local definitions made explicit:\nDavid Galvin and Adrian Pacurar, *Total non-negativity of some combinatorial\nmatrices*, arXiv:1807.08658v3 (2019), Theorem1.2; published JCTA172 (2020),\n105179. For a nondecreasing real sequence a_l, define C_ik by\nproduct_(t<=i)(X-e_t)=sum_k C_ik product_(l<=k)(X-a_l).\nThen C is totally nonnegative iff e is restricted growth relative to a:\nthe cap initially is a_1, an e_t below the cap leaves it unchanged,\nan equality advances it to the next a_l, and exceeding it is forbidden.\nThe proof constructs a planar path matrix; zero pivots at cap hits move\nall negative edge weights out of the network without changing its path\nmatrix. Disjoint-path determinant cancellation then proves the minor signs.\n\nThe exact PDF and extracted text were read, including Definition1.1,\nTheorem1.2, the pivot lemmas3.7/3.9, and the complete proof of1.2.\nFiles: downloads/1807.08658v3.pdf and downloads/1807.08658.txt.\nMatlas was queried repeatedly for this statement and title but did not\nreturn the paper; the primary arXiv text, not a fabricated Matlas hit,\ngrounds this citation. No verifier acceptance is claimed for this application.\n\nHere is the complete applicability check. Set\n\n a_1=-1/d_0<...<a_m=-1/d_(m-1)<a_(m+1)=0,\n\nand continue the a sequence with zeros as far as necessary. Set\ne_(i+1)=-1/h_i. A first occurrence of d_j is exactly a hit of the\ncurrent cap a_(j+1). Between first occurrences, every level is smaller\nand hence its e is strictly below the next cap. After all levels have\nappeared, every e is negative and the cap remains0. Thus the theorem's\nrestricted-growth condition is satisfied, with actual repetitions and\nthe actual deleted last d_0, not an ordered surrogate schedule.\n\nLet N_k(X)=product_(r<k)(X+1/d_r), so\nphi_k(z)=(product_(r<k)d_r)N_k(-z). The exact Newton expansion gives\n\n B_ik=(product_(t<i)h_t) C_ik/(product_(r<k)d_r),\n 0<=k<=m.                                          (12)\n\nThe coefficients with k>m vanish upon evaluation at the m+1 nodes\n-1/d_0,...,-1/d_(m-1),0. Positive row and column scaling and truncation\npreserve all minor signs, proving that B is totally nonnegative.\n\nFor completeness the node-evaluation matrix V_kj=phi_k(1/d_j), with\nthe node0 last, is also totally nonnegative. After positive row scaling,\nits transpose is L_jk=product_(r<k)(a_(j+1)-a_(r+1)). It is lower\ntriangular. In Neville elimination its pivot in row i,column k is\n\n product_(r=i-k)^(i-1)(a_(i+1)-a_(r+1))>0, i>=k.\n\nIndeed the pivot is the ratio of determinants on consecutive rows\ni-k,...,i and initial columns0,...,k, and on rows i-k,...,i-1 and\ncolumns0,...,k-1. These determinants are Vandermonde determinants:\nthe monic Newton polynomials differ from monomials by a triangular\ncolumn change with diagonal1. Cancelling gives the displayed pivot.\nAll elimination multipliers are positive. Reversing the elimination\nexpresses L as a product of elementary lower bidiagonal matrices with\nnonnegative off-diagonal entries and a positive diagonal matrix.\nEach factor is totally nonnegative, as follows directly by expanding\nits minors, and products preserve this property by Cauchy--Binet.\nThus V is totally nonnegative. Now P=BV, proving the assertion for P.\nFinally A is a row and column positive scaling of P's residual submatrix,\nso A has the asserted minor signs and stochastic column sums.\n\nThe chronological matrix has full column rank: the queries immediately\nbefore first d_j, followed by any query after first d_(m-1), give a square\nupper triangular evaluation submatrix with strictly positive diagonal.\nThus none of these representations has silently removed a modal direction.\n\n## 4. Exact cancellation of ALL shared heights\n\nDefine the elapsed-time quadratic form\n\n J=sum_i [2s_(i+1)h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>\n          +s_(i+1)||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2].\n\nThe original interpolation inequalities have the algebraic identity\n\n ||x_0||^2-2S f_q+2sum_i h_i Q_(i,star)\n       +2sum_i s_(i+1)Q_(i+1,i)=||x_q||^2+J.        (13)\n\nTo check it, GD gives\n2sum_i h_i Q_(i,star)=2sum_i h_i f_i-||x_0||^2\n +||x_q||^2+sum_i h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2.\nAlso Q_(i+1,i)=f_(i+1)-f_i+h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>\n +||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2/2. Summation by parts with s_0=0 and s_q=S\ncancels the displayed common values and proves (13). All multipliers\nare nonnegative. This is a sufficient certificate combination and does\nnot claim that its selected constraints are sufficient for legal data.\n\n## 5. A Brownian-bridge covariance with the full diagonal cost\n\nPut D_i=g_i-g_(i+1)=sum_j A_ij w_j, where A is (11). Define increasing\nclock knots and nonnegative penalties\n\n beta_i=s_(i+1)(s_i+1), 0<=i<q,\n beta_q=S(S+1), beta_(-1)=0,\n c_i=(h_i-1)s_(i+1).\n\nAll h_i>1, so c_i>=0, and beta_0<...<beta_q. Expanding the squared\ndifferences in J gives\n\n J=sum_(i=0)^q(beta_i-beta_(i-1))||g_i||^2\n                          -sum_(i<q)c_i||D_i||^2. (14)\n\nFor example the coefficient of ||g_i||^2 at an interior node is\ns_i(h_(i-1)+h_i)+h_i; that of ||g_q||^2 is S h_(q-1).\nThese are exactly the differences of beta in (14).\n\nSince g_i=v+sum_(r>=i)D_r, changing the order of finite sums yields\n\n J=beta_q||v||^2+2<v,sum_i beta_i D_i>\n        +sum_(i,l<q)beta_min(i,l)<D_i,D_l>\n        -sum_i c_i||D_i||^2.\n\nDefine\n\n Kbr_(i,l)=beta_min(i,l)-beta_i beta_l/beta_q.\n\nCompleting the ONE constant-mode square proves the exact joint identity\n\n J=beta_q||v+(sum_i beta_i D_i)/beta_q||^2\n       +sum_(i,l<q)Kbr_(i,l)<D_i,D_l>\n       -sum_i c_i||D_i||^2.                         (15)\n\nThe kernel Kbr is a real covariance kernel. For a direct elementary\nconstruction, take independent centered random variables xi_r of variances\nbeta_r-beta_(r-1), 0<=r<=q, let Z_i=sum_(r<=i)xi_r, and set\nZbr_i=Z_i-(beta_i/beta_q)Z_q. Direct expansion gives\nE[Zbr_i Zbr_l]=Kbr_(i,l). Consequently Kbr is PSD. This use of\nprobability creates no randomness in the legal instance or its shared Gram.\n\nThe remaining sufficient claim is the EXPLICIT known-matrix domination\n\n A^T Kbr A >= A^T diag(c_i) A                        (16)\n\nin the real PSD order, for the actual nearcritical ruler words. It is\nequivalent to J>=0 on this temporal quotient, because the constant square\nin (15) can be minimized by a freely chosen formal vector v. Every cost\nis present, including all repeated small steps, the large-step penalties,\nand the final constant-gradient suffix through beta_q.\n\nThus (16), IF PROVED, combines with (13) and ||x_0||=1 to give\n2f_q<=1/S, and the ordinary smooth descent inequality then gives\n2F(x_q-bv)<=1/S for every b in [0,2]. The previous draft's exact-B\ncomparison would have to be included in a complete eventual submission;\nits conditional target constant is lambda/exp(1)<1. Neither (16) nor\nthat resulting whole-interface bound is asserted established here.\n\nEquation (16) is a fixed, concrete covariance inequality on stochastic\nNewton columns. It has no unknown interpolation multipliers or heights.\nIt remains a hard restricted weighted Hardy inequality, not a routine\ncheck and not an equivalent restatement of the original all-h minimax goal.\n\n## 6. Why total nonnegativity does NOT settle the energy sign\n\nHere is an exact counterexample to that GENERIC implication. It is not a\ncounterexample to (16) for the actual m-dependent parameters.\n\nTake levels t=3/2 and u=4 and the ruler word (t,u,t). On its first two\nsteps, choose the single residual column at rate1/u, namely\n\n g_0=1, g_1=5/8, g_2=0, v=0.\n\nThe post-annihilation third step contributes zero. The ordered Newton\nmatrix and birth matrix are totally nonnegative by the same construction\nas above. Nevertheless the first step contributes\n\n 2t^2(5/8)+t(3/8)^2+t(1-t)=291/128,\n\nand the second contributes\n\n (t+u)(5/8)^2+u(1-u)(5/8)^2=-325/128.\n\nTherefore J=-17/64<0. The hypotheses used to prove the temporal minor\nsigns all hold, but weighted-energy positivity fails. These scalar data\nare a formal certificate test, not an asserted legal interpolation array.\n\nAccordingly, a planar network for A cannot by itself be called a proof of\n(16). A successful path argument must ALSO absorb the explicit costs c_i\nusing the actual relation between ruler levels and chronological mass.\n\n## 7. Current proof state\n\nComplete draft structure: lossless finite birth chain; invariant occupation\nbudgets (9)-(10); audited total nonnegativity of the actual temporal and\njump matrices; common-height cancellation (13); and the exact conditioned\ncovariance formula (15), with all cross terms and all penalties retained.\n\nStill open: the uniform quantitative domination (16), or an actual legal\npositive-height object escaping its intended bound. Two direct attempts\ndid not prove it: local scalar square completion discards required past\ncovariance; comparison to a continuous positive Laplace kernel has no\nproved uniform error bound on signed modal combinations. The new ordered\nkernel result does not fill this quantitative gap, as Section6 demonstrates.\n\nNo whole-interface charge or benchmark-scale legal construction has been\ncompleted. The original problem remains unresolved, and no new fact entered\nthe graph. The fourth launch's second round does not authorize another round,\nnumerical budget, or verifier submission.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "max2_newton_birth_covariance", "plan_id": "max2_elapsed_time_joint_storage", "attempt_type": "direct", "status": "partial_blocked", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "finding_ids": ["2613cd9edf4f8207", "7bc886cf037bdcd4"], "artifact": "research/newton_birth_chain_joint_storage.md", "external_refs": [{"key": "GP20", "authors": ["David Galvin", "Adrian Pacurar"], "title": "Total non-negativity of some combinatorial matrices", "arxiv": "1807.08658", "year": 2020, "cited_for": "Theorem1.2 with Definition1.1 and proof via Lemmas3.7/3.9", "retrieval": "Repeated Matlas queries did not index the theorem; exact primary arXiv v3 PDF and extracted HTML text were downloaded and read."}], "counterexample_search_for_stuck_subgoal": {"performed": true, "result": "not_refuted", "summary": "Actual weighted-kernel PSD is not refuted. A separate exact generalized-ruler counterexample shows that chronological total nonnegativity alone cannot imply the weighted-energy sign."}}, "glossary": {"B_ik": "Marginal probability of state k at query i in the exact finite pure-birth representation (equation4).", "V_k": "Fixed state gradient vector v+sum_j phi_k(1/d_j)w_j; V_m=v.", "U_k": "State residual V_k-v.", "Y_k": "State position residual sum_(r>=k)d_r U_r.", "phi_k": "Distinct-root Newton polynomial product_(r<k)(1-d_r z).", "A_ij": "Jump probability (h_i/d_j)p_ij; columns sum to one.", "D_i": "Actual gradient jump g_i-g_(i+1)=sum_j A_ij w_j.", "beta_i": "Clock s_(i+1)(s_i+1), with beta_q=S(S+1) and beta_(-1)=0.", "c_i": "Required diagonal jump penalty (h_i-1)s_(i+1).", "Kbr": "Covariance kernel beta_min(i,l)-beta_i beta_l/beta_q.", "J": "Elapsed-time joint quadratic form defined in Section4.", "eta": "Unnormalized common optimum height -C_min-||v||²/2.", "delta_i": "Single shared tilted value height f_i-<v,x_i>-C_min-||R_i||²/2.", "P": "Chronological Newton evaluation matrix with residual-rate columns and the constant column last.", "C_ik": "Newton change-of-basis coefficients in the audited Galvin-Pacurar theorem."}}
