{"id": "c91fb520fcf58307", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T23:45:02.376822+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C67 PARTIAL full-horizon program, with complete unverified component proofs: for every power epigraph p>2, derive one global legal F_p, true initial distance, exact GD, all ordered pairs, uniform no-turn E_p(T), and the moving-normal clock law Delta C_p<=-h+|r|^(p-2)Delta v/(p-2). The scalar clock transfer with no vertical charge is REFUTED by an exact first-actual-step quartic example with clock increase>3. No full-horizon power-family or original-target bound follows.", "evidence": "# C67: an exact moving-normal clock and a failed scalar-clock transfer\n\nStatus: COMPLETE UNVERIFIED statements and proofs below; the proposed\nfull-horizon power-epigraph program remains PARTIAL. No fact_submit,\ntrajectory experiment, optimization, installation, or new allocation.\nC65 and C66 are not mathematical premises. All power-epigraph claims\nused here are derived in this document.\n\n## 1. Statements and scope\n\nFor any real p>2 put phi_p(r)=|r|^p/p and define the single global function\n\n    K_p={(r,s):s>=phi_p(r)},\n    F_p(X)=max_(g in K_p)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2).\n\nFor r real and v>=0 use the states\n\n    eta=v+phi_p(r),\n    u=eta*|r|^(p-2),\n    X_p(r,v)=(r(1+u),-v),\n    G_p(r)=(r,phi_p(r)).\n\nF_p is globally convex and differentiable with 1-Lipschitz gradient.\nIts minimizer set is {(0,y):y<=0}, its minimum value is zero, and\n\n    grad F_p(X_p(r,v))=G_p(r),\n    2F_p(X_p(r,v))\n      =r^2+2(p-1)v|r|^p/p+(2p-1)|r|^(2p)/p^2.             (1)\n\nFor every nonzero initial r_0 and v_0>=0 the true initial squared\nclosest-minimizer distance is\n\n    R_0^2=r_0^2[1+(v_0+|r_0|^p/p)|r_0|^(p-2)]^2=x_0^2.  (2)\n\nHere x is the horizontal coordinate of X_p. All p,r,v,u,eta and clock\nsymbols in this document are local variables, not redefinitions of the\naccepted project glossary.\n\nFor an actual consecutive run with nonnegative steps of total mass T,\nwhose nonzero horizontal gradients all have the same sign, the normalized\npayoff with its TRUE entry horizontal distance obeys, if T>=1/(2p-2),\n\n    2F_p(X_end)/x_entry^2 <= E_p(T),\n    E_p(T)=[(2p-2)T+2p-1]^2\n             /[4p(p-1)(T+1)^2(2T+1)].                    (3)\n\nNo v-coordinate is reset when applying this to a later block. Its ratio\nto the ORIGINAL initial payoff includes x_entry^2/x_0^2 explicitly.\n\nDefine, for r>0 and v>=0,\n\n    C_p(r,v)=log r+(p-1)v*r^(p-2)/(p-2)\n                    +(2p-1)r^(2p-2)/[p(2p-2)].           (4)\n\nAt a single sign-preserving step h>0 the exact moving-state identity\nin Section4 implies\n\n    C_p(|r_plus|,v_plus)-C_p(|r|,v)\n       <=-h+|r|^(p-2)(v_plus-v)/(p-2)\n       =-h+h|r|^(2p-2)/[p(p-2)].                         (5)\n\nFor a whole one-sign interval [A,B] this gives the inherited-state budget\n\n    C_p(|r_B|,v_B)-C_p(|r_A|,v_A)\n       <=-T+J_(A,B)\n       <=-T+|r_A|^(p-2)(v_B-v_A)/(p-2),                 (6)\n    J_(A,B)=sum_(i=A)^(B-1)\n                 |r_i|^(p-2)(v_(i+1)-v_i)/(p-2).\n\nThe tempting stronger statement with J omitted is FALSE, even at the\nfirst actual small step of H_m. For p=4, r_0=2 and v_0=0, the first\nstep of every actual H_m, m>=128, preserves the positive sign and has\n\n    C_4(r_1,v_1)-C_4(2,0)>3.                             (7)\n\nThus the fixed-profile scalar rule that every noncrossing step decreases\nits clock by at least h does not transfer to this changing normal state.\nThis does not refute a full-horizon power-epigraph payoff bound, nor C65.\n\n## 2. Global legality, values, and every ordered interpolation pair\n\nThe epigraph K_p is nonempty, closed and convex. Completing the square\nin the definition of F_p gives a coercive, strictly convex projection\nproblem, so there is a unique maximizing gradient Pi_(K_p)(X).\nAdding the two projection normal inequalities gives\n\n    ||Pi_(K_p)(X)-Pi_(K_p)(Y)||^2\n       <=<Pi_(K_p)(X)-Pi_(K_p)(Y),X-Y>.\n\nThus the projection is 1-Lipschitz. Comparison of the two defining\nmaxima bounds the first-order remainder of F_p between zero and\n||Y-X||^2. This proves differentiability and the stated gradient.\nConvexity follows from the supremum-of-affine-functions definition.\n\nThe difference X_p-G_p equals eta*(phi_p'(r),-1). For any (t,s) in K_p,\n\n    <X_p-G_p,(t,s)-G_p>\n       <=eta*[phi_p'(r)(t-r)-phi_p(t)+phi_p(r)]<=0.\n\nThe final inequality is the defining tangent inequality for the convex\ndifferentiable function phi_p. This proves the gradient formula. Direct\nsubstitution into <X_p,G_p>-||G_p||^2/2 proves (1).\n\nFor arbitrary two such states, write f_i=F_p(X_i). The complete ordered\ninterpolation residual, with no restriction on chronological distance, is\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2\n       =<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>\n       =eta_j*[phi_p'(r_j)(r_i-r_j)-phi_p(r_i)+phi_p(r_j)]\n       <=0.                                               (8)\n\nEvery point (0,y), y<=0, has F_p=0 by testing zero and bounding all other\nfeasible inner products. For x!=0, testing (t,phi_p(t)) for sufficiently\nsmall t with the sign of x gives a positive value: the linear term xt\ndominates the terms of orders two and higher. For x=0 and y>0, a small\npositive vertical gradient gives positive value. These tests determine\nthe minimizer set exactly and prove (2).\n\nAt a physical step h>=0 the exact GD recurrence is\n\n    v_plus=v+h|r|^p/p,\n    x_plus=x-hr,\n    x_plus=r_plus+v_plus*r_plus*|r_plus|^(p-2)\n                         +r_plus*|r_plus|^(2p-2)/p.        (9)\n\nThe last right-hand side is odd, continuous, strictly increasing and\nsurjective. Its derivative off zero is\n1+(p-1)v_plus|r_plus|^(p-2)+(2p-1)|r_plus|^(2p-2)/p>=1;\nthe derivative at zero is 1. Thus (9) defines the unique actual next\nstate of the SAME function. Its normal coefficient satisfies\n\n    eta_plus-eta=(h-1)phi_p(r)+phi_p(r_plus)>=0 for h>=1.   (10)\n\nThe effective turning variable u=eta|r|^(p-2) need not increase.\n\n## 3. The no-turn payoff retains accumulated v and the entry radius\n\nBy symmetry suppose the whole block is on the positive ray. For fixed\nv define T_v(r)=r+v r^(p-1)+r^(2p-1)/p on r>=0. At each noncrossing\npositive step, x_plus<=x and v_plus>=v, so its inverse in (9) gives\n0<r_plus<=r. Consequently, with terminal r>0 and u=v*r^(p-2)+r^(2p-2)/p,\n\n    x_entry=x_end+sum h_i r_i >=r(T+1+u),\n    v_end=v_entry+sum h_i r_i^p/p >=T r^p/p.               (11)\n\nSet z=r^(2p-2)/p and w=v_end*r^(p-2), so u=w+z and w>=Tz.\nEquation (1), divided by r^2, is\n\n    2F_p(X_end)/r^2=1+(2-2/p)u+z/p.\n\nSince z<=u/(T+1), the ratio in (3) is at most\n\n    [1+k_T u]/[T+1+u]^2,\n    k_T=2-2/p+1/[p(T+1)].                                (12)\n\nFor u>=0 the derivative in (12) has the sign of\nk_T(T+1)-2-k_T u. When T>=1/(2p-2), its maximum is attained at\nu=T+1-2/k_T>=0 and equals k_T^2/[4(k_T(T+1)-1)]. Simplification is\nexactly (3). Negative-ray blocks give the same result by reflection.\nAn absorbing endpoint has zero payoff and requires no logarithmic clock.\n\nFor p=2 the rational expression E_p(T) formally reduces to the parabolic\nexpression, but this argument and the clock statements here are stated\nfor p>2. For fixed p>2,\n\n    lim_(T->infinity) T E_p(T)=(p-1)/(2p).                (13)\n\nThis is a no-turn result, not a full-family bound in the presence of\nrepeated turns. It does not assume a fresh v=0 at a later block.\n\n## 4. Exact horizontal clock and the positive vertical-state cost\n\nLet g_v be the inverse of T_v on the positive half-line and set\nPsi_p(x,v)=C_p(g_v(x),v), for x>0. Direct differentiation gives\n\n    d C_p(r,v)/dr=T_v'(r)/r,\n    partial Psi_p(x,v)/partial x=1/g_v(x),\n    partial Psi_p(x,v)/partial v=g_v(x)^(p-2)/(p-2).       (14)\n\nFor the last identity, differentiating T_v(g_v(x))=x at fixed x gives\npartial g_v/partial v=-g_v(x)^(p-1)/T_v'(g_v(x)). The chain rule then\nsubtracts r^(p-2) from (p-1)r^(p-2)/(p-2), leaving the last expression\nin (14). It is POSITIVE.\n\nFor one positive noncrossing step, write x_plus=x-hr>0. The exact split\ninto horizontal motion at the old v followed by the actual v increment is\n\n    C_p(r_plus,v_plus)-C_p(r,v)\n       =-integral_(x_plus)^x [dy/g_v(y)]\n        +1/(p-2)*integral_v^(v_plus) g_s(x_plus)^(p-2) ds. (15)\n\nThe first integral is at least (x-x_plus)/r=h, since g_v(y)<=r.\nFor the second integral, x_plus<=x and s>=v imply g_s(x_plus)<=r.\nThis proves (5); summing it over the same-sign interval proves (6),\nbecause its gradient magnitudes decrease. By evenness the same\ncalculation applies to a negative interval using absolute radii.\n\nThe cost J is an actual weighted vertical increment. It is not a\nfreely chosen auxiliary value or a discarded initial height. A proposed\nblock argument for power epigraphs must bound it, or replace this clock\nwith a potential that pays for it. Merely knowing that eta grows does\nnot justify deleting the positive second integral in (15).\n\n## 5. An exact actual-H_m counterexample to omitting the cost\n\nFor m>=128 keep the enclosing-horizon physical parameters\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j).\n\nThe elementary bounds 2/5<a<5/12 give\n7/5<h=1+a<17/12<3/2 for the first actual step. For completeness,\nsqrt(2)<99/70 gives c<(169/70)(129/128)<73/30 and hence\na>(127/128)(30/73)>2/5. Also a<sqrt(2)-1<5/12.\n\nFix p=4,r_0=2,v_0=0. Then\n\n    X_0=(34,0), R_0^2=1156,\n    x_1=34-2h>31,\n    v_1=4h>28/5.\n\nThe unique positive r_1 is determined by T_(v_1)(r_1)=x_1, where\nT_v(r)=r+v r^3+r^7/4. At r=3/2,\n\n    T_(v_1)(3/2)=3/2+(27/2)h+2187/512,\n    x_1-T_(v_1)(3/2)=65/2-(31/2)h-2187/512\n                     >37/4-2187/512>0.\n\nStrict monotonicity implies r_1>3/2. Also r_1<2 follows from x_1<x_0\nand v_1>v_0. Thus the step is genuinely positive and sign-preserving.\nBy (4),\n\n    C_4(r_1,v_1)-C_4(2,0)\n       >log(3/4)+(3/2)(28/5)(9/4)\n                    +(7/24)[729/64-64]\n       =log(3/4)+189/10-23569/1536\n       >-1/3+189/10-23569/1536>3.                        (16)\n\nHere log(4/3)<1/3 follows by integrating 1/t<1 on (1,4/3).\nThe final rational difference above 3 is 1707/7680=569/2560>0.\nNo scalar root was approximated or trajectory numerically evaluated.\nEquations (1),(2),(8),(9) give the global function, true denominator,\nevery ordered interpolation pair, and actual prescribed step.\n\nThis refutes any universal claim C_plus-C<=-kappa h with kappa>=0\nfor this moving-state clock. It is not a counterexample to payoff\nconvergence or to the separate parabolic argument.\n\n## 6. Literature grounding and the open quantitative interface\n\nThe exact power projection equation agrees with Propositions3.2--3.3\nof Chierchia, Pustelnik, Pesquet and Pesquet-Popescu, \"Epigraphical\nsplitting for solving constrained convex formulations of inverse\nproblems with proximal tools\", arXiv:1210.5844v3. Its exact downloaded\npaper and extracted text are in downloads/c66_epigraph_projection_1210.5844.\nThe Hilbert product is Euclidean, the generator has full domain and is\nproper closed convex, and their parameters beta=p,tau=1/p,zeta=-v give\nT_v(r)=x. The definitions and both projection proofs were re-read.\nNo theorem from that paper is a premise of this self-contained proof.\n\nThe precise failure is a proof migration from a FIXED scalar gradient\nprofile to an epigraph whose vertical coordinate changes along the\nsame legal trajectory. Equation (15) supplies the omitted term.\nThere is still no estimate of the sum of the J costs and crossing\ngains over every chronological block on H_m, in terms of R_0 and the\nexact benchmark. Consequently the full p>2 family, including quartic\nmultiple-turn trajectories, remains open. No new original-target\nclaim or verifier allocation follows from this result.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "a2b90d65ce72ba76", "branch_id": "C67", "attempt_type": "direct", "status": "partial", "component_status": {"global_realization": "solved in this draft", "moving_state_clock": "solved with retained positive vertical cost", "fixed_clock_transfer": "refuted", "full_word_cost_budget": "stuck"}, "counterexample_search_for_stuck_subgoal": {"performed": true, "result": "refuted", "summary": "Actual H_m first step, p4 r0=2,v0=0 gives a sign-preserving clock increase>3."}, "artifact": "workers/max3/research/c67_power_epigraph_moving_clock.md", "verification_status": "UNVERIFIED; no fact_submit available"}, "glossary": {"phi_p": "Local power epigraph generator |r|^p/p for real p>2.", "K_p": "Epigraph of phi_p in R^2.", "F_p": "Global convex 1-smooth function max_(g in K_p)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2).", "C_p": "Moving-normal clock log r+(p-1)v r^(p-2)/(p-2)+(2p-1)r^(2p-2)/(p(2p-2)), r>0.", "J_(A,B)": "Actual weighted vertical increment sum |r_i|^(p-2)(v_(i+1)-v_i)/(p-2) over the named one-sign interval.", "E_p(T)": "No-turn payoff cap [(2p-2)T+2p-1]^2/[4p(p-1)(T+1)^2(2T+1)].", "T_v": "Positive horizontal coordinate map r+v r^(p-1)+r^(2p-1)/p; its inverse is g_v."}}
{"id": "ae1fe666152d2dcf", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T23:49:11.519997+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max4 round15: the proposed signed product-character policy is quantitatively weak for ALL positive amplitude laws, not merely adjacent saturation. With rho_0=-1/2 and rho_l=1-2^(-l-10), every actual legal trajectory on the unchanged H_m,m>=64, has ||g_i||<=||g_0|| and TRUE-distance payoff V_chr<5(99/100)^((n+1)/8), n=2^m-1. Hence B_n V_chr<5(1+4n^log_2(3))(99/100)^((n+1)/8)->0. Includes an explicit one-function legal realization of these same weights and every original ordered pair. One complete UNVERIFIED candidate; no original-target claim.", "evidence": "# The proposed signed product weights fail for every positive amplitude law\n\nStatus: **one self-contained, unverified round-15 candidate**. No verifier,\noptimization, parameter search, or installation was used. The mathematical\npredecessors are accepted facts `1252b8823bb5a290` (necessary smooth-convex\ninterpolation) and `b98bb807d514e80d` (the exact benchmark and its crude upper\nbound). The round-14 drafts are motivation, not proof premises. All further\ncalculations used in this candidate are supplied here.\n\nThis resolves the proposed policy in finding `9c015a3328a5fcf9` more broadly\nthan requested: the weights are fixed, but the positive chronological\namplitudes, common anchor, values, and realizing function are arbitrary.\nNeither adjacent equality nor a specific amplitude recurrence is assumed.\nThis does not exclude other real character weight policies.\n\n## Statement and notation\n\nFix an integer m>=64. Put\n\n    sigma=1+sqrt(2), delta=1-1/m,\n    c_chr=sigma(1+1/m), a_chr=delta/c_chr,\n    N=2^m, n=N-1.\n\nThe actual chronological word is\n\n    H_chr,0=(),\n    H_chr,j+1=(H_chr,j,1+a_chr c_chr^j,H_chr,j), 0<=j<m.\n\nThe parameters a_chr,c_chr are fixed by the enclosing m in every subword.\nWrite h=H_chr,m, so h_i=1+a_chr c_chr^(nu_2(i+1)), 0<=i<n, where nu_2(v)\nis the exponent of 2 dividing the positive integer v. These are n physical\nsteps and N sampled gradients. No sample or algorithm step is padded.\n\nGive sample i its standard m-bit binary label. For s in {0,1}^m define\n\n    chi_s(i)=(-1)^(sum_(l=0)^(m-1) s_l i_l),\n    rho_0=-1/2, rho_l=1-2^(-l-10) for 1<=l<m,\n    w_s=2^(-m) product_(l=0)^(m-1)(1+rho_l(-1)^(s_l)).       (1)\n\nEvery w_s is strictly positive and their sum is one. For ANY positive\nnumbers Gamma_0,...,Gamma_n prescribe\n\n    g_i=(Gamma_i sqrt(w_s) chi_s(i))_s.                     (2)\n\nThe conclusions below also hold for any Euclidean realization of the same\nGram matrix. Suppose that these really are all the gradients along GD on\nONE convex differentiable 1-smooth function F with a minimizer:\n\n    X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i g_i, g_i=grad F(X_i), 0<=i<=n,\n\nwhere the recursion uses only i<n. The function and anchor are unrestricted.\nLet R_dist=dist(X_0,argmin F)>0 and F_star=min F. Then\n\n    ||g_i||<=||g_0|| for all 0<=i<=n,                       (3)\n\nand the actual normalized objective satisfies\n\n    V_chr := 2(F(X_n)-F_star)/R_dist^2\n          < 5(99/100)^((n+1)/8).                          (4)\n\nIn particular, for the original exact benchmark B_n from accepted fact\n`b98bb807d514e80d`, with p=log_2(3),\n\n    B_n V_chr\n      < 5(1+4n^p)(99/100)^((n+1)/8) -> 0.                 (5)\n\nThe bound is uniform over all positive amplitudes and all actual legal\nrealizations of (2). Thus this precise product-weight policy cannot supply\na uniform all-schedule lower witness matching 1/B_n. This is not an upper\nbound on the unrestricted W_n(h). A legal realization of this policy is\nexplicitly given at the end, so the assertion is not merely vacuous.\n\nFor L-smooth objectives, apply the statement to F=f/L and g_i=grad f(X_i)/L;\nthe recursion is the original one with steps h_i/L, and (4) has the original\ndenominator L R_dist^2.\n\n## 1. Real kernel and the original inequalities\n\nSumming (1) separately in each bit gives sum_s w_s=1. The same finite\nfactorization gives\n\n    <g_i,g_j>=Gamma_i Gamma_j\n                 product_(l: i_l != j_l) rho_l.           (6)\n\nIn particular ||g_i||=Gamma_i. No interpolation property is inferred from\nthis factorization. All gradients in (2) are independent, because the full\ncharacter matrix is invertible and all its row and column scaling factors\nare nonzero. Independence is not needed for the obstruction.\n\nFor actual values f_i=F(X_i)-F_star, accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290` gives\nevery original ordered inequality\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.        (7)\n\nThe argument uses necessary sums of these inequalities. It does not assert\nthat those sums are sufficient for a trajectory. In particular every legal\nfunction in the statement must also obey all the unused pairs and optimum\ncomparisons.\n\nFor consecutive samples with step h=1+v, normalized correlation d, and\ngradient norm ratio r_amp>0, summing the two inequalities (7) gives\n\n    r_amp^2+(v-1)d r_amp-v <=0.                            (8)\n\nIt follows from this necessary condition that, for every v>=0 and -1<=d<=1,\n\n    r_amp<=max(1,v).                                      (9)\n\nIndeed the polynomial in (8) has a single positive root when v>0. At 1\nits value is (1-v)(1-d), nonnegative if v<=1. At v its value is\nv(v-1)(1+d), nonnegative if v>=1. We use (9) only for v>0.\n\n## 2. A two-step cap allowing arbitrary adjacent slack\n\nUse local indices 0,1,2 for three consecutive samples. Suppose their steps\nare 1+t,1+u and their normalized correlations are\n\n    corr(g_0,g_1)=-1/2,\n    corr(g_0,g_2)=z,\n    corr(g_1,g_2)=-z/2.                                  (10)\n\nAssume\n\n    2/5<=t<1, u>=29/5, 1023/1024<=z<=1.                  (11)\n\nPut A=Gamma_0, r_amp=Gamma_1/Gamma_0, q_amp=Gamma_2/Gamma_0, and let\nR_leaf(t) be the positive root of\n\n    R_leaf^2+(1-t)R_leaf/2-t=0.                           (12)\n\nThe first adjacent sum gives 0<r_amp<=R_leaf. Substitution of 1/2 into\nthe polynomial (12) gives 1/2-5t/4<=0, so R_leaf>=1/2.\n\nConsider the ORIGINAL directed three-sample cycle\n\n    T_cyc=Q_02+Q_10+Q_21.\n\nThe three function values cancel. Substitution of the two actual steps in\n(7) gives, with K_ij=<g_i,g_j>,\n\n    T_cyc=-t K_00+K_11+K_22\n                 +(t-u-1)K_01+u K_12-K_02.\n\nUsing (10), this is exactly\n\n    T_cyc/A^2=P(q_amp,r_amp),\n    P(q,r)=q^2-zq-t+r^2+r(1+u-t-u zq)/2.                 (13)\n\nNo equality has been imposed on either adjacent edge. Define\n\n    q_cap=u R_leaf/2.\n\nThen q_cap>=29/20>1. For q>=q_cap and 0<=r<=R_leaf,\n\n    dP/dr <= R_leaf(2-z u^2/4)+(1+u-t)/2\n           <=13/10+u/2-(1023/1024)u^2/8\n           <=-183/204800 <0.                             (14)\n\nFor the second inequality, the coefficient 2-z u^2/4 is negative under\n(11), so R_leaf>=1/2 and t>=2/5 give the indicated upper bound. The scalar\nquadratic on the second line decreases for u>=29/5, and its value at\n29/5 is exactly -183/204800. This proves every inequality in (14).\n\nConsequently P(q,r_amp)>=P(q,R_leaf) for q>=q_cap. Equation (12) simplifies\nthe latter expression to\n\n    P(q,R_leaf)=q^2-zq(1+q_cap)+q_cap\n       =(q-1)(q-q_cap)+(1-z)q(1+q_cap).                  (15)\n\nIf q>q_cap this is strictly positive, contradicting T_cyc<=0 from (7).\nTherefore every legal local triple obeys\n\n    Gamma_2/Gamma_0 <= (u/2) R_leaf(t).                   (16)\n\nIf z<1, equality is also impossible. In quantitative form, any attempt with\nq_amp>q_cap has an original ordered residual among Q_02,Q_10,Q_21 at least\n\n    (A^2/3)[(q_amp-1)(q_amp-q_cap)\n                  +(1-z)q_amp(1+q_cap)] >0.              (17)\n\nThis is independent of the anchor and of every assigned value. It is a\nconstraint on the precise new PRIMAL Gram, not a tensor-dual obstruction.\n\n## 3. Every large bridge of the actual word satisfies the hypotheses\n\nSet t=a_chr and write u_k=a_chr c_chr^k=delta c_chr^(k-1) for the level-k\nbridge. Leaves have k=0, and their next sample differs only in bit 0.\nThus every leaf has correlation -1/2 and gradient norm ratio at most\n\n    R_leaf=R_leaf(a_chr).                                (18)\n\nFor m>=64,\n\n    2/5<a_chr<sqrt(2)-1<1.\n\nFor the lower bound, sigma<29/12 gives\na_chr> (63/65)(12/29)=756/1885>2/5. The upper bound follows from\na_chr=(sqrt(2)-1)(m-1)/(m+1).\n\nA level-k bridge with k>=1 has an immediately preceding leaf. At the\nthree samples straddling this pair, the first change flips bit 0, the\nouter change flips exactly bits 1,...,k, and the second change flips\nbits 0,...,k. Formula (6) therefore gives (10) with\n\n    z=z_k=product_(l=1)^k(1-2^(-l-10)).\n\nThe elementary finite inequality product_l(1-e_l)>=1-sum_l e_l for\n0<=e_l<=1 follows by induction after multiplying one more factor.\nIt yields z_k>1-2^(-10)=1023/1024; also z_k<1.\n\nFor every k>=3,\n\n    u_k>=u_3=sigma^2(1-1/m)(1+1/m)^2>sigma^2>29/5.\n\nThe strict middle inequality uses\n(1-e)(1+e)^2=1+e-e^2-e^3>1 for 0<e<=1/64.\nThe last inequality follows from sqrt(2)>7/5.\nHence (16) applies at every such bridge, wherever that bridge occurs.\n\nThe two smaller bridge levels need only adjacent inequalities:\n\n* u_1=delta<1, so its gradient norm ratio is at most 1 by (9).\n* 1<u_2=delta c_chr=sigma(1-1/m^2)<sigma<5/2. Its adjacent correlation\n  is -z_2/2 with 0<z_2<=1. The polynomial in (8) at r_amp=2 is\n  4-(u_2-1)z_2-u_2>=5-2u_2>0, so its ratio is at most 2.\n\nThese comparisons refer to the original m-dependent word. No subword is\ngiven a reset physical parameter or a different initial function.\n\n## 4. An exact full-block product and a uniform strict contraction\n\nWrite\n\n    Theta_chr=R_leaf^4 delta c_chr^3.                     (19)\n\nFor any occurrence of H_chr,j in the actual word, j>=3, pairing each\nnonleaf bridge with its immediately preceding leaf and leaving the final\nleaf unpaired gives\n\n    Gamma_after/Gamma_before\n      <= 2 delta^(-1)c_chr^(-j) Theta_chr^(2^j/8).          (20)\n\nHere is the complete count. There are 2^(j-1) leaves. Level 1 contributes\nno further factor, level 2 contributes at most 2 once for each of its\n2^(j-3) occurrences, and each level k>=3 contributes u_k/2 in addition\nto the R_leaf for its preceding leaf. The remaining counts are\n\n    sum_(k=3)^(j-1)2^(j-k-1)=2^(j-3)-1,\n    sum_(k=3)^(j-1)(k-1)2^(j-k-1)=3*2^(j-3)-j.\n\nBoth equalities follow by induction in j, starting with the empty sums\nat j=3. Thus the product is\n\n    R_leaf^(2^(j-1)) 2^(2^(j-3))\n       delta^(2^(j-3)-1)c_chr^(3*2^(j-3)-j)\n       2^(-(2^(j-3)-1)),\n\nwhich is the right side of (20). In particular this identity includes j=3.\n\nWe next prove, uniformly in the enclosing m,\n\n    0<Theta_chr<5926527/6002500<99/100.                    (21)\n\nLet t_inf=1/sigma=sqrt(2)-1 and R_inf=R_leaf(t_inf).\nDividing (12) by t and writing y=R_leaf(t)/sqrt(t) gives\n\n    y^2+A(t)y-1=0, A(t)=(1-t)/(2sqrt(t)).\n\nFor positive t the function A(t) decreases, whereas the positive root\n(-A+sqrt(A^2+4))/2 decreases as A increases. Consequently\nR_leaf(t)/sqrt(t) increases with t. Since\nt=a_chr=t_inf delta/(1+1/m),\n\n    Theta_chr\n      <= R_inf^4 sigma^3 delta^3(1+1/m)\n      < R_inf^4 sigma^3.                                (22)\n\nThe last inequality uses (1-e)^3(1+e)<1 for 0<e<1.\n\nFor explicit rational bounds, sqrt(2)<99/70 because 99^2>2*70^2, so\nt_inf<29/70. The polynomial in (12), at t=29/70 and R=18/35, is\n1/1225>0. It decreases as t increases and increases as R>0 increases\nfor t<1. Hence R_inf<18/35. Also sigma<29/12, since sqrt(2)<17/12.\nIt follows that\n\n    R_inf^4 sigma^3\n      < (18/35)^4(29/12)^3\n      =5926527/6002500 <99/100.\n\nThis proves (21) without any numerical parameter selection.\n\nThe short blocks separately satisfy\n\n    H_chr,1: Gamma_after/Gamma_before<=R_leaf<1,\n    H_chr,2: Gamma_after/Gamma_before<=R_leaf^2<1.          (23)\n\n## 5. No prefix gradient exceeds the initial gradient\n\nWe prove by induction on j that every gradient encountered inside any\noccurrence of H_chr,j has norm at most the norm at that occurrence's\nstart. This compares the actual inherited amplitude at each boundary;\nno trajectory state is reset.\n\nThe empty word has only its initial sample, and the one-step word obeys\n(18). In H_chr,j+1=(H_chr,j,1+u_j,H_chr,j), the induction hypothesis bounds\nevery sample in the left block. It suffices to show that the gradient just\nafter the middle bridge is no larger than the gradient at the start of\nthe left block. The same induction hypothesis will then apply to the\nright block with its inherited starting norm.\n\nFor j=1, combine the first line of (23) with u_1<1 and (9).\nFor j=2, combine the second line with (9) and u_2<sigma:\n\n    R_leaf^2 u_2 < (18/35)^2(29/12)=783/1225<1.\n\nFor j>=3, the middle bridge has u_j=delta c_chr^(j-1)>1. Multiply (20)\nby its generic adjacent expansion bound u_j from (9). The result is\n\n    (2/c_chr)Theta_chr^(2^j/8)<1                         (24)\n\nbecause c_chr>2 and Theta_chr<1. This completes the induction and proves\n(3) at every sample of the entire original H_chr,m.\n\nAt its terminal sample, (20) gives the stronger specific bound\n\n    Gamma_n/Gamma_0\n      <=2 delta^(-1)c_chr^(-m)Theta_chr^(N/8).             (25)\n\n## 6. Convert to the true-distance objective without a Gram inverse\n\nThe minimizer set of F is nonempty and closed. In finite dimension the\ndistance from X_0 is attained: a minimizing sequence can be restricted to\na bounded closed ball, which has a convergent subsequence. Let X_star be\na closest minimizer, so ||X_0-X_star||=R_dist. Differentiability gives\ngrad F(X_star)=0. Smoothness then gives\n\n    Gamma_0<=R_dist.                                     (26)\n\nThe exact mass of the original word is\n\n    M_chr=1+sum_(i=0)^(n-1) h_i\n         =N+a_chr(c_chr^m-N)/(c_chr-2)<c_chr^m.            (27)\n\nTo verify the equality, the number of level-k entries is 2^(m-k-1),\nand summing their geometric series gives\nsum_k 2^(m-k-1)c_chr^k=(c_chr^m-N)/(c_chr-2).\nFor the strict inequality, c_chr>sigma>2 and\nsigma(sigma-2)=1 imply c_chr(c_chr-2)>1>delta. Thus\n0<a_chr<c_chr-2. The expression in (27) is a strict convex combination\nof N and c_chr^m, and c_chr^m>N.\n\nThe actual chronological update, (3), and (26) yield\n\n    ||X_n-X_star||\n       <=R_dist+sum_i h_i Gamma_i\n       <=M_chr R_dist<c_chr^m R_dist.                    (28)\n\nConvexity at X_n gives\nF(X_n)-F_star<=<g_n,X_n-X_star><=Gamma_n||X_n-X_star||.\nUsing (25), (26), and (28),\n\n    V_chr < (4/delta)Theta_chr^(N/8)\n          < 5(99/100)^(N/8),                             (29)\n\nsince delta>=63/64 implies 4/delta<=256/63<5. This proves (4).\nEvery radius in this argument is the TRUE closest-minimizer distance.\nNo equality with b^T K^(-1)b, no unknown polar maximizer, and no chosen\nnonclosest minimizer is used. The lower estimate R_dist>=Gamma_0 is\nexplicit in (26); its loss is already included in (29).\n\nAccepted fact `b98bb807d514e80d` defines B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 using the\nproject's exact primitive/dominant recursions and proves\nB_n<=1+4n^p, p=log_2(3). Multiplication gives (5). The limit follows, for\nexample, by taking logarithms: p log n+((n+1)/8)log(99/100) tends to\nnegative infinity. Thus the conclusion is at the exact original B_n,\nnot a mass surrogate or a padded horizon.\n\n## 7. A nonempty legal family for these very weights\n\nThis paragraph verifies nonvacuity only; it is not offered as a competitive\npayoff construction. Fix the same original word and the same weights (1).\nWrite H_max=max_i h_i and set\n\n    kappa_chr=min(1,(min_i h_i-1)/4)>0,\n    eta_chr=kappa_chr/[8(H_max+kappa_chr+1)],\n    Gamma_i=eta_chr^i,\n    f_i=(kappa_chr+1/2)Gamma_i^2.                         (30)\n\nUse gradients (2) and their invertible Gram K. Define\n\n    b_i=(kappa_chr+1)Gamma_i^2+sum_(t<i)h_t K_ti,\n    X_0=sum_i (K^(-1)b)_i g_i,\n    X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t.                            (31)\n\nAll these quantities are finite explicit formulas in the predetermined\nword. There is no parameter optimization or adaptive gradient oracle.\nIn particular <g_i,X_i>=(kappa_chr+1)Gamma_i^2. If desired the common\nanchor has the character-coordinate formula\n\n    (X_0)_s=[1/(N sqrt(w_s))]sum_i (b_i/Gamma_i)chi_s(i),  (32)\n\nby finite character orthogonality. No norm of this anchor is identified\nwith the true distance.\n\nFor i<j, direct expansion of the original residuals gives\n\n    Q_ij=(kappa_chr+1-h_i)Gamma_i^2-kappa_chr Gamma_j^2\n             -sum_(i<t<j)h_t K_it-K_ij,\n\n    Q_ji=(kappa_chr+1)Gamma_j^2-kappa_chr Gamma_i^2\n             +(h_i-1)K_ij+sum_(i<t<j)h_t K_jt.            (33)\n\nHere |K_it|<=Gamma_i Gamma_t by (6). Since h_i-1>=4kappa_chr and\neta_chr<1/2, the first line of (33), divided by Gamma_i^2, is at most\n\n    -3kappa_chr+H_max eta_chr/(1-eta_chr)+eta_chr\n    <=-3kappa_chr+2(H_max+1)eta_chr\n    <=-11kappa_chr/4<0.\n\nThe second line, divided by Gamma_i^2, is at most\n\n    -kappa_chr+(kappa_chr+1)eta_chr^2\n                       +H_max eta_chr/(1-eta_chr)\n    <=-kappa_chr+(2H_max+kappa_chr+1)eta_chr\n    <=-3kappa_chr/4<0.                                  (34)\n\nSet the optimum jet to (0,0,0). Equation (31) gives exactly\nQ_(i,star)=0 and Q_(star,i)=-kappa_chr Gamma_i^2<0.\nThus every original ordered pair, including both optimum directions,\nhas been retained.\n\nFor an explicit single function let C_chr=conv(0,g_0,...,g_n) and define\n\n    F_chr(X)=max_(v in C_chr)(<X,v>-||v||^2/2).            (35)\n\nThe squared-distance minimizer P_C(X) is the unique maximizer in (35).\nThe normal condition for that minimizer is\n<X-P_C(X),v-P_C(X)><=0 for all v in C_chr. Adding this condition for\ntwo projected points proves\n||P_C(X)-P_C(Y)||^2<=<X-Y,P_C(X)-P_C(Y)>;\ntherefore projection is 1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizing\nexpressions at X and X+d then bounds the difference from\n<P_C(X),d> between 0 and ||d||^2. Hence F_chr is differentiable with\ngradient P_C and is convex and 1-smooth.\n\nAt the prescribed samples, (31) and (33) give\n\n    <X_i-g_i,g_j-g_i>=Q_ji<=0,\n    <X_i-g_i,-g_i>=-kappa_chr Gamma_i^2<0.\n\nEvery convex combination also satisfies these normal comparisons, so\nP_C(X_i)=g_i and F_chr(X_i)=f_i. Thus the actual GD trajectory of the\nONE function (35) is exactly the specified trajectory, with no free\nfunction values assigned separately to characters. The function is\nnonnegative and minimized at 0, since 0 is a vertex. Its actual initial\ndistance is positive because g_0 is nonzero. It falls under the uniform\ntrue-distance bound (29); no explicit polar-distance equality is claimed\nfor this nonvacuity example.\n\n## 8. {"id": "03abbfd5bd957e79", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T00:31:35.960378+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max4 round16: ALL positive-amplitude realizations of the explicit dependent horizon-sensitive conditional-prefix law35ed2eb7df2b2b56 have true-distance objective V<7(199/200)^((n+1)/8) on unchanged actual H_m,m>=1024. Complete self-contained UNVERIFIED candidate.", "evidence": "# The conditional-prefix sign policy has a uniform all-amplitude obstruction\n\nStatus: **self-contained unverified round-16 candidate**. No optimizer,\nparameter search, installation, or fact submission is used.\n\nThe conclusion concerns the explicitly specified DEPENDENT, horizon-dependent\nmeasure below. It allows every positive amplitude law, arbitrary adjacent\nslack, every common anchor, and every actual realizing function. It does not\nexclude other dependent measures, signed amplitudes, different label maps, or\nthe unrestricted worst-case value.\n\n## 1. Statement, actual steps, and every probability\n\nFix an integer m>=1024. Define\n\n    sigma=1+sqrt(2), delta=1-1/m,\n    c_chr=sigma(1+1/m), a_chr=delta/c_chr,\n    N=2^m, n=N-1, epsilon=1/N, p_chr=1-epsilon.\n\nThe physical step word is\n\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_chr c_chr^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.\n\nEvery subword retains these ORIGINAL enclosing-m parameters. Equivalently,\nh_i=1+a_chr c_chr^(nu_2(i+1)), 0<=i<n. Here nu_2(k) is the exponent of 2\ndividing a positive integer k. Put\n\n    t=a_chr, l=1+t, u_k=delta c_chr^(k-1) for k>=1,\n    D_chr=1+l p_chr, beta_chr=7/10.\n\nThus every leaf has physical step l, and a level-k nonleaf bridge has\nphysical step 1+u_k. No step is deleted, permuted, or padded.\n\nGive sample i its standard m-bit binary label i=(i_0,...,i_(m-1)). For a\ncharacter index s=(s_0,...,s_(m-1)) in {0,1}^m, define\n\n    chi_s(i)=(-1)^(sum_l s_l i_l),\n    B(s)=(-1)^s_0,\n    C_j(s)=(-1)^(s_1+...+s_j), 1<=j<m,\n    w_s=[(1+p_chr B(s))/2]\n             product_(j=1)^(m-1) [(1+C_j(s)(1/10-4 B(s)/5))/2].   (1)\n\nThe map s -> (B,C_1,...,C_(m-1)) is bijective: s_0 is recovered from B,\n(-1)^s_1=C_1, and (-1)^s_j=C_(j-1)C_j for j>=2. Formula (1) is therefore\nan explicit probability law. Its conditional means are\n\n    P(B=1)=1-epsilon/2, P(B=-1)=epsilon/2,\n    E[C_j | B=1]=-7/10, E[C_j | B=-1]=9/10,\n\nwith the C_j conditionally independent given B. Every finite weight is\nstrictly positive and the weights sum to one. The bits s_j themselves are\nnot independent under this law.\n\nFor arbitrary positive numbers Gamma_0,...,Gamma_n, prescribe real vectors\n\n    g_i=(Gamma_i sqrt(w_s) chi_s(i))_s.                    (2)\n\nThe conclusion also holds for any Euclidean realization of the same Gram.\nAssume these are the gradients of ONE convex differentiable 1-smooth\nfunction F with a nonempty minimizer set along the actual GD trajectory:\n\n    X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i g_i, g_i=grad F(X_i).\n\nWrite R_dist=dist(X_0,argmin F)>0 and F_star=min F. Then\n\n    Gamma_i <= (6/5) Gamma_0 for every original sample i,          (3)\n    V_chr:=2(F(X_n)-F_star)/R_dist^2\n             <7(199/200)^((n+1)/8).                             (4)\n\nConsequently, at the original exact benchmark B_n of accepted fact\n`b98bb807d514e80d`, with p_bench=log_2(3),\n\n    B_n V_chr <7(1+4n^p_bench)(199/200)^((n+1)/8) ->0.             (5)\n\nThis is an upper bound on this precise adversary family along the specified\ndyadic diagnostic words. It is not an upper bound on unrestricted W_n(H_m),\nand it does not settle the original all-h minimax problem. Failure on this\nunchanged subsequence already precludes an all-h matching claim based on\nthis policy. The conclusion holds with F=f/L and g_i=grad f(X_i)/L for any\nL>0, giving the original normalization 2(f(X_n)-f_star)/(L R_dist^2).\n\n## 2. Exact kernel and numerical constants proved analytically\n\nFor a binary difference d=i xor j, set d_m=0 and\nk(d)=sum_(r=1)^(m-1) (d_r xor d_(r+1)). Since\nchi_s(d)=B(s)^d_0 product_r C_r(s)^(d_r xor d_(r+1)), conditioning on B gives\n\n    <g_i,g_j>/(Gamma_i Gamma_j)\n      =(1-epsilon/2)(-7/10)^k(d)\n           +(epsilon/2)(-1)^d_0(9/10)^k(d).                       (6)\n\nIn particular ||g_i||=Gamma_i. At an odd-sample transition consisting of\na level-k bridge and its following leaf, the three signs, after multiplication\nby the first sign, are 1, B C_k, C_k. Thus their correlations are\n\n    w_chr=E[B C_k]=-7/10-epsilon/10,\n    p_chr=E[B]=1-epsilon,\n    z_chr=E[C_k]=-7/10+4epsilon/5.                               (7)\n\nAt two consecutive odd transitions of levels 1 and 2, the three odd signs\nare 1,C_1,C_1 C_2. The two intervening even signs are B C_1 and B C_1 C_2.\nThe additional correlations are\n\n    z2_chr=E[C_1 C_2]=49/100+4epsilon/25,\n    w2_chr=E[B C_1 C_2]=49/100-13epsilon/20.                      (8)\n\nThese statements also hold in every translated occurrence of the corresponding\nruler pattern: multiplying each character coordinate by its sign at the\nfirst odd sample cancels that prefix from every inner product. The two\nchanged prefix variables are still C_1,C_2.\n\nThe following rational bounds will be used:\n\n    0<epsilon<=1/1000000, 999/1000<delta<1,\n    413/1000<t<83/200,\n    1413/1000<l<283/200,\n    12/5<c_chr<2417/1000,\n    603/250<D_chr<483/200,\n    2413/1000<u_2<483/200.                                     (9)\n\nHere 707/500<sqrt(2)<99/70 follows by squaring positive rationals.\nThe lower bound for t follows from\nt>(70/169)(1023/1025)>413/1000, and t<sqrt(2)-1<29/70<83/200.\nThe upper bound for c_chr follows from\nc_chr<(169/70)(1025/1024)<2417/1000. The lower bound for D_chr follows\nfrom D_chr>1+(1413/1000)(999999/1000000)>603/250.\nFinally u_2=sigma(1-1/m^2), so\nu_2>(1207/500)(1-1/1024^2)>2413/1000 and u_2<169/70<483/200.\nThe remaining bounds follow directly from m>=1024, including\nepsilon=2^(-m)<2^(-20)<1/1000000. No machine search enters these bounds.\n\n## 3. Original pair constraints, with the intervening gradient retained\n\nFor the actual values f_i=F(X_i)-F_star, accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`\ngives EVERY ordered inequality\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.             (10)\n\nWe use necessary sums of these original inequalities, not a sufficient\nsurrogate. Every function in the statement must also obey all unused pairs\nand both optimum directions.\n\nAt consecutive samples with step 1+u, normalized correlation d, and norm\nratio r>0, the sum of the two ordered inequalities is\n\n    r^2+(u-1)d r-u<=0.                                      (11)\n\nFor u>0 and -1<=d<=1 this implies r<=max(1,u): the polynomial has one\npositive root; its value at 1 is (1-u)(1-d)>=0 if u<=1, and its value\nat u is u(u-1)(1+d)>=0 if u>=1. In particular every leaf has next norm\nat most its preceding norm, since t<1. This step uses no saturation.\n\nNow take one bridge of excess u=u_k and its following leaf. Let A be the\nnorm at the original odd sample before the bridge, rA the norm at the\nintervening even sample, and qA the norm at the following odd sample.\nThe leaf bound gives r>=q. The two physical updates are retained:\n\n    X_after-X_before=-(1+u)g_before-l g_middle.\n\nSumming (10) just for the two odd endpoints, and substituting (7), gives\nexactly\n\n    [Q_(before,after)+Q_(after,before)]/A^2\n      =q^2-u+(u-1)z_chr q+l r(p_chr q-w_chr).                  (12)\n\nSince p_chr q-w_chr>0, replacing r by its lower bound q makes the\nright side no larger. Using (7) then yields the necessary inequality\n\n    D_chr q^2+beta_chr(D_chr-u)q\n                    +(4epsilon/5)(u+t)q-u <=0.                (13)\n\nAll terms involving the discrepancy have their displayed signs. In\nparticular the last positive term can be dropped for a weaker NECESSARY\nbound. Define R_chr(v), for v>0, to be the positive root of\n\n    R_chr(v)^2+(7/10)(1-v)R_chr(v)-v=0.                        (14)\n\nThen every such odd-to-odd transition obeys q<=R_chr(u/D_chr).\nThis cap does not discard the middle vector: (12) is its exact contribution\nand r>=q is a proved inequality on its amplitude.\n\nWe need the following explicit consequences of (9),(14):\n\n    level 1: q<=r_low:=59/125,\n    level 2: q<=501/500,\n    level k>=3: q<eta_chr u_k/D_chr, eta_chr:=9/10.             (15)\n\nFor level 1, u_1=delta<1. At q=r_low the left side of (13) without its\npositive discrepancy term is at least\n(603/250)(r_low^2+(7/10)r_low)-1-(7/10)r_low>0.\nFor level 2, (14) and the argument for (11) give\nR_chr(u_2/D_chr)<=max(1,u_2/D_chr)<(483/200)/(603/250)<501/500.\nFor k>=3, v=u_k/D_chr is at least u_3/D_chr, which is greater than\n[(999/1000)(12/5)^2]/(483/200)>37/18. At q=(9/10)v, the polynomial\nin (14) equals v[(9/50)v-37/100]>0. This proves the last cap.\n\nFor later use the physical level-2 bridge itself satisfies\n\n    norm_after_bridge <=(15/7) norm_before_bridge.             (16)\n\nIndeed its correlation is w_chr. In (11), at r=15/7, its polynomial is\nbounded below by\n(15/7)^2-(283/200)(7/10+1/10000000)(15/7)-483/200>0,\nusing u_2-1<283/200 and -w_chr<=7/10+1/10000000.\n\n## 4. A three-odd-sample cycle forces a fixed loss for ALL amplitudes\n\nConsider consecutive odd samples before and after a level-1 transition and\nthen a level-2 transition. At the first H_3 occurrence these are the ORIGINAL\nsamples 1,3,5, with ORIGINAL intervening even samples 2,4. Use temporary\nnames O_0,O_1,O_2 for these odd samples and E_1,E_2 for the two even samples.\nTheir gradient norms are\n\n    ||g_(O_0)||=A,\n    ||g_(O_1)||=r A, ||g_(O_2)||=q A,\n    ||g_(E_1)||=a A, ||g_(E_2)||=b A.                          (17)\n\nThe already derived original comparisons give\n\n    a>=r, q<= (501/500)r, r<=59/125,\n    b<= (15/7)r,\n    q<= (501/500)(59/125)<473/1000.                            (18)\n\nLet u=u_1=delta and v=u_2. Form one ORIGINAL directed cycle\n\n    T_cyc=Q_(O_0,O_2)+Q_(O_1,O_0)+Q_(O_2,O_1).                (19)\n\nAll values and the common anchor cancel. The two actual displacements are\n\n    X_(O_1)-X_(O_0)=-(1+u)g_(O_0)-l g_(E_1),\n    X_(O_2)-X_(O_1)=-(1+v)g_(O_1)-l g_(E_2).\n\nExpanding (19) with these displacements and (7),(8) gives EXACTLY\n\n    T_cyc/A^2 = -u+r^2+q^2+(u-v-1)z_chr r\n                +v z_chr r q-z2_chr q\n                +l a(p_chr r-w_chr)+l b(p_chr q-w2_chr).     (20)\n\nThe intervening vectors have not been merged or identified with an odd\ngradient. Both a and b remain in this identity. By (9),(18),\n\n    p_chr r-w_chr>0,\n    p_chr q-w2_chr<473/1000-(49/100-13/20000000)<0.\n\nTherefore a>=r and b<=(15/7)r imply\n\n    T_cyc/A^2 >= -u+D_chr r^2+q^2+C_chr r+E_chr r q-z2_chr q,\n    C_chr=(u-v-1)z_chr-l w_chr-(15/7)l w2_chr,\n    E_chr=v z_chr+(15/7)l p_chr.                            (21)\n\nThe constants obey\n\n    C_chr>119/100, E_chr>4/3, z2_chr<491/1000.               (22)\n\nFor C_chr, since u<=1, (7),(8) give the lower bound\n\n    C_chr >= v(7/10-4epsilon/5)-(7/20)l\n             > (2413/1000)(7/10-4/5000000)\n                         -(7/20)(283/200)>119/100.\n\nThe discarded epsilon contribution in -w_chr-(15/7)w2_chr is positive;\nits zero-epsilon value is -7/20. For E_chr,\n\n    E_chr >= -(7/10)v+(15/7)l(1-epsilon)\n             >-(7/10)(483/200)\n               +(15/7)(1413/1000)(999999/1000000)>4/3.\n\nThe last bound in (22) follows directly from (8),(9).\n\nSuppose q>=2/5. By (18), r>=(500/501)q>(499/500)q. Dropping only\npositive quantities in (21),(22) gives\n\n    T_cyc/A^2 >= P_chr(q),\n    P_chr(q)=[(12/5)(499/500)^2+1+(4/3)(499/500)]q^2\n                +[(119/100)(499/500)-491/1000]q-1.           (23)\n\nBoth displayed coefficients are positive, so P_chr increases for q>=0.\nDirect rational arithmetic at q=2/5 gives\n\n    P_chr(2/5)=1594697/46875000 >3/100.                     (24)\n\nThus T_cyc>3 A^2/100. At least one of the THREE original ordered residuals\nin (19) is greater than A^2/100. This contradicts (10), regardless of the\nanchor and of every assigned function value. Every legal realization must\ntherefore satisfy the strict compound cap\n\n    Gamma_(O_2)/Gamma_(O_0)<2/5.                           (25)\n\nThis argument allowed arbitrary nonsaturating amplitudes and all the\nslack they could supply at BOTH intervening leaves. It is a direct\nobstruction in the actual primal Gram, not an imported tensor-dual sign\ncondition or an assumption of bounded conditioning.\n\n## 5. Pay the total loss over the entire original word\n\nIt is convenient to index the odd samples by r, writing\nO_r=Gamma_(2r+1), 0<=r<N/2. Between O_r and O_(r+1) there are exactly\nthe original bridge of level 1+nu_2(r+1) and its following leaf. This is\nindexing only: all the underlying gradients remain in (12),(20).\n\nLet J_0 be the empty list and J_j=(J_(j-1),j,J_(j-1)). The list of these\nodd-transition levels is J_(m-1). A block J_2=(1,2,1) first obeys (25)\nand then the level-1 cap in (15), so its total amplitude ratio is less than\n\n    Q_chr=(2/5)(59/125)=118/625.                            (26)\n\nFor any occurrence of J_(j-1), j>=3, multiply this bound over its\n2^(j-3) disjoint J_2 blocks, and the last bound in (15) over the remaining\nlevels k>=3. The exact counts are\n\n    sum_(k=3)^(j-1)2^(j-k-1)=2^(j-3)-1,\n    sum_(k=3)^(j-1)(k-1)2^(j-k-1)=3*2^(j-3)-j.              (27)\n\nThese follow by induction in j, starting at j=3 where both sums are empty.\nSince u_k=delta c_chr^(k-1), the resulting bound is\n\n    O_after/O_before <= L_j,\n    L_j=[D_chr/(eta_chr delta)] c_chr^(-j)\n                            Theta_chr^(2^j/8),\n    Theta_chr=Q_chr eta_chr delta c_chr^3/D_chr.             (28)\n\nNo overlapping transition is charged twice. In particular the first leaf\nof the ORIGINAL H_j is outside this odd-transition block and only contributes\nan extra factor at most one.\n\nThe bounds (9) imply\n\n    0<Theta_chr\n      < (118/625)(9/10)(2417/1000)^3/(603/250)\n      <199/200.                                           (29)\n\nThis is an explicit rational inequality; delta<1 was dropped upwards.\nFormula (28) quantifies the TOTAL loss, including every repeated low block\nand every high bridge of the unchanged original horizon. In particular\n\n    Gamma_n/Gamma_0\n      <=[D_chr/(eta_chr delta)]c_chr^(-m)\n                                  Theta_chr^(N/8).         (30)\n\n## 6. Prefix control includes the even samples\n\nFirst every odd sample has norm at most Gamma_0. To prove this, induct on\nthe recursive list J_j. For J_1 the cap is r_low<1. For J_2, the norm after\nits middle level-2 transition is at most r_low(501/500) times its start,\nwhich is less than one, and the two level-1 edges contract. For j>=3,\nthe left J_(j-1) has norm ratio at most L_j by (28). Its following level-j\ntransition has norm ratio less than eta_chr delta c_chr^(j-1)/D_chr.\nTheir product is\n\n    c_chr^(-1) Theta_chr^(2^j/8)<1.                        (31)\n\nThe induction hypothesis controls every prefix in the left block; (31)\ncontrols the starting norm of the right block, where the same induction\nhypothesis applies. Initially O_0<=Gamma_0 by the first leaf comparison.\nThis proves the claim for ALL original odd samples with inherited state.\n\nEvery remaining even sample except X_0 lies immediately after one bridge.\nFor a level-1 bridge, (11) gives even norm at most the preceding odd norm,\nsince u_1<1. For a level-2 bridge the preceding J_1 supplies a factor r_low;\n(11) gives even norm at most u_2 r_low Gamma_0, with\n\n    u_2 r_low<(483/200)(59/125)<6/5.\n\nFor a level-j bridge with j>=3, the preceding J_(j-1) and (11),(28) give\n\n    even norm/Gamma_0 <=u_j L_j\n       =[D_chr/(eta_chr c_chr)]Theta_chr^(2^j/8)\n       <(483/200)/[(9/10)(12/5)]<6/5.                     (32)\n\nAt every occurrence the starting odd norm is already at most Gamma_0.\nTogether these estimates prove (3), including every original intermediate\ngradient that was not an endpoint in the cycle calculation.\n\n## 7. True closest-minimizer normalization\n\nThe minimizer set is nonempty and closed. Its distance from X_0 is attained:\na minimizing sequence may be restricted to a bounded closed ball in the\nfinite-dimensional Euclidean space and hence has a convergent subsequence.\nTake a closest minimizer X_star, without assuming it is the origin. Since\ngrad F(X_star)=0, smoothness implies\n\n    Gamma_0<=R_dist.                                      (33)\n\nThe exact original mass is\n\n    M_chr=1+sum_(i<n)h_i\n         =N+a_chr(c_chr^m-N)/(c_chr-2)<c_chr^m.             (34)\n\nThe equality follows by counting the 2^(m-k-1) level-k steps, including\nleaves, and summing the geometric progression. The inequality follows from\nc_chr>sigma>2 and c_chr(c_chr-2)>sigma(sigma-2)=1>delta,\nso 0<a_chr<c_chr-2. Thus the displayed expression is a strict convex\ncombination of N and c_chr^m, the latter being larger.\n\nUsing (3),(33), the actual chronological updates give\n\n    ||X_n-X_star|| <=R_dist+sum_(i<n)h_i Gamma_i\n       <=[1+(6/5)sum_(i<n)h_i] R_dist\n       <=(6/5)M_chr R_dist<(6/5)c_chr^m R_dist.             (35)\n\nConvexity at X_n gives F(X_n)-F_star<=<g_n,X_n-X_star>.\nCombine (30),(33),(35) to obtain\n\n    V_chr < (12/5)[D_chr/(eta_chr delta)]Theta_chr^(N/8)\n      <7(199/200)^(N/8),                                 (36)\n\nbecause (12/5)(483/200)/[(9/10)(999/1000)]<7. This proves (4).\nEvery radius in this argument is the TRUE distance. If F is an anchored\nprojection interpolant its minimizers form the appropriate polar cone;\nX_star may be nonzero. No inverse-Gram expression for the radius, forced\npositive-cone anchor, or origin-distance substitute is used. The cost of\nthe conservative bound R_dist>=Gamma_0 and the path estimate is visible\nin the finite prefactor in (36).\n\nAccepted fact `b98bb807d514e80d` gives B_n<=1+4n^log_2(3). This proves (5).\nFor completeness its right side tends to zero because the logarithm of\nits exponential factor is ((n+1)/8)log(199/200), whereas the logarithm of\nits polynomial factor is O(log n). This keeps the original B_n and horizon.\n\n## 8. Scope and proof status\n\nThe proposed low-bit discrepancy epsilon=1/N does NOT cure the fixed\nconditional-prefix policy (1), even if one chooses an arbitrary positive\nnonsaturating amplitude law. The obstruction does not use continuity at\na singular Gram: every finite measure has all weights positive, every\nfinite trajectory must satisfy (10), and the same explicit constants work\nfor every original m>=1024. No condition-number assumption is made.\n\nThis is a quantified necessary-condition obstruction. It need not choose an\nanchor or manufacture another weak feasible family: it bounds every actual\nlegal anchor, amplitude law, and one-function realization of this measure.\nIn particular an illegal amplitude choice is not being used as a witness.\nThe earlier exponentially weak nonvacuity construction is not repeated or\nused as a premise.
