{"id": "edcfcdfc38e4e5b4", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T00:35:53.606147+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C68 COMPLETE UNVERIFIED global quartic-family exclusion and retained-cost budget. For every actual H_m with m>=512 and EVERY r_0!=0,v_0>=0 on the one global phi(r)=r^4/4 epigraph, arbitrary turn histories satisfy R_turn(t)<=c^(-popcount(t))R_0, R_t<=R_0, an explicit total J charge, and 2F_4(X_n)/R_0^2<=3/(8S_m). Consequently exact B_n times payoff is <625/1536<1/2, with uniform limsup <=3(1+sqrt(2))/(8e)<3/8. This is a full proper-family bound, not a W_n upper bound or original-target resolution; no fact_submit authorized or called.", "evidence": "# C68: a global chronological budget for the actual quartic epigraph\n\nStatus: COMPLETE UNVERIFIED candidate, fifth launch round 1 (cumulative 12).\nNo fact_submit, trajectory evaluation, numerical optimization, installation,\nor new allocation was used. The only accepted mathematical predecessor is\nb98bb807d514e80d, used for the exact benchmark recursions. All function,\ntrajectory, block, and combinatorial assertions used below are proved here.\nC65, C66, and C67 are context, not proof premises.\n\n## 1. Definitions and result\n\nThroughout this document the power is FIXED at 4. For each integer m>=512 set\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/m,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,\n    d_j=a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.\n\nThe H_j here are local schedule words, not the conjugate quadratics called\nH_j in a different accepted fact. Every embedded word uses this SAME a,c.\nIndex physical steps by t=1,...,n. Let nu_2(t) be the exponent of 2 in t and\nb(t) the number of 1 bits in the binary expansion of t, with b(0)=0. Then\n\n    h_t=1+d_(nu_2(t)).                                     (1)\n\nDefine the single global function and its state coordinates by\n\n    K_4={(r,s) in R^2:s>=r^4/4},\n    F_4(X)=max_(g in K_4) (<X,g>-||g||^2/2),\n    eta=v+r^4/4, u=eta r^2=vr^2+r^6/4,\n    X(r,v)=(r(1+u),-v), G(r)=(r,r^4/4), v>=0.\n\nHere r,v,u,eta and X,G denote local state variables. Write x for the\nhorizontal coordinate of X and R=|x|. Start from arbitrary r_0!=0 and\nv_0>=0, and run the given H_m on this same F_4:\n\n    X_t=X_(t-1)-h_t grad F_4(X_(t-1)), 1<=t<=n.\n\nSection 2 proves global legality, the unique actual recurrence, and\n\n    min F_4=0, argmin F_4={(0,y):y<=0},\n    R_0=dist(X_0,argmin F_4)=|r_0|(1+u_0),\n    2F_4(X(r,v))=r^2+(3/2)v r^4+(7/16)r^8.               (2)\n\nA turn is a step t with x_(t-1)x_t<0. The following bounds hold jointly\nfor EVERY such initial state, without restricting the number or levels of\nturns:\n\n    R_t<=R_0 for every t,\n    R_t<=c^(-b(t))R_0 at every turn t,                    (3)\n    P_4:=2F_4(X_n)/R_0^2 <=3/(8S_m).                     (4)\n\nThe retained clock cost, including the nonnegative contributions at every\nstep, is\n\n    J_all=(1/2)sum_(t=1)^n |r_(t-1)|^2(v_t-v_(t-1))\n         =(1/8)sum_(t=1)^n h_t |r_(t-1)|^6.\n\nIt satisfies the explicit global charge\n\n    J_all <= min{\n       |r_0|^2(v_n-v_0)/2,\n       R_0 |r_0|^5 [2(1+1/c)^m-1]/8\n    },                                                   (5)\n\nand, using only the original closest-minimizer distance,\n\n    J_all <=4^(5/7) R_0^(12/7)[2(1+1/c)^m-1]/8.           (6)\n\nNo assertion that J_all vanishes, or that a logarithmic clock decreases\nacross zero, is made. The quantitative objective bound (4) uses the\nchronological block budget directly; it does not delete J_all.\n\nFor the EXACT Zhang--Jiang denominator B_n defined in accepted fact\nb98bb807d514e80d, Section 8 proves\n\n    B_(2^m-1) P_4 <625/1536<1/2       for all m>=512,      (7)\n    limsup_(m->infinity) sup_(r_0!=0,v_0>=0)\n        B_(2^m-1) P_4 <=3lambda/(8e)<3/8.                (8)\n\nThis excludes the whole stated quartic epigraph family as a matching\nadversary on these schedules. It is NOT an upper bound for unrestricted\nW_n and does not settle the original all-schedule profile problem.\n\n## 2. The global function, ordered pairs, distance, and physical recursion\n\nK_4 is nonempty, closed, and convex. Completing the square in the defining\nmaximum shows that its unique maximizing g is the Euclidean projection\nPi_(K_4)(X). The normal inequalities at projections of X and Y give\n\n    ||Pi_(K_4)(X)-Pi_(K_4)(Y)||^2\n       <=<Pi_(K_4)(X)-Pi_(K_4)(Y),X-Y>.\n\nThus the projection is 1-Lipschitz. Comparing the defining maxima gives\n\n    0<=F_4(Y)-F_4(X)-<Pi_(K_4)(X),Y-X>\n      <=<Pi_(K_4)(Y)-Pi_(K_4)(X),Y-X><=||Y-X||^2.\n\nIt follows that F_4 is differentiable with gradient Pi_(K_4), hence has\n1-Lipschitz gradient. Convexity follows from its supremum of affine\nfunctions representation.\n\nFor phi(r)=r^4/4, one has\n\n    X(r,v)-G(r)=eta*(phi'(r),-1).\n\nFor any (z,s) in K_4 its inner product with (z,s)-G(r) is at most\n\n    eta*[phi'(r)(z-r)-phi(z)+phi(r)]<=0.\n\nThe last inequality is the tangent inequality for the convex function\nphi. This proves grad F_4(X(r,v))=G(r). Substitution into the maximum\nproves the value in (2). In particular, if f_i=F_4(X_i), every ordered\npair of states in this construction has the exact residual\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2\n        =<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>\n        =eta_j*[r_j^3(r_i-r_j)-r_i^4/4+r_j^4/4]<=0.      (9)\n\nThere is no restriction on temporal separation in (9).\n\nIf x=0 and y<=0, the maximum is zero: zero is feasible, and every feasible\ng=(r,s) has s>=0, so ys-||g||^2/2<=0. If x!=0, test g=(r,r^4/4)\nwith sufficiently small r of the sign of x; the positive term xr\ndominates the terms of order 2 or higher. If x=0 and y>0, a sufficiently\nsmall g=(0,s), s>0, gives positive value. This proves the exact minimizer\nset in (2). Its closest point to (x,-v), v>=0, is (0,-v), proving the\ntrue distance formula without retaining a spurious initial vertical norm.\n\nThe actual GD step of size h>=0 is exactly\n\n    v_plus=v+h r^4/4,\n    x_plus=x-hr,\n    x_plus=T_(v_plus)(r_plus),\n    T_v(r)=r+v r^3+r^7/4.                               (10)\n\nT_v is odd and strictly increasing onto R, since\n\n    T_v'(r)=1+3vr^2+7r^6/4>=1.\n\nIt therefore determines the unique actual r_plus. In particular\nsign(r)=sign(x). If r reaches zero, x=0 and G(r)=0, so the trajectory is\nabsorbed with zero subsequent objective and clock cost.\n\nOn any interval where all states are nonzero and have the same sign,\nR decreases and v increases. Equation (10) then shows that |r| decreases.\nReflection allows every such interval to be treated on the positive ray.\n\nFor later use, smoothness and the minimizer (0,-v) give 2F_4(X)<=R^2.\nThis can also be checked from the explicit state formula: with z=r^6/4,\n\n    2F_4(X)/r^2=1+(3/2)u+z/4,\n    R^2/r^2=(1+u)^2,\n\nand their difference is u/2+u^2-z/4>=u/4+u^2>=0 because 0<=z<=u.\n\n## 3. A no-turn block pays for the loss of normal state\n\nConsider any actual occurrence of H_l, l>=1, along which every state is\nnonzero and has the same sign. No entry v is reset. Let r>0 and u>0 be\nthe terminal gradient magnitude and effective normal state, and let\nrho>0 and u_mid be their counterparts immediately BEFORE the middle\nstep 1+d_(l-1). Since that step does not turn or annihilate,\n\n    u_mid>d_(l-1).                                      (11)\n\nMonotonicity on this interval gives rho>=r and v_mid<=v_end. Consequently\n\n    u_mid=v_mid rho^2+rho^6/4\n          <=(rho/r)^6 (v_end r^2+r^6/4)\n          =(rho/r)^6 u.                                (12)\n\nLet Apre_l=S_(l-1)+1+d_(l-1)=M_(l-1)+d_(l-1) be the sum of steps\nthrough the middle step. Every gradient used by those steps has\nmagnitude at least rho; every later gradient has magnitude at least r.\nTelescoping the actual horizontal motion, with R_entry its input radius,\ntherefore gives\n\n    R_entry/r\n      >=M_l+u+Apre_l*((d_(l-1)/u)^(1/6)-1)_+.            (13)\n\nHere (z)_+=max(z,0). If u>=d_(l-1)/2, the explicit u in (13) is at\nleast d_(l-1)/2. If u<d_(l-1)/2, then\n\n    (d_(l-1)/u)^(1/6)>2^(1/6)>11/10,\n\nbecause (11/10)^6=1771561/1000000<2. Since Apre_l>=d_(l-1), the\nlast term in (13) exceeds d_(l-1)/10. Thus in both cases\n\n    R_entry >=Aend_l |r_end|,\n    Aend_l:=M_l+d_(l-1)/10.                             (14)\n\nFor the empty word define Aend_0=1; the same gradient bound is the state\nidentity R=|r|(1+u)>=|r|. If an endpoint is absorbed, the gradient bound\nwith its zero terminal gradient holds as well.\n\nThis is the key replacement for a frozen-normal argument. A decrease in\nterminal u is permitted. Equation (12) charges that decrease to earlier\ngradient magnitudes, and equation (13) charges those magnitudes to the\nTRUE horizontal entry distance. No inequality u_end>=u_mid is used.\n\n## 4. Uniform capacity of every no-turn H_l in the enclosing word\n\nThe word recursion implies M_(l+1)=2M_l+d_l and M_0=1. Set\n\n    A_mass=a/(c-2).\n\nSolving this elementary recurrence gives\n\n    M_l=A_mass c^l+(1-A_mass)2^l.                        (15)\n\nSince lambda(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda, one has c(c-2)>1>1-epsilon.\nIt follows that 0<A_mass<1. In particular,\n\n    M_l/d_l>=1/(c-2).                                  (16)\n\nFor m>=512, sqrt(2)<99/70 gives\n\n    c<(169/70)(513/512)<121/50.\n\nThe function z-1/(z-2)-1/(10z) is strictly increasing for z>2. At\nz=121/50 its value is -289/127050<0. Therefore\n\n    1/(c-2)+1/(10c)>c.\n\nCombining this with (14) and (16) proves\n\n    Aend_l>=c d_l=a c^(l+1)        for l>=1.             (17)\n\nFor l=0 the same conclusion holds because\n\n    Aend_0=1>ac=1-1/m.                                  (18)\n\nEquations (17)--(18) are uniform over the block position, initial state,\nand l. They use the enclosing a,c throughout.\n\n## 5. A binary interval lemma and chronological turn accounting\n\nWe first prove the combinatorial statement required to combine real\none-sign intervals. If integers 0<=s<t satisfy\n\n    q=b(t-1)-b(s)>=1,\n\nthen [s,t] contains an aligned closed dyadic interval [A,A+2^q], where\nA is a multiple of 2^q.\n\nProof. Put B=2^q, write s=kB+r with 0<=r<B, and suppose no such\ninterval is contained. If r=0, the first possible interval starts at s,\nso t<s+B. Then t-1 has high binary part k and a low part at most B-2;\nhence b(t-1)-b(s)<=q-1, a contradiction.\n\nIf r>0, the first possible start is (k+1)B, so t<(k+2)B. If t-1\nhas high part k, its low part has at most q ones, whereas b(r)>=1,\nagain giving difference at most q-1. If t-1 has high part k+1, its\nlow part is at most B-2 and has at most q-1 ones. Adding 1 to k\nincreases its bit count by at most 1. Thus the difference is at most\n1+(q-1)-b(r)<=q-1. This contradiction proves the lemma.             (19)\n\nAn aligned interval [A,A+2^q] corresponds to the actual subword H_q\nat physical step indices A+1,...,A+2^q-1: for 0<j<2^q,\nnu_2(A+j)=nu_2(j). This uses the same physical a,c.\n\nNow let s<t be consecutive actual turn times, allowing s=0 for the\ninitial boundary before the first turn. The steps s+1,...,t-1 form\none same-sign interval. Set\n\n    q=max{0,b(t-1)-b(s)}, ell=nu_2(t).\n\nIf q>=1, (19) finds an actual no-turn H_q inside this interval. Its\nentry horizontal radius is at most R_s, and its terminal gradient\nmagnitude is at least |r_(t-1)|, by monotonicity on the entire interval.\nEquations (14) and (17) therefore give\n\n    R_s>=Aend_q |r_(t-1)|>=a c^(q+1)|r_(t-1)|.           (20)\n\nFor q=0, use R_s>=|r_(t-1)| and (18) to get the same result. At the\nactual turn t, equation (10) gives\n\n    R_t=(d_ell-u_(t-1))|r_(t-1)|\n        <=a c^ell |r_(t-1)|\n        <=c^(ell-q-1)R_s.                              (21)\n\nThe elementary binary identity\n\n    b(t-1)=b(t)-1+nu_2(t)\n\nand q>=b(t-1)-b(s) imply\n\n    R_t<=c^(b(s)-b(t))R_s.                              (22)\n\nStarting at s=0 and telescoping (22) over the ACTUAL turns proves the\nsecond part of (3). Between turns the radius decreases, proving the\nfirst part of (3). This argument allows arbitrary repeated rises and\nfalls in turn level. It neither optimizes right copies independently\nnor assumes that u is monotone.\n\n## 6. Global payment of the retained moving-normal cost\n\nFor context, on a positive one-sign interval define the local clock\n\n    C_4(r,v)=log r+(3/2)vr^2+(7/24)r^6,\n    Psi(x,v)=C_4(T_v^(-1)(x),v), x>0.\n\nDifferentiating T_v(r)=x gives\n\n    partial_x Psi=1/r, partial_v Psi=r^2/2.\n\nFor one positive noncrossing step, first move horizontally at the old v\nand then increase v at the new horizontal coordinate. Monotonicity of\nT_v^(-1) bounds the two resulting integrals and yields\n\n    C_4(r_plus,v_plus)-C_4(r,v)\n       <=-h+(r^2/2)(v_plus-v).                          (23)\n\nReflection covers a negative one-sign interval. This derivation retains\nthe positive cost, and does not extend the logarithm across annihilation\nor sign changes.\n\nThe global radius result (3), together with v_t>=v_0 and monotonicity of\nT_v on the positive ray, implies\n\n    |r_t|<=|r_0|  for every t.                           (24)\n\nThe first bound in (5) now follows by summing the defining weighted\nvertical increments of J_all.\n\nTo obtain the second bound, set TV_x=sum_(t=1)^n h_t|r_(t-1)|, the\ntotal absolute horizontal displacement. At a nonturn step (including\none that reaches zero), its contribution is R_(t-1)-R_t. At a turn it\nis R_(t-1)+R_t. Consequently the exact telescoping identity is\n\n    TV_x=R_0-R_n+2 sum_(t a turn) R_t.                   (25)\n\nUse (3), and expand the product over the m independent binary digits:\n\n    sum_(t=0)^(2^m-1)c^(-b(t))=(1+1/c)^m.\n\nIt follows that\n\n    TV_x<=R_0[2(1+1/c)^m-1].                            (26)\n\nSince J_all=(1/8)sum h_t|r_(t-1)|*|r_(t-1)|^5, (24)--(26) prove\nthe second bound in (5). Finally R_0>=|r_0|^7/4 by the state formula,\nso R_0|r_0|^5<=4^(5/7)R_0^(12/7), proving (6).\n\nThus the sum of all one-sign J costs is bounded by (5)--(6), since it\nis no larger than J_all. The bounds retain the initial radius and,\nin the first version, the actual terminal vertical state. They remain\nvalid for trajectories that annihilate; their subsequent contributions\nare zero. No lower bound on nonzero |r_t| is needed.\n\n## 7. Terminal payoff, with every entry-radius factor retained\n\nConsider any nonzero same-sign interval with total step mass T>=1/6.\nWrite r>0 for its terminal gradient magnitude and u=vr^2+r^6/4 for\nits terminal normal state. The actual recursion, without resetting v,\ngives\n\n    R_entry>=r(T+1+u),\n    v_end>=T r^4/4.\n\nPut z=r^6/4. Then z<=u/(T+1), and (2) implies\n\n    2F_4(X_end)/R_entry^2\n       <=[1+k_T u]/(T+1+u)^2,\n    k_T=3/2+1/[4(T+1)].\n\nThe derivative of the last rational function has the sign of\nk_T(T+1)-2-k_T u. Since T>=1/6, its maximum on u>=0 is attained at\nu=T+1-2/k_T and equals\n\n    E_4(T)=(6T+7)^2/[48(T+1)^2(2T+1)]<=3/(8T).          (27)\n\nFor the last comparison the numerator of the difference, over denominator\n48T(T+1)^2(2T+1), is 6T^2+23T+18>0. An absorbing terminal state has\nzero payoff and satisfies the desired bound separately.\n\nIf the full run is nonabsorbed, let s be its last actual turn, or let\ns=0 if it has no turns. Set b=b(s) and q=m-b. Apply (19) with\nt=2^m. The final same-sign interval contains an actual H_q. If q>=1,\nits full mass T is at least S_q>=S_1>1, so (3) and (27) give\n\n    P_4<=c^(-2b)*3/(8S_q).                              (28)\n\nThe factor c^(-2b) in (28) is the ACTUAL entry-radius ratio bounded in\nterms of the original R_0. A later block has not been renormalized as a\nfresh problem.\n\nFor j>=1, (15) gives\n\n    S_(j+1)-c S_j=c-1-(c-2)(1-A_mass)2^j<=c-1.\n\nSince S_j>1 and c>1, this implies\n\n    S_(j+1)<(2c-1)S_j<c^2 S_j.\n\nIterating yields S_m<=c^(2b)S_q, so (28) is at most 3/(8S_m).\n\nIf q=0, then b(s)=m and s=n, so the last step turns. Use the terminal\nstate bound 2F_4<=R_n^2 and (3) to obtain P_4<=c^(-2m). Since (15)\ngives S_m<c^m and c^m>8/3 for m>=512, this is smaller than 3/(8S_m).\nThis proves (4) for every initial state, including all turn patterns.\n\n## 8. The exact benchmark and the uniform comparison\n\nAccepted fact b98bb807d514e80d defines the exact primitive and dominant\nbenchmark recursions. In their mass coordinates C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and\nB_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 they are\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    C_n=max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(C_k,C_l),\n    B_n=max_(k+l=n-1) G(C_k,B_l),\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.\n\nWe prove directly from these recursions that\n\n    B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1).                             (29)\n\nSet alpha=2lambda-1=lambda(4-lambda) and\nLlin=(4x+lambda^2 y)/alpha. Both coefficients in Llin exceed 1, so\nLlin>=x+y. Direct expansion, using lambda^2=2lambda+1, gives\n\n    alpha^2[Llin^2-(x+y)Llin-xy]\n      =(2x/lambda-sqrt(2)lambda y)^2>=0.\n\nThus Llin>=Omega(x,y), the positive root of z^2-(x+y)z-xy. At\nz=lambda Omega(x,y), the polynomial with larger root G(x,lambda y) is\n\n    z^2-(4x+lambda y)z+4x^2\n      =x[4x+lambda^2 y-alpha Omega(x,y)]>=0.\n\nAlso z>=lambda(x+y)>=(4x+lambda y)/2, so z is to the right of that\npolynomial's vertex. Hence G(x,lambda y)<=lambda Omega(x,y).\nInduction in the exact recursions proves B_n<=lambda C_n.\n\nPut gamma=log_2(lambda)>1, D(t)=(1+t)^gamma-1, and\nell(z)=log D(exp z). For t=exp z, differentiation gives\n\n    ell''(z)=gamma*t*(1+t)^(gamma-2)\n      *[(1+t)^gamma-1-gamma*t]/D(t)^2>0.\n\nThe last bracket has value zero at t=0 and positive derivative for t>0.\nConvexity therefore implies D(t)D(1/t)>=D(1)^2=2. For x,y>0 set\nZ=(x^(1/gamma)+y^(1/gamma))^gamma. Taking t=(y/x)^(1/gamma) shows\n(Z-x)(Z-y)>=2xy. Thus Z^2-(x+y)Z-xy>=0; also Z>=x+y, by convexity\nof the positive power. Consequently Omega(x,y)<=Z. The zero cases\nfollow by direct substitution. Induction yields C_n<=(n+1)^gamma,\nwhich, with B_n<=lambda C_n, proves (29).\n\nFor a finite comparison, c<121/50 and m>=512 imply\n\n    A_mass=(1-1/m)/[c(c-2)]\n      >(511/512)/(2541/2500)>49/50.\n\nSince c>2 and m>=512, c^(-m)<1/50. Equation (15) therefore gives\n\n    S_m/c^m>24/25.\n\nThe first three binomial terms show\n\n    (1+1/m)^m>=2+(m-1)/(2m)>=12/5,\n\nwhere the final inequality already holds for m>=5. Combining (4),\n(29), lambda<5/2, and (lambda/c)^m<=5/12 gives\n\n    B_n P_4\n       <(3/8)*(5/2)*(5/12)*(25/24)\n       =625/1536<1/2,\n\nproving (7).\n\nAs m tends to infinity, A_mass tends to 1, c tends to lambda, and\n\n    S_m/c^m=A_mass+(1-A_mass)(2/c)^m-c^(-m) ->1,\n    (lambda/c)^m=(1+1/m)^(-m) ->1/e.\n\nEvery bound above is uniform over r_0!=0,v_0>=0, so (4) and (29)\ngive (8). Finally e>1+1+1/2=5/2>lambda, proving 3lambda/(8e)<3/8.\nNo limiting formula for the exact sequence B_n has been assumed.\n\n## 9. Scope, literature, and symbolic audit\n\nThe proof addresses the whole unscaled quartic epigraph model stated in\nSection 1, at all initial horizontal radii and nonnegative vertical\nstates, including horizon-dependent choices. Its global charge and\npayoff combine arbitrary actual turn histories. Lower-level reactivation\nis allowed. This is a proper-family exclusion, not a conclusion about\nall functions, other powers, or the original all-h target.\n\nThe projection background is consistent with Propositions 3.2--3.3 of\nChierchia, Pustelnik, Pesquet, and Pesquet-Popescu, \"Epigraphical splitting\nfor solving constrained convex formulations of inverse problems with\nproximal tools\", arXiv:1210.5844v3. Their Hilbert product is Euclidean;\ntheir scalar generator has full domain and is proper closed convex;\nbeta=4, tau=1/4, zeta=-v yield T_v(r)=x. The exact locally downloaded\npaper's definitions and projection proof were read. No external result\nfrom it is used as a proof premise. The dedicated theorem searches for\nthe moving-state budget and the binary interval lemma returned no usable\nresult; both have been proved directly here.\n\nSymbolic boundary checks:\n\n* Consecutive actual turns have an empty intervening H_0. The necessary\n  charge is exactly Aend_0>=ac; no unqueried lower-level step is invented.\n* The aligned case [s,t]=[A,A+2^q] uses the full actual H_q, with no\n  missing first or last step and no change of enclosing parameters.\n* Negative binary-count differences are handled by q=max(0,difference)\n  in (20)--(22), not by a fictitious word of negative length.\n* A turn at the final step has q=0 and uses the smoothness bound, not\n  formula (27) at zero mass.\n* Exact zero is absorbing. No logarithm is evaluated there, and the\n  main payoff proof uses no logarithm at all.\n* Very small nonzero gradients cause no reciprocal estimate with an\n  uncontrolled constant; (13) charges small terminal u explicitly.\n* The proof does not freeze v, assume monotonicity of u, or discard the\n  positive moving-normal cost. The complete all-pair function and true\n  denominator were established before the block argument.\n\nThe separate possible concave-clock integral explored in rough local\nnotes is unnecessary here and is not a premise. No verification success\nis asserted until an authorized fact_submit actually accepts this joint\nstatement and proof.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"branch_id": "C68", "plan_id": "6d57760a2c1afc36", "attempt_type": "direct", "status": "complete_unverified", "record_type": "complete_joint_candidate", "artifact": "research/c68_quartic_global_chronological_budget.md", "artifact_sha256": "b278c6a6de8eafc9af8fb7b9b64d37ab173ed65c1516df035d6fa39642107acc", "predecessors": ["b98bb807d514e80d"], "context_only_findings": ["c91fb520fcf58307", "158db4315a158fe4"], "key_mechanism": "Actual no-turn block charges terminal-normal loss to prior gradient drop; dyadic interval lemma telescopes all actual crossings with the original radius.", "counterexample_search_for_stuck_subgoal": {"performed": true, "result": "not_refuted", "summary": "Symbolic boundary audit: consecutive turns use H0, aligned gaps use the full actual Hq, negative bit differences are truncated to zero, final-turn suffix and exact annihilation handled separately. No numeric trajectory."}, "verification_delta_calls": 0, "numerical_delta_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"F_4": "Global smooth convex conjugate of ||g||^2/2 plus the indicator of the quartic epigraph, explicitly defined in the evidence.", "R_t": "Absolute horizontal coordinate; R_0 is the true closest-minimizer distance.", "J_all": "Full retained positive cost (1/2) sum |r_(t-1)|^2(v_t-v_(t-1)), including crossings.", "a,c,d_j,H_j,S_j,M_j": "Local actual-word parameters and masses, defined together in Section 1; the same enclosing a,c are used at every level.", "b(t)": "Number of 1 bits in the nonnegative integer t, with b(0)=0.", "Aend_l": "Uniform entry-radius to terminal-gradient coefficient for a no-turn H_l, M_l+d_(l-1)/10 for l>=1 and 1 for l=0.", "P_4": "Exact normalized terminal objective 2F_4(X_n)/R_0^2.", "u": "Effective normal state vr^2+r^6/4, not assumed monotone.", "A_mass": "Coefficient a/(c-2) in M_l=A_mass c^l+(1-A_mass)2^l."}}
{"id": "3d57065c9de5e34b", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T00:38:43.607947+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max2 full chronological kernel: complete explicit local square factorization in lossless birth coordinates, including the entire constant-gradient charge and original x0 square. Uniform first-boundary signs proved, with r1>3/(2m) and r1~(3sqrt2-1)/(2m). Bulk/right-boundary signs remain OPEN; full m128 interval specialization passes. UNVERIFIED, not original-target closure.", "evidence": "# Chronological kernel: an explicit local square decomposition\n\nStatus: **unverified proof draft**, fifth launch, first continuation round.\nThere is a complete algebraic factorization below, including the constant\ngradient column and the original initial-position variable. Its coefficient\nsigns have passed an outward-interval evaluation at the actual depth128.\nThis is not a uniform proof for all m>=128, a verifier-accepted fact, or a\nsolution of the original minimax problem. No fact_submit was allocated or used.\n\nThe new theoretical question is the sign of explicitly displayed scalar\ncoefficients in the birth coordinates. This is a sufficient condition for\nthe old covariance inequality; equivalence of their positivity is NOT claimed.\n\n## 1. Original word, shared data, and exact certificate identity\n\nFor an integer m>=128 set epsilon=1/m, lambda=1+sqrt(2),\nc=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c, d_j=1+a*c^j, and\nH_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j). Let q=2^m-2 and let h_0,...,h_(q-1)\nbe H_m with its final d_0 removed. Define s_i=sum_(r<i)h_r and S=s_q.\nFor real vectors v,w_0,...,w_(m-1), all using one arbitrary common Gram,\nput p_ij=product_(r<i)(1-h_r/d_j), g_i=v+sum_j p_ij*w_j, and\nx_i=x_0-sum_(r<i)h_r*g_r. The first d_j kills its column at query2^j.\nThus g_q=v and x_q=x_0-S*v-sum_j d_j*w_j.\n\nFor one common array of sampled values and the zero optimum jet, use\nQ_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2. Necessity and sufficiency\nof the entire interpolation array are in accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290.\nDefine, purely algebraically,\n\n J=sum_(i<q) [2s_(i+1)h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>\n             +s_(i+1)||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2].\n\nThe GD norm identity and summation by parts give\n\n ||x_0||^2-2S*f_q+2sum_i h_i Q_(i,star)\n                  +2sum_i s_(i+1)Q_(i+1,i)=||x_q||^2+J.       (1)\n\nIndeed the optimum terms equal\n2sum h_i f_i-||x_0||^2+||x_q||^2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2;\nthe chain terms equal 2S f_q-2sum h_i f_i plus the first two terms of J.\nAll selected multipliers are nonnegative. No unused original interpolation\nconstraint is deleted from the definition of legal data.\n\nLet T be the symmetric matrix of J in (w_0,...,w_(m-1),v). For a word W,\nput P_W(z)=product_(h in W)(1-hz) and S_W=sum W. The zero-clock kernels\nK,T satisfy, for concatenated words U,V,\n\n K_UV=K_U+P_U(z)P_U(w)K_V,\n T_UV=T_U+P_U(z)P_U(w)[T_V+2S_U K_V],                         (2)\n K_[h]=h-h^2(z+w-zw)/2,\n T_[h]=h(h+1)-h^3(z+w-zw).\n\nThese follow by separating the defining time sums, retaining the offset\nS_U in every second-block clock. At z=1/d_j and z=0 these compute the\nactual matrix with O(m^3) scalar operations, without enumerating2^m steps.\nDeletion of the last d_0 changes only T_(v,v), to S(S+1), because all\nresidual columns are already zero. This final diagonal is retained below.\n\nFor comparison with the earlier covariance formulation, D_i=g_i-g_(i+1),\nA_ij=h_i*p_ij/d_j, beta_i=s_(i+1)(s_i+1), beta_q=S(S+1), and\nc_i=(h_i-1)s_(i+1). Expansion of J and completion of the constant square give\n\n J=beta_q||v+sum_i beta_i D_i/beta_q||^2\n       +sum_(i,l)[beta_min(i,l)-beta_i beta_l/beta_q]<D_i,D_l>\n       -sum_i c_i||D_i||^2.                                    (3)\n\nFor example the coefficient of ||g_i||^2 after collecting adjacent terms\nis beta_i-beta_(i-1); this proves (3) by writing g_i=v+sum_(r>=i)D_r.\nConsequently positivity of T is exactly the old covariance domination,\nincluding its constant-mode charge. This is only an equivalence of formal\nquadratic forms, not a test of whether a negative formal direction is legal.\n\n## 2. Lossless birth coordinates and exact sparsity\n\nDefine phi_k(z)=product_(r<k)(1-d_r*z),\nV_k=v+sum_j phi_k(1/d_j)w_j for0<=k<=m, and U_k=V_k-v.\nIn particular V_m=v and U_m=0. The triangular node-evaluation matrix has\npositive diagonal, so these coordinates lose no modal direction.\n\nThe identity\n(1-hz)phi_k=(1-h/d_k)phi_k+(h/d_k)phi_(k+1)\ngives g_i=sum_k B_ik V_k, where B starts at the point mass at0 and\nat step h moves mass from state k to k+1 with fraction h/d_k.\nAn occupied state has d_k>=h. Lower states have already been killed by\ntheir first physical level. Thus B_ik>=0, sum_k B_ik=1, and\n\n B_ik=0 if k>i or i>=2^k, for k<m.                           (4)\n\nThe first zero follows because one step advances at most one state; the\nsecond follows by induction on first physical occurrences.\n\nSet xi_j=d_j*w_j, z=(S+1)v, and y_k=d_k U_k. Then y=R*xi, with\n\n R_kj=(d_k/d_j)phi_k(1/d_j) for j>=k; R_kj=0 for j<k.\n\nLet M=diag(1/d_0,...,1/d_(m-1),1/(S+1)) T diag(same).\nDefine the symmetric matrix\n\n H=diag(R^(-T),1) M diag(R^(-1),1)\n       = [ E, ell ; ell^T, D ],       D=S/(S+1).               (5)\n\nThus J=sum_kl E_kl<y_k,y_l>+2sum_k ell_k<y_k,z>+D||z||^2.\nAll scalings are positive and R is invertible. The vectors remain in one\nreal Euclidean space, with their original cross terms.\n\nThere are useful EXACT zeros:\n\n                 E_kl=0 if l>2^k and k<l<m.                  (6)\n\nEach term in J uses only g_i and g_(i+1). If its state-k coefficient is\nnonzero, (4) forces i+1<=2^k. All states at either query are then <=2^k,\nso state l>2^k cannot enter that term. In particular rows0,1 have no\noff-diagonal entries beyond columns1,2 respectively. Numerical arithmetic\nmay be replaced by zero at precisely these positions because of this proof;\nno other small entries are discarded.\n\n## 3. Exact square-factor lemma, with the complete constant load\n\nThe following is an algebraic identity for ANY symmetric real m-by-m matrix\nE and real column ell, with m>=3. Define\n\n e_i=E_(i,i+2),                    0<=i<=m-3,\n b_i=E_(i,i+1)-2(e_(i-1)+e_i),    0<=i<=m-2,\n r_i=E_ii-E_(i-1,i)-E_(i,i+1)+e_(i-2)+e_i\n                   -sum_(|k-i|>=3)|E_ik|, 0<=i<m.             (7)\n\nAn out-of-range e or adjacent entry is zero. Put alpha_(-1)=0 and\n\n alpha_i=ell_i-alpha_(i-1), 0<=i<m,\n C=sum_(i<m-1)alpha_i^2/b_i + alpha_(m-1)^2/r_(m-1),\n Delta=D-C.                                                   (8)\n\nIf b_i>0 and r_(m-1)>0, with no other sign assumption yet, then\n\n J=sum_(i<m-1)r_i||y_i||^2\n     +r_(m-1)||y_(m-1)+(alpha_(m-1)/r_(m-1))*z||^2\n     +sum_(i<m-1)b_i||y_i+y_(i+1)+(alpha_i/b_i)*z||^2\n     +sum_(i<m-2)e_i||y_i+2y_(i+1)+y_(i+2)||^2\n     +sum_(k<l, l-k>=3)|E_kl| ||y_k+sgn(E_kl)y_l||^2\n     +Delta||z||^2.                                          (9)\n\nFor a zero long-range coefficient use sgn(0)=0; its whole term is zero.\n\nProof. The triple squares supply off-diagonal entry e_i at distance2 and\nentries2e_i at each of its two neighboring pairs. Subtracting them leaves\nthe adjacent coefficient b_i in (7). Their diagonal contributions, together\nwith those of the pair squares, are\nE_(i-1,i)+E_(i,i+1)-e_(i-2)-e_i. Each long-range square contributes\n|E_ik| to the corresponding diagonal. The remaining diagonal is exactly r_i.\nThis proves the residual identity. Finally\n\n sum_i ell_i y_i=sum_(i<m-1)alpha_i(y_i+y_(i+1))\n                                      +alpha_(m-1)y_(m-1)\n\nby ell_i=alpha_i+alpha_(i-1). Completing precisely those displayed squares\nspends C and leaves Delta. This proves (9), including all cross terms.\n\nTherefore the explicit scalar conditions\n\n e_i>=0, b_i>0, r_i>=0 (all i), r_(m-1)>0, Delta>=0            (10)\n\nare SUFFICIENT for the full T to be positive semidefinite. This supplies an\nactual square-factor formula, not an LDL recurrence with unknown pivots.\nThe constant column is paid by the exact alternating loads (8), without a\nmatrix inverse or pseudoinverse. No individual adjacent-swap sign is used.\n\nThe original certificate in (1) has the additional square\n\n ||x_0-S*z/(S+1)-sum_j xi_j||^2.                              (11)\n\nThe change from x_0 to that square's vector is an invertible unit triangular\nchange of coordinates. Thus (9) plus (11) is a factorization on the FULL\noriginal formal space, including x_0. There is no hidden range condition.\n\n## 4. Uniform exact boundary calculation: a coefficient vanishes as 1/m\n\nWrite t=d_0, u=d_1, d=d_2. The first three gradients in birth coordinates\nare V_0,V_1,V_2. Direct expansion of the first two physical steps gives\n\n E_00=(2-t)/t, E_01=(t-1)/u,\n E_11=[2t+u(2-u)]/u^2,\n E_12=(t+u)(u-1)/(u*d).                                     (12)\n\nBy (6), e_0=e_1=0 and both rows have no longer tails. Consequently\nr_0=E_00-E_01 and r_1=E_11-E_01-E_12. Also b_0=E_01,b_1=E_12.\nThe actual parameters are\n\n t=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],\n u=2-epsilon, d=sqrt(2)+2-lambda*epsilon^2.\n\nFor 0<epsilon<=1/128, one has 7/5<t<283/200, 199/100<u<2, and d<7/2.\nThe bound on t follows, for instance, from sqrt(2)>141/100 and\n2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]<5/768. Thus\n\n r_0>117/283-83/398>1/5,       b_0>1/5,       b_1>12/25.       (13)\n\nFor b_1, use (t+u)(u-1)> (339/100)(99/100) and\nu*d<683/100, since sqrt(2)<283/200 gives d<683/200.\n\nMore significantly,\n\n r_1>3epsilon/2, and r_1/epsilon -> (3sqrt(2)-1)/2.            (14)\n\nHere is a direct sign proof. Put delta=d-t-2. Then\ndelta/epsilon=2/[lambda(1+epsilon)]-lambda*epsilon>3/4,\nusing lambda<5/2 and epsilon<=1/128. Multiplication by the positive u^2*d\ngives\n\n u^2*d*r_1\n =epsilon[(t+4)(t+2)+2-epsilon(2t+7)+epsilon^2]\n       +[2+epsilon(1+t)-epsilon^2]*delta.\n\nThe bracket in the first term exceeds509/25-5/64, and the coefficient\nof delta is at least2. The total therefore exceeds21epsilon, whereas\nu^2*d<14. This proves the first assertion of (14). Dividing by epsilon\nand taking the explicit parameter limits gives\n(8+10sqrt(2))/(8+4sqrt(2))=(3sqrt(2)-1)/2.\n\nIn particular the finite observed minimum around .01267 cannot be promoted\nto a uniform positive lower bound for every r_i. The factorization only\nneeds r_1>=0; its constant-column completion uses r_(m-1), not r_1.\n\nThe first constant loads are also exact:\n\n ell_0=1/(S+1),\n ell_1=[t^2/u+t+1]/(S+1),\n alpha_0=1/(S+1), alpha_1=[t^2/u+t]/(S+1).                    (15)\n\nThese follow by summing the U_0-v and U_1-v coefficients in the same two\nsteps. No independent scalar heights have been chosen.\n\n## 5. Bounded computation, complete definitions, and its limits\n\nThe public ledger is research/max2_chronological_kernel_budget.json.\nIt records attempts BEFORE evaluation and measured cost afterward. Outward intervals, full word m128, precision100: all factors in (10) passed.\n\nThe point m128 test has min residual LDL pivot .0355585548633133 and final\nconstant-column pivot .744470636576883 in the positive scaling preceding(5).\nNo pivot is used as a uniform theorem. At precision150 the smallest physical\nratio retains101 decimal digits beyond the leading one; reconstruction\nresiduals are at most2e-150 for T and1e-149 for the augmented matrix.\n\nThe interval run encloses each exact algebraic entry using rounded-down and\nrounded-up Decimal operations. {"id": "9b6c78ae964f4861", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T00:39:17.261091+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max4 round17: a finite-moment criterion excludes BOTH the original conditional-prefix law and an explicit nonproduct low-pair/common-tail mixture with epsilon=N^-1 and full-support weight eta=N^-2, including exact singular boundaries and all positive amplitudes. For actual H_m,m>=1024, V<7(199/200)^((n+1)/8) and the exact B_n ratio tends0. Conditional independence and bounded conditioning are unnecessary. Complete joint UNVERIFIED derivation.", "evidence": "# A finite-moment obstruction for dependent character measures, including singular limits\n\nStatus: **self-contained unverified round-17 joint candidate**. This final\nallowed round extends the measure screen and includes the full proof below.\nThe only correctness predecessors are accepted facts `1252b8823bb5a290`\n(necessary ordered interpolation) and `b98bb807d514e80d` (the exact benchmark\nand its bound). The previous draft03abbfd5bd957e79 is motivation, not a\nmathematical premise. No optimizer, parameter sweep, installation, or fact\nsubmission is used.\n\nThe criterion is applied to TWO entirely specified probability laws in\nSection 8, including a dependent low-prefix distribution and a common-sign\ncoarse tail with vanishing full support. It is a necessary-condition\nobstruction, not a claimed parameterization or solution of arbitrary Grams.\nAll positive amplitude laws and all actual common anchors are covered.\n\n## 1. Statement, actual steps, and the exact finite-moment hypotheses\n\nFix an integer m>=1024. Define\n\n    sigma=1+sqrt(2), delta=1-1/m,\n    c_chr=sigma(1+1/m), a_chr=delta/c_chr,\n    N=2^m, n=N-1.\n\nChoose ANY epsilon in [0,1/1000000] and put p_chr=1-epsilon. The physical\nstep word is\n\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_chr c_chr^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.\n\nEvery subword retains these ORIGINAL enclosing-m parameters. Equivalently,\nh_i=1+a_chr c_chr^(nu_2(i+1)), 0<=i<n, where nu_2(k) is the exponent of 2\ndividing a positive integer k. Put\n\n    t=a_chr, l=1+t, u_k=delta c_chr^(k-1) for k>=1,\n    D_chr=1+l p_chr, beta_chr=7/10.\n\nEvery leaf has physical step l, and a level-k nonleaf bridge has physical\nstep 1+u_k. No step is deleted, permuted, or padded. Give original sample i\nits standard m-bit binary label (i_0,...,i_(m-1)). For a character index\ns=(s_0,...,s_(m-1)) in {0,1}^m define\n\n    chi_s(i)=(-1)^(sum_l s_l i_l),\n    B(s)=(-1)^s_0,\n    C_j(s)=(-1)^(s_1+...+s_j), 1<=j<m.\n\nTake any finite probability weights w_s>=0 summing to one that satisfy\nEXACTLY these first and joint moment conditions:\n\n    E B =1-epsilon,\n    E C_k=-7/10+4epsilon/5 for every 1<=k<m,\n    E[B C_k]=-7/10-epsilon/10 for every 1<=k<m,\n    E[C_1 C_2]<=1/2, E[B C_1 C_2]<=1/2.                    (1)\n\nHere E is the finite sum against the specified weights. The map\ns -> (B,C_1,...,C_(m-1)) is bijective: (-1)^s_1=C_1 and\n(-1)^s_j=C_(j-1)C_j for j>=2. Thus specifying a probability on these signs\nspecifies every character weight. Section 8 gives concrete formulas satisfying\n(1). No conditional independence is assumed in the theorem, and zero weights\nare allowed.\n\nFor arbitrary positive numbers Gamma_0,...,Gamma_n prescribe real vectors\n\n    g_i=(Gamma_i sqrt(w_s) chi_s(i))_s.                    (2)\n\nThe conclusion also holds for any Euclidean realization of the same Gram.\nAssume these really are gradients of ONE convex differentiable 1-smooth\nfunction F with a nonempty minimizer set along the actual GD trajectory:\n\n    X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i g_i, g_i=grad F(X_i).\n\nWrite R_dist=dist(X_0,argmin F)>0 and F_star=min F. Then\n\n    Gamma_i <=(6/5)Gamma_0 for every original sample i,          (3)\n    V_chr:=2(F(X_n)-F_star)/R_dist^2\n              <7(199/200)^((n+1)/8).                          (4)\n\nConsequently the ORIGINAL benchmark B_n in accepted fact\n`b98bb807d514e80d`, with p_bench=log_2(3), satisfies\n\n    B_n V_chr <7(1+4n^p_bench)(199/200)^((n+1)/8) ->0.           (5)\n\nAll constants are uniform for epsilon in the displayed CLOSED interval,\nall measures satisfying (1), all positive amplitudes, and all actual common\nanchors and realizing functions. In particular epsilon may depend on the\noriginal horizon in any way and may equal zero. The proof makes no passage\nto a limiting Gram and assumes no condition-number bound.\n\nThis only bounds this measure class on the unchanged dyadic diagnostic\nwords. It is not an upper bound on unrestricted W_n(H_m) and does not settle\nthe original all-h minimax question. The conclusion for F=f/L,\ng_i=grad f(X_i)/L gives the original normalized objective\n2(f(X_n)-f_star)/(L R_dist^2) for every L>0.\n\n## 2. Kernel identities and analytic rational bounds\n\nFor a binary difference d=i xor j put d_m=0. The character identity is\n\n    chi_s(d)=B(s)^d_0 product_(r=1)^(m-1) C_r(s)^(d_r xor d_(r+1)),\n    <g_i,g_j>/(Gamma_i Gamma_j)=E[chi_s(i xor j)].            (6)\n\nThis follows by inserting (-1)^s_j=C_(j-1)C_j with C_0=1. In particular\n||g_i||=Gamma_i. At an odd-sample transition comprising a level-k bridge and\nits following leaf, the three signs relative to the first sign are\n1,B C_k,C_k. The moments in (1) therefore give their correlations\n\n    w_chr=E[B C_k]=-7/10-epsilon/10,\n    p_chr=E[B]=1-epsilon,\n    z_chr=E[C_k]=-7/10+4epsilon/5.                           (7)\n\nAt two consecutive odd transitions of levels 1 and 2, the three odd signs\nare 1,C_1,C_1 C_2 and the two even signs are B C_1,B C_1 C_2. Define\n\n    z2_chr=E[C_1 C_2]<=1/2,\n    w2_chr=E[B C_1 C_2]<=1/2.                               (8)\n\nThese identities apply at every translated occurrence of that ruler pattern.\nMultiplying each coordinate by its sign at the first sample does not change\nany inner product; the changed prefix variables are still C_1,C_2. Thus no\nindependence or distributional symmetry of higher prefixes enters (7),(8).\n\nWe shall use\n\n    0<=epsilon<=1/1000000, 999/1000<delta<1,\n    413/1000<t<83/200,\n    1413/1000<l<283/200,\n    12/5<c_chr<2417/1000,\n    603/250<D_chr<483/200,\n    2413/1000<u_2<483/200.                                 (9)\n\nThe inequalities 707/500<sqrt(2)<99/70 follow by squaring positive rationals.\nThen t>(70/169)(1023/1025)>413/1000 and\nt<sqrt(2)-1<29/70<83/200. Also\nc_chr<(169/70)(1025/1024)<2417/1000 and\nD_chr>1+(1413/1000)(999999/1000000)>603/250. Finally\nu_2=sigma(1-1/m^2) lies between\n(1207/500)(1-1/1024^2)>2413/1000 and 169/70<483/200.\nAll remaining bounds follow directly from m>=1024 and the chosen epsilon.\nThese are finite analytic estimates, including epsilon=0.\n\n## 3. Original pair constraints, with the intervening gradient retained\n\nFor the actual values f_i=F(X_i)-F_star, accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`\ngives EVERY ordered inequality\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.             (10)\n\nWe use necessary sums of these original inequalities, not a sufficient\nsurrogate. Every function in the statement must also obey all unused pairs\nand both optimum directions.\n\nAt consecutive samples with step 1+u, normalized correlation d, and norm\nratio r>0, the sum of the two ordered inequalities is\n\n    r^2+(u-1)d r-u<=0.                                      (11)\n\nFor u>0 and -1<=d<=1 this implies r<=max(1,u): the polynomial has one\npositive root; its value at 1 is (1-u)(1-d)>=0 if u<=1, and its value\nat u is u(u-1)(1+d)>=0 if u>=1. In particular every leaf has next norm\nat most its preceding norm, since t<1. This step uses no saturation.\n\nNow take one bridge of excess u=u_k and its following leaf. Let A be the\nnorm at the original odd sample before the bridge, rA the norm at the\nintervening even sample, and qA the norm at the following odd sample.\nThe leaf bound gives r>=q. The two physical updates are retained:\n\n    X_after-X_before=-(1+u)g_before-l g_middle.\n\nSumming (10) just for the two odd endpoints, and substituting (7), gives\nexactly\n\n    [Q_(before,after)+Q_(after,before)]/A^2\n      =q^2-u+(u-1)z_chr q+l r(p_chr q-w_chr).                  (12)\n\nSince p_chr q-w_chr>0, replacing r by its lower bound q makes the\nright side no larger. Using (7) then yields the necessary inequality\n\n    D_chr q^2+beta_chr(D_chr-u)q\n                    +(4epsilon/5)(u+t)q-u <=0.                (13)\n\nAll terms involving the discrepancy have their displayed signs. In\nparticular the last positive term can be dropped for a weaker NECESSARY\nbound. Define R_chr(v), for v>0, to be the positive root of\n\n    R_chr(v)^2+(7/10)(1-v)R_chr(v)-v=0.                        (14)\n\nThen every such odd-to-odd transition obeys q<=R_chr(u/D_chr).\nThis cap does not discard the middle vector: (12) is its exact contribution\nand r>=q is a proved inequality on its amplitude.\n\nWe need the following explicit consequences of (9),(14):\n\n    level 1: q<=r_low:=59/125,\n    level 2: q<=501/500,\n    level k>=3: q<eta_chr u_k/D_chr, eta_chr:=9/10.             (15)\n\nFor level 1, u_1=delta<1. At q=r_low the left side of (13) without its\npositive discrepancy term is at least\n(603/250)(r_low^2+(7/10)r_low)-1-(7/10)r_low>0.\nFor level 2, (14) and the argument for (11) give\nR_chr(u_2/D_chr)<=max(1,u_2/D_chr)<(483/200)/(603/250)<501/500.\nFor k>=3, v=u_k/D_chr is at least u_3/D_chr, which is greater than\n[(999/1000)(12/5)^2]/(483/200)>37/18. At q=(9/10)v, the polynomial\nin (14) equals v[(9/50)v-37/100]>0. This proves the last cap.\n\nFor later use the physical level-2 bridge itself satisfies\n\n    norm_after_bridge <=(15/7) norm_before_bridge.             (16)\n\nIndeed its correlation is w_chr. In (11), at r=15/7, its polynomial is\nbounded below by\n(15/7)^2-(283/200)(7/10+1/10000000)(15/7)-483/200>0,\nusing u_2-1<283/200 and -w_chr<=7/10+1/10000000.\n\n## 4. The original directed cycle needs only the two moment upper bounds\n\nConsider original odd samples O_0,O_1,O_2 before and after a level-1\ntransition and then a level-2 transition. At the first occurrence they are\nsamples1,3,5, and the intervening even samples E_1,E_2 are samples2,4.\nThese names are temporary indices, not new iterates. Set\n\n    ||g_(O_0)||=A,\n    ||g_(O_1)||=r A, ||g_(O_2)||=q A,\n    ||g_(E_1)||=a A, ||g_(E_2)||=b A.                        (17)\n\nThe original comparisons in Section3 give\n\n    a>=r, q<=(501/500)r, r<=59/125,\n    b<=(15/7)r,\n    q<=(501/500)(59/125)<473/1000.                          (18)\n\nLet u=u_1=delta, v=u_2 and form the ORIGINAL directed cycle\n\n    T_cyc=Q_(O_0,O_2)+Q_(O_1,O_0)+Q_(O_2,O_1).              (19)\n\nAll function values and the common anchor cancel. The displacements are\nexactly\n\n    X_(O_1)-X_(O_0)=-(1+u)g_(O_0)-l g_(E_1),\n    X_(O_2)-X_(O_1)=-(1+v)g_(O_1)-l g_(E_2).\n\nSubstitution into (19), retaining both original even gradients and using\n(7),(8), yields\n\n    T_cyc/A^2 = -u+r^2+q^2+(u-v-1)z_chr r\n                +v z_chr r q-z2_chr q\n                +l a(p_chr r-w_chr)+l b(p_chr q-w2_chr).   (20)\n\nThe two upper bounds in (8) may be used monotonically because q,b>0:\n-z2_chr q>=-q/2 and b(p_chr q-w2_chr)>=b(p_chr q-1/2).\nBy (18), p_chr q-1/2<0; therefore b<=(15/7)r gives\nb(p_chr q-1/2)>=(15/7)r(p_chr q-1/2).\nAlso p_chr r-w_chr>0, so a>=r gives the corresponding lower bound for\nthe other even gradient. No sign assumption on p_chr q-w2_chr is needed.\nConsequently\n\n    T_cyc/A^2 >= -u+D_chr r^2+q^2+C_chr r+E_chr r q-q/2,\n    C_chr=(u-v-1)z_chr-l w_chr-(15/14)l,\n    E_chr=v z_chr+(15/7)l p_chr.                           (21)\n\nThe constants obey\n\n    C_chr>29/25, E_chr>4/3.                               (22)\n\nFor C_chr, since u<=1, (7),(9) give\n\n    C_chr >= v(7/10-4epsilon/5)-(13/35)l\n       >(2413/1000)(7/10-4/5000000)\n                           -(13/35)(283/200)>29/25.\n\nThe discarded epsilon contribution in -w_chr-(15/14) is epsilon/10>=0.\nFor E_chr, the lower estimate is\n\n    E_chr >= -(7/10)v+(15/7)l(1-epsilon)\n       >-(7/10)(483/200)\n          +(15/7)(1413/1000)(999999/1000000)>4/3.\n\nSuppose q>=2/5. Then (18) gives r>=(500/501)q>(499/500)q. Equations\n(21),(22), u<=1 and D_chr>12/5 imply\n\n    T_cyc/A^2 >= P_chr(q),\n    P_chr(q)=[(12/5)(499/500)^2+1+(4/3)(499/500)]q^2\n                 +[(29/25)(499/500)-1/2]q-1.              (23)\n\nBoth coefficients are positive. Hence P_chr increases on q>=0, and\n\n    P_chr(2/5)=216143/11718750>1/100.                      (24)\n\nIt follows that T_cyc>A^2/100. One of the THREE ORIGINAL ordered residuals\nin (19) exceeds A^2/300. This is illegal for any function obeying (10),\nregardless of the anchor and all chosen values. Thus every actual legal\ntrajectory must obey\n\n    Gamma_(O_2)/Gamma_(O_0)<2/5.                           (25)\n\nThis is an all-amplitude constraint. Both intervening leaf losses were\nallowed to vary freely and both intervening even gradients were retained.\nIt uses only the finite moment upper bounds (8), not conditional independence,\nstrict Gram definiteness, a saturated adjacent inequality, or a limiting\ncontinuity argument.\n\n## 5. Pay the total loss over the entire original word\n\nIt is convenient to index the odd samples by r, writing\nO_r=Gamma_(2r+1), 0<=r<N/2. Between O_r and O_(r+1) there are exactly\nthe original bridge of level 1+nu_2(r+1) and its following leaf. This is\nindexing only: all the underlying gradients remain in (12),(20).\n\nLet J_0 be the empty list and J_j=(J_(j-1),j,J_(j-1)). The list of these\nodd-transition levels is J_(m-1). A block J_2=(1,2,1) first obeys (25)\nand then the level-1 cap in (15), so its total amplitude ratio is less than\n\n    Q_chr=(2/5)(59/125)=118/625.                            (26)\n\nFor any occurrence of J_(j-1), j>=3, multiply this bound over its\n2^(j-3) disjoint J_2 blocks, and the last bound in (15) over the remaining\nlevels k>=3. The exact counts are\n\n    sum_(k=3)^(j-1)2^(j-k-1)=2^(j-3)-1,\n    sum_(k=3)^(j-1)(k-1)2^(j-k-1)=3*2^(j-3)-j.              (27)\n\nThese follow by induction in j, starting at j=3 where both sums are empty.\nSince u_k=delta c_chr^(k-1), the resulting bound is\n\n    O_after/O_before <= L_j,\n    L_j=[D_chr/(eta_chr delta)] c_chr^(-j)\n                            Theta_chr^(2^j/8),\n    Theta_chr=Q_chr eta_chr delta c_chr^3/D_chr.             (28)\n\nNo overlapping transition is charged twice. In particular the first leaf\nof the ORIGINAL H_j is outside this odd-transition block and only contributes\nan extra factor at most one.\n\nThe bounds (9) imply\n\n    0<Theta_chr\n      < (118/625)(9/10)(2417/1000)^3/(603/250)\n      <199/200.                                           (29)\n\nThis is an explicit rational inequality; delta<1 was dropped upwards.\nFormula (28) quantifies the TOTAL loss, including every repeated low block\nand every high bridge of the unchanged original horizon. In particular\n\n    Gamma_n/Gamma_0\n      <=[D_chr/(eta_chr delta)]c_chr^(-m)\n                                  Theta_chr^(N/8).         (30)\n\n## 6. Prefix control includes the even samples\n\nFirst every odd sample has norm at most Gamma_0. To prove this, induct on\nthe recursive list J_j. For J_1 the cap is r_low<1. For J_2, the norm after\nits middle level-2 transition is at most r_low(501/500) times its start,\nwhich is less than one, and the two level-1 edges contract. For j>=3,\nthe left J_(j-1) has norm ratio at most L_j by (28). Its following level-j\ntransition has norm ratio less than eta_chr delta c_chr^(j-1)/D_chr.\nTheir product is\n\n    c_chr^(-1) Theta_chr^(2^j/8)<1.                        (31)\n\nThe induction hypothesis controls every prefix in the left block; (31)\ncontrols the starting norm of the right block, where the same induction\nhypothesis applies. Initially O_0<=Gamma_0 by the first leaf comparison.\nThis proves the claim for ALL original odd samples with inherited state.\n\nEvery remaining even sample except X_0 lies immediately after one bridge.\nFor a level-1 bridge, (11) gives even norm at most the preceding odd norm,\nsince u_1<1. For a level-2 bridge the preceding J_1 supplies a factor r_low;\n(11) gives even norm at most u_2 r_low Gamma_0, with\n\n    u_2 r_low<(483/200)(59/125)<6/5.\n\nFor a level-j bridge with j>=3, the preceding J_(j-1) and (11),(28) give\n\n    even norm/Gamma_0 <=u_j L_j\n       =[D_chr/(eta_chr c_chr)]Theta_chr^(2^j/8)\n       <(483/200)/[(9/10)(12/5)]<6/5.                     (32)\n\nAt every occurrence the starting odd norm is already at most Gamma_0.\nTogether these estimates prove (3), including every original intermediate\ngradient that was not an endpoint in the cycle calculation.\n\n## 7. True closest-minimizer normalization\n\nThe minimizer set is nonempty and closed. Its distance from X_0 is attained:\na minimizing sequence may be restricted to a bounded closed ball in the\nfinite-dimensional Euclidean space and hence has a convergent subsequence.\nTake a closest minimizer X_star, without assuming it is the origin. Since\ngrad F(X_star)=0, smoothness implies\n\n    Gamma_0<=R_dist.                                      (33)\n\nThe exact original mass is\n\n    M_chr=1+sum_(i<n)h_i\n         =N+a_chr(c_chr^m-N)/(c_chr-2)<c_chr^m.             (34)\n\nThe equality follows by counting the 2^(m-k-1) level-k steps, including\nleaves, and summing the geometric progression. The inequality follows from\nc_chr>sigma>2 and c_chr(c_chr-2)>sigma(sigma-2)=1>delta,\nso 0<a_chr<c_chr-2. Thus the displayed expression is a strict convex\ncombination of N and c_chr^m, the latter being larger.\n\nUsing (3),(33), the actual chronological updates give\n\n    ||X_n-X_star|| <=R_dist+sum_(i<n)h_i Gamma_i\n       <=[1+(6/5)sum_(i<n)h_i] R_dist\n       <=(6/5)M_chr R_dist<(6/5)c_chr^m R_dist.             (35)\n\nConvexity at X_n gives F(X_n)-F_star<=<g_n,X_n-X_star>.\nCombine (30),(33),(35) to obtain\n\n    V_chr < (12/5)[D_chr/(eta_chr delta)]Theta_chr^(N/8)\n      <7(199/200)^(N/8),                                 (36)\n\nbecause (12/5)(483/200)/[(9/10)(999/1000)]<7. This proves (4).\nEvery radius in this argument is the TRUE distance. If F is an anchored\nprojection interpolant its minimizers form the appropriate polar cone;\nX_star may be nonzero. No inverse-Gram expression for the radius, forced\npositive-cone anchor, or origin-distance substitute is used. The cost of\nthe conservative bound R_dist>=Gamma_0 and the path estimate is visible\nin the finite prefactor in (36).\n\nAccepted fact `b98bb807d514e80d` gives B_n<=1+4n^log_2(3). This proves (5).\nFor completeness its right side tends to zero because the logarithm of\nits exponential factor is ((n+1)/8)log(199/200), whereas the logarithm of\nits polynomial factor is O(log n). This keeps the original B_n and horizon.\n\n## 8. Two explicit weight laws and an actual vanishing-support sequence\n\nAll formulas below are probabilities on the signs (B,C_1,...,C_(m-1)),\nconverted uniquely to weights w_s by the bijection in Section1. These are\nfinite real vectors (2), not an arbitrary unspecified Gram.\n\nFirst, the conditional-product proposal is exactly\n\n    w_s=[(1+(1-epsilon)B(s))/2]\n          product_(j=1)^(m-1)[(1+C_j(s)(1/10-4B(s)/5))/2].  (37)\n\nThe first moment identities (1) follow by conditioning on B. Given B=1,\neach C_j has mean -7/10; given B=-1, its mean is9/10. Directly,\n\n    E C_1 C_2=49/100+4epsilon/25<=1/2,\n    E B C_1 C_2=49/100-13epsilon/20<=1/2.\n\nThus (37) satisfies every hypothesis for ANY epsilon in [0,10^(-6)]. In\nparticular it covers the original specified sequence epsilon=N^(-1), any\nother sequence epsilon_m eventually in this interval, and the exact boundary\nepsilon=0. For positive epsilon all weights are positive. The proof is\nuniform over all of these choices without using continuity.\n\nSecond, here is a genuinely nonproduct low-prefix distribution and a highly\ndependent coarse-tail mixture. Choose eta in [0,1]. For b in {-1,1} set\n\n    mu_1=-7/10, mu_(-1)=9/10,\n    tau_1=1/2-epsilon, tau_(-1)=9/10,\n    pi_b(c_1,c_2)=[1+mu_b(c_1+c_2)+tau_b c_1 c_2]/4.       (38)\n\nThese four probabilities are explicit. For b=1 they are\n(1/10-epsilon)/4 for(++), (1/2+epsilon)/4 for each mixed pair, and\n(29/10-epsilon)/4 for(--). For b=-1 they are37/40 for(++) and1/40 for\neach of the other three pairs. All are positive in the stated epsilon range,\nand their sum is one. Their two means are mu_b and their product mean is\ntau_b, as follows by summing these four values.\n\nLet c_tail=(c_3,...,c_(m-1)); m>=1024 ensures this tail is nonempty. Define\n\n    T_b(c_tail)=(1-eta)[(1+mu_b)/2 * 1_(all tail signs=1)\n                        +(1-mu_b)/2 * 1_(all tail signs=-1)]\n                    +eta product_(j=3)^(m-1)(1+mu_b c_j)/2. (39)\n\nHere 1_(condition) is1 if the condition holds and0 otherwise. The first\npart assigns one common sign to EVERY tail prefix. The second part is a\nfinite product distribution with full support. Both parts give each tail\nsign mean mu_b; their probabilities sum to one. The complete weight is\n\n    w_s=[(1+(1-epsilon)B(s))/2]\n             pi_(B(s))(C_1(s),C_2(s))\n             T_(B(s))(C_3(s),...,C_(m-1)(s)).              (40)\n\nThis specifies every atom. Each prefix has the conditional mean mu_b, so\nthe first moments in (1) are identical to those for (37). The low joint\nmoments are now\n\n    E C_1 C_2=1/2-(4/5)epsilon+epsilon^2/2<=1/2,\n    E B C_1 C_2=1/2-(17/10)epsilon+epsilon^2/2<=1/2.        (41)\n\nThus (40) satisfies the theorem for EVERY eta in [0,1], regardless of\nhow strongly its tail prefixes depend on one another. It is not the\nconditional-product policy (37): already pi_b has a different product mean,\nand for eta<1 the tail variables share a common component.\n\nOne definite original-horizon policy is\n\n    epsilon=N^(-1), eta=N^(-2), standard chronological labels. (42)\n\nFor every finite N=2^m all weights in (40),(42) are strictly positive:\nP(B=b)>0, all four pi_b are positive, and the eta product component is\npositive at every tail pattern. Its full-support tail component tends to\nzero at the specified rate, and its low-bit discrepancy also tends to\nzero. Nevertheless EVERY positive-amplitude realization on actual H_m that\nis legal for one global function has the bound (4),(5). No tuning of a\nnonsaturating amplitude or choice of common anchor is left out of that bound.\n\nThe exact singular boundary epsilon=eta=0 also satisfies (1): at each\nfinite m it is supported on eight sign patterns (B=1, four low pairs,\ntwo constant tails). The same inequalities (12),(20),(28),(35) apply\nthere directly and give the same bound. This makes no continuity assertion\nbetween ambient spaces or between inverse Gram matrices.\n\n## 9. What is closed and what is still open\n\nThe proof excludes the two explicit policies (37),(42), all rates at which\ntheir displayed epsilon and eta approach zero, and more generally every\nfinite sign measure satisfying the moment hypotheses (1). It allows zero\nweights, unbounded conditioning, all positive amplitudes with arbitrary\nslack, every common anchor, and nonzero closest minimizers. The quantitative\nloss pays for every repeated low block and all high bridges at the actual\noriginal horizon. C62 and any proposed Gram-inverse distance formula are not\nused. The accepted fixed C59 witness is not constrained by (1) and is not\ncontradicted.\n\nThis is NOT an obstruction to all dependent character constructions. For\nexample, keep the same law of B, choose ONE sign V conditionally of mean\nmu_B, and put C_j=V for every j. Its four atom probabilities are\nP(B=b)(1+mu_b v)/2 for b,v in {-1,1}. It has all the required FIRST moments\nbut E C_1 C_2=1 and E B C_1 C_2=1-epsilon, so it violates the two joint\nmoment upper bounds. Mixing this law with (37) with coefficient eta=N^(-2)\nretains full support at positive epsilon and keeps the first joint moment\nstrictly above1/2. No legal trajectory, amplitude rule, useful payoff, or\ncommon anchor for that different policy is asserted here. It is a precise\nunresolved escape from this theorem's hypotheses, not a counterexample to\nits conclusion.\n\nWithin the fixed first-moment chart and positive-amplitude convention,\na competitive construction must therefore violate at least one of\nE C_1 C_2<=1/2 and E B C_1 C_2<=1/2, or change its labels or the stated\nfirst moments. Arbitrary further dependence of the higher prefixes alone\ncannot escape this result while (1) is retained. Changing the two lowest\nprefix joint moments still needs a new full interpolation and true-distance\nargument; Gram positivity alone says nothing about that missing feasibility.\n\nThis entire document is an UNVERIFIED candidate. The original all-schedule\nminimax target remains unresolved; no claim is promoted to the fact graph.\nMatlas searches returned no applicable theorem, and no external theorem is\nused beyond the two accepted facts named at the start.
