UNVERIFIED; original target open.", "evidence": "# Two actual children in one projection body\n\nStatus: joint proof draft, **UNVERIFIED**. Eighth launch, cumulative round18.\nNo solver, parameter sweep, installation or verifier was used. The original\nall-schedule exact-profile target is unresolved. Accepted facts\n1252b8823bb5a290 and b98bb807d514e80d specify the original interpolation model,\nperformance W_n, and exact comparison B_n; every new construction below is\nderived here. Earlier unverified packages are not premises.\n\n## 1. Full input, function, values, and true distance\n\nA child consists of a nonnegative finite word p of length ell, actual vectors\nx_i,g_i for 0<=i<=ell, and the optimum sample x_*=g_*=0. Set\n\n    K=conv(0,g_0,...,g_ell), y_i=x_i-g_i,\n    x_(i+1)=x_i-p_i g_i,\n    <g_j-g_i,y_i><=0 for every i,j,\n    <g_i,y_i>>=0 for every i,\n    x_ell=g_ell=w !=0.                                      (1)\n\nInclude ALL samples, even duplicated ones or those at zero steps. Nonzero\nresiduals y_i are permitted. Set\n\n    A=1+sum p_i, R_i=sum_(t=i)^(ell-1) p_t,\n    R^2=||x_0||^2, Delta=A^2||w||^2-R^2,\n    kappa=A^2||w||^2/R^2.\n\nThe values and common function are specified, not freely shifted:\n\n    F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2),\n    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, f_*=0.                        (2)\n\nThe maximizer is the unique Euclidean projection onto K: completing the\nsquare proves this, and its exact optimality condition is (1). Adding the\ntwo projection normals for x,x' proves\n||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2<=<x-x',P_K(x)-P_K(x')>. Thus it is 1-Lipschitz.\nEvaluation of the defining maxima at each other's maximizers bounds the\ndifferentiability remainder by ||x-x'||^2. Consequently F_K is finite,\nconvex, differentiable and 1-smooth, with gradient P_K and values (2).\n\nThe minimizer set is exactly K^polar={z:<z,g><=0 for every g in K}.\nThe forward implication follows by using epsilon*g in (2) and sending\nepsilon down to0; the reverse implication follows directly from (2).\nThe endpoint and GD identity give\n\n    x_0=w+sum_(i<ell) p_i g_i.                              (3)\n\nHence <x_0,z><=0 for every minimizer z, so 0 is a closest minimizer and\ndist(x_0,argmin F_K)^2=R^2. No arbitrary chosen-optimum normalization is used.\nFor the original signed interpolation residual of fact1252b8823bb5a290,\nsubstitution of (2) gives the identity\n\n    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>.                                    (4)\n\nThis includes Q_i*=0 and Q_*i=-<g_i,y_i><=0. Thus checking (1) checks EVERY\noriginal interpolation inequality, including both optimum incidences.\n\nThe input contains these vectors and values, not merely Delta or kappa.\n\n## 2. A real cross Gram block and its exact legality conditions\n\nTake two inputs L,R with capacities A,C, initial squared distances R_L^2,\nR_R^2, and endpoints w_L,w_R. Choose a bridge 1+r with r>0. Write\ne=w_L/||w_L|| and d=x_R,0/R_R. Embed the two spaces in\nR direct-sum e^perp direct-sum d^perp by\n\n    J_L u=(<u,e>, u-<u,e>e, 0),\n    J_R v=(-<v,d>, 0, v-<v,d>d),\n    a_scale=r||w_L||/R_R.                                 (5)\n\nBoth J are real isometries. Use J_L x_L,i,J_L g_L,i for the complete left\ntranscript and a_scale J_R x_R,j,a_scale J_R g_R,j for the complete right\ntranscript. The bridge is exact because\n\n    J_L w_L-(1+r)J_L w_L=-r J_L w_L=a_scale J_R x_R,0.\n\nAll child updates are unchanged. The endpoint is a_scale J_R w_R. The\ncross Gram block, on any pair of child vectors, is explicitly\n\n    <J_L u,a_scale J_R v>\n       =-r <u,w_L><v,x_R,0>/R_R^2.                         (6)\n\nReal PSD follows from (5), not from an unspecified positive block matrix.\nLeft values are unchanged; right values are a_scale^2 f_R,j. Let the parent\nbody be the convex hull of0 and EVERY displayed parent gradient, including\nthe left bridge gradient J_L w_L and the terminal gradient.\n\nWithin-child and optimum normals remain (1), with the right ones scaled\nby a_scale^2. The only additional inequalities are, for every i,j,\n\n    -r <g_R,j,x_R,0><w_L,y_L,i>/R_R^2\n          <= <g_L,i,y_L,i>,                               (7L)\n    -<g_L,i,w_L><x_R,0,y_R,j>\n          <= r||w_L||^2 <g_R,j,y_R,j>.                     (7R)\n\nThese follow by substituting (6) into the two cross normals; conversely they\nare those normals. They include every old gradient against every new residual\nand the bridge gradient against all right samples. Thus (7L)-(7R) are exact\nnecessary and sufficient conditions for this SPECIFIED function and data.\nWhen they hold, (2)-(4) provide the common function, values, all Q inequalities,\nand true initial distance R_L, since (3) uses retained left gradients.\n\nThe physical parent capacity is P=A+C+r. Its exact ratio is\n\n    kappa_P=P^2 r^2 kappa_L kappa_R/(A^2 C^2).              (8)\n\nFor the source primitive bridge rP=AC, equivalently\nr=(-A-C+sqrt((A+C)^2+4AC))/2, this is\n\n    kappa_P=kappa_L kappa_R,\n    Delta_P=R_L^2[(1+Delta_L/R_L^2)(1+Delta_R/R_R^2)-1].    (9)\n\nThe cross term in (9) is an exact consequence of a legal construction ONLY\nwhen (7) holds. No favorable interaction is presumed from two scalar defects.\n\n## 3. The planar core blocks even arbitrary transverse correlations\n\nHere is the previously detected planar core, rederived. Set s=sqrt2,\nlambda=1+s, theta=s-1. At its source word (s,2,s), take\n\n    U=1/lambda^2, eta=-(1/2+theta)/s,\n    u=(1/lambda,0), v=(eta,sqrt(1/2-eta^2))/lambda,\n    w=-u-s v,\n    (x_i,g_i)=((lambda u,u),(u,u),(-u,v),(w,w)).             (10)\n\nWe have eta=-1+1/(2s), so eta^2<1/2. The residuals are s u,0,-u-v,0.\nAt sample0 all four vertex normals against0,u,v,w are nonpositive:\n<v,u>=eta U<U and <w,u>=(s-3/2)U<U. At sample2,\n<v,y>=(1/2-1/(2s))U>0, the u normal is -U/2, and\n<w-v,y>=0 by direct substitution. The zero normal is -<v,y><0.\nThus (10) satisfies the full input contract. It has R^2=1,\n||w||^2=1/lambda^4, A=lambda^2 and kappa=1.\n\nConsider ANY isometric endpoint-aligned splice with this right child,\nregardless of how transverse coordinates are correlated. The actual bridge\ngradient necessarily represents -x_R,0/r in right-child units. At right\nsample2 its required normal is\n\n    <-x_R,0/r-v,y_2><=0,\n    r >= -<x_R,0,y_2>/<v,y_2>=lambda^2=A.                  (11)\n\nThe equality follows from\n-<x_R,0,y_2>=lambda(1+eta)U and\n<v,y_2>=theta(1/2)U/s. The balanced primitive bridge is r=theta A<A.\nIt therefore fails this ORIGINAL cross-child inequality even at zero defect.\nChanging the remaining cross Gram block cannot affect (11).\n\nFor completeness the actual modified planar cores use\np0=(16-11s)/3, q0=(8s-10)/3, 0<=t<=1/50,\na=s-p0 t, b=2+q0 t, z=b-1, A=1+2a+b,\n\n    U=1/(a+1)^2,\n    eta=-[z^2+(a+1)/2]/[z(a+2)],\n    u=(1/(a+1),0), v=(eta,sqrt(1/2-eta^2))/(a+1),\n    w=-z u-a v,\n    (x_i,g_i)=(((a+1)u,u),(u,u),(-z u,v),(w,w)).             (12)\n\nSquaring rational endpoints gives 1.4142<s<1.4143. It follows that\n1.411<a<1.415, 1<=z<1.01, -.654<eta<-.639,\n-.1<<u,w>/U<0, -.062<<v,w>/U<0. Also\n||w||^2=J U with\nJ=[z^2(2-a)+a(a^2-2)/2]/(a+2), and .169<J<.177.\nThese estimates follow by separately bounding the displayed numerators;\nin particular .654^2<1/2 proves a real Gram realization. At sample0 the\nu-normal tests hold by these correlations. At sample2 the normals against0\nand u are (z eta+1/2)U<0 and\n[-z+(z-1)eta+1/2]U<=-U/2; the w normal is identically0.\nSamples1 and3 have zero residual. Thus these are actual cores, not formal jets.\n\nWriting N=z^2(2-a)+a(a^2-2)/2 and mu=q0-2p0=10s-14>0,\ndifferentiation of A^2 J/(a+1)^2 gives logarithmic derivative\n\n    2mu/A+2p0/(a+1)+N'/N+p0/(a+2),\n    N'=2z(2-a)q0-p0[-z^2+(3a^2-2)/2].\n\nThe rational bounds .147<p0<.149, .437<q0<.439, .578<N<.603\ngive N'>.361 and the displayed logarithmic derivative>3/4.\nIts initial value is1; integration proves kappa>=1+3t/4.\nThese defective inputs do not repair the alignment obstruction:\n-<x_0,y_2>/<v,y_2>=(a+1)(z+eta)/(-z eta-1/2)>4.97,\nwhereas the balanced source bridge theta A<2.43. The ratio bound uses\n(a+1)(z+eta)>2.411*.346 and -z eta-1/2<1.01*.654-.5.\nThus this entire parameter interval fails the bridge normal, independently\nof any free transverse correlation.\n\n## 4. A second actual core retains its nonzero first post-step residual\n\nFor arbitrary a>=0, b>1 put c=a, H=a+1, z=b-1, xi=z/H>0, and take\northogonal vectors U=(1,0), V=(0,sqrt(xi)). The word is (a,b,a), and\n\n    x_0=(H+z)U+H V,       g_0=U,\n    x_1=b U+H V,         g_1=U,\n    x_2=H V,             g_2=V,\n    x_3=V,               g_3=V.                            (13)\n\nThe body is conv(0,U,V). At sample0 the V normal is z-(a+b-1)=-a.\nAt sample1 it is z-(b-1)=0. At sample2 the residual is aV and its U and0\nnormals are -a||V||^2. At sample3 the residual is0. At samples0 and1,\nthe zero normals are -(a+b-1) and -(b-1). This lists every vertex normal.\nThe value formula (2) and true distance proof (3) apply; in particular\ny_1=zU+HV is nonzero and is retained everywhere below.\n\nWriting D=1+3xi+xi^2, its exact data are\n\n    A=H(2+xi), R^2=H^2 D, ||w||^2=xi,\n    kappa=xi(2+xi)^2/D,\n    kappa-1=(1+xi)(xi^2+2xi-1)/D.                          (14)\n\nAll four boundary pairings <g_i,w>,<y_i,w>,<g_i,x_0>,<y_i,x_0>\nare nonnegative. Therefore ANY two such cores satisfy (7): each left side\nis nonpositive and each right side nonnegative. Formula (9) is consequently\nan actual, loss-free, simultaneous two-defect aggregation for these inputs.\n\nFor the same modified word as (12), H=lambda-p0 t and z=1+q0 t.\nThen xi>theta for t>0; (14) proves kappa>1. This is a SECOND defective\ncore for the SAME physical three-step word, including the face missed by\nthe x_1=g_1 restriction. In fact xi-theta=(q0+theta p0)t/H>t/5,\nand differentiating the middle expression in (14) gives\nkappa'(xi)=1+(2xi+3)/D^2>1. Thus kappa>=1+t/5 uniformly on 0<=t<=1/50.\nAt t=0, kappa=1 exactly. This is a direct algebraic proof, not a reused draft.\n\n## 5. Exact boundary state and a finite obstruction to repeating the join\n\nFor samples with e_i=<g_i,y_i>>0 define\n\n    a_i=<g_i,w>/||w||^2, c_i=<g_i,x_0>/R^2,\n    B_i=<w,y_i>/e_i, D_i=<x_0,y_i>/e_i,\n    k=<x_0,w>/R^2, zeta=||w||^2/R^2.                       (15)\n\nInclude all gradient samples in a,c. If a residual has e_i=0, use the original\ninequalities (7); division by e_i is not licensed. In the seed (13), e_i=0\nonly for zero residuals, and this property survives isometric joins.\nFor two identical children the tests reduce exactly to\n\n    r max_(i,j)(-c_j B_i)<=1,\n    max_(i,j)(-a_i D_j)<=r.                                (16)\n\nThe parent's retained boundary data are\n\n    a_left=-[k/(r zeta)]a,       a_right=a,\n    B_left=-r k B,              B_right=B,\n    c_left=c,                   c_right=-r k c,\n    D_left=D,                   D_right=-[k/(r zeta)]D,\n    k_parent=-r k^2, zeta_parent=r^2 zeta^2.                (17)\n\nThese follow by substituting (6), and preserve each sample rather than\nassuming extrema are sufficient for an arbitrary Gram completion.\n\nNow repeat the identical-child construction with source primitive bridges:\nA_j=lambda^j A_0, r_j=theta A_j, H_0=(a,b,a), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+r_j,H_j).\nUse seed (13) with xi>=theta. Its length is3, so H_j has length4*2^j-1.\nSet omega=H/A_0=1/(2+xi)<=theta and q=A_0 k_0=omega kappa_0.\nThe exact formulas give 0<q<1 because\nD-xi(2+xi)=1+xi>0. Set u=lambda omega<=1 and v=theta q<1.\nIn the seed, max a=1, max B=1, max D_i=A_0, and\nmax c=c_*=(1+xi)/(H D); all four lists are nonnegative. The extrema are\nattained: B=1 and D=A_0 at sample1, and c=c_* at sample0.\n\nAfter the first join their signed extrema are\n\n    a in[-u,1], B in[-v,1], c in[-v c_*,c_*], D_i in[-u A_0,A_0]. (18)\n\nFor later formal joins, (17) gives A_j k_j=-q^(2^j) and\nk_j/(r_j zeta_j)=-lambda omega^(2^j) for j>=1.\nThus the newly multiplied pieces in (17) have factors between0 and1,\nand every extremum in (18) persists at an actual inherited sample.\nConsequently, for every j>=1, the exact tests (16) are\n\n    r_j v c_*<=1,      u A_0<=r_j.                         (19)\n\nThe second holds for all j>=1 because u<=1 and r_1=A_0. For the first set\n\n    T(xi)=xi(2+xi)^2(1+xi)/(1+3xi+xi^2)^2.\n\nThen r_j v c_*=lambda^(j-2) T(xi). We have 0<T(xi)<1 because\nD^2-xi(2+xi)^2(1+xi)=xi^3+3xi^2+2xi+1>0.\nAlso T is increasing: its positive logarithmic derivative has numerator\n2+xi+xi^2+xi^3 after multiplication by xi(1+xi)(2+xi)D.\nAt xi=theta, T=2-s>theta. Therefore joins j=0,1,2 are legal, producing\nactual H_1,H_2,H_3 of lengths7,15,31, with kappa_j=kappa_0^(2^j).\nBut join j=3 necessarily fails since lambda T(xi)>=lambda(2-s)=s>1.\n\nThis failure is a SPECIFIC original inequality. In the proposed H_4 of\nlength63, left sample25 inherits B=-v (base sample1, then two right-copy\nembeddings); right sample32 inherits c=c_* (the right child's first sample).\nIts signed interpolation residual is\n\n    Q_(32,25)=e_25[lambda T(xi)-1]>0,                       (20)\n\nwhere e_25>0. Both children H_3 are legal, including for every t>0 above,\nand both carry positive defects. Their next rank-one cross block is real\nPSD and satisfies GD, but violates (20). This pinpoints why the formal\nproduct kappa_0^16 is NOT a valid next-level witness.\n\n## 6. Calibration, zero mass, and scope\n\nAt t=0 the actual seed and its first three legal joins have kappa=1,\nso the construction gives no spurious positive source defect. The illegal\nnext join is discarded rather than used against an upper certificate.\nThe exact finite comparison remains B_n, not an assumed Silver equality.\nA subsequent objective-tail conversion with the exact source ConPD bridge\nhas payoff (1+delta)/B_concat with its actual mass B_concat; its derivation\nand calibration are included in the next joint continuation package.\n\nThe generic input and cross tests in sections1-2 include empty or zero-mass\nchildren: then A=1, all child points and gradients equal w, and y_i=0.\nNo test is divided by a zero residual or a zero mass. The obstruction in\nsection5 concerns the explicitly nonempty orthogonal seed with b>1;\nit does not extend a nonempty-right dilution claim to empty right blocks.\n\nThe modified-seed repeated-word family changes the leading primitive mass:\nA_j(t)=lambda^j[lambda^2+(10s-14)t], whereas A_j(0)=lambda^(j+2).\nTheir ratio is the fixed number1+(10s-14)t/lambda^2>1. This is not the\npreviously closed common-offset family with only O(n) additional mass.\nThe actual unbuffered child hypotheses have been proved only through H_3;\n(20) forbids extending this particular construction further. No all-h\ndecomposition, primitive-capacity bound, or asymptotic conclusion follows.\n\nThe next operation is to retain both actual children but add explicit\nconstant-gradient coordinates to pay for their signed boundary correlations.\nIts true-distance cost must be carried in the ratio law; it is not a free\ntranslation, a free function-value shift, or a consequence of PSD alone.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max18_two_child_endpoint_alignment", "attempt_type": "direct", "subgoal": "Full actual two-child join, seed audit and repeatability.", "status": "partial", "artifact": "research/max_two_child_projection_join.md", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d"], "used_examples": ["26bdb71d65c1f949", "34ec3c403f4b605d"], "counterexample_search_for_stuck_subgoal": {"performed": true, "result": "refuted", "summary": "Right planar sample2 versus the actual bridge gradient; then cross pair(32,25) at the fourth repeated orthogonal-transverse join."}, "key_stuck_points": ["Free transverse correlation cannot repair the planar bridge violation.", "Conditional-orthogonal composition has signed boundary ratios which persist; a growing bridge eventually violates a left normal."], "new_fact_submit_calls": 0, "new_solver_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"kappa_core": "A^2||w||^2/R^2 for an actual core-ended projection transcript of capacity A and true initial distance R.", "Delta_core": "A^2||w||^2-R^2 for that same transcript.", "J_L": "Explicit left isometric embedding along the common endpoint/initial axis in equation5.", "J_R": "Explicit right isometric embedding with the opposite common axis in equation5.", "a_scale": "Right-child scale r||w_L||/R_R used by the actual bridge1+r.", "T_seed": "xi(2+xi)^2(1+xi)/(1+3xi+xi^2)^2, the exact scalar in the original cross-normal obstruction.", "xi_seed": "(b-1)/(a+1)>0 for the orthogonal-prefix three-step core."}}
{"id": "dda58b63602b99f9", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-27T00:52:02.249333+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C70 COMPLETE UNVERIFIED frontier extension: for actual enclosing H_m, m>=512, ALL powers 2<p<=m log(m)/64 fixed during each run and ALL r_0!=0,v_0>=0 obey the original-radius chronological turn budget, the full retained J charge, P_p<=1/(2S_m), and exact B_n P_p<125/256<1/2, uniformly with limsup<=lambda/(2e). The optimized capacity criterion mu(2p-2,(c-1)/(c-2)) >= c[c(c-2)-1]/(c-2) also covers every eventual range p<=kappa m log(m), kappa<1/[4(1+sqrt(2))^2]. Full joint proof reproduced; no numerical or verifier call. This is a proper-family exclusion, not unrestricted W_n or original-target closure.", "evidence": "# C70: logarithmically enlarged power range with a complete chronological budget\n\nStatus: COMPLETE UNVERIFIED joint candidate, fifth launch round 2,\ncumulative round 13. No verifier request or numerical experiment was made.\nThe accepted predecessor b98bb807d514e80d supplies only the exact benchmark\nrecursions. Every function, state, capacity, chronology, and benchmark\nestimate used below is derived here; C68/C69 are not proof premises.\n\n## 1. Statement and notation\n\nFor an integer m>=512 put\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/m,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c, d_j=a c^j,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),\n    n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.\n\nThese H_j are schedule words, not the conjugate quadratics with the same\nletter in another project fact. All embedded words use the SAME a,c.\nThe physical steps are h_t=1+d_(nu_2(t)), 1<=t<=n, where nu_2(t) is\nthe exponent of 2 in t. Write b(t) for the binary digit sum, b(0)=0.\n\nChoose a real power p fixed throughout the run, satisfying\n\n    2<p<=m log(m)/64.                                      (1)\n\nThe logarithm is natural. Define\n\n    phi_p(r)=|r|^p/p, K_p={(r,s):s>=phi_p(r)},\n    F_p(X)=max_(g in K_p)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2),\n    eta=v+phi_p(r), u=eta |r|^(p-2),\n    X_p(r,v)=(r(1+u),-v), G_p(r)=(r,phi_p(r)), v>=0.\n\nStart at any r_0!=0,v_0>=0 and run X_t=X_(t-1)-h_t grad F_p(X_(t-1)).\nWrite x_t for its horizontal coordinate, R_t=|x_t|, and call t a turn\nwhen x_(t-1)x_t<0. The exact closest-minimizer initial distance is R_0.\nThe joint conclusions are\n\n    R_t<=R_0 at every state;\n    R_t<=c^(-b(t))R_0 at every turn;\n    P_p:=2F_p(X_n)/R_0^2<=1/(2S_m).                       (2)\n\nFor the exact denominator B_n from b98bb807d514e80d,\n\n    B_n P_p<125/256<1/2,\n    limsup_(m->infinity) sup_(2<p<=m log(m)/64,r_0!=0,v_0>=0)\n         B_(2^m-1) P_p<=lambda/(2e)<1/2.                 (3)\n\nHere e=exp(1). In particular the initial state and the power may both\ndepend on the enclosing horizon. The retained moving-normal cost is\n\n    J_all=sum_(t=1)^n |r_(t-1)|^(p-2)(v_t-v_(t-1))/(p-2).\n\nIt obeys the explicit global charge\n\n    J_all<=min{\n       |r_0|^(p-2)(v_n-v_0)/(p-2),\n       R_0 |r_0|^(2p-3)[2(1+1/c)^m-1]/[p(p-2)]\n    },                                                   (4)\n    J_all<=p^(-2/(2p-1)) R_0^((4p-4)/(2p-1))\n                         [2(1+1/c)^m-1]/(p-2).           (5)\n\nEvery factor p-2 is retained. No uniform assertion about J_all as p\ndecreases to 2 is made. The objective estimate does not use (5) as an\no(1) error. This is a proper-family exclusion, not a bound on W_n for\nall convex smooth functions and not a solution of the original target.\n\nSection 4 also gives an explicit sufficient condition wider than (1).\nAsymptotically it covers every range 2<p<=kappa m log(m) with fixed\n0<kappa<1/[4lambda^2], once m is sufficiently large.\n\n## 2. Global legality, all ordered pairs, and the actual state\n\nK_p is closed, convex, and nonempty. Completing the square shows that\nthe defining maximum is finite and attained uniquely at Pi_(K_p)(X),\nthe Euclidean projection. Its two normal inequalities imply\n\n    ||Pi_(K_p)(Y)-Pi_(K_p)(X)||^2\n       <=<Pi_(K_p)(Y)-Pi_(K_p)(X),Y-X>.\n\nIn particular the projection is 1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two defining\nmaxima gives\n\n    0<=F_p(Y)-F_p(X)-<Pi_(K_p)(X),Y-X>\n      <=<Pi_(K_p)(Y)-Pi_(K_p)(X),Y-X><=||Y-X||^2.\n\nThe remainder divided by ||Y-X|| tends to zero, proving differentiability\nwith gradient the projection. F_p is convex as a supremum of affine\nfunctions and has 1-Lipschitz gradient.\n\nSince phi_p'(r)=r|r|^(p-2),\n\n    X_p(r,v)-G_p(r)=eta*(phi_p'(r),-1).\n\nFor any (z,s) in K_p the inner product of this vector with\n(z,s)-G_p(r) is at most\neta*[phi_p'(r)(z-r)-phi_p(z)+phi_p(r)]<=0 by convexity.\nThus grad F_p(X_p(r,v))=G_p(r), and direct substitution gives\n\n    2F_p(X_p(r,v))\n      =r^2+(2-2/p)v|r|^p+(2p-1)|r|^(2p)/p^2.             (6)\n\nFor any ordered pair of such states, putting f_i=F_p(X_i), its exact\nsmooth-convex interpolation residual is\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2\n        =<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>\n        =eta_j*[phi_p'(r_j)(r_i-r_j)-phi_p(r_i)\n                                        +phi_p(r_j)]<=0. (7)\n\nThis includes every pair, regardless of temporal separation.\n\nZero is feasible in the maximum. At (0,y), y<=0, all feasible inner\nproducts minus half the squared norm are nonpositive. At x!=0 a\nsufficiently small r with the sign of x gives a positive value, since\nxr dominates the terms of orders 2,p,2p. At x=0,y>0 a sufficiently\nsmall positive vertical g gives a positive value. Therefore\n\n    min F_p=0, argmin F_p={(0,y):y<=0},\n    dist(X_p(r,v),argmin F_p)=|r|(1+u)=R.                 (8)\n\nThe actual step, with the original v inherited, is\n\n    v_plus=v+h|r|^p/p, x_plus=x-hr,\n    x_plus=T_(p,v_plus)(r_plus),\n    T_(p,v)(r)=r+v r|r|^(p-2)+r|r|^(2p-2)/p.             (9)\n\nThis T is odd and strictly increasing onto R. Off zero its derivative\nis 1+(p-1)v|r|^(p-2)+(2p-1)|r|^(2p-2)/p>=1; at zero the derivative\nis 1. Thus (9) uniquely determines the true next state on this same\nglobal F_p. Zero is absorbing. Along any nonzero one-sign interval,\nR and |r| decrease and v increases, directly from (9).\n\nPut z=|r|^(2p-2)/p. Then 0<=z<=u and\n\n    2F_p(X)/r^2=1+(2-2/p)u+z/p<=(1+u)^2=R^2/r^2,        (10)\n\nbecause the difference is (2/p)u+u^2-z/p>=u/p+u^2.\n\n## 3. The exact no-turn tradeoff\n\nConsider an actual one-sign nonzero occurrence of H_l, l>=1, with\nterminal magnitude r>0 and normal variable u>0. Let rho and u_mid\nbe the corresponding values immediately before its middle step\n1+d_(l-1). Nonturning and nonabsorption imply u_mid>d_(l-1).\nSince rho>=r and v_mid<=v_end,\n\n    u_mid<=u*(rho/r)^(2p-2).                             (11)\n\nIndeed the v term has exponent p-2 and the pure-power term exponent\n2p-2, and rho/r>=1. Let Apre_l=M_(l-1)+d_(l-1), the mass of the\nsteps through the middle step. All gradients used by those steps\nhave magnitude at least rho; the later ones have magnitude at least r.\nTelescoping actual horizontal displacements therefore proves\n\n    R_entry/r>=M_l+u\n       +Apre_l*((d_(l-1)/u)^(1/(2p-2))-1)_+.             (12)\n\nHere (w)_+=max(w,0). The radius is the true entry radius of this\nparticular block; its vertical state has not been reset.\n\nFor k>0 and beta>0 define the explicit scalar minimum\n\n    mu(k,beta)=min_(t>0) [t+beta*(t^(-1/k)-1)_+]\n      =1,                                      if k<=beta;\n      =(k+1)*(beta/k)^(k/(k+1))-beta,            if k>beta. (13)\n\nFor t>=1 the minimum is 1 at t=1. For 0<t<1 the derivative is\n1-(beta/k)t^(-1-1/k). If k<=beta it is nonpositive throughout this\ninterval, again giving the boundary minimum 1. Otherwise it changes\nsign at t=(beta/k)^(k/(k+1)), giving (13).\nThe minimum is nondecreasing in beta and nonincreasing in k because\neach function being minimized has those monotonicities.\n\nThe mass recursion M_(l+1)=2M_l+d_l, M_0=1, solves to\n\n    M_l=A c^l+(1-A)2^l, A=a/(c-2).                      (14)\n\nSince c(c-2)>lambda(lambda-2)=1>1-epsilon, one has 0<A<1.\nConsequently M_l/d_l>=1/(c-2), and\n\n    Apre_l/d_(l-1)>=beta_c:=(c-1)/(c-2).\n\nTaking k=2p-2, t=u/d_(l-1) in (12) gives\n\n    R_entry>=Aend_l |r_end|,\n    Aend_l=M_l+mu(2p-2,beta_c)d_(l-1), l>=1;\n    Aend_0=1.                                           (15)\n\nFor a zero terminal gradient this inequality holds without division;\nthe trajectory has already been absorbed.\n\n## 4. Quantitative power range and the sharper sufficient condition\n\nDefine\n\n    D_c=[c/(c-2)]*[c(c-2)-1].\n\nBy (14)--(15), the condition\n\n    mu(2p-2,beta_c)>=D_c                                (16)\n\nimplies Aend_l>=c d_l for l>=1. For l=0 the same inequality follows\nfrom Aend_0=1>ac=1-epsilon. We next prove (16) throughout (1).\n\nFor m>=512, sqrt(2)<99/70 gives\n\n    c<(169/70)*(513/512)<121/50<5/2.\n\nThus beta_c>3. Also lambda>12/5 and z/(z-2) decreases for z>2,\nso c/(c-2)<6. Expanding about lambda gives\n\n    c(c-2)-1=2lambda(lambda-1)/m+lambda^2/m^2<8/m,\n    D_c<48/m.                                          (17)\n\nFor the first strict inequality, use lambda<5/2, lambda-1<3/2,\nand (15/2)+(25/4)/512<8.\n\nPut q=2p-1>3 and Q=m log(m)/32. From (1), q<Q. With beta=1 in\n(13), since k=q-1>2,\n\n    mu(q-1,1)=q(q-1)^(-(q-1)/q)-1>log(q)/q.              (18)\n\nTo check this, the logarithm of the positive expression before minus\n1 equals log(q/(q-1))+log(q-1)/q, which exceeds log(q)/q;\nuse exp(z)-1>=z. Since beta_c>3>1, (18) is a lower bound for (16).\nIf 3<q<=9 it yields\n\n    mu(2p-2,beta_c)>log(3)/9>1/9>48/512>=48/m.\n\nThe elementary strict inequality log(3)>1 follows by integrating the\nstrictly convex function 1/t on [1,3] and using its midpoint lower\nbound. If q>=9, apply (13) with beta=3 instead. Its logarithmic form\ngives\n\n    mu(q-1,3)>3 log(q/3)/q.\n\nIndeed log[(mu(q-1,3)+3)/3]\n=log(q/(q-1))+log((q-1)/3)/q>log(q/3)/q.\nThe function log(q/3)/q decreases on [9,infinity), since log(3)>1.\nAlso sqrt(m) log(m)>96: at m=512 use sqrt(512)>16 and\nlog(512)=9log(2)>6, where log(2)>2/3 follows from the strict midpoint\nbound on [1,2]; both factors increase with m. Hence\n\n    log(Q/3)=log(m log(m)/96)>log(m)/2,\n    mu(2p-2,beta_c)>=mu(q-1,3)\n       >3 log(Q/3)/Q>48/m>D_c.                          (19)\n\nThis proves (16) uniformly over (1), without a hidden p-dependent\nconstant.\n\nThere is also a sharper asymptotic consequence of the explicit test\n(16). Fix 0<kappa<1/(4lambda^2) and put p=kappa m log(m). Then\nbeta_c tends to beta_*=(lambda-1)/(lambda-2)=2+sqrt(2), and\n\n    m D_c ->2lambda^2 beta_*.\n\nFor q=2p-1 set\nL_m=log(q/(q-1))+log((q-1)/beta_c)/q. Formula (13) gives\nmu(2p-2,beta_c)=beta_c*(exp(L_m)-1). Direct limits give\nL_m->0 and m L_m->1/(2kappa). Therefore\n\n    m mu(2p-2,beta_c)->beta_*/(2kappa)>2lambda^2 beta_*.\n\nCondition (16) holds for sufficiently large m at this endpoint power.\nIts monotonicity in p proves the same statement UNIFORMLY for every\n2<p<=kappa m log(m). For kappa>1/(4lambda^2), the displayed sufficient\ntest fails eventually at the endpoint power; that fact concerns only\nthis test and is not a trajectory counterexample. No conclusion at\nequality is asserted.\n\n## 5. Actual chronological turns\n\nWe need an elementary binary interval lemma. If integers 0<=s<t obey\nq=b(t-1)-b(s)>=1, then [s,t] contains an interval [A_0,A_0+2^q]\nwhose left endpoint is a multiple of 2^q.\n\nProof: put B=2^q and s=kB+r, 0<=r<B. If r=0 and no such interval\nfits, t<s+B, so t-1 has the same high part k and a low part at most\nB-2. Its digit sum exceeds that of s by at most q-1. If r>0 and\nno interval fits, t<(k+2)B. When t-1 has high part k, its low part\nhas at most q ones while r has at least one. When it has high part\nk+1, its low part is at most B-2 and has at most q-1 ones, while\nb(k+1)-b(k)<=1 and b(r)>=1. Both cases again give at most q-1,\ncontradicting the definition of q.\n\nThe physical steps A_0+1,...,A_0+2^q-1 in such an interval are the\nactual H_q, since nu_2(A_0+j)=nu_2(j) for 0<j<2^q.\n\nLet s<t be consecutive actual turns, allowing s=0 before the first.\nTheir intervening steps form a one-sign interval. Set\n\n    q=max(0,b(t-1)-b(s)), ell=nu_2(t).\n\nFor q>=1 the lemma locates a real no-turn H_q inside that interval.\nIts entry radius is at most R_s and its terminal gradient magnitude\nis at least |r_(t-1)|. Equations (15)--(16) imply\n\n    R_s>=a c^(q+1)|r_(t-1)|.\n\nFor q=0 use R_s>=|r_(t-1)| and ac<1 to get the same bound. At the\nturn, (9) gives\n\n    R_t=(d_ell-u_(t-1))|r_(t-1)|\n       <=c^(ell-q-1)R_s<=c^(b(s)-b(t))R_s,               (20)\n\nusing b(t-1)=b(t)-1+ell. Induction over the actual turns yields\nthe turn estimate in (2). The radius decreases between turns, and\nb(t)>=1 at each positive turn time, so R_t<=R_0 everywhere.\nSince v_t>=v_0 and T_(p,v) increases with v at each positive r,\n(9) also gives |r_t|<=|r_0| everywhere. This proof permits arbitrary\nincreases and decreases in turning level and never resets a right copy.\n\n## 6. Paying all retained normal costs\n\nOn a positive one-sign interval define\n\n    C_p(r,v)=log r+(p-1)v r^(p-2)/(p-2)\n                  +(2p-1)r^(2p-2)/[p(2p-2)].\n\nAt x=T_(p,v)(r), implicit differentiation of C_p(T_(p,v)^(-1)(x),v)\ngives partial_x=1/r and partial_v=r^(p-2)/(p-2). For a positive\nnoncrossing step, first decrease x by hr at fixed v and then increase\nv at the resulting fixed x. The inverse r decreases during both\nmotions. The first integral is therefore at most -h, while the\nsecond is at most r^(p-2)(v_plus-v)/(p-2), with r from before the\nstep. Reflection proves the negative-ray version. This retains the\npositive cost and uses no logarithm at zero or across a turn.\n\nEquation (9) gives\n\n    J_all=sum h_t |r_(t-1)|^(2p-2)/[p(p-2)].\n\nThe bound |r_t|<=|r_0| proves the first part of (4). For the other\npart, the exact total horizontal variation is\n\n    TV_x:=sum h_t|r_(t-1)|=R_0-R_n+2sum_(t a turn)R_t.\n\nA nonturn step contributes the radius difference, including a step\nthat reaches zero; a turn contributes the radius sum. Summing gives\nthe equality. By (20) and the product expansion over m binary digits,\n\n    sum_(t=0)^(2^m-1)c^(-b(t))=(1+1/c)^m,\n    TV_x<=R_0[2(1+1/c)^m-1].                            (21)\n\nCombine |r|^(2p-2)=|r|*|r|^(2p-3) with (21) to prove (4).\nFinally R_0>=|r_0|^(2p-1)/p gives (5). This also bounds the sum\nof J over all noncrossing intervals, as those terms are a subset of\nJ_all. Absorbing states contribute zero thereafter.\n\n## 7. Terminal payoff without loss of the original radius\n\nOn a nonzero one-sign interval of total mass T, terminal magnitude\nr and terminal normal u satisfy, by the same exact displacement sum,\n\n    R_entry>=r(T+1+u), v_end>=T r^p/p,\n    z=r^(2p-2)/p<=u/(T+1).\n\nEquation (6) gives the upper bound [1+k_T u]/[T+1+u]^2 on\n2F_p(X_end)/R_entry^2, where k_T=2-2/p+1/[p(T+1)]. For\nT>=1/(2p-2), the derivative has the sign of\nk_T(T+1)-2-k_T u and maximization yields\n\n    E_p(T)=[(2p-2)T+2p-1]^2\n               /[4p(p-1)(T+1)^2(2T+1)]<=1/(2T).         (22)\n\nFor the last inequality, put A_1=p-1>1 and clear the positive\ndenominator 4p A_1 T(T+1)^2(2T+1). The difference numerator is\n4A_1 T^3+(2A_1^2+6A_1)T^2+(4A_1^2+4A_1-1)T+2A_1(A_1+1)>0.\n\nFor a nonabsorbed full run let s be its last turn, or 0 for no\nturns, and put b=b(s), q=m-b. If q>=1, the binary lemma applied\nwith t=2^m gives an actual H_q in the final one-sign interval.\nIts mass T>=S_q>1 is sufficient for (22), and its entry radius is\nat most c^(-b)R_0. Hence\n\n    P_p<=c^(-2b)/(2S_q).                                (23)\n\nThis is the actual radius ratio; the block has not been renormalized\nas a separate trajectory. From (14), for j>=1,\n\n    S_(j+1)-c S_j=c-1-(c-2)(1-A)2^j<=c-1.\n\nSince S_j>1, it follows that S_(j+1)<(2c-1)S_j<c^2 S_j.\nIteration in (23) proves P_p<=1/(2S_m). If q=0 then s=n,\nand (10),(20) give P_p<=c^(-2m)<1/(2S_m), since S_m<c^m and\nc^m>2. Absorption gives P_p=0 directly. This proves all of (2).\n\n## 8. Exact benchmark comparison\n\nThe accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d defines C_n=S_circle(n)+1,\nB_n=2S_bullet(n)+1. Its exact recursions are\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    C_n=max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(C_k,C_l),\n    B_n=max_(k+l=n-1) G(C_k,B_l),\n    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,\n    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.\n\nWe derive B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1). Set\nalpha=2lambda-1=lambda(4-lambda), Llin=(4x+lambda^2 y)/alpha.\nBoth coefficients in Llin exceed 1. Expansion using lambda^2=2lambda+1\ngives\n\n    alpha^2[Llin^2-(x+y)Llin-xy]\n       =(2x/lambda-sqrt(2)lambda y)^2>=0.\n\nThus Llin>=Omega(x,y). At z=lambda Omega(x,y), the polynomial\nwhose larger root is G(x,lambda y) has value\n\n    z^2-(4x+lambda y)z+4x^2\n       =x[4x+lambda^2 y-alpha Omega(x,y)]>=0.\n\nMoreover z>=lambda(x+y)>=(4x+lambda y)/2, so it is to the right\nof the vertex. Consequently G(x,lambda y)<=lambda Omega(x,y),\nand induction in the exact recursions gives B_n<=lambda C_n.\n\nPut gamma=log_2(lambda)>1, D(t)=(1+t)^gamma-1 and ell(z)=log D(exp z).\nFor t=exp z, differentiation gives\n\n    ell''(z)=gamma*t*(1+t)^(gamma-2)\n         *[(1+t)^gamma-1-gamma*t]/D(t)^2>0.\n\nThe last bracket has value zero at 0 and strictly positive derivative\nfor t>0. Hence D(t)D(1/t)>=D(1)^2=2 by convexity. For x,y>0,\nZ=(x^(1/gamma)+y^(1/gamma))^gamma therefore satisfies\n(Z-x)(Z-y)>=2xy, so Z^2-(x+y)Z-xy>=0 and Z>=x+y.\nThis proves Omega(x,y)<=Z; the zero cases follow by substitution.\nInduction gives C_n<=(n+1)^gamma, and hence\n\n    B_(2^m-1)<=lambda^(m+1).                             (24)\n\nThe finite constants are independent of p. From c<121/50,\n\n    A=(1-1/m)/[c(c-2)]>(511/512)/(2541/2500)>49/50.\n\nAlso c^(-m)<1/50. Thus (14) implies S_m/c^m>24/25. The first\nfive binomial terms, using 1-j/m>99/100 for j=1,2,3, give\n\n    (1+1/m)^m>2+49/100+4/25+1/25>8/3.\n\nCombining (2),(24), lambda<5/2, and (lambda/c)^m<3/8 proves\nB_n P_p<(1/2)*(5/2)*(3/8)*(25/24)=125/256.\n\nAs m tends to infinity, A->1 and\nS_m/c^m=A+(1-A)(2/c)^m-c^(-m)->1, while\n(lambda/c)^m=(1+1/m)^(-m)->1/e. This proves (3), uniformly in all\nadmissible powers and initial states. The same uniform limiting\ncomparison holds in every asymptotic range from Section 4. Finally\ne>1+1+1/2=5/2>lambda, so lambda/(2e)<1/2.\n\n## 9. Scope and audit\n\nThe expanded range (1) replaces the earlier sufficient range m/256\nby m log(m)/64. Condition (16) is explicitly checkable and can be\nused beyond the simpler stated range. A failure of (16) alone is\nnot a failed trajectory or a result about the whole power family.\n\nAll endpoints, zero states, consecutive turns, and negative digit-sum\ndifferences were retained in the proof. All functions are one global\nobjective per run and all embedded blocks retain the enclosing a,c.\nThe positive clock cost is paid with the original horizontal radius\nand optional actual terminal vertical state. No p-dependent factor\nhas been placed in an unspecified error term.\n\nThe literature search returned generic fixed-short-step descent and\nthe usual silver-schedule background, not a result covering this\nmoving-state inequality on the present perturbed word. No external\npaper is a premise. Projection definitions and proofs were already\naudited locally in arXiv:1210.5844v3, Propositions 3.2--3.3; Section 2\nabove supplies the needed proof in full.\n\nThis remains an UNVERIFIED proof candidate. The exact all-horizon,\nall-schedule profile theorem is unresolved.
